# Whereas, the draft regulations as required under section 40 of the Boilers Act, 2025(12 of 2025) were published vide notification of the Government of India in the Ministry of Commerce and Industry Department for Promotion of Industry and Internal Trade

NOTIFICATION New Delhi, the 7th August, 2026 F. No. P-30014/3/2021-Boiler.—Whereas, the draft regulations as required under section 40 of the Boilers Act, 2025(12 of 2025) were published vide notification of the Government of India in the Ministry of Commerce and Industry (Department for Promotion of Industry and Internal Trade) vide number G.S.R. 31(E), dated 14th January, 2026, inviting objections or suggestions from all persons likely to be affected thereby, before the expiry of the period of thirty days from the date on which the said Gazette notification was made available to the public;

AND WHEREAS, the said notification was made available to the public on the 27th January, 2026;

AND WHEREAS, no objections or suggestions were received from the public on the said draft regulations;

NOW, THEREFORE, in exercise of the powers conferred by section 40 of the Boilers Act, 2025, and in supersession of the Indian Boiler Regulations, 1950, except as respects things done or omitted to be done before such supersession, the Central Boilers Board hereby makes the following regulations, namely: ― PRELIMINARY

1. Short title, application and commencement. ─ (1) These regulations may be called the Indian Boiler Regulations,

2026.

(2) These regulations shall apply to all boilers including those working on the principles of natural circulations, forced circulation and forced flow with no fixed steam and water line.

(3) They shall come into force on the date of their publication in the Official Gazette.

2.

Definitions. ─ (1) In these regulations, unless the context otherwise requires,-

(a) "Act" means the Boilers Act, 2025 (12 of 2025);

(b) "Appraisal Committee" means a Committee constituted by the Central Boilers Board to evaluate the performance of a firm or a company applying for recognition as Inspecting Authority;

(c) "Central Boilers Board Examination Standing Committee" means a Committee constituted by the Central Boilers Board to conduct examination for recognition of competent persons;

(d) "design pressure of boiler", -

(i) in relation to a natural or assisted circulation boiler means the maximum allowable working pressure in the steam drum of the boiler;

(ii) in relation to an once through forced-circulation boiler means the maximum allowable working pressure at the final superheater steam outlet;

(e) "design temperature",-

(i) for unheated components means the fluid temperature inside the component, under operation;

(ii) for heated components, means the fluid temperature inside the component under sustained conditions increased by specified code allowances;

(f) "Evaluation Committee" means a committee constituted by the Central Government to evaluate the performance of firm or a company applying for recognition as competent authority, well-known material testing laboratory, well-known steel maker, well-known foundry or forge, well-known tube or pipe maker and well-known remnant life assessment organisation;

(g) "firm" means a firm and includes a company;

(h) "Form" means the forms appended to these regulations;

(i) "maximum allowable working pressure" means the design pressure of any part, adjusted to take into account the pressure drop and hydraulic head;

(j) "pipe" means the cylindrical hollow parts, other than tube, drum or header with a design intent to carry a flow of steam or water or water-steam mixture during normal operation, including those that carry intermittent flow, such as drain pipes;

Explanation.─ For the purpose of this clause , the expression 'pipe' implies that there is no design intent to effect heat transfer through the wall of the pressure part;

(k) "tube" means a tubular pressure part that is either exposed over much of its length to hot gases for purposes of heat transfer or is directly butt welded to such a tubular pressure part including a tube stub on a drum or header.

(2) The words and expressions used herein and not defined but defined in the Act or the rules made thereunder shall have the meanings respectively assigned to them in that Act or the rules.

CHAPTER-1

3. General requirements, application of standard conditions and exception thereto.─

(1) No boiler shall be registered under sub-section (5) of section 12 of the Act and no certificate shall be issued under sub-section (6) of that section, unless the standard conditions in respect of material, design and construction, specified in these regulations are satisfied in respect of such a boiler.

(2) Notwithstanding anything contained in sub-regulation (1), the Chief Inspector may, subject to the provisions of regulation 29, register a boiler and issue a certificate authorising the use thereof, under any of the following circumstances, namely:-

(a) when the material used in the construction of a boiler, steam-pipe, economiser or superheater is not in conformity with these regulations but used in other countries as being suitable for use in the construction of boilers and steam-pipes, provided that such material is not specifically prohibited by these regulations and that the methods of manufacture, fabrication and heat treatment conform to the specified Codes or Standards;

(b) when the constructional features of a boiler, steam-pipe, economiser or superheater are not in conformity with these regulations but are not considered by the Board to be inferior in strength to those prescribed in these regulations and form part of the usual manufacturing practice of boilers and steam-pipes in other countries;

(c) when a boiler, steam-pipe, economiser or superheater has been built in conformity with these regulations but no certificate as required under the regulations is forthcoming;

(3) No structural part of a boiler which is subject to pressure, shall be made of Bessemer process steel or of Cast or Malleable Cast Iron.

(4) Where no specific provision is made in these regulations for design or manufacture of any pressure part, the Inspecting Authority may permit the design, manufacture, stage inspections and certification of such pressure parts including the valves; mountings and fittings conforming to the Codes or Standards like British Standards

(BS), European Standards (EN), American Society of Mechanical Engineers (ASME) Boiler and Pressure Vessel Code, Tubular Exchanger Manufacturers Association (TEMA), Technical Requirements Document

(TRD), GOsudarstvennyy Standrat(GOST) and Japanese Industrial Standards (JIS), which are known to be commonly used in industrially advanced countries and the decision of Inspecting Authorities shall be binding on all Registering Authorities.

(5) The tubes of boilers and heat exchangers made of titanium and other exotic metals may be approved as per international codes, including American Society of Mechanical Engineers (ASME), British Standards (BS), European Standards (EN), Deutsches Institut fur Normung(DIN), Tubular Exchanger Manufacturers Association (TEMA)with the minimum thickness specified in those codes of manufacture.

4. Inspection of boiler to comply with foreign code.─ Notwithstanding anything contained in these regulations, the inspecting authority may inspect any boiler meant for export during the various stages of its construction to comply with the requirements of any foreign code and may grant a certificate in Part-A of Form-I.

5. Exemption of boiler or boiler components.─ Any boiler or boiler component may be exempted under sub-section

(4) of section 38 of the Act in the whole or any part of the State from the operation of all or any of the provisions of the Act, subject to the following conditions, namely:-

(1) design and construction of boiler or boiler component is in accordance with international code or standard including British Standards (BS), European Standards (EN), American Society of Mechanical Engineers (ASME) Boiler and Pressure Vessel Code, Tubular Exchanger Manufacturers Association

(TEMA), Technical Requirements Document (TRD), GOST and Japanese Industrial Standards (JIS);

(2) materials used in the construction are not specifically prohibited under these regulations;

(3) design, construction and materials of boiler or boiler components are satisfied by the State Governments by necessary tests and examination like Remnant Life Assessment, which shall be carried out by the owner.

6. Fee not prescribed in regulations. ─ For any activity, which in the opinion of a State Government involves time and labour and for which no fee has been specified in these regulations, may be determined by the State Government.

7.

Standard requirements. ─ (1) The construction of boilers shall comply with the following requirements, namely:-

(a) all plates and bars used in the construction of boilers shall be tested and found to conform with the regulations hereinafter contained.

(b)

(i) all boilers during construction shall be under the supervision of a competent person and shall be inspected at all stages of construction as specified in Appendix-A.

(ii) tubes and steam-pipes shall also be inspected at the makers' works at the stages as specified in Appendix-A, and the tests conducted by the makers shall also be witnessed by the Competent Person;

(iii) the welders engaged in welding of boilers, boiler components, economisers, feed pipes, steampipes and super-heaters shall possess certificate in Form-II;

(iv) boilers shall be manufactured by a boiler manufacturer of appropriate class;

(v) any firm seeking recognition shall apply to an Inspecting Authority operating in the State in which the recognition is sought in questionnaire 'Form-III' alongwith the following fee:

SI. No.

Class of manufacturer Fee for recognition Fee for renewal of (Rs.) recognition (Rs.)

(i) Special Class 20,000/10,000/-

(ii) Class I 15,000/7,500/-

(iii) Class II 10,000/5,000/-

(iv) Class III 7,500/3,750/-

(v) Class IV 5,000/2,500/-

(vi) Boiler Component 5,000/2,500/-

(2) A firm seeking recognition as a manufacturer shall meet the following requirements depending upon the class of boilers, namely:-

(a) special class boiler manufacturer for manufacture of boilers of any type and capacity.─ A special class boiler manufacturer shall have minimum covered area of 7500 square meters for manufacturing facilities and shall have following design, fabrication and testing facilities:-

(i) machinery, tools and equipment:

(A) mandatory: Submerged Arc Welding(SAW) machine for seam welding, TIG welding machine, panel bending machine, welding and grinding equipment, radial drilling machine, chamfering machine, tube bending machine, tube swaging machine, burners for pre-heating and post heating, heat treatment facilities, hydraulic test pump ( upto 500 Kgs./cm² or above), band saw cutting machine, air compressor, measuring tools.

(B) optional: Plate bending or rolling machine of appropriate thickness, tube expander, dished end making machine.

(ii) quality assurance and testing facilities:

Magnetic Particle Inspection(MPI), Liquid Penetrant Inspection(LPI), Positive Material Identification (PMI) facilities, Universal Testing Machine (UTM), Radiographic Testing (RT), Ultrasonic Testing (UT).

(iii) manpower:

(A) graduate engineers in relevant discipline with minimum five years experience in the field of boilers, engineers with experience in erection, commissioning and supervision, Non Destructive Testing(NDT) personnel with Level-III NDT Certificates from Indian Society for Non-destructive Testing(I.S.N.T.) or American Society for Non-destructive Testing( A.S.N.T.) and IBR certified welders of appropriate class.

(B) manufacturer shall provide in-house facilities for training of engineers, technicians and welders and shall have in house research and development facilities or tie up for research and development.

Note:1. Manufacturers need not have manufacturing facilities for manufacture of dished ends, pumps and fittings if they outsource manufacturing of these items.

2. Based on the functional requirement manufacturing of any boiler part or component may be outsourced to an approved manufacturer.

3. Rolling and Edge preparation process may be outsourced and manufactures need not have manufacturing facilities for the same.

4. Design office may be located within any of their manufacturing facilities or outside the manufacturing facilities .

(b) Class-I boiler manufacturer for manufacture of boilers of working pressure upto 180 Kgs./cm².─ A class-I boiler manufacturer shall have minimum covered area of 5000 square meters for manufacturing facilities and shall have following design, fabrication and testing facilities:

(i) machinery, tools and equipment:

(A) mandatory: Submerged Arc Welding(SAW) machine for seam welding, Tungsten Inert Gas(TIG) welding machine, panel bending machine, welding and grinding equipments, radial drilling machine, chamfering machine, tube bending machine, tube swaging machine, burners for pre-heating and post heating, heat treatment facilities, hydraulic test pump upto 300 Kgs./cm² or above, band saw cutting machine, air compressor, measuring tools.

(B) optional: Plate bending or rolling machine of appropriate thickness, tube expander, dished end making machine.

(ii) quality assurance and testing facilities:

Mandatory: Magnetic Particle Inspection(MPI), Liquid Penetrant Inspection(LPI), Positive Material Identification (PMI) facilities, Universal Testing Machine (UTM), Radiographic Testing (RT), Ultrasonic Testing (UT).

(iii) manpower:

(A) graduate engineers in relevant discipline with a minimum of four years experience in the field of boilers, engineers with erection, commissioning and supervision experience, NDT personnel with Level-II NDT Certificates from Indian Society for Non-destructive Testing(I.S.N.T.) or American Society for Non-destructive Testing( A.S.N.T.) and IBR certified welders of appropriate class.

(B) manufacturer shall provide in-house facilities for training of engineers, technicians and welders.

Note: 1. Manufacturers need not have manufacturing facilities for manufacture of Dished Ends, pumps and fittings which may be outsourced.

2. Based on the functional requirement manufacturing of any boiler part or component may be outsourced to an approved manufacturer.

3. Rolling and edge preparation process may be outsourced and manufactures need not have manufacturing facilities for the same.

4. Design office may be located within any of their manufacturing facilities or outside the manufacturing facilities .

(c) Class-II boiler manufacturer for manufacture of boilers of working pressure upto 120 Kgs./cm².─ A class-II boiler manufacturer shall have minimum covered area of 3000 square meters for manufacturing facilities and shall have following design, fabrication and testing facilities:

(i) machinery, tools and equipment:

(A) mandatory: Submerged Arc Welding(SAW) machine for seam welding, Tungsten Inert Gas (TIG) welding machine, welding and grinding equipments, radial drilling machine, chamfering machine, tube bending machine, burners for pre-heating and post heating, heat treatment facilities, hydraulic test pump upto 200 Kgs./cm² or above, band saw cutting machine, air compressor, measuring tools.

(B) optional: Plate bending or rolling machine of appropriate thickness, panel bending machine, tube expander, dished end making machine, tube swaging machine.

(ii) quality assurance and testing facilities:

(A) mandatory: Magnetic Particle Inspection (MPI), Liquid Penetrant Inspection(LPI)

(B) optional: Universal Testing Machine (UTM), Radiographic Testing (RT), Ultrasonic Testing (UT)

(iii) manpower: graduate or diploma engineers in relevant discipline with a minimum of three years experience in the field of boilers for graduate engineers and five years for diploma Engineer', NDT personnel with Level-II NDT Certificates from Indian Society for Non-destructive Testing(I.S.N.T.) or American Society for Non-destructive Testing( A.S.N.T.) and IBR certified welders of appropriate class.

Note: 1. Manufacturers need not have manufacturing facilities for manufacture of Dished Ends, pumps and fittings which may be outsourced.

2.

Based on the functional requirement manufacturing of any boiler part or component may be outsourced to an approved manufacturer.

3.

Rolling and edge preparation process may be outsourced and manufactures need not have manufacturing facilities for the same.

4. Design office may be located within any of their manufacturing facilities or outside the manufacturing facilities .

(d) Class-III boiler manufacturer for manufacture of boilers of working pressure upto 50 Kgs./cm².─ A ClassIII boiler manufacturer shall have minimum covered area of 1500 square meters for manufacturing facilities and shall have following design, fabrication and testing facilities:

(i) machinery, tools and equipment:

(A) mandatory: Plate bending or rolling machine of appropriate thickness, Tungsten Inert Gas (TIG) welding machine, welding and grinding equipments, radial drilling machine, chamfering machine, tube bending machine, burners for pre-heating and post heating, heat treatment facilities, hydraulic test pump upto 100 Kgs./cm² or above, band saw cutting machine, air compressor, measuring tools.

(B) optional: Submerged Arc Welding(SAW) machine for seam welding, panel bending machine, tube expander, dished end making machine, tube swaging machine.

(ii) quality assurance and testing facilities:

(A) mandatory: Magnetic Particle Inspection (MPI), Liquid Penetrant Inspection(LPI)

(B) optional: Universal Testing Machine (UTM), Radiographic Testing (RT), Ultrasonic Testing (UT)

(iii) manpower: graduate or diploma engineers in relevant discipline with a minimum of two years experience in the field of boilers for graduate engineers and four years for diploma engineers, NDT personnel with Level-II NDT Certificates from Indian Society for Non-destructive Testing(I.S.N.T.) or American Society for Non-destructive Testing( A.S.N.T.) and IBR certified welders of appropriate class.

Note: 1. Manufacturers need not have manufacturing facilities for manufacture of Dished Ends, pumps and fittings which may be outsourced.

2.

Based on the functional requirement manufacturing of any boiler part or component may be outsourced to an approved manufacturer.

3.

Rolling and edge preparation process may be outsourced and manufactures need not have manufacturing facilities for the same.

4. Design office may be located within any of their manufacturing facilities or outside the manufacturing facilities.

(e) Class-IV boiler manufacturer for manufacture of boilers of working pressure upto 17.5 Kgs./cm².─A classIV boiler manufacturer shall have adequate covered area for manufacturing facilities and shall have following design, fabrication and testing facilities:

(i) machinery, tools and equipment: mandatory: Plate bending or rolling machine of appropriate thickness, radial drilling machine. welding and grinding equipments, hydraulic test pump upto 50 Kgs./cm² or above, tube expander, lathe machine, power hacksaw, drill machine, measuring tools .

(ii) manpower: graduate or diploma engineers in relevant discipline with a minimum of one year experience in the field of boilers for graduate engineers and three years for diploma engineers and IBR certified welders of appropriate class.

Note: 1. Manufacturers need not have manufacturing facilities for manufacture of Dished Ends, pumps and fittings which may be outsourced.

2. Based on the functional requirement manufacturing of any boiler part or component may be outsourced to an approved manufacturer.

3. Heat Treatment, Non-Destructive Testing(NDT) and mechanical testings may be outsourced.

4. Design office may be located within any of their manufacturing facilities or outside the manufacturing facilities.

(f) Boiler component manufacturer: A boiler component manufacturer shall have adequate covered area for manufacturing facilities and have adequate design and fabrication facilities for the boiler components which they intend to manufacture. In case boiler component is to be manufactured as per the design of boiler manufacturer, design facilities may not be required.

Note: 1. A higher class boiler manufacturer shall be eligible to manufacture lower class boilers.

2. A boiler manufacturer shall also be eligible to manufacture boiler components as boiler component manufacturer.

(3) The Inspecting Authority shall scrutinise and evaluate the application along with the replies to the questionnaire and after satisfying itself that the requirements under sub-regulation (2) are fulfilled, shall recognise the firm as a manufacturer in the category applied for.

(4) The recognition of the firm as a manufacturer shall be for a period of three years and the firm may apply for renewal of their recognition at least ninety days before the expiry of said period.

8. Certificates, drawings and specifications. ─ An application for registration of a boiler under sub-section (1) of section 12 and clause (c) of sub-section (1) of section 19 of the Act, the following certificates and drawings or specifications shall be furnished to the Chief Inspector, the certificates and drawings or specifications may also be submitted through electronic means along with digital signatures, namely:-

(a)

(i) A certificate in Form-I(Part-B) or Form-I(Part-C), as the case may be, from an Inspecting Authority, certifying that the material was tested and the boiler was built under their supervision in respect of the inspection of the boiler during construction and the hydraulic test applied on completion.

(ii) In case of a boiler which is to be assembled only at site, requirement of the hydraulic test on a completely assembled boiler by the Inspecting Authority shall not apply provided that the individual parts of such boiler have been hydraulically tested and certified by the Inspecting Authority separately as required under these regulations.

(iii) In case of waste heat boilers, a certificate in Form-I(Part-B) or Form-I(Part-C) as applicable may be issued by the Inspecting Authority of the State, where the boiler is installed, after completion of construction at site on the strength of the certificates supplied to him in prescribed forms by the owners for the individual components which are required to be furnished for registration of a boiler under this regulation, shall be submitted to the Inspecting Authority of the State, where the boiler is installed, before the commencement of construction of such boiler at the site.

(iv)The Inspecting Authority may grant a certificate in Form-I(Part-D) in lieu of Form-I(Part-B) or FormI(Part-C), for boilers for which variations from the standard conditions in respect of material, design and construction features permitted by the Board or the Inspecting Authority.

(v) The Inspecting Authority shall issue certificate in Form-I(Part-E) after completion of boiler erection and satisfactory hydrostatic test at the assembly site.

(b) A certificate in Form-IV(Part-A) of manufacture and test signed by the maker of the boiler or its representative, containing a description of the boiler, its principal dimensions, particulars of the kind of material used in its construction, the thickness of all plates, particulars of any departure from ordinary practice in making the shell, such as, solid rolling or welding, the hydraulic test to which the boiler was subjected, the intended working pressure, the area of heating surface, the maximum continuous evaporative capacity, the year and place of manufacture, and the works number of the boiler.

Note: The Inspecting Authority may approve a modified form of certificate omitting items which do not pertain to a particular boiler.

(c)

(i) In case of shop assembled boilers, drawings to an appropriate scale showing the principal dimensions, sections, maker's number, position of Inspecting Authority's stamp, bill of material, welding details and design parameters.

(ii) In case of site assembled boilers, drawings showing general and pressure parts arrangement, drawings of coils, panels, headers and drums with details of principal dimensions, bill of materials and design parameters.

Manufacturer may use appropriate scale for drawings and in case of identical or similar multiple subassemblies, drawings for only one sub-assembly may be submitted.

(d)

(i) A certificate in Form-V(Part-A) from the steel maker and a certificate from the maker of the plates or bars, of the nature referred to in regulations 45 and 46 respectively and the certificate from the maker of the plates, or bars, shall show the charge numbers, the plate or bar numbers and the number and dimensions of the various plates tested, their chemical analysis, their ultimate tensile strength in kilograms per square millimetre of section, the percentage of elongation and the length on which measured, the number, kind and result of bend or other tests made and the date of tests .

(ii) In case any question arises in respect of the fitness of the boilers for the working pressure approved by the Inspecting Authority within a period of three years from the date of their registration, the owner shall if requested by the Chief Inspector obtain and furnish the original documents specified in the said clauses:

Provided that in respect of the steel made and tested by Well-known steel makers recognised by the Central Boilers Board in the manner laid down in regulations 17 to 23, a certificate of Well-known steel maker in Form-V(Part-A) shall be accepted in lieu of a certificate from an Inspection Authority.

(iii) In case where the original certificate from well-known steel makers in Form-V(Part-A) is not produceable, owing to such certificate containing details of plates used for other purposes also, an extract from the original certificate duly signed by the makers of the boiler and countersigned by the Inspecting Authority shall be accepted in lieu of the certificate in Form-V(Part-A), provided all information required in Form-V(Part-A) are furnished in the extract.

(iv) The extract of all test certificates of materials used in boiler and boiler components in Form-V(Part-B) along with Form-IV(Part-A).

(v) In the case of fusion welded drums, diagram of weld repairs and temperature charts of heat-treatment shall also be furnished.

(vi) Alloy steel materials used in creep range in furnace or super-heater zone─

(A) two numbers of 1000 hour stress rupture tests shall be carried out at 50°C above the maximum service temperature for each grade of alloy steel and Nickel alloys; when used in furnace or in super heater zone;

(B) the stress to cause rupture in 1000 hours at the above temperatures may be taken from the master curve corresponding to - 20 percent line at this stress, a minimum rupture life of 1000 hours is expected and both the samples shall pass 1000 hour tests at the above stress and temperature;

(C) the short-term stress rupture tests for 1000 hours as mentioned above are carried out by National Metallurgical Laboratory or well known steel makers or any other Material Testing Laboratory recognised by the Central Boilers Board for creep testing and collecting elevated temperature data of alloy steel produced indigenously or from a nationally recognised or accredited testing laboratory for creep testing in that country of manufacture for alloy steel produced outside India, for the purpose of checking whether the steel is up to the specification and also to ensure that the steel is capable of meeting the long-term rupture stress values or maximum allowable stress values given in American Society of Mechanical Engineers

(ASME) or British Standards (BS) or European Standards (EN) Code, as the case may be, and a certificate is given to this effect; alternately the testing can also be done in any other laboratory if the tests are witnessed by a Competent Person working with a recognised Inspecting Authority;

(D) these samples may be selected at random by the Competent Person and may be in the form of semi-finished products, say, forged bars of about 25 mm2, which shall undergo heat treatment as prescribed by the relevant specifications.

(vii) For such boilers having a capacity of 20 tons per hour and above which are required to be assembled at site, the mountings may be supplied separately and all boilers of capacity less than 20 tons per hour shall carry all the mountings or fittings certificates in respective forms with details mentioned in Form-IV(Part-A), issued at the time of manufacture of boilers.

9. Maker's stamp.─ The boiler shall have a stamp in a conspicuous place such asMAKER'S NAME —————————————————————————————————————————————

Work's Number————————Year of Make —————————- Tested to ——————————Competent Person's or Inspecting Authority's Official Stamp

10.

Certificates for pipes.─ A certificate of manufacture and test in Form-IV(Part-B) signed by the maker and the Inspecting Authority shall be furnished and well-known pipe makers may also furnish digitally signed on-line certificate of manufacture and test in Form-IV(Part-B) through electronic mode along with digital signature.

11.

Certificates for tubes.─ A certificate of manufacture and test in Form-IV(Part-C), signed by the maker and the Inspecting Authority shall be furnished and well-known tube makers may also furnish certificate of manufacture and test in Form-IV(Part-C) through electronic mode along with digital signature.

Note:

In case of pipes or tubes made by well-known Pipe or Tube Makers recognised by the Central Boilers Board in the manner as laid down in regulations 17 to 23 in India or other countries, material testing including mechanical tests may be carried out by them and the particulars regarding testing of material including mechanical tests as certified by them and the particulars appropriate column or paragraphs in the certificate in Form-IV(Part-B), or Form-IV(Part-C), and in case, certificate from the "well-known Pipe or Tube Makers" as aforesaid is produced, such certificate may be accepted in lieu of the certificate from the Inspecting Authority insofar as it relates to testing of material including mechanical tests specified in this form.

12. Certificates for mounting and fittings.─ A certificate of manufacture and test in Form-IV(Part-D), signed by the maker and the Inspecting Authority in respect of boiler mountings and steam-pipe fittings during manufacture, shall be furnished.

Note: A Photocopy of certificate in Form-IV(Part-D) shall be accepted provided it is endorsed by the manufacturer or the Inspecting Authority who has signed the original certificate.

13. Certificate for Castings.─ A certificate of manufacture and test in Form-IV(Part-E), signed by the maker and the Inspecting Authority shall be furnished. However, in the case of castings manufactured by well known foundries recognised by the Central Boilers Board, signature of Inspecting Authority shall not be required.

14. Certificate for Forgings.─ A certificate of manufacture and test in Form-IV(Part-F), signed by the maker and the Inspecting Authority shall be furnished. However, in the case of forgings manufactured by well known forges recognised by the Central Boilers Board, signature of Inspecting Authority shall not be required.

15. Certificate of inspection during manufacture of Headers, Desuperheaters or Attemperators, Blowdown Tanks, Feedwater Tanks, Accumulators and Dearators.─A certificate of manufacture and test in FormIV(Part-G), signed by the maker and the Inspecting Authority, shall be furnished.

16.Certificate of inspection during manufacture of dished ends or end covers.─ A certificate of manufacture and test in Form-IV(Part-H), signed by the maker and the Inspecting Authority shall be furnished.

17. Application for recognition.─(1) An application for recognition as Competent Authority, Inspecting Authority, Well-known Material Testing Laboratory, Well-known Steel Maker, Well-known Foundry or Forge, Well-known Tube or Pipe Maker and Well-known Remnant Life Assessment Organisation, in one of the areas of activity in which they are engaged, shall be made by a firm to the Secretary, Central Boilers Board, along with specified fee.

(2) An application under sub-regulation (1) shall be made in duly filled in Forms VI (Part-A) to VI (Part-G) applicable to the area of activity and may also be submitted through electronic means .

(3) Any firm applying for recognition as Competent Authority and Inspecting Authority under sub-regulation

(1) shall have a minimum experience of two years in the area of activity for which recognition is applied for and for recognition as Well-known Material Testing Laboratory or Well-known Steel Maker or Well-Known Foundry or Forge, Well-Known Tube or Pipe Maker or Well-Known Remnant Life Assessment Organisation, experience of two years shall be required under these regulations.

(4) In case of firms in foreign countries seeking recognition as Competent Authority or Well-Known Steel Maker, Well Known Pipe or Tube Maker, Well Known Foundry or Forge, a fee of ten thousand US dollars shall be deposited along with the completed form to meet the expenses of the visit of the Evaluation Committee specified under regulation 23:

Provided that where the firm has more than one manufacturing unit in the same country, an additional fee at the rate of two thousand US dollars per additional unit shall be deposited.

(5) In case of firms in foreign countries seeking recognition as Inspecting Authority, a fee of one thousand US dollars shall be deposited along with the completed forms.

(6) In case of firms in India seeking recognition under sub-regulation (1), a fee of rupees fifteen thousand shall be deposited along with the completed form and for renewal of recognition, a fee of rupees ten thousand shall be deposited along with the completed form.

(7) The Evaluation Committee shall carry out the evaluation of the manufacturing works of the firms within ninety days of receipt of the fees in case of manufacturing works in foreign countries and within sixty days in case of manufacturing works in India.

(8) The Appraisal Committee or Evaluation Committee, as the case may be, shall also evaluate the performance of the firm applying for recognition as Competent Authority or Inspecting Authority within sixty days of receipt of fee.

(9) The certificate of recognition shall be valid for a period of five years from the date of visit of the plant by the Evaluation Committee or meeting of the Appraisal Committee, as the case may be and in case of renewal of the recognition, if an application is received for renewal along with the required fee, firm's certificate of recognition may be renewed after following the procedure specified under these regulations and the certificate shall be issued for a further period of five years.

18. Scrutiny of applications by Evaluation Committee or Appraisal Committee.─(1) The Secretary, Central Boilers Board shall forward all the applications except that for recognition as Inspecting Authority received under sub-regulation (1) of regulation 17, along with replies to the questionnaire form under sub-regulation (2) of that regulation, to the Evaluation Committee.

(2) For recognition as Inspecting Authority all the applications along with replies to the questionnaire shall be forwarded to the Appraisal Committee.

(3) The Evaluation Committee or the Appraisal Committee, as the case may be, shall examine all the applications and replies to the Questionnaire specified under sub-regulation (1) and :

(a) where the application is for recognition as Inspecting Authority, the Appraisal Committee, may.─

(i) call any of the applicants if it considers necessary, to appear before it to give clarification for additional information that may be required by the said Committee;

(ii) visit any of the applicant firms on a specific request in writing from such firm or if decided so by the Appraisal Committee to evaluate the performance of the said firm and for this purpose the Appraisal Committee may depute three members to visit the applicant firm;

(b) where the applications are for recognition as Competent Authority or Well-Known Material Testing Laboratory, Well-Known Remnant Life Assessment Organisation, Well-Known Steel Maker, Well-Known Foundry or Forge or Well-Known Tube or Pipe Maker, the Evaluation Committee shall inspect the laboratories of such Material Testing Laboratory and Remnant Life Assessment Organisation or the factories of such steel makers, foundry or forge and tube or pipe makers, where the testing and manufacturing activities are being carried out in order to evaluate the performance quality of the tests conducted and products manufactured.

(4) The Evaluation Committee or the Appraisal Committee, as the case may be, after satisfying itself that the requirements specified in regulations 23 and 24 are fulfilled, shall submit a report to the Secretary, Central Boiler Board recommending either to grant or refuse recognition.

19. Recognition of a firm as Competent Authority, Inspecting Authorities, Well Known firms ─On examining the report of the Evaluation Committee or the Appraisal Committee, as the case may be, the Secretary, Central Boilers Board may issue a certificate of recognition to the applicant in the applicable Form:

Provided that if he decides to refuse the recognition, he shall inform the applicant with the reasons to be recorded in writing.

20. Validity of certificate of recognition:- A certificate of recognition issued under regulation 19, shall be valid for a period of five years.

21. Renewal of certificate of recognition.─ (1) A firm may apply for renewal of the certificate of recognition at least three months before its expiry to the Secretary, Central Boilers Board who after following the procedure specified in these regulations may renew the certificate of recognition or refuse renewal, and in case of renewal, it shall be valid for a further period of five years.

(2) The period of validity shall be deemed to have been extended till such time the decision on the renewal is not taken after receipt of the application under sub-regulation (1).

22. Appeal.─ (1) Any firm aggrieved with the decision given by the Secretary, Central Boilers Board may prefer an appeal to the Chairperson, Central Boilers Board.

(2) All applications received by the Chairperson, Central Boilers Board, under sub-regulation (1), shall be decided in consultation with the Central Boilers Board.

23. Evaluation Committee.─ (1)(a) For the purpose of these regulations, the Central Boilers Board constitutes the Evaluation Committee consisting of the following persons, namely:-

(i) Technical Adviser (Boilers) — Chairperson

(ii) Chief Inspector (Boilers) of the State where the unit is located — Member

(iii) One representative from Board representing Central Government or Bureau of Indian Standards or Boiler and Boiler component manufacturers or National Laboratories or Engineering Consultancy agencies or user of boilers and other interests - Member.

(b) Secretary, Central Boilers Board shall nominate the members;

(2) Functions of Evaluation Committee.─(a)The Evaluation Committee shall evaluate the performance of a firm applying for recognition in accordance with the provisions of these regulations, in particular in the following areas, namely:-

(i) Quality System;

(ii) Organisation;

(iii) Review of quality system;

(iv) Documentation—

(A) Inspection and test procedures;

(B) Records;

(C) Technical data;

(v) Inspection equipment;

(vi) Inspection of purchased material or services—

(A) Purchasing;

(B) Purchasing data;

(C) Receiving inspection;

(D) Verification of purchased material;

(vii) In-process inspection;

(viii) Workmanship;

(ix) Inspection and test of completed items;

(x) Sampling procedure;

(xi) Control of non-conforming material;

(xii) Indication of inspection status;

(xiii) Protection and preservation of product quality—

(A) material handling;

(B) storage;

(C) delivery;

(xiv) Training.

(b)For recognition as Competent Authority, the Evaluation Committee shall evaluate the performance of a firm applying for such recognition in accordance with the provisions of these regulations, in particular in the following areas, namely:-

(i) The firm shall be a registered entity and not a sole proprietorship.

(ii) For operation in India, the firm shall be registered in India also;

(iii) The authorised signatory responsible to issue a certificate to qualified welders on behalf of the Competent Authority shall be an employee of the Competent Authority and shall be an engineer with five years experience in the field of welding having a degree in mechanical or production or metallurgical or welding engineering and certificate of Level-II in radiography techniques;

(iv) The firm or company shall have a workshop with the following facilities, namely: -

(A) electric welding sets for conducting welding of the specimens;

(B) pre-heating facilities;

(C) platforms for welding;

(D) the following in-house testing machines:-

(I) Universal testing machine;

(II) Non-Destructive Testing (NDT) – dye penetration tests;

(III) Machines for preparation of specimens;

(IV) Hardness tester (optional);

(E) Radiography, (micro and macro examination of samples) may be outsourced to approved agencies.

(F) For operation in India, workshop with above facilities shall be required to be available in India also;

(v) The firm shall be familiar with the requirements for examination of welders under these regulations;

(vi) An inspecting Authority may also work as a Competent Authority for in-house certification of welders and for this purpose it may avail the facility of an outside agency having above facilities for testing of weld specimens for in-house certification of welders, provided it has in-employment an authorised signatory as mentioned in sub-clause (iii) above.

(c) For recognition as Remnant Life Assessment Organisation(RLAO), the Evaluation Committee shall evaluate the performance of a firm applying for recognition as RLAO in accordance with the provisions of these regulations, in particular in the following areas, namely:i.

For undertaking Remnant Life Assessment (RLA) of boilers operating above 120 kgs./cm² pressure―

(A) Knowledge on boiler design, manufacturing and field knowledge on operation and maintenance of boilers, materials, codes and standards;

(B) Expertise in failure analysis, stress analysis to evaluate pressure-part component life;

(C) Expertise on metallurgy, NDT and other RLA assessment techniques;

(D) Internal Oxide Thickness (IOT) measurement and analytical capabilities;

(E) Firm or company shall have ,―

(I) National Accreditation Board for Testing and Calibration Laboratories (NABL) accredited laboratory with experts in the field and necessary equipment or instruments for testing of sample tubes, deposits;

(II) Laboratory with experts for analysis of Water Wall (WW) deposits and solvent selection with a capability to issue guidelines for chemical cleaning of boilers; (it may also be outsourced);

(III) NDT laboratory with qualified personnel;

(IV) Skilled and experienced manpower for site work to carry out RLA tests as per regulations 313 to 315;

(V) All measuring and testing instruments required for RLA study of boilers as stipulated in regulation 313 to 315; and

(VI) A minimum experience of five of RLA studies under Inspecting Authorities. ii.

For undertaking RLA of boilers operating upto 120 kg/cm² pressure―

(A) Knowledge on boiler design, manufacturing and field knowledge on operation and maintenance of boilers, materials, codes and standards;

(B) Knowledge in failure analysis, stress analysis to evaluate pressure part component life;

(C) Knowledge on metallurgy, NDT and other RLA assessment techniques;

(D) IOT measurement and analytical capabilities;

(E) Firm shall have―

(I) National Accreditation Board for Testing and Calibration Laboratories (NABL) accredited laboratory with experts in the field and necessary equipment or instruments for testing of sample tubes, deposits(It may also be outsourced);

(II) Laboratory with experts for analysis of Water Wall (WW) deposits and solvent selection with a capability to issue guidelines for chemical cleaning of boilers (It may also be outsourced);

(III) NDT laboratory with qualified personnel;

(IV) Skilled and experienced manpower for site work to carry out RLA tests as per regulation 313 to 315;

(V) All measuring and testing instruments required for RLA study of boilers as stipulated in regulation 313 to 315; and

(VI) A minimum experience of five RLA studies under Inspecting Authorities.

24. Appraisal Committee.─(1)(a)Composition of Appraisal Committee.─ For the purpose of these regulations, the Central Boilers Board constitutes the Appraisal Committee consisting of the following persons, namely:-

(i) Technical adviser (Boilers) – Chairperson;

(ii) Five Chief Inspectors or Directors of Boilers from States [including Chief Inspectors or Directors of Boilers of the States in which registered offices of applicant firm are located] – Members;

(iii) Five Members from Board representing Central Government or Bureau of Indian Standards or Boiler and boiler component manufacturers or National Laboratories or Engineering Consultancy agencies or users of boilers and other interests; - Members

(b) Secretary, Central Boilers Board shall nominate the members;

(2) Functions of Appraisal Committee.─ The Appraisal Committee shall evaluate the performance of a firm applying for recognition as an Inspecting Authority under these regulations, in the following areas, namely:-

(i) the firm shall be a registered entity and for its operation in India, the entity shall be registered in India;

Explanation. - For the purposes of this regulation, it is hereby declared that the sole proprietorship firm is not eligible to apply for recognition as Inspecting Authority.

(ii) the firm shall, ─

(a) not be involved in any commercial activity other than the inspection, certification and related activities under the Indian and International Codes and Standards;

(b) have an in-house design and drawing inspection office and for operation in India the in-house design and drawing inspection office shall be situated in India ;

(c) have various codes in its office and the knowledge of various international codes along with the Act and these regulations;

(d) have employed the Competent Persons possessing Central Boilers Board authorisation cards for operation in India issued under regulation 25(5)(c)(i);

(e) have a documented quality programme established and maintained to fulfil the inspection requirements under these regulations;

(f) have complete details of each and every Competent Person employed in its organisation for inspection and certification of boilers;

(g) have a training programme for Competent Persons.

25. Procedure for recognition of Competent Person.─

(1)(a) This regulation provides requirements for the uniform qualification, examination, issuance and renewal of Central Boiler Boards authorisation to competent persons under the Act for inspection and certification of boilers and boiler components during manufacture, erection, operation and maintenance throughout India.

(2) Minimum qualifications and experience required are as under.─

(a) shall be a graduate in Mechanical Engineering or Production or Power Plant or Metallurgical Engineering from a recognised institute;

(b) minimum five years' experience singly or cumulatively in the following fields related to boilers:-

(i) Design;

(ii) Manufacture;

(iii) Commissioning;

(iv) Operation and maintenance;

(v) Inspection and certification during manufacture or operation and maintenance;

(vi) High Pressure Welding-Inspection;

(c) Level-II NDT Certificates from Indian Society for Non-Destructive Testing (ISNT) or American Society for Non-Destructive Testing (ASNT) or any other professional body recognised by ISNT or ASNT in NonDestructive methods of testing in radiography and ultrasonic testing.

(3) Central Boilers Board Examination Standing Committee Composition: -(a)For the purpose of these regulations, the Central Boilers Board constitutes the 'Central Boilers Board Examination Standing Committee' consisting of the following persons-

(i) Technical adviser (Boilers) — Chairperson

(ii) Two Chief Inspectors or Directors of Boilers from States — Members

(iii) Two Members from Board representing Central Government or Bureau of Indian Standards or Boiler and boiler component manufacturers or National Laboratories or Engineering Consultancy agencies or user of boilers and other interests - Members

(b) Secretary, Central Boilers Board shall nominate the members;

(4) The Process for Central Boilers Board Examination shall be as under─

(a) an examination shall be conducted by the Central Boilers Board Examination Standing Committee or an agency or Regional Examination Standing Committees authorised by it, as per the following examination methods, namely: -

(i) written examination consisting of design, manufacture, operation and maintenance of the boiler, Non Destructive Testing Techniques, inspection and certification of boilers during manufacture and use as per these regulations (70% weightage);

(ii) candidate shall be required to secure at least 45% marks in each written examination papers and at least 60% marks in aggregate to qualify for viva-voce;

(iii) viva-voce on the above (30% weightage);

(iv) the candidate shall secure at least 60% marks in aggregate (written exam with 70% weightage and viva-voce with 30% weightage) in order to pass the examination;

(v) the Central Boilers Board shall issue a passing grade certificate to the candidate after passing the examination;

(b) application for appearing in the Central Boiler Board Examination shall be made in the format as provided by the Central Boilers Board Examination Standing Committee at such place and time as decided by the Central Boilers Board Examination Standing Committee or an agency or Regional Examination Standing Committees authorised by it.

(5) The process for Central Boilers Board authorisation shall be as under: ─

(a) the Competent Person shall perform the work without conflict of interest;

(b) an applicant for a Central Boiler Board Authorisation shall meet the following requirements, namely:-

(i) the applicant shall be in regular employment of, and exclusively engaged by a Central Boilers Board recognised Inspecting Authority:

Provided that for inspection and certification of boilers during use under section 13 of the Act, the applicant can work independently also without taking employment with the Central Boilers Board recognised Inspecting Authority ;

(ii) the applicant shall meet the minimum qualification and experience requirements as referred in subregulation (2) of this regulation;

(iii) the applicant shall have taken and passed the Central Boilers Board examination as referred to in sub-regulation (4) of this regulation;

(iv) the applicant shall apply for an authorisation not later than three years after passing the Central Boilers Board Examination and a person whose authorisation has not been issued within the three year period shall be required to take the Central Boiler Board Examination and receive a passing grade; and

(v) the maximum age for the Competent Person shall be sixty-five years provided the person is medically fit;

(c) (i) when the applicant meets the requirements as referred to in clause (b)and at the request of the applicant's employer; a Central Boilers Board authorisation card and a certificate shall be issued:

Provided that the request for authorisation card, only for undertaking inspection and certification of boilers during use, under section 13 of the Act, may also be made by the applicant;

(ii) the application for an authorisation shall be on forms available with the Central Boilers Board; and

(iii) a Competent Person may possess only one valid authorisation card;

(d)

(i) the validity of an authorisation certificate shall be three years from the date of issue or in co-terminus with the validity period of recognition of employing Inspecting Authority (wherever applicable), whichever is earlier;

(ii) for undertaking inspection and certification of boilers during manufacture and erection, the Competent Person must be in regular employment of the Central Boilers Board recognised Inspecting Authority failing which the authorisation lapses;

(iii) Where the Competent Person leaves the employment, Central Boilers Board Authorisation Card shall be surrendered by the Inspecting Authority to the Secretary, Central Boilers Board within a period of seven days from the date of such leaving;

(iv) the request for renewal shall be made by the Competent Person's employer and the request shall certify that the Competent Person maintained inspection proficiency by performing or supervising inspection activity and that the individual has met the continuing updation requirement as laid down in sub-clause (vi):

Provided that request for renewal of authorisation, only for undertaking inspection and certification of boilers during use under section 13 of the Boilers Act, 2025, can also be made by the Competent Person, giving details of inspections undertaken in the last three years, and confirming that he has met the continuing updation requirement as given in clause (vi) of this sub-regulation;

(v) on change of employment, a request for authorisation card shall be made by the Competent Person's current employer on forms obtained from the Central Boilers Board;

(vi) each Central Boilers Board authorised Competent Person, at least once every three years, shall either attend a Central Boilers Board seminar or receive other instruction related to inspections and the instruction may be in any format, e.g., classroom, home study, or web-based; the topics may include any subject of relevance to the inspection process, such as new methods, products, materials, technology or changes to construction or repair codes;

(vii) a person whose authorisation has not been renewed for five years or less may, on proper application, shall have the authorisation reinstated;

(viii) the employer or Competent Person, if working independently, as the case may be, shall provide verification of continuing updation as mentioned in sub-clause (vi); and

(ix) a person whose authorisation has not been renewed for more than five years shall be required to qualify the examination conducted by Central Boilers Board;

(e)

(i) a Central Boilers Board authorisation may be revoked by the Secretary, Central Boilers Board on the ground of falsification of any statement contained in any application;

(ii) when an evaluation reveals that a Competent Person authorised by Central Boilers Board is found negligent in his duty or made false statements on forms used for documentation of his duties, a Central Boilers Board Committee formed for the purpose may examine the evaluation and recommend to the Secretary, Central Boilers Board, a suitable action against such Competent Person and the Secretary, Central Boilers Board shall take the decision;

(iii) if any person is aggrieved by a decision of the Secretary, Central Boilers Board, he may prefer an appeal against such decision to the Central Boilers Board within thirty days from the date of communication of the decision;

(iv) the decision of the Central Boilers Board shall be binding; and

(v) any person aggrieved by,-

(A) an order made or purporting to be made by a Competent Person in the exercise of any power conferred by or under the Act; or

(B) a refusal of a Competent Person to make any order or to issue any certificate which he is required or enabled by or under the Act to make or issue , may, within thirty days from the date on which such order or refusal is communicated to him, prefer an appeal against such order or refusal, to the Chief Inspector of Boilers of the State in which the boiler is located;

(C) fees for each authorisation issued, renewed or reinstated fixed by the Central Boiler Board examination standing committee shall be paid including through electronic mode.

The Central Boilers Board authorisation number for the purpose of its usage shall be recorded on all forms used by him and on the items inspected and stamped as a proof of inspection and the authorisation number shall also be entered into the Competent Person's diary and other relevant records.

(7)(a) A diary shall be maintained by the Competent Person for,-

(i) inspection of construction activities; and

(ii) in-service inspection including repairs and alterations for each location or site as per the format laid down by the Central Boilers Board;

(b) the diary shall be bound and numbered and the purpose of the diary shall be to provide a record of the Competent Person's activity and to support the continuity of inspections;

(c) the Competent Person's diary shall be the property of the employer Inspecting Authority, wherever applicable, and shall be available at the location or site of the inspection; and

(d) the diary shall be maintained for a minimum period of five years from the date of last entry and it may also be maintained in digitised form with provision for online entries:Duties and responsibilities of a Competent Person shall include the following, namely:-

(a) on receipt of an application for renewal of certificate for use of a boiler, the Competent Person shall, within fifteen days from the date of such receipt inspect the boiler on a date communicated to the owner in advance;

(b) a copy of memorandum of inspection book shall be obtained from the concerned boiler inspectorate though the Inspecting Authority or directly, if working independently, for in-service boilers which shall be returned within a period of seven days from the date of completion of final inspection for making necessary entries therein;

(c) the Competent Person shall also discharge the following duties at various stages, namely:-

(i) Inspection during manufacture and commissioning:-

(A) work to the satisfaction of the Inspecting Authority for inspection during manufacture, erection and commissioning of boilers as per the provisions of these regulations;

(B) Inspect during manufacture and erection as per the procedure given in Appendix-A;

(ii) In – service inspection:-

(A) verify whether the stamping or nameplate is legible and represents the item described on the inspection documentation;

(B) perform external and internal inspection and witness any required pressure tests;

(C) report the inspection results, including any nonconforming conditions to the owner of the boilers;

(iii) Repairs and alterations:-

(A) verify that the boiler owner has informed the chief inspector in writing and the activity is permitted under the scope of the certificate or the structural alteration, addition or renewal is made under his supervision;

(B) verify that the boiler repairer has a valid certificate as specified under these regulations;

(C) monitor the quality control system and verify the system is being implemented as required by regulations;

(D) verify that all materials comply with these regulations;

(E) verify that all welding procedure specifications, procedure qualification records, and welder and welding operation records conform to these regulations;

(F) verify that heat treatments as required under these regulations have been performed and properly documented;

(G) verify that non-destructive examinations and tests as required under these regulations have been performed and properly documented;

(H) witness pressure tests as required under these regulations;

(I) verify that information in the forms is correct and that the details have been properly punched;

(J) verify the correctness of the stamping;

(9) Competent Person or Inspecting Authority may charge a fee for the inspection subject to the condition that the fee shall not be less than the fee specified by the concerned State.

(10) Inspection shall be carried out as provided under these regulations and the relevant certificate shall be issued as follows, namely:-

(a) For in-service Boilers inspection: -

(i) one copy shall be given to the owner of the boiler on the day of inspection;

(ii) one copy shall be given to the Chief Inspector along with the Memorandum of Inspection Book (Form-XI);

(iii) one copy shall remain in Book.

(iv) In case inspection is not carried out as provided under these regulations, the Chief Inspector of Boilers shall report the matter to the Secretary, Central Boilers Board for appropriate action against the Competent Person which may include cancellation of his authorisation.

(b) For inspection during manufacture: -

(i) one copy shall be given to the manufacturer;

(ii) one copy shall remain with the Inspecting Authority.

(c) For inspection during erection:-

(i) one copy shall be given to the owner of the boiler;

(ii) one copy shall be given to the Chief Inspector;

(iii) one copy shall remain with the Inspecting Authority.

26. Modification of formulae. ─

(1) For determining the maximum allowable working pressure to be allowed on various parts of boilers, the material to which the formulae apply shall, in the absence of specific provision to the contrary, be still complying with the requirements of Chapter-2.

(2) Where no test certificates for plates are produced, the material may be treated as iron, if the Inspecting Authority is satisfied that the material is of suitable boiler quality. If in such cases the Inspecting Authority is clearly satisfied that the material is of good quality, a higher strength than that allowed for iron may be permitted but the strength of the plates shall not, save for special reasons, be assumed to be more than 37kg/mm². In such cases the values of tensile and shear strength shall not be more than 37kg/mm² and 30kg/mm² for steel, and 30kg/mm² and 25kg/mm² for iron. For iron across the grain the tensile strength may be 25kg/mm².

(3) For flat plates of copper, the working pressure as found from the formulae, reduced by fifty percent, shall be the permitted working pressure.

(4) When the quality of material and the make of steel pipe have not been supported by certificates from the manufacturers in Form-IV(Part-B), the pipes shall be tested in a test house and the maximum permissible working stress shall be such as the Inspecting Authority may determine after reasons to be recorded in writing.

27. Procuring of materials. ─ (1) Where procuring materials directly from mills is not feasible due to small quantity, the following method shall be adopted, namely:-

(a) materials fully identifiable, supported by a materials test report by material manufacturer but not covered by a form under these regulations,─

(i) when a material is received with complete identification marks applied by the mill and supported by a materials test report, the manufacturer of boiler, boiler component, and spares shall demonstrate to the satisfaction of the Inspecting Authority that all requirements of the material specification specified under these regulations have been met;

(ii) the materials test report issued by materials manufacturer shall be presented to the Inspecting Authority for verification;

(iii) any additional tests as determined by the Inspecting Authority shall be conducted in a test house and results documented;

(iv) Form-IV(Part-B) shall be prepared with specification, size, quantity, reported test results and jointly signed by manufacturer of boiler, boiler component or spares and the Inspecting Authority;

(b) materials fully identifiable with mill markings for a production lot but not supported by materials test reports and forms under these regulations,─

(i) the manufacturer of boiler, boiler components or spares shall present such materials to the Inspecting Authority for as marked by the mill. Shipping document or purchase order or as the case may be, drawing are also to be presented to establish required specification of materials;

(ii) testing of materials shall be conducted as per materials specification and additional requirements of these regulations, if any;

(iii) number of samples for tests shall be determined by the Inspecting Authority but not less than 10% of the quantity under consideration;

(iv) test results are to be recorded in Form-IV(Part-B), signed by manufacturer and the Inspecting Authority;

(v) plates or sheets qualified in the manner as provided above shall not be permitted for use in boiler shell or drum construction;

28. Standard specifications for materials.─ The standard specifications for steel wrought iron and copper plates and bars, and the cast steel shall be such as provided in Chapter -2 of these regulations.

29. Registration of second-hand boilers not in accordance with the standard conditions.─ In case a second hand boiler is not conforming to the requirements of these regulations for its registration, then the same shall not be registered except in the following cases, namely:─

(1) Water Tube Boilers: Where the documents required under their code of manufacture for their registration are furnished and its workmanship is not in any way doubtful, the second-hand Water Tube Boiler may be registered by reducing the working pressure of the parts of the boiler as calculated from the regulations by ten percent:

Provided that a higher working pressure, being not more than the working pressure allowed by the code of manufacture, may be allowed on the recommendation of the Board made by it on the basis of life assessment tests carried out on the boiler.

(2) Shell Type Boilers: Where the documents required under their code of manufacture, for their registration are furnished, the second-hand Shell Type Boiler may be registered by reducing the working pressure of the parts of the boiler as calculated under these regulations as per the table given below:

Table 1/1 Age of boiler (from the date of first use) in years Reduction in working pressure (percent)

CHAPTER-2 MATERIALS OF CONSTRUCTION PART-I

30.Plates, Pipes, Tubes, Castings, Forgings and Fittings of Carbon, Alloy, Stainless Steel and Nickel Alloys Grades.─(1)The regulation cover boiler plates, tubes, pipes, castings, forgings and fittings of carbon, alloy, stainless steel and nickel alloy grades and shall conform in all respects with the requirements herein specified.

(2) The material conforming to other national or international standards which are known to be commonly used as being suitable for use as boiler plates, tubes, pipes, castings, forgings and fittings can also be used with the designations of the national or international standards, provided such materials are not specifically prohibited by these regulations.

(3) While making boiler plates, tubes, pipes, castings, forgings and fittings to other national or international standards the allowable stresses, chemical and mechanical properties of the respective standards shall be followed while designing.

(4) The different grades of material used in boiler plates, tubes, pipes, castings, forgings and fittings and their design metal temperatures shall be as per Table 2/1.

(5) M number grouping in the Table 2/1 indicates alphanumeric designations that are assigned to base metals for the purpose of grouping different grades of materials for welding and post weld heat treatment (PWHT).

5. In addition to the above, materials conforming to IS or to any of the International codes BS, EN, ASME, DIN and JIS can also be used.

6. In case of any variations between the requirements (Chemical, Mechanical and Dimensional tolerances) specified in IBR and the actual material specifications, the requirements of the latter shall govern.

PART-II

31. Materials of construction for plates. ─ (1) Steel for Carbon, alloy, stainless steel and nickel alloy plates shall be made by the open hearth, electric furnace or basic oxygen process or any other process which gives steel having equivalent specified properties.

(2) General deoxidation practice shall be appropriate to the type of steel used, particularly where the deoxidation practice influences the level of the elevated temperature properties of steel.

(3) Semi-killed steel may be used for plates in carbon(C) and carbon manganese(C-Mn) steel with an upper limit of the tensile strength not exceeding 56 kg /mm2 and with thickness not exceeding 50 millimeter (mm).

(4) The steel may be strand cast or cast in stationary molds.

(5) If heats of the same nominal chemical composition are consecutively strand cast at one time, the heat number assigned to the cast product (slab) may remain unchanged until all the steel in the slab is from different heats.

(6) When two consecutively strand cast heats have different nominal chemical composition ranges, the manufacturer shall remove the transition material by any procedure that positively separates the grades.

(7) The ratio of reduction of thickness from a strand-cast slab to plate shall be at least 3:1, except that reduction ratios as low as 2:1 are permitted if all the following limitations are complied with, namely:-

(a) The specified plate thickness is seventy-five millimeter(mm)or more.

(b) One or more of the following low hydrogen practices are used, namely:-

(i) vacuum degassing during steelmaking;

(ii) controlled soaking of the slabs or plates;

(iii) controlled slow cooling of the slabs or plates.

(c) The sulphur content is 0.004 percent or less, based upon heat analysis.

(d) One or more of the following practices are used, namely:-(i) electromagnetic stirring during strand casting;

(ii) soft reduction during strand casting;

(iii)heavy pass reductions or other special practices during plate rolling;

(iv) combined forging and rolling during plate rolling.

(e) The plates are ultrasonically examined based on continuous scanning over hundred percent of the plate surface.

(f) The plates are through-thickness tension tested.

(g) Unless otherwise specified in the purchase order, plates shall be produced from plate-as-rolled.

(8) Plates of fusion welded boiler drums, vessels, separators, headers and other pressure parts shall conform to the chemical composition given in Table 2/2 in this chapter.

(9) The steel shall not contain more than 0.05 per cent of sulphur or phosphorus.

(10) When the material is required for flame cutting or welding or both, the carbon content shall not exceed 0.30% and due precautions shall be taken when the carbon content exceeds 0.26 percent.

(11) When steels are intended for service temperatures over 371°C, the silicon content shall be not less than 0.10percent or alternatively, the material shall be required to pass the proof test for creep quality of carbon steel plates of boiler quality.

(a) In case of Carbon Steel,─

(i) Plates having thickness of twelve millimeter (mm)or less than that, not intended for hot forming, can be supplied in unnormalised or as rolled condition subject to the condition that the code of manufacture provides for the same.

(ii) For plates having thickness more than twelve millimeter (mm), not intended for hot forming, shall be supplied in the normalised conditions:

(iii) If normalising is to be performed by the fabricator, the plates shall be either normalised or heated uniformly for hot forming. However, the temperature to which the plates are heated for hot forming does not significantly exceed the normalising temperature:

Provided that such normalising may be exempted by the Competent Person if it is demonstrated by the manufacturer that equivalent properties can be produced by the rolling subsequent cooling.

(iv) The boiler manufacturer may, if he so wishes order a check analysis.

(b) In case of Alloy Steel,─

(i) Except for Grade 91 plates, all plates shall be thermally treated either by annealing, normalising and tempering, or, when permitted by the purchaser, accelerated cooling from the austenitising temperature by air blasting or liquid quenching, followed by tempering.

(ii) Grade 91 plates shall be thermally treated, either by normalising-and-tempering or by accelerated cooling from the austenitising temperature by air blasting or liquid quenching, followed by tempering.

(iii) Grade 91 plates shall be austenitised at 1040°C to 1080°C and shall be tempered at 730°C to 800°C.

(c) In case of Stainless Steel,─ The material shall be solution annealed to meet the mechanical property requirements of the applicable material grades.

(d) In case of Nickel Alloys,─ Grade N06617 shall be solution annealed at 1150°C to 1230°C and quenched in water or rapidly cooled by other means.

32. Removal of defects, etc.─ (1) Minor surface defects of the plates may be removed by mechanical means to achieve a smooth level surface provided that the minimum specified thickness is maintained.

(2) Surface defects may be repaired by welding, only with approval of the Inspecting Authority provided that the plate is stress relieved after welding where necessary.

33. Rolling margin for carbon steel. ─No plate or rolled section shall be under the specified thickness at any part, nor more than five per cent over the calculated weight, except that in the case of thin plates and wide plates the weight tolerances shall be as per Table 2/3 mentioned below:

34. Testing and inspection. ─The tests and inspections shall be made at the place of manufacture prior to despatch; but in the event of any of the material proving unsatisfactory in the course of being worked into boilers, such material shall be rejected, despite any previous certificate of satisfactory testing, and such further tests of the material from the same charge shall be made as the Competent Person may consider desirable.

35. Selection of test pieces.─ All test pieces of material shall be selected by the Competent Person and tested in his presence, and thereafter he shall satisfy himself that the conditions herein described are fulfilled.

36. Tensile test pieces. ─(1)The tensile test pieces shall be as per the relevant material specifications.

(2) For plate thickness exceeding 60 millimeter (mm), test pieces shall be taken from the exterior third of the plate cross-section and wherever practicable, the rolled surface shall be retained on two opposite sides of the test piece.

(2) In case of Carbon Steel,─ (i) The upper yield point at room temperature shall not be less than 50% of the specified minimum tensile strength at room temperature.

(ii) The maximum permissible stress shall be taken as provided in governing boiler codes of the country of the material to which it belongs and in case of non-availability of the same, for the purpose of evaluating the maximum permissible stress, the minimum values of the stress at proof limit 0.2% at elevated temperature (Et) may be calculated by multiplying the minimum specified tensile strength at room temperature (R20) by the value of the ratio (Et/R20) given in Table 2/5below:
