38. Number of tensile tests.─ For the purpose of test of shell plates, butt straps and plates, gusset plates, end plates, furnace plates and flanging plates, one tensile test piece shall be cut from each plate as rolled.

39. Bend tests in case of cold bends,─(1)Test pieces shall be sheared lengthwise or crosswise from plates or bars, and shall not be less than 38 millimeter(mm) wide, but for small bars the whole section may be used.

(2) (a)In all cold bend tests, on samples 13 millimeter (mm) in thickness and above, the rough edge or bur caused by shearing may be removed by filling or grinding and samples 25 millimeter(mm) in thickness and above may have the edges machined, but the test pieces shall receive no other preparation.

(b) The test pieces shall not be annealed unless the material from which they are cut is similarly annealed and in such case the test piece shall be similarly treated with the material before testing.

(3) For bend test of plates of all grades of steel, the test piece shall withstand, without fracture, being bent cold through 180° around a mandrel, the radius of which shall have a relationship to the plate thickness as given below:─ Serial No.

Tensile strength Mandrel Radius

1. 1 T*

2. 1 T

3.

1.5 T *Where, T = Plate thickness

(4) For small sectional material the bend tests may be made from the flattened bar.

(5) Bend tests may be made either by pressure or by blows.

40.Number of bend tests.─ A bend test shall be taken from each plate as rolled. The bend tests from shell plates, butt straps and other plates which have not to be flanged or worked in the fire or which when in use are not to be exposed to flame, shall be cold bend tested.

41. Additional tests before rejection.─(1)(a) If the test pieces first selected by the Competent Person fail to fulfil the test requirements under these regulations, two further tests of the same heat may be made, but if either of these fail, the plates or bars from which test pieces were cut shall be rejected.

(b) In all such cases further tests shall be made before any material from the same charge can be accepted.

(2) If the unsatisfactory result of a test is due to technical conditions of the testing method or to a closely limited defect of a test piece, then, the failure can be left out of consideration in the decision on the fulfilment of the requirement and another test piece may be substituted.

(3) If the unsatisfactory result of test is due to an unfavourable heat treatment, the plate and the test strip may be heat treated again and thereafter, the entire test shall be repeated.

42. Branding.─(1)Every plate, section and bar shall be clearly and distinctly marked by the maker at two places with identification mark number by which they can be traced to the charge from which the material was made.

Every plate shall also be stamped by the steel maker as provided in IS: 2002 and with the following information, namely:-

(a) Name of manufacturer;

(b) Specification;

(c) Heat Number;

(d) Plate Number, and

(e) Stamp.

43. Defacing of rejected material.─ In the event of the material failing to withstand the prescribed tests, the identification mark of the Competent Person which has been stamped on the material, shall be defaced by punch marks extending beyond the identification mark, in the form of a cross, thus denoting that the material has failed.

44. Facilities for inspection.─ The maker shall adopt a system of making the ingots, billets, slabs, plates, bars, etc. so as to enable the finished material to be traced to the original charge, and the Inspector or Competent Person, as the case may be, shall be given every facility by maker for tracing all plates and bars to their respective charges, and for witnessing the required tests and when Inspector or Competent Person, as the case may be is satisfied with the material and with the results of the tests, the Inspector or Competent Person, as the case may be, shall be provided two copies of test reports of the material for his signature.

45. Steel not produced where rolled.─(1)Where steel is not produced in the works at which it is rolled, a certificate in Form V(Part-A)or Form IV(Part-C), as the case may be, shall be supplied to the Competent Person deputed to witness the testing of the material, stating the Open Hearth or Electric Process or any of the Oxygen Processes by which it was made, the name of the Steel-Maker who supplied it and the numbers of the charges for reference to the books of the Steel-Maker.

(2) The number of the charge shall be marked on each plate or bar for the purpose of identification.

46. Maker's certificate.─ Before the mill sheets are signed, the maker shall furnish to the Competent Person with a certificate in Form V(Part-A)or Form IV(Part-C), as the case may be, guaranteeing that the material has been made by an Open Hearth or an Electric Process, acid or basic, and that it withstands satisfactorily test described above in the presence of the Competent Person.

PART-III

47. Seamless Tubes of Carbon, Alloy, Stainless Steel and Nickel Alloys for Boiler, Superheater, Reheater and Heat Exchanger.─ (1) These regulations shall apply on hot finished and cold drawn seamless boiler, super heater, re-heater and heat exchanger tubes of carbon, alloy, stainless steel and nickel alloy grades and shall conform the requirements herein specified in Part III in addition to the requirements specified in regulation30.

(2) For design metal temperatures Table 2/1 of regulation 30 shall be considered.

(3) (a)The tubes shall be seamless and manufactured from fully killed steel produced by an open hearth or electric process or any of the oxygen process.

(c) The grain size of cold worked grade TP 321H shall be No.7 or coarser.

(4) (a)Hot finished tubes of Carbon Steel grades need not be heat treated. Cold drawn tubes of Carbon Steel grades shall be given a sub-critical annealing, a full anneal, or a normalising heat treatment after the final cold finishing process.

(b) All tubes of grades provided in Table 2/6, except Carbon steel grades, T91, 12X1M, X20CrMoV121 and N06617 grades shall be reheated and furnished in full annealed, isothermal annealed or normalised and tempered condition. If furnished in the normalised and tempered condition, the minimum tempering temperature for Grades T1 and T11 shall be 650°C and the minimum tempering temperature for Grades T5, T9 and T22 shall be 675°C.

(c) Tubing of Grades T1, T2 and T12 either hot finished or cold drawn, may be given a final heat treatment at 650°C to 730°C, instead of heat treatments specified in clause (b) above, at the option of the manufacturer.

(d) Grade T91 shall be normalised at 1040°C minimum and tempered at 730°C minimum as a final heat treatment.

(e) Grade 12x1Mo shall be normalised at 950°C - 980°C and tempered at 720°C - 750°C for a period of 1 to 3 hours.

(f) Grade X20CrMoV121 shall be normalised at 1020°C - 1070°C and tempered at 730°C - 780°C for a period of 1 hour minimum.

(g) All stainless steel tubes shall be furnished in the heat treated condition and the heat treatment procedure, except for the H grades steel which shall consist of heating the material to a minimum temperature of 1040°C and quenching in water or rapidly cooling by other means.

(h) All H grades steel shall be furnished in the solution treated condition. If cold working is involved in processing, the minimum solution treating temperatures for grades TP321H and TP347H shall be 1100°C and for grades TP304H and TP316H shall be 1040°Cand if the P grade is hot rolled, the minimum solution treatment for grades TP 321 H and TP 347 H shall be 1050°C and for grades TP 304H and TP 316H shall be 1040°C.

(i) Grade N06617 shall be solution annealed at 1150°C to 1230°C and quenched in water or rapidly cooled by other means.

(5) (a) The tubes shall be well finished, cleaned and free from harmful defects and they shall be reasonably straight, smooth, cylindrical and subject to the following tolerances, namely:-,

(i) They shall have smooth ends free of burrs;

(ii)They shall have a workmanlike finish;

(iii) Surface imperfections like any discontinuity or irregularity found in the tube may be removed by grinding, provided that smooth a curved surface is maintained, and the wall thickness is not decreased to less than that permitted;

(iv) The outside diameter at the point of grinding may be reduced by the amount so removed.

48.Selection of Tubes for Test.─(1) (a) (i)Test specimens required for the flattening, expanding or flaring test specified herein below shall be taken from the ends of finished tubes prior to upsetting swaging, expanding or any other forming operations or being cut to length.

(ii)They shall be smooth on the ends and free from burrs and flaws.

(b) (i) If desirable and practicable tensile test may be carried out on full section of the tubes up to the capacity of the testing machine.

(ii) For large size tubes, the tensile test specimen shall consist of strip cut longitudinally from the tube and which is not straightened within the gauge length and further heat treated.

(c) If any test specimen shows flaws or defective machining it shall be discarded and another specimen be taken into consideration.

(d) All specimens shall be tested at room temperature.

(2) (a)The test specified herein below shall be made on minimum two tubes for first hundred tubes and one per hundred or part thereof for tubes over 100 numbers from each lot.

(b)Lot applies to all tube of same size and wall thickness of same heat and heat treatment.

(3) (a)The test specimen shall comply with the requirements as specified in table 2/19.

(b)The hardness test may be carried out on the wall cross section or on a flat on the outside surface of the tube sample.

Note: For longitudinal strip tests of Carbon steel a deduction in percentage elongation 1.5% shall be made for each 0.8 mm decrease in wall thickness under 8 mm from the minimum elongation value requirement.

(4) A section of the tube not less than 63 mm in length shall be flattened cold between two parallel flat surfaces to a distance between the plates (H) as calculated by the formula given below, without showing any sign of a crack or flaw:

H = (1 + e) t / e + t/D Where, t = specified thickness of tube (mm) D = specified outside diameter of the tube (mm) e = a constant, as given below e = 0.07 for Carbon steel with maximum specified carbon above 0.18% e = 0.09 for Carbon steel with maximum specified carbon 0.18% or less. e = 0.08 for alloy steel e = 0.09 for Austenitic steel Note: No flattening test required for grade N06617.

(5) A section of the tube approximately 100 mm in length shall stand being flared with a tool having a 60° included angle until the tube at the mouth of the flare has been expanded to the percentages given below, without cracking:

(6) (a)(i) If any one or more tests specified in these regulations fail, two further tests of the same kind may be made on two other tubes of the same batch.

(ii) If any of these tests fails, the batch of the tubes represented may be given a further heat treatment and entire set of test shall be repeated.

(b) If the repeat tests are satisfactory the tubes shall be accepted. If any failure in the tests should occur, the entire batch of the tubes shall be rejected.

(7) (a)Each tube shall be tested by the manufacturer and shall withstand a hydraulic pressure of one and half times of the design pressure subject to a minimum of 1 kg/mm2, but not greater than pressure calculated by the following formula:

P = 2 S t / D Where, P = test pressure D = specified outside diameter of the tube t = specified wall thickness of the tube S = stress which shall be taken as 40% of the minimum tensile strength at room temperature for carbon and alloy steel and 80% of the minimum yield strength at room temperature for austenitic stainless steel.

(b) Despite anything contained in the clause(a), the hydraulic test at the maker's works may be dispensed with, provided that the tubes are subject to non-destructive testing by an appropriate method like Ultrasonic or Eddy Current or Stray Flux testing.

PART-IV

49.Seamless Carbon, Alloy Steel and Nickel Alloy Pipes.– (1)This regulation shall apply on hot finished and cold drawn seamless carbon, alloy steel and Nickel alloy pipes for high temperature service and the pipes shall conform in all respects with the requirements under this regulation in addition to the requirements specified in regulation 30.

(2) For design metal temperatures Table 2/1 of regulation 30 shall be considered.

(3) (a)The pipes shall be seamless and manufactured from fully killed steel produced by an open hearth or electric process or any of the oxygen processes.

(4) (a)(i) Carbon Steel hot finished pipes need not be heat treated.

(ii)Cold drawn pipes shall be given a sub-critical anneal, a full anneal, or a normalising heat treatment after the final cold finishing process.

(b) (i)Pipes of grades P1, P2, P5, P9, P11, P12, and P22 except P91, X20CrMoV121 and N06617 shall be reheated and furnished in full annealed, isothermal annealed or normalised and tempered condition.

(ii) If furnished in the normalised and tempered condition, the minimum tempering temperature for grade P1, P2, P11 and P12 shall be 650°C and the minimum tempering temperature for grades P5, P9 and P22 shall be 675°C.

(c)Pipes of grades P1, P2 and P12 either hot finished or cold drawn may be given a final heat treatment at 650°C to 705°C, instead of heat treatment specified in clause (b) above at the option of the manufacturer.

(d) Grades P91 shall be normalised at 1040°C minimum and tempered at 730°C minimum as a final heat treatment.

(e) Grades X20CrMoV121 shall be normalised at 1020°C - 1070°C and tempered at 730°C - 780°C for a period of one hour minimum.

(f) Grade N06617 shall be solution annealed at 1150°C to 1230°C and quenched in water or rapidly cooled by other means.

(a) (i)The pipes shall be finished, cleaned free from harmful defects.

(ii)They shall be reasonably straight, smooth, cylindrical and subject to the tolerance specified in this subregulation. Pipes manufacturer shall explore a sufficient number of visual inspections to provide reasonable assurance that they have been properly evaluated.

(6) (a)Test specimen required for flattening and expanding or flaring test specified in sub-regulations herein below shall be taken from ends of finished pipes prior to upsetting, swaging, expanding or any other forming operations or being cut to length and they shall be smooth on ends and free from burrs and flaws.

(b) If desirable and practicable, tensile test may be carried out on full section of the pipe up to the capacity of the testing machine and for large size pipes, the tensile test specimen shall consist of strip cut longitudinally from the pipe and which is not straightened within the gauge length and further heat treated.

(c) If any test specimen shows flaws or defective machining it may be discarded and another specimen substituted.

(d) All specimens shall be tested at room temperature.

(7) The tests specified in sub-regulations below shall be made on minimum 2 pipes for first 100 pipes and 1 per 100 or part thereof for pipes over 100 numbers from each lot. Lot applies to all pipes of same nominal size and wall thickness of same heat and heat treatment.

(8) (a)The tensile test may be carried out on the test pieces cut out from the pipe in the longitudinal direction which shall not be further heat treated nor straightened within the gauge length. As an alternative, pipes may be tested on full cross section.

(b)Test pieces cut from the ends of the selected pipes shall comply with the requirements specified in this sub-regulation.

(9) For pipes of outside diameter 60.3 millimetre(mm) and under, a bend test shall be conducted. A sufficient length of pipe shall stand being bent cold through 90° around a cylindrical mandrel, the diameter of which is 12 times the nominal diameter of the pipe, without developing cracks.

Provided that bend test shall be required for grade N06617.

(10) (a) For pipes of outside diameter over 60.3 mm, a flattening test shall be conducted and a section of the pipe not less than 63 mm in length shall be flattened cold between two parallel flat surfaces to a distance between the plates (H) as calculated by the formula given below, without showing any sign of a crack or flaw:

(1 + e) t H = —————e + t/D where, t = specified thickness of pipe (mm) D = specified outside diameter of the pipe (mm) e = a constant, as given below e = 0.07 for Carbon steel with maximum specified carbon above 0.18% e = 0.09 for Carbon steel with maximum specified carbon 0.18% or less. e = 0.08 for alloy steel

(b) For pipe whose diameter equals or exceeds 254 mm, a bend test may be conducted instead of the flattening test. The bend test specimens shall be bent at room temperature through 180° without cracking on the outside of the bent portion. The inside diameter of the bend shall be 25 mm.

Substitution of the bend test for the flattening test shall be subject to the approval of the purchaser.

(c)For pipe whose diameter exceeds 635 millimetre(mm) and whose diameter to wall thickness ratio is

7.0 or less, the bend test described in sub-regulation (9) shall be conducted instead of the flattening test.

Provided that flattening test shall be required for grade N06617.

(11) (a) If any one or more tests specified in these regulations fail, two further tests of the same kind may be made on two other pipes of the same batch and if any of these tests fails, the batch of the pipes represented may be given a further heat treatment and entire set of tests shall be repeated.

(b) If the repeat tests are satisfactory the pipes shall be accepted and in case of any failure in the tests, the entire batch of the pipes shall be rejected.

(12) (a) Each pipe shall be tested by the manufacturer and shall withstand a hydraulic pressure to one and half time the design pressure, but not greater than pressure calculated by the formula given below and in the absence of working pressure indicated in the Test Certificate then the test pressure indicated in the Test Certificate divided by 1.5 shall be the maximum working pressure:

P = 2St/D Where, P = test pressure D = specified outside diameter of the pipe t = specified wall thickness of the pipe S = stress which shall be taken as 40% of the minimum tensile strength at room temperature.

(b) Despite anything contained in the clause (a) above, the hydraulic test at the maker's works may be dispensed with, provided that the pipes are subject to non-destructive testing by an appropriate method like Ultrasonic or Eddy Current or Stray flux testing.

PART-V

50.Castings of carbon, alloy and stainless steel.–(1) This regulation shall cover carbon, alloy and stainless steel castings used in the construction of boilers and steam pipes, including their fittings.

(2) (a) All castings shall be supplied in the heat-treated condition as following:-

(i) carbon steel castings: annealing, or normalising, or normalising and tempering.

(ii) alloy steel castings: annealing, or normalising and tempering, or hardening or quenching and tempering.

(iii) stainless steel castings: solution annealing.

(b) Heat treatment shall be carried out either at the discretion of the manufacturer or the Competent Person and shall be carried out at suitable temperatures to give the mechanical properties specified in regulation 54.

(3) All castings shall be properly fettled and dressed and all surfaces shall be satisfactorily cleaned for inspection purposes.

(4) The castings can be supplied as cast, rough machined or after final machining. The Non-Destructive Testing may be performed in as cast or in machined condition as per the non-destructive examination(NDE) requirements of relevant material specifications.

51. Process of manufacture.– (1) The steel used for castings shall be made by an Open Hearth or Electric Furnace or any other process which gives steel having equivalent properties.

(2) The steel shall comply with the chemical composition specified in the Table 2/32.

52. Freedom from and rectification of defects.–The steel castings shall have clean surfaces and shall be free from defects and the specified permissible variations in size or thickness shall not be exceeded. Defects may be repaired by welding only, with the approval of the Inspecting Authority provided that the castings are stressrelieved after welding if necessary.

53. Number of tests and provision of test samples.–Tensile and bend test pieces shall be prepared from test samples representative of each melt or heat-treatment batch as following:-

(i)

(a)The test samples shall be cast attached to or separate from the castings as agreed between the manufacturer and the Competent Person.

(b) The agreement for attached test samples shall include the precise location and method of attachment.

If the Competent Person fails to make such an agreement with the manufacturer, the decision as to the manner of providing test samples shall rest with the manufacturer.

(c)The discretion of the manufacturer shall not, however, apply to the testing of castings made from more than one melt as indicated in sub-regulation (4) below.

(ii)

(a)If the test samples are attached to the castings, at least one tensile test, and when required one bend test, shall be made from each melt.

(b) Such test samples shall not be detached from the castings until heat-treatment of the castings has been completed and they have been stamped by the Competent Person.

(c) Test samples shall be stamped by the Competent Person after that heat-treatment process.

(iii)

(a)If the test samples are cast separately, they shall be provided to the extent of 2 per cent of the number of castings from each melt, but in no case there shall be less than 2 samples per melt, except by agreement between the manufacturer and the Competent Person.

(b) The test samples shall be cast in moulds of the same material as is used for the castings and shall be made at the same time as the castings, and run from the same ladle.

(c) The samples shall be stamped by the Competent Person so as to identify the castings to which they relate.

(iv)

(a)When a casting is made from more than one melt, at least four tensile tests, and where required four bends tests, shall be made from test samples situated as far apart as possible on the casting.

(b) Some of the test samples shall be taken from as near the top and others near from the bottom of the casting as is practicable.

(v) Test samples shall be heat-treated with the castings they represent.

54. Tensile test.– (1) The upper yield point at room temperature shall be not less than fifty percent of the specified minimum tensile strength at room temperature.

(2) The minimum value of stress at proof limit of 0.2% at elevated temperature (Et) of the material may be calculated by multiplying the minimum specified tensile strength at room temperature (R20) by the value of ratio Et/R20 given in Table 2/5 under regulation 37.

(3) If, however, the tensile tests are carried out on specimen conforming to any other standards, the material will be accepted provided the tensile strength and elongation computed on the basis of the dimensions of the test pieces under the regulation shall conform to the above requirements.

(4) The test specimen shall comply with the requirements given below:

Table 2/33 Serial No.

55. Bend tests.–(1) Cold bend tests shall be made upon test pieces having rectangular section of 25 mm wide by 19 mm thick.

(2) The test pieces shall be machined and the edges rounded to a radius of 1.6 mm and shall be bent over the thinner section.

(3) Bend tests may be made by pressure or by blows, and the test pieces shall without fracture withstand being bent round a former having a radius of 25 mm through an angle not less than that given in the Table 2/34. 120° Table 2/34 Serial No.

Grade of Casting Angle of Bend minimum

1.

M1 Group 1 & 2 M3 Group 1 & 2, M4 Group1 90°

2.

M5A Group1, M5B Group1, M15E Group1

3.

M8 Group 1 & 2 and M43 No test

(4) Alternatively, the specimen preparation for bend test, test method and acceptance criteria may conform to the relevant material specifications.

56. Additional Tests.–(1)(a)In case any of the original test pieces fail to pass the mechanical tests, two further samples which represent the same castings shall be selected and tested in the same manner.

(b) The manufacturer shall have the right, if he so desires to re-heat-treat the castings before the two further samples are selected.

(c) In case the two further test satisfy the requirements of this standard, castings represented by them shall be accepted and in case either of the re-tests fail, the castings represented shall be liable to rejection.

(2) The Competent Person may call for hardness testing of the heat-treated castings.

(3) (a)(i) The Competent Person may call for radiographic examination or other non-destructive testing of the castings and castings found to be unsound may be treated in accordance with regulation 52.

(ii) If the welding repairs are sanctioned by the Competent Person, they shall be carried out in accordance with regulation 57.

(b) High class Castings for which a factor greater than 80% is taken in computation of permissible working stress shall be subject to the following inspection requirements, namely:

(i) (a)Each casting shall be examined by radiographic or ultrasonic methods at all critical locations and found free from harmful defects.

(b) All the castings shall be fully machined to such an extent that all critical sections are exposed for full thickness.

(ii) All castings shall be examined at all critical locations using magnetic-particle or penetrant-fluid procedure, or by grinding or machining and etching.

(iii) (a) Castings found to be defective shall be rejected by the Inspecting Authority or repaired to its satisfaction.

(b) If repairs by welding are carried out, the castings shall be subsequently stress-relieved or heat treated.

(c) Castings found to be unsound may be treated in accordance with regulation 57.

(d) Repaired areas of castings shall be re-examined by the Competent Person in accordance with subclause (i) of this sub-regulation and shall be shown to be free from harmful defects.

(iv) Castings to which a quality factor of 0.90 is assigned shall be clearly and permanently marked with 'G' suitable symbol after being examined as above and found satisfactory.

The provisions of clause(a)of sub-regulation 3shall apply to the castings other than high class castings for which a factor greater than 80% is used in the computation of permissible working stress for shells of Boiler and integral super-heater Drums and Headers under regulation 170.

57. Procedure for welding.–(1) In order to maintain a high standard of workmanship in the practice of arcwelding repair to steel castings, a written down procedure taking care of the following requirements shall be followed.

(2) (a) The surfaces of the steel, it is intended to weld shall be cleaned and freed from all foreign matter, otherwise it may result in poor penetration and unsound weld metal.

(b) The preparation for the welding of cracks shall be of the U or V types. When the later is used, the included angle shall be not less than 70. Where cracks extend through the section of the casting, the choice between single and double preparation, U or V will be governed by:

(i) the thickness of the casting;

(ii) the location of the defect. In double preparation, U or V the root of the U or V should be sufficiently open to ensure complete penetration.

(3) The selection of electrodes shall be compatible with the relevant material groups. The electrode manufacturer's recommendations for the use of the electrode shall be strictly followed.

(4) Variations in composition, mass design and the extent of welding required make it impossible to lay down specific rules to establish conditions under which pre-heating should be applied. When after consideration of each casting in the light of the above variables it is deemed necessary to pre-heat, such pre-heating should be carried out at a temperature of 150°C-300°C.

(5) (a)When the welding is carried out after the casting has received its normal heat treatment, a further heat treatment may be considered necessary. This treatment need be applied only in cases where welding has been such as to induce either internal stresses which would be detrimental to the casting, or locally hardened areas which would cause difficulty in machining. Where this further beat treatment is applied, the casting, where possible, should not be allowed to go cold after welding, but should be charged into a warm furnace and reheated to a suitable tempering temperature which in general should be not less than 550°C.

(b) When considering the advisability of applying this further treatment, its effect on the physical properties of the castings shall be borne in mind. If the treatment, or the welding itself is such that the physical properties will be adversely affected, the full heat treatment required to produce satisfactory qualities shall be substituted.

PART-VI

58.Forgingsof carbon, alloy, stainless steel and nickel alloys.–(1) The steel used for the forgings shall be made by an Open Hearth or Electric or basic Oxygen process or by any other process which gives steel having equivalent properties.

(2) (a)The steel shall not contain more than 0.05 per cent of sulphur or of phosphorus.

(b) The steel shall comply with the chemical composition specified in the Table 2/35.

1. Maximum when single values specified

2. For SA 234 WPB, Fittings made from bar or plate may have 0.35 max carbon.

3. For SA 234 WPB & SA 234 WPC Fittings:

(i) The sum of copper, nickel, chromium, molybdenum and vanadium shall not exceed 1.00%.

(ii) Fittings made from forgings may have 0.35 max carbon and 0.35 max silicon with no minimum

(iii) For each reduction of 0.01 % below the specified carbon maximum, an increase of 0.06 % manganese above the specified maximum will be permitted, up to a maximum of 1.65 %.

4. For SA 350 LF1, LF2 & SA 105 Forgings

(i)The sum of chromium and molybdenum shall not exceed 0.32%.

(ii) The sum of copper, nickel, chromium, molybdenum and vanadium shall not exceed 1.00%.

5. J Grade F 321 shall have a titanium content of not less than five times the carbon content and not more than 0.70 %.

K Grade F 321H shall have a titanium content of not less than four times the carbon content and not more than 0.70 %.

M Grades F 347H shall have a columbium content of not less than eight times the carbon content and not more than 1.10 %.

(3) Heat treatment shall be as per relevant material specifications.

(4) Where not machined, the forgings, shall have workman like surfaces as normally obtained by rolling, forging or drawing.

Provided that the minimum required thickness is maintained, minor surface defects may be admitted and other defects removed by mechanical means so as to achieve a smooth surface. Surface defects may also be repaired, by welding only, with the approval of the Inspecting Authority provided that the parts are stressrelieved after welding where necessary.

59. Selection of test pieces.– (1) For testing, either the parts themselves shall be used or additional material or sufficient size for test pieces shall be available. In the case of headers for examples, surplus lengths for test rings may be used. In the case of flanges swaged in dies, the punching left may be used as test pieces, in which case the identity of the material of the punching with that of the flange shall be evidenced in an adequate manner. The test may be performed also on the semi-finished material for example a bar or a billet, provided it is given approximately the same reduction and heat-treatment as the finished forging. For headers with closed ends, the test rings shall be cut off before dishing or reducing and shall be subjected to the same heat treatment as the headers themselves. In the case of headers, which are closed by special covers, the test ring shall be cut off after heat treatment. Similar procedure shall be followed for test pieces of other forgings.

(2) For testing, piece of one and the same cast of the same heat treatment batch as well as of similar dimensions may be gathered into one group and at least one test piece shall be made from each group.

(3) Tangential test piece shall be used for headers where practicable. For header with outside diameter less than 200 mm, longitudinal test pieces may be allowed.

(2) The upper yield point at room temperature shall be not less than 50% of the specified minimum tensile strength.

(3) The minimum values of the stress at proof limit 0.2% at elevated temperature (Et) may be calculated by multiplying the minimum specified tensile strength of the material at room temperature (R20) by the value of the ratio Et/R20 given in the Table 2/5 under regulation 37.

61. Bend tests.─ (1) Bend test pieces shall be of rectangular cross-section machined to a finished size of 25 mm wide by 19 mm thick. The edges shall be rounded to a radius of 1.6 mm. The test pieces shall be bent over the thinner section.

Test pieces shall be bent when cold through an angle of 180° without fracture, the internal radius of the bend being not greater than that specified in Table 2/37 for the 25 mm wide by 19 mm thick test piece.

M43 No test Alternatively, the specimen preparation for bend test, test method and acceptance criteria may conform to the relevant material specifications.

62. Additional tests before rejection.─ (1) In case either a tensile or a bend test fails, two further tests of the type which failed may be made on test pieces out from the same forging. If the results obtained from these retests are satisfactory, the forging shall be accepted, provided that in other respects they fulfil the conditions laid down above for steel forgings. If these retests do not give satisfactory results, the forgings represented may be re-heattreated together with the remainder of the test blocks and presented for further testing.

(2) In all cases where final retests do not give satisfactory results, the forgings represented by the test pieces which fail shall be rejected.

PART-VII

63.GENERAL GREY IRON CASTINGS (GRADE A).─ (1) The castings shall be cast from metal melted or refined in any metallurgical plant other than an iron ore smelting furnace, for the use of which furnace permission in writing must be received from the Inspecting Authority.

(2) The Composition of the iron as cast shall be left to the discretion of the manufacturer but the maximum percentage of phosphorus or sulphur or both may be specified by the Inspecting Authority, if it so desires.

(3) The castings shall be accurately moulded in accordance with the pattern or working drawings supplied by the Inspecting Authority with the addition of such letters, figures or marks as may be specified and the drawings shall include the tolerances specified in the regulation.

(4) (a)The castings shall be sound, clean out of twist and free from blow holes distortion and all surface and other defects.

(b) They shall be well dressed or fettled and shall be machinable by normal methods.

64. Provision of test bars.─(1) Prior to testing, the Inspecting Authority shall state whether it requires tensile or transverse tests, or both, and it may also specify cast on bars where the design of the casting and method of running permit.

(2)(a) When the test bars are cast separately they shall be poured at the same time and from the same ladle, or metal as the casting or castings they represent.

(b) The number of test specified in regulation 69 shall be applicable to all castings of each melt.

(3) When the bars are cast-on, the mould for the casting and the mould for the test piece shall be joined together in such a manner that the liquid metal fills both moulds at the same operation.

(4 ) All test bars shall be cast in green sand or dry sand or in loam moulds according to whether the casting or castings they represent are moulded in green sand, or in loam or dry sand, respectively.

(5) The test bars shall not be subjected to any heat treatment after leaving the moulds.

66. Mechanical test.─(1)The casting shall comply with the transverse and tensile tests specified in regulations 67 and 68.

(2) All test pieces shall be selected by the Competent Person and tested to his satisfaction.

(2) The supports and the point of application of the load shall be rounded to a radius of not less than 3 mm.

69. Number of transverse and tensile tests.─(1)The number of tests required for each batch of castings shall be in accordance with the Table 2/42, and various classes of castings divided into four representative groups.

(2) One test shall refer to one transverse and one tensile test, whether taken from one or two test bars as cast.
