PART-VIII

71.COVERED ELECTRODES FOR SHIELDED METAL ARC WELDING.─(1) (a)(i)This regulation shall apply to covered electrodes of sizes 2.0 mm and larger in diameter for shielded metal arc welding of carbon steel, low alloy steels, stainless steels and non-ferrous materials;

(ii)Electrodes of less than 2.0 mm in diameter shall not be used in boilers.

(b) The covered electrode shall comply with the requirements specified in these regulations.

(c) The characteristics and behaviour under ordinary working conditions shall be such that satisfactory weld deposits can be made by a welder of average skill and experience and that the following characteristics are obtained, namely:-

(i) relative ease in the control of the slag and weld metal during welding;

(ii) good degree of stability of the arc;

(iii) no undue degree of spatter;

(iv) desired rate of deposition and depth of penetration.

(v) easy removability of the slag;

(vi) no undue tendency to under-cut;

(vii) desired contour of the weld deposits: and

(viii) low internal porosity of the weld metal.

(2) The covered electrodes shall be classified into following categories and in accordance with applicable Specifications for Filler Alloys (SFA) of American Society of Mechanical Engineers (ASME) Part IIC specifications or AWS (American Welding Society) specifications or equivalent:

(a) Carbon steel electrodes

(b) Low alloy steel electrodes

(c) Stainless steel electrodes

(d) Nickel and nickel-alloy welding electrodes

(3) Covered electrodes may be made by any method that shall yield a product conforming to the requirements of these regulations and relevant Specifications for Filler Alloys (SFA) or American Welding Society

(AWS) specification.

(4) (a)The nominal size of the covered electrodes shall refer to the diameter of the core wire and the length shall not exceed 450 mm with provision for end gripping.

(b)The tolerance on the specified diameter of the core wire of the electrodes shall be ± 0.05 mm.

(c)The tolerance on the length of the electrodes shall be ±10 mm or as specified in relevant Specifications for Filler Alloys (SFA) or American Welding Society (AWS) specifications.

(5) (a)The core wire and covering shall be free from defects that would interfere with the uniform deposition of electrode.

(b)The flux covering shall be sufficiently robust to withstand the normal conditions of transport, storage, handling and use, without damage and shall be uniform in thickness all over so that it fuses evenly.

(c) The covering shall be concentric with core and the permissible variation on maximum core plus one covering shall not exceed the minimum core plus one covering as under:

(i) For diameter up to 2.5 mm 7 % of mean dimension,

(ii) For diameter 3.2 to 4.0 mm 5 % of mean dimension, and

(iii) For diameter 5 mm and larger 4 % of mean dimension.

(6) At least one imprint of electrode classification shall be imprinted at the covering within 65 mm of grip end. The imprinting shall be legible both before and after welding.

(7) (a)Covered electrodes shall be suitably packed to guard against damage during transportation. When stored, the electrodes shall be kept in their original bundles or packing in a dry store room and under such conditions, the electrodes shall be capable of giving results similar to those which they would have given on the date of their dispatch from the manufacturer for a period of two years or as recommended by the electrode manufacturer whichever is less. Usage of electrodes beyond this period may be permitted after conducting check tests as per specification, provided it is not restricted by the electrode manufacturer.

(b)Each package shall be marked with the following information.

(i) Name of the Manufacturer.

(ii) Trade name / Brand name of the electrodes.

(iii) Size and quantity of electrodes.

(iv)Batch Number and date of manufacture.

(v)Recommended current range.

(vi)AWS Classification.

(8) (a) The electrodes shall be subjected to initial tests and periodic check tests to ensure that the requirements of these regulations are fulfilled.

(b) Electrodes intended for use with more than one type of current or polarity shall be tested using each type of current and polarity shall be tested using each type of current and polarity.

(c) Where any test specimen fails to satisfy the requirements of any particular test, two further test specimens shall be prepared using the electrodes from the same batch and submitted to the same test.

(d) The batch of electrodes shall be accepted as having passed the test provided that the tests of both the additional specimens are satisfactory.

(9) Test certificate provided by the manufacturer of covered electrode shall fulfil the requirements of the specification. The manufacturers, when called upon, shall produce the results of the most recent periodic check test carried out within the preceding 12 months, for electrodes supplied.

PART-IX

72.RODS AND ELECTRODES FOR GAS SHIELDED ARC WELDING.─(1)(a)This regulation apply to electrodes and rods of sizes 0.8 mm and larger in diameter for gas shielded arc welding of carbon steels, low alloy steels, stainless steels and non-ferrous materials used in these regulations.

(b) The rods and electrodes shall comply with this regulation and the applicable requirements of AWS or SFA specifications.

(c) Their characteristics and behaviour under ordinary working conditions shall be such that satisfactory weld deposits can be made by a welder of average skill and experience with following characteristics.

(d) The rods and electrodes shall be suitable for manual, semi-automatic and machine welding.

(i) relative ease in the control of the molten pool during welding;

(ii) desired degree of stability of the arc;

(iii) no undue degree of spatter;

(iv) desired rate of deposition and depth of penetration.

(v) no undue tendency to under-cut;

(vi) desired contour of the weld deposits: and

(vii) low internal porosity of the weld metal.

(2) The electrodes and rods shall be classified into following four categories, namely:-

(a) Carbon steel Electrodes and rods;

(b) Low-alloy steel electrodes and rods;

(c) Stainless steel electrodes and rods; and

(d) Nickel and nickel-alloy welding electrodes and rods

(3) Electrodes and rods shall be made by any method to yield a product conforming to the requirements of these regulations and relevant Specifications for Filler Alloys (SFA) or American Welding Society

(AWS) specification.

(4) The size and tolerance of the electrodes and rods for straight length and coils form shall meet the appropriate Specifications for Filler Alloys (SFA) or American Welding Society (AWS) specification.

(5) (a)The rods and electrodes shall have smooth finish and reasonably free from slivers, depressions, scratches, scale, seams, laps and foreign matter so that these do not affect the properties of the weld metal.

(b)The core ingredients shall be uniformly distributed throughout the length of the electrode ensuring consistent weld metal properties.

(6) (a)Coils, spools and drums, containing electrodes shall have tag or label suitably and securely affixed with suitable information.

(b) Rods shall have at least one imprint for positive identification of Specifications for Filler Alloys

(SFA) or American Welding Society (AWS) specification.

(7) (a)Electrodes and rods shall be suitably packed to guard against damage during transportation.

(b) Product information shall be legibly marked on each package.

(8) Each package shall be marked with the following information, namely:-

(a) Name of the Manufacturer;

(b) Trade name / Brand name of the electrodes;

(c) Size and quantity of electrodes;

(d) Lot, Control or heat number; and

(e) American Welding Society (AWS) Classification

(9) (a) The electrodes and rods shall be subjected to initial tests and periodic check tests to ensure that the requirements of these regulations are fulfilled.

(b) Electrodes and rods intended for use with more than one type of gas shall be tested using each type of gases.

(c) Where any test specimen fails to satisfy the requirements of any particular test, two further test specimens shall be prepared using the electrodes from the same batch and submitted to the same test.

(d) The batch of electrodes shall be accepted as having passed the test provided that the tests of both the additional specimens are satisfactory.

(10) (a)Test certificate provided by the manufacturer of electrodes and rods shall fulfil the requirements of the specification.

(b) The manufacturers, when called upon, shall produce the results of the latest Periodic check test carried out within the preceding twelve months, for electrodes and rods supplied.

PART-X

73.ELECTRODES FOR FLUX CORED ARC WELDING.─(1)(a)This regulation apply to electrodes for flux cored arc welding either with or without shielding gas.

(b) The electrodes shall be suitable for flux cored arc welding of carbon steels, low alloy steels, stainless steels and nonferrous materials used in these regulations.

(c) The electrodes shall comply with the applicable requirements of AWS or SFA specification.

(d)Their characteristics and behaviour under ordinary working conditions shall be such that satisfactory weld deposits can be made by a welder of average skill and experience and that the following characteristics are also obtained.

(e)The electrodes shall be suitable for manual, semi-automatic and machine welding as per following parameters,-

(i) relative ease in the control of the molten pool during welding;

(ii) desired degree of stability of the arc

(iii) no undue degree of spatter;

(iv) desired rate of deposition and depth of penetration.

(v) no undue tendency to under-cut;

(vi) desired contour of the weld deposits

(vii) low internal porosity of the weld metal and

(viii) desired degree of slag removal

(2) The electrodes shall be classified into following four categories, namely:-

(a) Carbon steel Electrodes;

(b) Low-alloy steel electrodes;

(c) Stainless steel electrodes ; and

(d) Nickel and nickel-alloy welding electrodes

(3) Electrodes shall be made by any method to yield a product conforming to the requirements of these regulations and relevant AWS or SFA specification.

(4) The size and tolerance of the electrodes and rods for straight length and coils form shall meet the appropriate AWS or SFA specification.

(5) Finish and uniformity.─ The electrodes shall have smooth finish and reasonably free from slivers, depressions, scratches, scale, seams, laps and foreign matter so that these do not affect the properties of the weld metal. The core ingredients shall be uniformly distributed throughout the length of the electrode ensuring the weld metal properties.

(6) Coils, spools and drums containing electrodes shall have tag or label suitably and securely affixed with suitable information.

(7) (a) Electrodes shall be suitably packed to guard against damage during transportation and product information shall be legibly marked on each package.

(b) Each package shall be marked with the following information, namely:-

(i)Name of the Manufacturer;

(ii) Trade name /Brand name of the electrodes;

(iii) Size and quantity of electrodes;

(iv) Lot, Control or heat number; and

(v) American Welding Society (AWS) Classification

(8) (a) The electrodes shall be subjected to initial tests and periodic check tests to ensure that the requirements of these regulations are fulfilled.

(b) Where any test specimen fails to satisfy the requirements of any particular test, two further test specimens shall be prepared using the electrodes from the same batch and submitted to the same test.

(c) The batch of electrodes shall be accepted as having passed the test provided that the tests of both the additional specimens are satisfactory.

(9) (a)Test certificate provided by the manufacturer of electrodes shall fulfil the requirements of the specification.

(b) The manufacturers, when called upon, shall produce the results of the most recent periodic check test carried out within the preceding twelve months, for the electrodes supplied.

PART-XI

74. ELECTRODES AND FLUXES FOR SUBMERGED ARC WELDING.─ (1) (a)This regulation apply to electrodes and fluxes for submerged arc welding process.

(b) The electrodes and flux combination shall be suitable for submerged arc welding of carbon steels, low alloy steels and nickel alloys in these regulations.

(c) The electrodes and flux combination shall comply with the applicable requirements of AWS or SFA specification.

(d) The wire flux combination shall be suitable for multi run and single run technique, using DC or AC current and producing weld with desired physical and metallurgical properties.

(e) The electrode flux combination shall be suitable for either semi-automatic or machine welding process.

(f) The characteristics and behaviour of the flux electrode combination under ordinary working conditions shall be such that satisfactory weld deposits can be made by a welder of average skill and experience with following characteristics, namely:-.

(i) desired rate of deposition and depth of penetration;

(ii) no undue tendency to under-cut;

(iii) desired contour of the weld deposits;

(iv) low internal porosity of the weld metal; and

(v) desired degree of slag removal

(2) The electrodes shall be classified into following three categories, namely:-

(a) Carbon steel electrodes with suitable flux;

(b) Low-alloy steel electrodes with suitable flux; and

(c) Nickel alloy electrodes with suitable flux

(3) (a)Electrodes shall be made by any method to yield a product conforming to the requirements of these regulations and relevant AWS or SFA specification.

(b)The method of manufacture of flux shall be fused, agglomerated and mixed and Crushed slag or recrushed flux shall not be used for manufacture of flux.

(4) (a)The size and tolerance of the electrodes shall meet the appropriate AWS or SFA specification.

(b) Flux shall be granular for smooth feeding and ensure proper shielding. The particle size distribution shall be uniform and consistent.

(5) (a)The electrodes shall have smooth finish and free from slivers, depressions, scratches, scale, seams, laps and foreign matter.

(b) These shall not affect the properties of the weld metal.

(c)The core ingredients shall be uniformly distributed throughout the length of the electrode ensuring the weld metal properties.

(6) (a)Coils, spools and drums containing electrodes shall have tag or label suitably and securely affixed with suitable information.

(b) Bags containing flux shall have suitable identification.

(7) (a) Electrodes and fluxes shall be suitably packed to guard against damage during transportation and product information shall be legibly marked on each package.

(b) Each package shall be marked with the following information, namely:-

(i) Name of the Manufacturer ;

(ii) Trade name / Brand name of the electrodes or flux;

(iii) Size and quantity of electrodes or flux;

(iv) Lot, batch or heat number; and

(v) American Welding Society (AWS) Classification

(8) (a) The electrodes and flux shall be subjected to initial tests and periodic check tests to ensure that the requirements under these regulations are fulfilled.

(b) Where any test specimen fails to satisfy the requirements of any particular test, two more test specimens shall be taken and subjected to the same test.

(c) The batch of electrodes and flux shall be accepted as having passed the test provided that the tests of both the additional specimens are satisfactory.

(9) (a)Test certificate provided by the manufacturer of electrodes and flux shall fulfil the requirements of the specification.

(b) The manufacturers, when called upon, shall produce the results of the latest periodic check test carried out within the preceding twelve months, for the electrodes or flux supplied.

75.(1) Initial tests of each brand of electrodes shall be carried out in accordance with these regulations and relevant AWS or SFA specification.

(2) (a) All weld metal tensile test specimens shall be prepared and tested in accordance with the method described in Appendix-C.

(b) The ultimate tensile stress, yield stress and elongation of each test specimen shall meet the relevant AWS or SFA specification.

(3) One fillet weld hot cracking test shall be carried out as specified in Appendix-C.

(4) (a)The electrodes shall be deemed to be satisfactory provided no hot cracking occurs under the conditions of test.

(b)Crater cracks may be neglected provided they do not run into the full section of the weld.

(5) One transverse tensile test shall be carried out for each welding position for which the electrode is recommended by the manufacturer except two tests shall be required for the flat position. The ultimate tensile stress shall meet the relevant AWS or SFA specification. The method of preparation of the test pieces is specified in Appendix-C.

(6) Two bend tests one with the face and the other with the root in tension shall be carried out for each welding position for which the electrode is recommended by the manufacturer, except that two in each shall be carried out for those intended for flat position only. The method of preparation and carrying out the test shall be in accordance with Appendix-C. The electrode shall be deemed to be satisfactory provided that on completion of the test, no crack or defect at the outer surface is greater than 3 mm measured across the specimen or 1.6 mm measured along its length. Premature failures at corners of the test specimen shall not be considered a cause for rejection. For nickel alloy electrodes three transverse side bend test shall be carried out as per the applicable AWS or SFA specification.

(7) Five impact test specimens are required for carbon steel and low alloy steel electrodes only. The size of the electrode shall be as per the relevant AWS or SFA specification. Charpy V notch testing shall be carried out as per the applicable AWS or SFA specification.

(8) Each classification of electrode shall be tested for the chemical analysis of undiluted weld metal as per the relevant AWS or SFA specification.

(9) (a) All weld test plate shall be radiographed to evaluate the usability of the electrode and the soundness of the weld deposits prior to taking up for other tests.

(b) For the purpose of radiography, the backing plate shall be removed and both the surfaces of the weld metal shall be machined or ground smooth and flush without reducing the thickness of the weld metal.

(c) Test procedure and acceptance norms shall be in accordance with the relevant AWS or SFA specification.

(d) A length of 25 mm from both the ends of the test plate shall be discarded and need not be considered in evaluation of the radiograph.

76. Periodic check test.─ The following periodic check tests shall be carried out on each brand of electrodes, namely:-

(i) Periodic check tests consist of a selection of the tests mentioned under regulation 75 and they shall be repeated at intervals of not more than 12 months to provide evidence that the electrodes currently produced continue to possess the properties recorded in the initial tests.

(ii) All-weld tensile test with any two sizes of electrodes within the limits mentioned in sub-regulation (2) of regulation 75.

(iii)One Tee joint fillet weld hot cracking test as mentioned in sub-regulation (3) of regulation 75.

CHAPTER-3 CONSTRUCTION AND WORKMANSHIP PART-I

77. Preparation of plates.─ (1)The edges of all plates and butt straps shall be machined or flame cut by machine and the caulking edges shall be bevelled to an angle not sharper than 20° to the plane of the plate and between 70° and 80°wherever practicable. Gusset Plates may be sheared except on those edges, which are to be welded.

(2) Plates which are to be flame-cut by machine without heat treatment or subsequent grinding or machining shall not have a carbon content higher than 0.25 per cent.

(3) Where the carbon content exceeds 0.25 percent, heat treatment, grinding or machining shall be carried out.

(4) The edges of all plates shall have a smooth finish.

(5) Before commencing welding of the joint (apart from tack welding), it shall be ascertained that the chamfered edges are in alignment and that the defects in alignment between the surfaces of the plates do not exceed as given in the table 3/1:

Table 3/1: ALIGNMENT TOLERANCE OF SECTIONS TO BE BUTT-WELDED Serial Section Thickness(t), in mm Direction of joints in cylindrical shells No.

Longitudinal Circumferential Up to 13 mm incl.

Over 13 mm up to 19 mm 3 mm Over 19 mm and up to 38 3 mm 5 mm Over 38 mm and up to 51 mm 3 mm 3 mm Over 51 mm Lessor of t/16 or 9 mm Lessor of t/8 or 19 mm

(6) If plate edges of unequal thickness are abutted and the difference between the surfaces exceeds the limit mentioned as above on either side, the thicker plate shall be thinned to a smooth taper for a distance not less than 4 times the offset including, if so desired, the width of the weld. In longitudinal joints, the middle line of the plates shall be in alignment within 10 per cent of the thickness of the thicker plate with a maximum of 3 mm.

78. Normalising or stress relieving of steel plates.─ (1) All plates which have been dished or flanged shall be stress relieved as components when cold worked and normalized when hot worked fully or partially.

(2) Stress relieving of components may be combined with final stress relieving when stress relieving is envisaged later.

79. Minimum thickness of plates.─ No boiler plate shall be less than 6 mm in thickness.

PART-II

80. Cylindrical shells.─ (1) Each ring shall be formed from not more than two plates and rolled to cylindrical form to the extreme ends of the plate. The bending shall be done entirely by machine. Local heating or hammering shall not be used for correction of profile.

(2) The longitudinal seam or seams may be placed in any suitable position but the seams in successive rings shall not fall in line and shall be staggered by minimum 150 mm.

(3) In the case of brick set boilers the seam or seams shall be kept as far as practicable clear of the brickwork.

81. Position of longitudinal seams.─ Each ring of plate forming the shell, barrel or drum shell, where practicable, be in one piece and have its longitudinal seam well out of line with those of the adjoining rings. In Lancashire, Cornish, and other types of boilers, where parts of the shell are exposed to flame, the longitudinal seams shell, where practicable, be in the steam space, arranged alternately on each side of the crown and clear of the brick work.

82. Circumferential and end seams.─ Joint efficiency of circumferential seams shall be determined as detailed in sub-regulation (1) of regulation 169. In the event of tube holes forming a ligament at the location of circumferential seams, the joint efficiency shall be calculated as per regulation 171.

83.Angle rings.─ Angle rings for the attachment of end plates to shell plates and/or flue sections shall be rolled or machined so that they fit closely to the flat and cylindrical surfaces to be connected, and shall be machined or machine gas cut on the caulking edges.

84. Thickness of shell angle rings.─ Where shell plates and end plate are connected by means of an external angle ring, the angle rings shall not be less in thickness than as follows: 90 per cent of the thickness and not less than 17 mm.

Table 3/2 For shell plates up to and including 16 mm in thickness 10 per cent in excess of the thickness of the thickness of the shell plate.

For shell plate exceeding 16 mm thickness and up to and including 25 mm in thickness.

For shell plates over 25 mm in thickness.

Made from angle bar having a section thickness of 25 mm.

85.Welded shell plates.─ The requirements of welded shells shall be covered by the provisions made in regulations in Chapter -4 or Chapter -12, as the case may be.

PART-III

86. Flat end plates.─ (1) Wherever practicable, the flat or dished end plates of boilers shall be in one piece made from one rolled plate. Where diameter of boiler is so large as to make this impracticable, the flat end plates may be made from two pieces by fusion butt-welding. (See figures 12/1 and 12/2). In the latter case, the line of welding shall be parallel to the horizontal axis of the boiler and shall be subjected to full radiographic examination.

(2) Flanging of plates shall be done by machine. Cold forming of flanged end plate shall be followed by heat treatment. Such flanging shall be done hot, preferably in one operation, but where this is impracticable creep machine flanging may be used provided that the plate is worked at a suitable temperature and heated for an adequate distance beyond that portion of the plate under immediate treatment.

(3) Hot forming of welded plates shall be permitted provided that the welded joint is subjected to 100 percent radiographic or Ultrasonic examination after hot forming.

(4) Welded plates up to and including 20 mm thickness can be cold formed provided it complies with following conditions.

(a) Before cold forming, the weld reinforcement shall be ground smooth, and the manufacturer shall take precautions, where necessary, in order to avoid crack formation in the weld metal or the heat affected zone.

(b) If the inside radius of curvature after forming is less than 10 times the thickness, stress relieving shall be carried out.

(c) After cold forming, the welded joints shall be visually examined and subjected to 100 percent radiographic or Ultrasonic examination throughout their length. Joints of any thickness which have been cold formed to a radius less than 10 times the thickness.

(d) Knuckles shall be inspected for cracks by magnetic particle or penetrant methods.

(e) After completion of the flanging operation flanges shall be of true peripheral contour (either circular or straight as necessary to ensure accurate alignment with the connecting parts), and of good surface, free from bulges, grooves or other local irregularities; flat portions of the plate shall be free from set or distortion.

(5) In the construction of unfired boilers when the dished ends do not form a part of the heating surface cold spun dished ends conforming to IS: 2825 may be used.

(6) Care shall be taken to see that the flanges are circular and of good surface, free from local irregularities, and that they are parallel and square with the flat part of the plate.

(7) Flat portions of end plates, on completion of all flanging and machining operations, shall be flat and free from set or distortion.

87. Strengthening of flat end plate at manhole.─ (1) In all cases, the front end plate gusset stays below the furnaces shall be placed as closely as possible to the mudhole frame.

(2) Where flat end plates are flanged for connection to the shell, the inside radius of flanging shall be not less than 1.75 times the plate thickness, with a minimum of 38 mm.

88. End Plates in steam spaces.─ When the end plates of boilers situated in the steam space are liable to contact with hot gases, the end plates shall be efficiently shielded from such contact.

89. Hemispherical crowns.─ (1) The segments for hemispherical shell crown plates shall be pressed in one heat to correct curvature and shall be butt-welded together in accordance with Figures 12/3, 12/4, 12/5 or 12/6. The cylindrical portion of the crown shall be tangential to the hemispherical portion.

(2) Where hemispherical furnace crowns are pressed from one plate, they shall be pressed to form by machine in progressive stages without thinning and shall be annealed on completion.

90. Dished end and crown plates.─ (1) The dished end plates of boilers shall be preferably in one piece made from one-rolled plate. If this is impracticable, owing to the large diameter, the dished end plate may be made from two plates, fusion butt welded together and in such cases the requirements of stress relieving and radiographic examination as provided for in regulation 86 shall apply and the line of weld shall be parallel to the horizontal axis of the boiler.

(2) The inside radius to which a plate is dished shall not be greater than the external diameter of the shell to which it is attached, except in the case of Lancashire and Cornish boilers when the radius shall not exceed 1½ times the diameter of the shell.

(3) The inside radius of curvature of the flanges to the shell or firebox shall not be less than four times the thickness of the crown or end plate and in no case less than 63 mm.

(4) The inside radius of curvature to uptakes shall be not less than twice the thickness of the crown plate and in no case less than 25 mm.

(5) Bunged mouth-pieces may be welded on to end plates in place of the orthodox flanging for the attachment of furnace tubes. Such welding shall comply with provisions of regulation 97.

(6) Manhole frames may be similarly welded on the front end plates, and will be subject to provisions of regulation 97.

91.Tube plates.─ Smoke box tube plates shall be flanged for attachment to the barrel or shell and shall be flat and connected thereto by welding.

92. Parts of flat tube plates within the tube nests.─(1) Where the total area of all tube nests in directly fired multi-tubular boilers exceeds 0.65 square meters, stay tubes shall be fitted.

(2) Where the total number of tubes in horizontal multi-tubular waste heat boilers and direct fired loco type boilers is arranged in one nest, the area of which exceed 1.95 sq. m stay tubes shall be fitted.

(3) In all cases where the total number of tubes is arranged in more than one nest, stay tubes shall be fitted.

(4) The parts of tube plates which lie outside the nests of tubes shall be stayed or supported wherever the size of the area of plate subject to steam pressure necessitates staying or support, either by marginal stay tubes or other means.

93.Flanging of end plates.─ All flanges shall be a good fit to the shell and flues. Flogging and/or hammering in the fitting of these parts is prohibited. The caulking edges of all flanged plates shall be machined or machine gas cut.

PART-IV

94. Furnaces.─(1)No furnace or firebox top, whether plain or corrugated, shall exceed 22 mm in thickness, and all circular sectioned furnaces when new, shall be as near the truly circular form as the type of joint will permit.

(2) The use of Z angle rings for furnace foundation seams shall be prohibited.

(3) Furnace crowns, uptakes and other plates not in tension may be joined by fusion welding provided the conditions laid down in regulation 95 to 101 are complied with. The end connections of such plates to the shell or shell crown shall also comply with such conditions. This construction shall not apply to furnaces of Lancashire and Cornish type boilers consisting wholly or plain sections.

95. Furnaces of horizontal boilers.─ (1) The sections of the internal flues shall each be in one plate and shall be bent while cold to circular form and shall be welded longitudinally.

(2) The maximum permissible variation in diameter at any cross-section shall not exceed the thickness of the plate.

(3) The weld shall be placed at the lower part of the flues, and shall break joint in successive sections by at least 305 mm.

(4) Each flange for the circular seams shall be formed at one heat by suitable machinery.

(5) The sections shall be allowed to cool gradually to avoid internal stresses.

(6) The caulking edges of all flue flanges shall be machined or machine gas cut.

(7) The circular seams shall be arranged so that they do not fall in line with those of the adjacent flue or with the circumferential seams of the shell and are at least 152 mm apart.

(8) When flues are flanged for attachment to both end plates the total length of each completed flue shall not exceed the length of the shell measured from the inner surface of the back end plate to the inner surface of the front end plate, both adjacent to the shell.

(9) Where the flues are flanged for attachment to the end plates, the end sections shall be 1.5 mm thicker than the remaining sections, except in cases where the calculated thickness is over 21 mm, when the end section shall be 22 mm in thickness.

(10) The flanged portion of furnace rings shall have radius of curvature of not less than 25 mm on water side.

(11) Sections of corrugated flues may be fusion butt welded circumferentially and afterwards stress relieved by heat treatment.

(12) As an alternative to adamson flanges, furnaces which are partly fitted with corrugated sections shall be strengthened by means of suitable stiffening rings adequately welded to the furnaces. In no case shall a flat stiffener be less than 63 mm x 13 mm.

96. Furnaces of vertical boilers.─ (1) The furnaces of vertical boilers may be constructed in one or more lengthwise sections, each section being rolled from one plate to a full circle. In such cases, the component sections may be jointed circumferentially by electrically butt welding and shall be stress relieved in accordance with regulation 123.

(2) In the case of vertical boilers where tube plates form part of the firebox, the tube plate portion may be constructed in two vertical sections and the vertical seams when welded shall be stress relieved. The maximum permissible variation in diameter, at any cross-section shall not exceed 6 mm for fireboxes up to 900 mm in diameter, or 10 mm for fireboxes over 900 mm in diameter, or half the thickness of the plate whichever is greater.

(3) Circular furnaces shall preferably be tapered, a tapper of 38 mm in diameter per 300 mm of height being recommended. The minimum water space at the bottom between the furnaces and the shell shall not be less than 50 mm for boilers up to 760 mm in diameter and shall be not less than 63 mm for boilers over 760 mm diameter.

(4) The method of welding shall be in accordance with regulation 151. Flats formed in the firebox for the insertion of water tubes shall have ample radius at the junction of the flat and the curved surfaces and shall be free from sharp corners or tool marks. The plate shall not be unduly thinned.

(5) Ogee flanging where integral with the firebox or in a separate ring shall preferably be formed in one heat by suitable machinery and shall be allowed to cool gradually to avoid internal stresses. Rings of fire holes mouthpieces or foundation rings shall be made of mild steel. Z sections shall not be used for foundation rings.

(6) Furnaces of vertical type boilers may be attached to the shell at the fire hole by a circular ring of substantial section fusion welded to shell and firebox. The opening in the shell for fire hole shall be substantially compensated by a ring welded on the outside of the shell plate.

(7) At the fire hole the firebox plate and the shell plate may be flanged and fusion welded together.

97. Fusion butt welded longitudinal seams.─ Where the longitudinal seam is fusion butt welded, the precautions shall be observed as follows:

(i) Plates 20 mm and above in thickness shall be bevelled from both sides of each abutting edge but the bevel need not necessarily be the same on each side (see Figs. 3/1 and 3/2). Plates less than 20 mm in thickness may be bevelled from one side only of each abutting edge (see Figs. 3/3 and 3/4).The included angle of the bevel shall be not less than 60o and the bevelling may be any one of the forms shown in Figs. 3/1 to3/4.

Fig. 3/1 Fig.3/2 Fig. 3/3 Fig. 3/4

(ii) In order to obtain full penetration of the weld metal, a gap shall be maintained between the plates when they are being welded. Joints shall be welded from both sides, and the slag shall be removed after making each run. Before the second side of the joint is welded any slag or defect at the bottom of the first run shall be removed by grinding, chipping or machining. There shall be no appreciable undercutting of the plate.

(iii) For the purposes of relieving internal stresses, the plates adjacent to a fusion weld shall be efficiently annealed after welding.

98. Furnace crown.─ (1) The furnace crown shall be made from one plate and the seam connecting it to the furnace shall not fall in line with any circumferential seam of the boiler shell.

(2) The furnace crown may be welded to the vertical portion of the firebox. The joint shall be fusion butt welded by the electric arc process, provided the conditions for fusion welding under regulation 97 are observed. Where the joint is to be welded, the edges of the furnace crown and the firebox body shall be bevelled in accordance with Fig. 3/3 or Fig. 3/4. The depth of the flange of the furnace crown plate from the commencement of the curvature of the flanging radius shall be not less than four times the plate thickness with a minimum of 38 mm. On completion of welding the firebox shall be effectively stress relieved by heat treatment (also refer regulation 100).

99. Cross tubes.─ (1) Cross tubes shall be made from seamless steel pipes or from plate or strip rolled and electric resistance or fusion butt welded into pipes by electric arc process. The fusion welding shall conform to the requirements of Chapter-12. Tolerances on these pipes shall conform to the requirements of regulation 251.

Where welded pipes are used, the longitudinal welds shall be so situated that they are not exposed to the direct impact of flame.

(2) Cross tubes shall not exceed 305 mm internal diameter. The minimum thickness shall be 8 mm.

(3) Where welded construction is employed, the tubes shall be of sufficient length to enter the furnace plate and the flush with the water side all round or project not less than 6 mm and not more than 16 mm into the water space at any part of the circumference. Where the tubes are fusion welded in position, the firebox plates shall be suitably chamfered and the joints shall be welded externally and internally (see Fig. 3/5).

Fig. 3/5: Fusion Welding of Cross Tubes of Vertical Cross Tube Boilers

100. Uptakes.─ (1) The uptakes shall be formed from seamless steel pipes or from plate or strip rolled and electric resistance welded or fusion butt welded into pipes by electric arc process. The tolerances on these pipes shall comply with the requirements of regulation 251.

(2) The uptake shall be fusion butt welded by the electric arc process to the upward flange of the opening in the firebox crown plate provided the conditions for fusion welding given in regulation 97 are observed.

The edges of the upward flange and the adjacent end of the uptake shall be bevelled in accordance Fig. 3/3 or Fig.3/4. The depth of the crown plate opening from the commencement of the curvature of the flanging radius shall be not less than twice the plate thickness with a minimum of 25 mm. On completion of welding the uptake and crown plate shall be effectively stress relieved by heat treatment.

(3) Where the firebox crown plate is also fusion welded to the body of the firebox, the firebox complete with uptake shall be effectively stress relieved by heat treatment on completion of the welding.

(4) Welded uptakes shall be so arranged that the weld is directly facing the longitudinal centre line of the manhole.

101. Loco type fireboxes.─ (1)The seams shall be welded subject to the depth of the flange being such that the welded seams fall between the 1st and 2nd rows of screwed stays or between the flange and the adjacent row screwed stay.

(2) The firehole mouthpiece may be similarly welded when the firebox and shell are flanged for the purpose.

(3) The welding shall comply with regulation 97.

PART-V

102. Bar stays.─ Bar stays shall not be welded at any part. They shall preferably have plus threads and the ends of the stays being upset or the middle portion being reduced for this purpose. Stay bars which have been worked in the fire shall be subsequently annealed.

103. Longitudinal stays.─(1) Longitudinal and similar solid screwed stays shall be efficiently secured with nuts at each end and not merely riveted over. Where they are over 3657 mm in length, they shall be supported at intervals of 1829 mm along its length.

(2) Where the stay is screwed through both plates, the stay and holes shall have a continuous thread and fitted with nuts and washers on the outside. Where the stay passes through clearing holes and internal nut and washer shall also be fitted. Where the stay is not normal to the surface of the plate the washer shall be shaped so as to form a true surface for the nuts or a knuckle joint may be fitted.

(3) In vertical boilers the stays shall either be screwed into both crown plates or screwed into the furnace crown plate and passed through clearing holes in the shell crown plate.

104. Nuts and Washers for Stays.─ (1) The washers shall be at least 2½ times the diameter of the threaded portion of the stay and not less than 6 mm in thickness.

(2) The thickness of the external nuts shall be at least equal to the diameter of the threaded portion of the stay. Where bar stays pass through clearing holes in end plates, internal nuts of a thickness not less than two-thirds of the diameter of the threaded portion of the stay shall be fitted.

105. Jointed stays.─ Where jointed longitudinal stay are fitted, they shall, where practicable, be fitted with pins having an effective sectional area 25 percent in excess of that of the stay. If the pins are slack in the holes, the total slackness shall not be more than 1.6 mm. The pins shall be as close as possible to the shoulder of the eye forging. The shoulder of the forging shall be at least 13 mm wide all round, i.e. the diameter at the shoulder shall be not less than the diameter of the hole plus 25 mm.

106. Diagonal bar or rod stays.─ The sectional area of diagonal rod or bar stay shall bear the same proportion to that of a direct stay as the length of the diagonal stay bears to the length of the perpendicular line from the end of the diagonal stay to the surface supported. The ends of diagonal stays, shall not be bent, but shall be attached to the plate with bevelled washers and nuts or with riveted tee, blocks or angles and shackle pins (refer regulation 105 for eyes and pins).

PART-VI

107. Screwed stays.─ (1) Screwed stays and the holes for these shall be screwed with a continuous thread.

(2) The screwed threads of the stay shall be clean and free from checks or imperfections, of full depth, correct whitworth form and a good fit in the holes. The middle portion shall preferably be turned down to the bottom of the thread.

(3) The stays shall be screwed with fine threads of not less than 11 threads per 25 mm.

(4) The diameter of the stay over the threads shall not be less than 19 mm or twice the thickness of the firebox plate whichever is the greater.

(5) The pitch of the stays at the furnace of vertical boilers shall not exceed 14 times the thickness of the furnace plate.

(6) Where the stays are not fitted with nuts, the ends shall be riveted over to form substantial heads.

Alternatively stays may be screwed through the shell and firebox plates and the projecting ends shall be substantially fillet welded in an approved manner. The projection of the end of the stay from the surface of the plate shall be not less than one quarter of the diameter of the stay but in no case less than 6 mm. The full end of the stay shall be visible on completion of the welding.

(7) Crown stays of Loco type boilers shall either be fitted with nuts or riveted over on the fire side.

108. Axial drilling.─ All screwed stays less than 355 mm long should preferably be drilled with a tell-tale hole 5 mm diameter to a depth of 13 mm beyond the inner face of the plate. Stays which are obscure on one side shall preferably be made from hollow stay bar.

109. Stay nuts.─ (1) Nuts of screw stays in combustion chambers and firebox shall not be less than 19 mm thick for stays up to 38 mm diameter over threads, 22 mm thick for 41 mm and 44 mm stays, 25 mm thick for 47 mm and 51 mm stays, and 28 mm thick for stays over 51 mm in diameter.

(2) The nuts shall be made of solid mild steel or of iron which shall be without weld, if exposed to flame.

110. Spacing of end stays-allowance for curves, etc.─ For the tops of fireboxes and combustion chambers, the distance between the rows of stays nearest to the tube plate or fire hole plate or back plate, as the case may be, and the commencement of curvature of these plates at their flanges shall not be greater than the horizontal pitch of the stays.

PART-VII

111. Girder stays for firebox and combustion chamber crowns.─ (1) Each girder when of the normal type, fitted with stay bolts and nuts, shall be of the double plate interconnected type of sufficient strength to support its proportion of the load on the crown to support plate independently of the crown plate, and the bolts or weld attachments shall have sufficient cross-sectional area to carry the applied load.

(2) The clear waterway between the crown plate and the underside of the girder bars shall be as large as practicable but in no case less than 38 mm as in Fig.3/8.

(3) The ends of the girders shall not rest on the landing of the flat crown plate but shall be carefully fitted to bed directly on the bends of the corners of the vertical end or side plates.

(4) The toes of the girder plates shall be solid with the girder plates and not separate pieces attached thereto.

(5) Girders shall be properly attached to the crown plate by bolts or screws.

(6) Where an all welded firebox is fitted, the girder stays may be welded to the firebox prior to stress relieving.

The girder stays shall be recessed for waterways as in sub-regulation (2) (Fig. 3/6 and Fig. 3/7). Such girders shall be securely welded by an approved method to the firebox crown. The method of welding and the proportions of attachment shall be to be satisfaction of the Inspecting Authority.

Fig. 3/6 Fig. 3/7 PART-VIII

112. Gusset stays.─(1) Gusset plates shall be flat and perpendicular to the end plates. The gusset angles shall be fitted to bed closely to the shell end plate and gusset plates. Cranking or setting of the plates is prohibited.

(2) Gusset stays, where fitted, shall comprise flat plates secured by double angles to the shell and end plate respectively, and shall be designed in accordance with the following.

113. Breathing space.─(1) Gusset stays shall be so arranged as to allow sufficient breathing space around furnace connections and the tube nests.

(2) For Lanchashire boilers, the proportions shown in the table below are recommended for portion of the end plates above the furnaces and flues.

Thickness of End Plate L (Fig.3/8) L (Fig. 3/9) Table 3/3 Sl.

No. mm mm mm Above 21 Fig. 3/8 Fig. 3/9

(3) Fig. 3/8 shows the breathing space recommended for the following conditions: Flat end, furnace or flues flanged for attachment to end plate, furnaces and flues formed with Adamson flanged seams.

(4) Fig. 3/9 shows similar conditions to Fig. 3/8 except that the end plate is flanged outwards for the furnace or flue ring connection, the breathing spaces allowed being 25 mm more than those for the same thickness of plate in Fig. 3/8

(5) Where a corrugated section not less than 1830 mm long is included in each internal flue or a Lancashire boiler, the dimensions given in the table above may be reduced by approximately 38 mm throughout.

(6) It is recommended that the breathing space below the furnaces and flues shall be approximately one half of the dimensions given in the table above. Whereas it is desired to use any other arrangement which gives a greater or less degree or flexibility, the amount of breathing space shall be specially considered and modified as the necessity of the case requires.

(7) For Gusset stays above tube nests, as fitted in waste heat boilers without internal furnaces, a breathing space of 203 mm from the center line of the top tubes of the center of the toe rivets of the gusset angles is recommended (see Fig. 3/9).

114. Gusset angles.─ The gusset angles connecting the stays to the shell and end plates shall be at least 7/8th of the thickness of the shell plate, but shall be not less than 13 mm thickness.

115. Load on gusset stay.─ Each gusset stay supporting the end plate of a boiler shall be designed to carry the whole load due to pressure on the area supported by it.

PART-IX

116.Boiler tubes subject to external pressure.─ Both plain and stay smoke tubes may be made either of wrought iron or steel and may be electric-resistance-welded or seamless.

117. Fitting of plain tubes.─(1) Plain tubes shall be expanded at both ends. The expanding shall be parallel throughout the full thickness of the tube plate. Plain tubes may also be bell-mouthed, beaded, or welded at the gas inlet end.

(2) Where stay tubes are not fitted, the ends of the tubes shall be beaded over at the fire box end and beaded or bell-mouthed at the other end. If bell-mouthed, the tubes shall protrude not less than 13 mm beyond the tube plate.

PART-X

118. Screw threads of stay tubes.─(1) Stay tubes shall be screwed at both ends with continuous threads, and the holes in the tube plates shall be tapped with continuous threads. The pitch of the threads shall not be finer than 11 threads per 25 mm. The stay tubes shall be expanded by roller expanders and not made right by caulking only.

(2) Stay tubes may be attached by welding in which case they shall not be adjacent within the same tube nest except in local areas, where not more than two adjacent stay tubes may be attached by welding. The form of attachment shall be such that there is a mechanical hold in addition to the welding, as shown in Fig.3/10. On completion of welding the stay tubes shall be lightly re-expanded.

Fig. 3/10: Typical Example of Welded Stay Tube

119. Stay tubes.─(1) Stay tubes are tubes having a weld depth equal to the nominal tube thickness plus 3 mm.

These stay tubes are not required within tube nests except when the tube nests comprise tubes which are expanded only.

(2) If tube nests comprise plain tubes that are expanded and beaded, expanded and belled or expanded and welded, welded stay tubes shall be used in boundary rows in sufficient numbers to carry the flat plate loadings outside the tube area.

(3) For plain tubes or stay tubes exposed to flame or gas temperatures exceeding 600°C, the ends of welded tubes shall be dressed flush with the welds. If not so exposed, the ends of welded tubes shall extend a maximum of 10 mm beyond the weld or, in the case of expanded tubes the tubes shall project beyond the tube plate up to a maximum of 15 mm.

(4) Each stay tube shall be designed to carry its due proportion of the load on the plates which it supports.

(5) The thickness of stay tubes welded into tube plates shall be such that the axial stress on the thinnest part of the tube does not exceed 7.14 kg/mm2.

(6) The thickness of stay tube at any part shall not be less than the values shown in the Table below. Minus tolerances shall be taken into account when ordering tubes.

TABLE 3/4 Serial Nominal outside diameter in mm Minimum Thickness in mm No.
