120. Thickened ends of stay tubes.─ If stay tubes are required to have their thickness increased at the screwed ends so that the thickness at the bottom of the threads is approximately the same as in the body of the tube the thickening shall be attained by upsetting and not by welding process, and the tubes shall be annealed after the upsetting.

121. Load on stay tubes and stay bars.─ Stay tubes shall be designed to carry the whole load due to pressure on the area to be supported. In each case the area to be calculated is as follows:

(i) For a stay tube within the tube nest the net area to be supported shall be product of the horizontal and vertical pitches in mm of the stay tube less the area of the tube holes embraced. Where the pitch of the stay tube is irregular the area shall be taken as the square of the mean pitch of the stay tubes (i.e. the square of ¼ of the sum four sides of any quadrilateral bounded by four adjacent stay tubes) less the area of the tube holes embraced.

(ii) For a stay in the boundary row the net area to be supported shall be the area enclosed by lines passing midway between it and the adjacent points of support less the area of any tubes embraced. The adjacent points of support may be other stay tubes or the commencement of curvature of flanging of center line of the circle or rivets securing the end plate to the shell angle.

(iii) In the case of bar stay where there are no stay tubes in the tube nest the area to be supported shall extend to the tangential boundary of the tube nest.

PART-XI

122.Boiler and super heater tubes subject to internal pressure steel tubes.─ (1) All tubes which are subject to internal pressure of water or steam shall be cold drawn or hot finished seamless or electric resistance welded in accordance with Chapter-2. Except as provided for in sub-regulation (2) below, they shall be without joint.

(2) Tubes having an external diameter not exceeding 127 mm may be jointed and such joints may be flash welded, machine forge welded arc or gas welded. Tubes above 127 mm diameter may also be welded provided they are located outside the furnace. Such welds must conform to the requirements of Chapter-7.

(3) Flash butt welding shall be undertaken as per the qualified procedure. The external fin caused by flash butt welding shall be removed subject to a maximum height of 5 percent of the tube outside diameter. The required flow area shall be ensured by conducting ball test using nearest ball diameter of 75% of the nominal Internal Diameter of the tube size.

(4) All fusion welded butt joints shall be post weld heat treated as per the requirements specified in regulation

123.

(5) When gas welding is implied the technique followed shall be approved by a Competent Person.

(6) For design metal temperatures and material requirements refer to Table 2/1 of regulation 30.

(7) The technique employed in all field welding of tubes shall be subject to the approval of Inspecting Authority or Chief Inspector as the case may be.

(8) All butt weld of tubes shall be tested as required hereunder:

(i) For tubes greater than 178 mm bore, all welds shall be non-destructively tested by radiographic or other approved methods.

(ii) For tubes greater than 102 mm bore and up to and including 178 mm bore, ten (10) per cent of the welds by each welder, selected at random with a minimum of 2 welds per welder, shall be subjected to non-destructive examination by radiographic or other approved method.

(iii) For tubes 102 mm bore and under, sample welds simulating the production welds shall be provided for mechanical testing either at a rate of 2 per cent or after each 150 welding hours with a minimum of one sample weld per welder.

(iv) Alternatively, 5 per cent of the welds made by each welder selected at random, with a minimum of one weld per welder, shall be subjected to non-destructive examination by radiographic or other approved method. This rate may be reduced to 2 per cent by arrangement with the Inspecting Authority after initial satisfactory results have been produced.

(v) In the event of any non-destructive test not meeting the requirements, defects in the welds represented by that test shall be rectified and the Inspecting Authority may call for an increase in the number of non-destructive tests until such time as a satisfactory standard is achieved.

Butt welded joints as indicated in sub-regulation (8) shall be subjected to non-destructive examination by radiographic, radioscopy or other approved methods such as ultrasonic testing including phased array ultrasonic testing, magnetic particle inspection or liquid dye penetrant inspection. When radioscopic examination is to be performed in lieu of radiography on welded components, the following requirements shall be met, namely:-

(a) A written procedure shall be submitted for approval to the Inspecting Authority which shall contain the following:

(i) material and the thickness range;

(ii) equipment qualifications;

(iii) test object scan plan;

(iv) radioscopic parameters;

(v) image processing parameters;

(vi) image display parameters;

(vii) image archiving requirements;

(viii) accept-reject criteria (Code reference);

(ix) performance evaluation;

(x) Operator identification.

(b) The system shall be aided with an image processor to enhance the quality of the radioscopic images and system performance quality shall exhibit-

(i) A thin section contrast sensitivity of 3 percent;

(ii) A thick section contrast sensitivity of 2 percent;

(iii) A spatial resolution of 3 line pairs per mm;

(iv) Image Quality Sensitivity (IQI) sensitivity - 2 percent of the joint thickness when wire IQI's are to be used, the wire diameter axis shall be oriented along the axis of the least sensitivity of the system.

(c) Radioscopes are to be properly marked to co-relate with particular part of joint represented.

(d) The radioscopic examination data shall be recorded and stored on a suitable storage media like videotape, magnetic disk or optical disk at the maker's plant for a sufficient period after the date of radioscopic examination as specified by the Inspecting Authority, efficient radioscopic examination record recall shall be made available at any time over the record retention period and shall be traceable to the test objects.

(e) When repair has been performed as a result of radioscopic examination, the repaired areas shall be re-examined using the same radioscopic technique to evaluate the effectiveness of the repair.

(f) To aid in proper interpretation of the radioscopic examination data, the details of the technique used shall accompany the data. The minimum information shall include the approved procedure requirements and system performance test data.

123. Requirements of Post Weld Heat Treatment(PWHT).─ The requirements of Post Weld Heat Treatment(PBWH) shall be as provided in Table 3/5 Table 3/5 Serial No.

Material group (as per Table 2/1) PWHT PWHT not required under the following conditions:

(i) For All Product Forms:

M1 Group 1, M1 Group 2 595°C min

(a) nominal weld thickness (t) ≤ 20 mm; and

(b) If preheated to 95°C min when nominal weld thickness (t) ≤ 20 mm and nominal base metal thickness ≤ 25 mm; and

(c) C ≤ 0.25%;

(ii) Only for tubes and pipes, if 0.25% < C ≤

0.3% and nominal thickness ≤ 9 mm; In case of welded stays, diameter of stay is not used to determine preheat requirements.

M3 Group 1 595°C min

(a) For all product forms nominal wall thickness (t) ≤ 16 mm except for tubes and pipes t ≤ 13 mm & OD ≤ 127 mm; and

(b) C ≤ 0.25% M4 Group 1 650°C min

(a) For all product forms nominal base metal thickness t ≤ 16 except for tubes and pipes t ≤ 13 mm & OD ≤ 127 mm; and

(b) C ≤ 0.15%; and

(c) Preheat 120°C min M5A Group 1 & M5B Group 1 675°C min

(a) For all product forms nominal base metal thickness t ≤ 16 except for tubes t ≤ 13 mm & OD ≤ 127 mm and for pipes t ≤ 13 mm & OD ≤ 127 mm; and

(b) Cr ≤ 3%; and

(c) C ≤ 0.15%; and

(d) Preheat 150°C min M15 E Group 1 730°C to 785°C NIL M8 PWHT : Nil NIL NIL MX20 PH: 250-300°C for GTAW and 400-450 for SMAW Post Heat:80-100°C for 90 minutes minimum PWHT: 740°C - 760°C NIL MX12 (t up to 52 mm) PH:220°C PWHT: 725°C +/15°C M43 950°C min

(a) For design temperature less than 540°C or

(b) for circumferential butt welds in pressure parts with a nominal base metal thickness of 13 mm or less at the weld Notes-1:

(a) All the heat treatment ranges shall be aligned with the Qualified Welding Procedure.

(b) When pressure parts of two different M‐Number groups are joined by welding, the post weld heat treatment shall be as specified in Table 3/5 for the material requiring the higher post weld heat treatment temperature, except M-No. 8 group.

(c) Fillet welds, partial penetration welds, and full penetration welds through the tube or pipe thickness, attaching M-No. 4 or M-No. 5A tubes and pipe to headers of lower M‐Number material, may be post weld heat treated at the temperature specified in Table 3/5 for the lower M‐Number material provided the tubes or pipe comply with all the following conditions:

(i) a maximum specified chromium content of 3 percent

(ii) a maximum size of NPS 4 (DN 100)

(iii) a maximum thickness of 13 mm.

(iv) a maximum specified carbon content of not more than 0.15 percent

(d) When non-pressure parts are welded to pressure parts, the post weld heat treatment temperature of the pressure parts shall control. Pressure part welds and attachment welds using ferritic filler metals that have a specified chromium content of more than 3% shall receive a post weld heat treatment. The post weld heat treatment time and temperature used shall be not less than that shown in Table 3/5 for a base metal of equivalent analysis.

(e) The nominal thickness to be considered for determining Post Weld Heat Treatment and soaking time shall be defined as:

(i) For full penetration butt welds, the nominal thickness is the thinner of the parts being joined.

(ii) For full penetration corner welds, the nominal thickness is the depth of the weld.

(iii) For partial penetration groove and material repair welds, the nominal thickness is the depth of the weld. The total depth of partial or full penetration groove welds made from both sides shall be taken as the sum of the depth of both sides at a given location.

(iv) For fillet welds, the nominal thickness is the weld throat. When a fillet weld is used in conjunction with a groove weld, the nominal thickness is either the depth of the groove weld or the fillet throat dimension, whichever is greater.

(v) Except as stipulated in clause (vi) below, the nominal thickness is the depth of the groove, if present, through the shell or nozzle or reinforcing pad, or the throat of any fillet weld, whichever is greater.

(vi) When multiple openings form a ligament where the spacing between centres is less than twice the average diameter, the nominal thickness of a combination groove and fillet weld shall be the sum of the groove depth and fillet weld throat.

(vii) The thickness of the pressure part at the point of attachment where a non-pressure part is welded to a pressure part, in case of butt welds;

(viii) The thickness of the fillet weld at the point of attachment where a non –pressure part is welded to a pressure part, in case of fillet welds.

(f) When it is impractical to carry out PWHT at the temperature specified in Table III/5 for M1 and M3 materials, it is permissible to carry out PWHT at lower temperatures for longer periods of time in accordance with table 3/6 given below.

Table 3/6: Alternate PWHT requirements for Carbon and Low alloy steels Serial Minimum holding time at decreased Notes No.

Decrease in temperature below minimum specified temperature, °C temperature, hour

[Note(1)] - - Notes:

(1) Minimum holding time per 25 mm for thickness up to and including 50 mm. Add 15 min per 25 mm of thickness for thickness greater than 50 mm.

(2) These lower Post Weld Heat Treatment temperatures permitted only for M-No 1, Group 1 and 2 materials.

(g) Soaking during heating / SR (or PWHT) shall be as per below guidelines: \Table 3/7: Soaking Serial No. HT Cycle Soaking Time Remarks 't' is the product thickness for Stress relieving.

Hot Working/ Normalizing For M5B, M-15E Gr1 (P91): 30 minutes per 25 mm of "t" or 30 minutes min whichever is greater & for all others 30 minutes per 25 mm of "t" or 15 minutes min whichever is greater.

SR/PWHT – Carbon Steels 1 hour per 25 mm of "t" for "t" up to 50 mm with an additional 15 minutes for every 25 mm above 50 mm (15 minutes minimum).

SR/PWHT – Alloy Steels 1 hour per 25 mm of "t" with below mentioned minimum soaking times:

(M3 & M4) – 30 minutes minimum.

(M5A) – 60 minutes minimum except for M5A pipes of OD ≤ 127mm and t ≤ 13mm, which require only 30 minutes) 't' is nominal thickness as per Note (b) of Table 3/5 for PWHT.

(M5B & M-15 E Gr 1) – 60 minutes minimum MX20 2 hrs minimum for thickness below 60 mm and 3 hrs minimum for thickness above 60 mm MX12 (t up to 52 mm) 1 hour per 25 mm of 't' - 30 minutes minimum.

SR/PWHT – Nickel Alloys 1 hour per 25 mm of 't' - 60 minutes minimum.

(h) The temperature of the furnace at the time of placing the component for SR/PWHT shall not be higher than 425°C. The component shall be kept at SR/PWHT temperature to the minimum soaking time.

After SR/PWHT the component shall be left to cool to in the furnace to a temperature not exceeding 425°C. After withdrawal from the furnace the part shall be allowed to cool in a still atmosphere.

(i) The rate of heating above 425°C and rate of cooling up to 425°C shall be as below. The rate of heating and rate of cooling should not exceed 335°C per hour divided by the thickness in inches, but in no case should it be more than 335°C per hour.

Notes 2:

(i) In case socket welded joints or other type of joints for carbon steel pipes, valves and fittings, PWHT is not required if the throat thickness of fillet weld does not exceed 20 mm or carbon content does not exceed 0.25%.

(ii) For carbon steel Headers and Mud boxes of water tube boilers, material having a maximum Carbon content of 0.25%, the Post Weld Heat Treatment requirement for tubes welded to Header is not mandatory when all the following conditions are fulfilled, namely:-

(a) The outer diameter (OD) of tubes is not more than 51 MM;

(b) The outer diameter of the Header is not more than 219.1 MM;

(c) The Header thickness is not more than 13 MM;

(d) A minimum pre-heat of 100°C is applied.

(iii) For Carbon steel, if the thickness of the small component of boiler exceeds 20 mm and the weld recess also exceeds 20 mm local stress relieving to the satisfaction of the Inspecting Authority shall be permitted.

(iv) Post-weld heat treatment in weld joints of carbon steel and low alloy steel may be exempted when heat treatment is not practical where inside is lined with special Stainless Steel which cannot be heated beyond 400°C (due to the associated problem of reduction of corrosion properties of such lining during heating to the temperature), when all the following conditions are satisfied –

(a) Built-up overlay welding (buttering is performed on both the welding edge bevels of the parts of the closing joint to be welded together. The buttering can be of:

(1) With Carbon Steel Electrode with low carbon (C) = 0.1 Percent max. (Ex: E7018-1) in case of Carbon Steel parts.

(2) With Stainless Steel Electrode E-309 or with Inconel electrode in case of low alloy steel parts.

(3) With low alloy steel electrodes, having carbon (C) = 0.12 percent maximum or with Inconel electrode in case of high strength low alloy steel parts having UTS greater than 56 kg/mm2 such as WB36, SA 533 Type B Cl 2;

(b) Buttering thickness shall be 15 mm minimum on the side where chip back is required to be done before re-welding and 8 mm minimum on the other side. Refer Figure given below:

Fig. 3/11

(c) Suitable pre-heat and other pass heating is applied during buttering:

(d) Individual parts are post-weld heat treated after buttering prior to welding the special stainless steel corrosion resistant liner plate or prior to applying final weld overlay of special corrosion resistant stainless steel lining;

(e) The final layer on the internal line side shall be compatible with liner material and final weld (carbon steel welding in carbon steel base metal, low alloy steel Inconel welding in case of high strength low alloy steel base metal and E309 Inconel welding in case of other low alloy steel base metals) on the base metal side shell be compatible with base metal in respect of mechanical properties. The welding procedure shall be established taking care of adequate toughness and ductility and the procedure approved by Inspecting Authority or Chief Inspector, as the case may be;

(f) Charpy – V Notch impact test is carried out at 20°C for:

(i) Base metal and heat affected zone,

(ii) Weld metal in case of Carbon Steel welding;

(g) Non-destructive testing as below (recommended):

(i) Magnetic particle test of weld bevels prior to buttering.

(ii) Dye-Penetrant test of buttering layer.

(iii) Dye-Penetrant test of rest of weld.

(iv) Dye-Penetrant test of final weld on internal liner side.

(v) Ultrasonic test of final weld after hydro test of the equipment with the closing seam.

124. Attachment of Steel Tubes.─ (1) Steel tubes shall be connected to the tube plates by one of the following methods:-

(i) Expanding.

(ii) Strength welding.

(iii) Mechanical bolted ball joint.

(2) Drift or roller expanded tubes shall project through the neck or bearing part in the holes by at least a quarter of 25 mm and shall be secured from drawing out by being bell mouthed to the extent of 0.8 mm for each 25 mm in diameter plus 1.6 mm.

(3) Tubes may be seal welded into fittings or headers for both boilers and super heaters after they have been expanded and flared provided the material in the fittings or headers does not contain carbon in excess of

0.35 per cent.

(4) In the case of drifted or roller expanded tubes, the tube holes in the tube plates of drums, pockets, or headers shall be formed in such a way that the tubes can be effectively tightened in them. Where the tube ends are not normal to the tube plate, there shall be a neck or belt of parallel seating of at least 13 mm depth measured in a plane normal to the axis of the tube at the holes.

(5) Where tubes are strength welded direct to the tube plates, the technique followed shall be approved by the Inspecting Authority and all welds shall be suitably heat treated. In the case of plates below 22 mm thickness, the requirement of stress relieving by heat treatment after welding of the tubes may be waived provided the weld satisfies the requirements of the maximum hardness and also the requirements concerning the impact values of the weld metal. The welding procedure adopted and the weld sequence selected shall also be subject to the approval of the Inspecting Authority. This provision in applicable to only shop welding of tubes at the Manufacturers Works.

(6) Tubes spacers, supporting clips and lugs may be welded to the tubes. Flash welding of studs for supporting refractories, etc, is also permissible.

(7) The tubes shall be so arranged that they are accessible for cleaning internally and externally.

PART-XII

125. Headers, mud boxes, etc. of water tube boilers.─(1) (a) Headers and mud boxes, etc. of water tube Boiler may be of seamless or welded steel or of cast steel complying with the requirements of the provisions contained in regulations 49 to 57. Where welded, the welding shall be stress relieved, radio graphed or ultrasonically examined and in all respects shall be to the satisfaction of the Inspecting Authority as per Table 3/5 of regulation

123.

(b) Open ends of seamless steel tube headers may be closed by forging or the ends may be secured by bolting, screwing or welding in an approved manner. Bolting shall not be used where the bolts are exposed to or are swept by gases of combustion.

(c) The method of attachment of the ends shall be subject to the ends shall be subject to the approval of the Inspecting Authority.

Each piece prior to being fitted in place shall be subjected to hydraulic tests to one-and-a-half times the design pressure of the boiler into which it is to be fitted.

(a) The sighting hold doors and header caps shall be substantial and be capable of being removed or replaced from time to time without loss of efficiency of safety. They may be held in position either by means of bolts or by seal welding.

(b) The bolts by which they are held in place shall be fitted to them in such a way as to satisfy the above conditions.

(c) The doors shall be so designed that they will not blow out in the event of breakage of the bolt.

(d) Circular holes also may be provided in addition to elliptical holes, if provision is made to prevent the door bolts from turning, while securing the joint.

All flanges shall have a fillet with a radius of curvature at least equal to the thickness of the necks to which they are attached.

PART-XIII

126. Stand pipes and pads etc..─Stand pipes and seating for carrying mountings shall be made of wrought, cast of fabricated steel. These shall take the form of short stand pipes, pressed steel plate saddles, forged pads or pads cut from round rolled bar as may be most convenient and secured to the boiler by welding. Where short stand pipes are used they shall be of solid forged, fabricated or cast steel. They shall be carefully bedded to the shell before attachment. The jointing faces to which mountings are to be bolted shall be machined. Pads shall have sufficient thickness to allow the drilling of stud holes for mountings without the inner surface being pierced and the length of the screwed portion of the stud in the pad shall be not less than the diameter of the stud.

127. Design of stand pipes.─ The design of the branches and stand-pipe shall be regulated in the following manner:

(i) in order to withstand the maximum allowable working pressure, the minimum thickness of branch and stand-pipe shall be determined in accordance with the provisions specified in sub-regulation (1) of regulation 244;

(ii) Compensation requirements for openings in the main pressure parts shall be determined in accordance with the provisions of regulation 179;

(iii) The additional thickness shall be considered to withstand superimposed loading due to connecting pipework or fittings.

(iv) The minimum thickness of the branch or standpipe shall be the greatest of the values required above and shall not be less than 0.015 do plus 3.2 mm where, do is the outside diameter of the branch (in mm);

Provided that the provisions of the regulations shall not apply to such tube stub which have been designated as tubes.

128. Pressed plate saddles.─ Where pressed plate saddles are employed they shall be formed to bed closely to the boiler and machined on the face jointing the mounting and on the edges. The studs for attachment of mountings, if screwed through the saddle, shall each be fitted with a nut on the inside having a thickness equal to the diameter of the stud. Where the stud holes do not penetrate through the saddle, the length of the screwed portion of the stud in the plate shall not be less than the diameter of the stud.

129. Seatings for mountings.─ (1) For pressures not exceeding 8.7 kg/cm2(g), mountings with the screwed ends not exceeding 1 inch S.P.T.(Straight Pipe Threads) may be used; the screwed portion of any such mounting being not less than 5 mm.

The mountings may be screwed: a. Directly into the boiler shell plate, nuts being fitted on the water side, or b. Into steel distance pieces the length of thread engaged being in no case less than the bore of the mounting plus 6 mm.

The distance pieces shall be made from solid mild steel. They shall be screwed into the plate and fitted with nuts on the water side. The walls of the distance pieces shall be not less than 6 mm thick at the bottom of the thread.

130. Attachment of mountings.─ (1) Mountings may be attached directly to any shell or end plate where the plate is of sufficient thickness to allow a suitable surface to be obtained for the attachment of the boiler mountings.

(2) The minimum thickness at the hole in the shell or end plate shall be not less than the thickness required for the maximum allowable working pressure considering the plate as being unpierced.

(3) Where the boiler mountings are secured by studs, the studs shall have a full thread holding in the plate for at least one diameter. If the stud holes penetrate the whole thickness of the plate. The stud shall be screwed right through the plate and be fitted with a nut inside having a thickness equal to the diameter of the stud. Where bolts are used for securing mountings they shall be screwed right through the plate with their heads inside the shell or end plate.

131. Insufficient thickness of end plates.─ (1) In cases where the thickness of the end plate is insufficient for this purpose the mountings shall be joined to suitable steel seatings as provided in regulation 126.

(2) The following constructions as to stand pipes attached to shells and end plates shall be permissible:

(i) Where the internal diameter of the stand pipe does not exceed 25 mm, the standpipe may be screwed into the plate and fitted with a nut on the waterside.

(ii) Where the internal diameter of the standpipe exceeds 25 mm but does not exceed 51 mm, it may be screwed in and seal-welded.

(iii) Where the diameter of the standpipe exceeds 51 mm, it may be welded to the end plate.

The foregoing provisions as to the stand pipe shall be regulated by the following conditions:

(i) When stand pipes are screwed, the screwing shall be of Standard Pipe Thread,

(ii) Where stand pipes, saddles or other forms of seatings are fabricated by fusion welding they shall be stress relieved by heat treatment before attachment to the boiler,

(iii) Where the bore of the stand pipe or seating and the hole in the plate do not exceed 127 mm plus twice the thickness of the plate, the seating may be welded to the plate without subsequent heat treatment of the weld so made. Where the hole in the plate exceeds 127 mm plus twice the thickness of the plate, the plate to which seating is attached shall be stress relieved by heat treatment.

132. Attachment of water and pressure gauges.─ Water gauge and pressure gauge siphons may be attached direct to the front end plates without the intervention of a pad or stand pipe, provided they are flanged and secured by studs. If the studs are screwed through the plate, nuts shall be fitted on the inside of the plate.

133. Mountings on flat places.─ Where the flanged mountings are attached direct to a flat plate, such mountings shall be provided with a substantial spigot, the full thickness of the boiler plate.

134. Bolts and nuts.─ Bolts and nuts shall be machined where they come in contact with the flanges, and all holes in saddles and pads shall be drilled.

Note: In all cases where the boiler is lagged the joint of the mountings shall be clear of the lagging surface or the lagging kept clear of flange so that the joint can be inspected and, when necessary, remade.

PART-XIV

135. Manholes, mudholes etc.─ (1) Sufficient sight and cleaning holes shall be provided to permit efficient inspection and cleaning. Such holes shall normally be elliptical in form and not less than 89 mm x 63 mm.

At least one manhole or sight hole shall be provided in the upper part of the cylindrical shell or in the shell crown, or end plate and this shall not be less than as given in the TABLE 3/8:

TABLE 3/8 Serial Size in mm No.

Boilers not exceeding 760 mm 230 x 180 Boilers over 760 mm diameter and not exceeding 915 mm 305 x 230 Boilers over 915 mm diameter and not exceeding 1060 mm 355 x 255 Boilers over 1060 mm diameter and not exceeding 1220 mm 380 x 280 Boilers over 1220 mm 410 x 305

(3) Where the size of construction of the boiler does not permit entry for cleaning and inspection, sufficient cleaning holes shall be provided in the shell for these purposes.

(4) Where cross tubes are fitted in Vertical boilers one cleaning hole shall be provided opposite at least one end of each tube. At the bottom of the narrow water spaces at least three cleaning holes shall be provided.

The cleaning holes shall be so arranged that the circumferential distance between them does not exceed 91 cm unless the foundation ring is sufficiently accessible to permit cleaning and inspection from the inside of the boiler.

Note: Wherever practicable the frames should be secured to the inside of the shell so that the shorter axis is parallel to the longitudinal centre line of the boiler.

(5) Cleaning holes or mud holes in Loco Tope boilers shall be provided at each bottom corner of the outer firebox casing, above the firehole ring and on each side of saddle plate in line with bottom of barrel.

(6) In the case of small boilers or where the foregoing provisions are impracticable the cleaning holes or mud holes shall be as near as possible in the corner of the outer firebox casing and the holes as large as circumstances permit.

136. Compensating rings and frames for openings in shells.─ (1) Compensating rings and frames for openings in shells shall be of same material as that of shell. The compensation provided shall preferably be flanged frames, which shall be fitted in all cases where the shell plate exceeds 14 mm thick. Flanged frames shall be weld-less and shall be flanged or pressed to provide a flat jointing surface for the cover.

(2) All compensating rings and frames shall be formed to bed closely to the surfaces to be connected. Any weld therein shall be on the transverse centre line.

(3) The flat jointing surfaces of all flanged or pressed frames and all doors or covers shall be machined.

137. Internal doors.─ Internal doors shall be of wrought steel in accordance with Chapter-2 and constructed in accordance with the following:-

(1) Doors shall be formed to fit closely to the internal joint surface and should be fitted with studs, nuts and crossbars;

(2) Doors for circular opening larger than 250 mm or elliptical/rectangular openings larger than 250 mm x 175 mm shall have two studs but for openings of 250 mm x 170 mm or less only one stud may be fitted.

Doors for openings not larger than 123 mm x 90 mm may have the stud forged integrally with door;

(3) Doors studs shall be of welding quality steel having a minimum specified tensile strength of not less than

36.7 kg/mm2 and those for manholes shall be not less than 30 mm and they shall be fixed in any of the following way:-

(a) screwed through the plate and fillet welded on inside;

(b) fillet welded each side of the plate with a leg length of not less than 10 mm;

(c) attached to the door by an intermediate plate or lugs so that the strength of the attachment is not less than the strength of the studs and the studs are prevented from turning; or

(d) provided with an integrated collar and be riveted or screwed onto the door plate and be prevented from turning in which case the strength of the attachment shall not be less than the strength of the studs;

Door spigot when the door is in the central position shall have a clearance of approximately 105 mm all around and at no point shall the clearance exceed 3 mm. The spigot depth shall be sufficient to trap the gasket;

The nuts shall be of appropriate material compatible with that of the bolts and be placed on the seating surface;

The cross-bars shall be of substantial proportions and of mild or wrought steel.

Note: Eye bolts of suitable legs on the door plate or headed bolts engaging with slotted sections on the door plate may be used instead of studs.

The minimum calculated thickness of the door of the flat plate construction (i.e. unstiffened and made from one plate shall be not less than that determined by the following formula: -

0.35 Pxd² + W t = —————————— for a circular door f

0.35 P (2-a/b) x a² + W t = ——————————————for an elliptical door f Where, t = is the minimum calculated thickness of the flat door (in mm) P = is the design pressure of boiler in N/mm² d = is the diameter of the opening to which the door is fitted, if round (in mm) a = is the minor axis of the opening to which the door is fitted if elliptical (in mm) b = is the major axis of the opening to which the door is fitted, if elliptical (in mm) W = is the full load capacity of one stud (effective stud area X design stress value at design temperature) in N f = is the maximum permissible stress values as available in the governing codes of the country of the material to which it belongs in N/mm2 Note:

A design stress value of 50 N/mm2 may be used for carbon steel bolts for design temperature not exceeding 300°C.

138. Raised manhole frames, cover plates and joint bolts.─ (1) (a) Raised circular manhole frames not exceeding 406 mm in diameter shall be at least 19 mm thick in all parts. The circular cover plates and joint flanges for such frames shall be not less than: 25 mm thick for pressures not exceeding 8.4 kg/cm2 (g)

(b) For pressures 14 kg/cm2 (g) and over, raised circular manhole frames shall not be fitted.

The cover plates shall be secured by at least sixteen steel bolts not less than 25 mm diameter, such that the stress in the bolts due to steam pressure shall not exceed:350 kg/cm2for 25 mm bolts. 421 kg/cm2for 28 mm bolts. 456 kg/cm2for 32 mm bolts

(c) For the purpose of calculation, the pressure shall be assumed to act on the whole area within the pitch circle of the bolts and the bolt area at the bottom of the screw thread shall be taken.

(2) External raised circular mouthpieces shall be:

(a) formed in one piece without welds,

(b) formed from a suitable rolled section and forge welded, or

(c) Fabricated by fusion welding provided they are stress relieved by heat treatment after welding and before attaching to the boiler.

(d) Welds shall be positioned so that they are located on the transverse center line of the boiler. The jointing flanges of mouthpieces and covers shall be machined on the face and edge and on the bearing surface for the bolts.

(e) Bolts and nuts shall be machined where in contact with the flanges and the joints should be formed inside and outside the bolts to preclude the possibility of flange distortion. Cover plates shall be dished outwards to a depth of approximately one-eighth of the internal diameter of the frames.

(f) All frames and mouthpieces shall be close to the surfaces to which they are to be connected. All caulking edges shall be machined or machine gas cut.

139. Positions of manhole in shell.─ Manholes and other openings in cylindrical shells shall be placed as far as possible from the seams, preferably towards the back end to the boiler top. Wherever practical, oval openings shall be arranged with the minor axis parallel to the longitudinal centre line of the boiler. In no case shall the circumferential length of any opening exceed twice the longitudinal width of the opening.

140. Compensation rings to manholes.─ Compensation shall be provided in every case for the part cut of a cylindrical shell for a manhole. The strength of the net section of compensating ring and also welds securing them to the shell, compared with that part of the shell cut out, shall not be less than the efficiency of the longitudinal seam.

141. Compensation for cutting large holes in shell type boilers.─ Where holes are cut in the cylindrical shell for the purpose of attaching a seating of frame or door, compensation shall be provided such that the added sectional area, including parts of the frame within 101.6 mm of the shell shall be not less than the sectional area of the plate removed which shall be the product of the diameter of the opening and the calculated thickness of the plate as found by Equation(4/2). Where holes are cut in the cylindrical shell for the purpose of fixing seating for mountings and the diameter of the holes is greater than 2¼ times thickness of the shell plate plus 70 mm, compensation shall be provided. Where a large opening is cut in a cylindrical shell to receive another part of the structure, the sides where cut away shall be efficiently cross-stayed or strengthened in some other effective manner.

CHAPTER -4 FUSION WELDED DRUMS FOR WATER TUBE BOILERS AND SUPERHEATERS

142. General requirements.─ Where applicable the general terms of Chapter-1 relating to Certificates from Makers, Inspecting Authorities, etc., and of Chapter-3 concerning construction shall be followed.

PART-I

143. Materials of construction for fusion welded Boiler Drums.─(1) For plates to be used in the construction of fusion welded boilers drums, requirements of Chapter-2 shall apply.

(2) Plates having thickness of more than 50 mm, before being fabricated, shall be uniformly heat treated to produce grain refinement and heat treatment involving quenching in a liquid medium is not permitted.

PART-II

144. Fusion Welded Drums.─ The term 'Fusion Weld', for the purpose of this Chapter, is applicable to all welded joints made by the metal arc process with covered electrodes or other electric arc process, in which the arc stream and the deposited weld metal are shielded from atmospheric contamination.

145. This Chapter shall apply to the single run or heavy run welding process and that welded boiler drums manufactured by that process shall conform with all regulations of this Chapter, except those in which divergence is necessary solely because of special requirements essential for the most efficient utilisation of that process and all regulations governing quality of material, construction workmanship and testing (both non-destructive and otherwise) are applicable.

146. Equipment of workshop.─ (1) The welding plant and equipment shall be of good quality and maintained in an efficient working condition.

(2) The welding apparatus shall be installed under cover and shall be arranged so that the welding work may be carried out in positions free from draughts and adverse weather conditions.

(3) The procedure shall be such that there is regular and systematic supervision of the welding works, and the welding operators are subjected to periodic tests for quality of workmanship by the works officials.

(4) Records of these periodic tests shall be kept and made available by works office to the Competent Person for scrutiny.

147.Testing Laboratory.─

(1) The works shall be equipped with an efficient testing laboratory which shall include apparatus suitable for carrying out tensile, bend and impact tests, micro-examination of specimens and x-ray examination of the actual joints in pressure vessels.

(2) The works shall also be equipped with a suitable heat treating furnace having satisfactory means for temperature control, but as an alternative arrangement may be made whereby the drums can be heat-treated elsewhere.

148. Constructional details and preparation for welding.─ (1) Before starting the construction work, the manufacturer shall furnish to Inspecting Authority, a fully dimensioned sectional drawing reflecting therein complete details of construction of the drum for approval.

(2) Fully dimensioned drawings of the proposed welds preparations of the main seams drawn to a scale, clearly reflecting therein all the relevant details shall be furnished.

(3) Sketches showing details of the welds preparations for the attachment of the stand pipes, branch pipes and seating and their locations relative to the longitudinal and circumferential seams and to other openings shall be furnished.

149. Preparation of plates.─ For the purpose of preparation of plates, requirements contained under regulation 77 shall apply.

150. Cylindrical Shell of Drums.─

(1) Each drum shell plate shall be of cylindrical form to the extreme edges of the plate.

(2) The bending shall be done entirely by machine and local heating or hammering is prohibited.

(3) Where the plates are bent to an internal diameter less than 20 times of the plate thickness, they shall be efficiently heat-treated after bending or combined with final post weld heat treatment.

151. Method of making welded joints.─(1) For the purpose of requisite quality of welding, the seams may be welded from both sides of the plate or from one side of the plate with or without a backing strip and If a backing strip is used, it shall be removed and the surface so exposed shall be dressed.

(2) Additional runs of metal shall be deposited at the surface of the welded seams so that the welded metal at the level of the surfaces of the plate is refined as far as possible.

(3) Thickness of such additional welding shall not exceed values given in Table 4/1 below.

Table 4/1 Maximum reinforcement in mm Serial Number Nominal thickness Circumferential joint in Pipe in mm and tubing Other Welds

(i) Upto 3

2.5

2.5

(ii) Over 3 to 5 incl.

3.0

2.5

(iii) Over 5 to 13 incl

4.0

2.5

(iv) Over 13 to 25 incl

5.0

2.5

(v) Over 25 to 50 incl

6.0

3.0

(vi) Over 50 to 75 incl Note

4.0

(vii) Over 75 to 100 incl Note

5.5

(viii) Over 100 to 125 incl Note

6.0

(ix) Over 125 Note

8.0 Note – The greater of 6 mm or 1/8 times width of weld in mm

(4) The surfaces of the welds which are to be subsequently radiographed shall be sufficiently free from coarse ripples, overlap, grooves, abrupt ridges and valleys, so as to avoid stress raisers and to permit proper interpretation of radiographs and other Non-Destructive Testing(NDT) methods. If there is a question regarding the surface condition of the weld when interpreting a radiographic film, the film shall be compared to the actual weld surface or provide smooth contours and to be flush with the respective surfaces of the plate before radiography examination is done.

(5) Undercut shall not exceed 0.8mm or 10% of wall thickness, whichever is less and undercut shall not encroach on design wall thickness.

(6) When affixing standpipes, branch pipes, seating, compensating plates, doubling plates or manhole frames, not less than two runs of metal shall be deposited at each weld.

(7) Each run of weld shall be thoroughly cleaned and free from slag that may adversely affect the quality of the succeeding runs.

(8) Welds shall be marked permanently to facilitate their location.

152. Type of welded joints.─ The longitudinal and circumferential seams shall be made with butt joints of the single or double U or Vee type.

153. Number of joints.─ Where, having regard to the approved design, the dimensions are such that the shell cannot be made from a single plate, it shall be made with the minimum number of joints and the longitudinal seams in successive rings shall not fall in line, except where the rings of the drum are in two halves of unequal thickness.

154. Position of the tube holes.─(1) Tube holes in welded seams shall be avoided as far as possible. Where the holes are unavoidable, they may be machined through welded seams after these seams are radiographed.

(2) In the case of vanadium steels, in addition to radiography, the welded seams shall be stress relieved.

(3) The efficiency of the ligament in the direction of weld shall be multiplied by a weld factor not exceeding 0.95 except where the distance from the edge of the tube hole to the edge of the weld is greater than 13 mm.

(4) The edge of the weld shall be considered to be the edge of the weld groove as machined in the plate prior to welding.

Notwithstanding anything in this regulation, efficiency of the ligament in the direction of weld shall not be multiplied by a weld factor 0.95 where 100 percent radiography of weld joint is done.

155. Circularity of drums.─

(1) (a)The difference between the internal diameter of the drum measured at any cross-section and the nominal internal diameter of the drum shall not exceed 1 per cent of the latter.

(b) Any departure of profile measured on the outside of the drum with a gauge of the designed form of the exterior of the drum and of length equal to a quarter of the internal diameter, shall not exceed the percentage given in Table 4/2 below:

Table 4/2 Serial Number Nominal internal diameter of drum Percentage of nominal internal diameter

(i) Up to and including 915 mm

0.375

(ii) Over 915 mm up to and including 1150 mm

0.35

(iii) Over 1150 mm

0.3 Flats at the welded seams shall not be permitted and any local departure from circularity shall be gradual.

(2) (a)The deviation from circularity in any section shall, in the case of normalised drums, not exceed 2% and in the case of stress-relieved drum 1% and the deviation from a straight line shall not exceed 0.3% of the cylindrical length.

(b) (i) In order to determine the deviation from circularity of the drums the maximum and minimum internal diameter at one cross section shall be measured.

(ii) From these measured values the percentage deviation from circularity shall be calculated according to the following formula, ─ 2(dmax – dmin) C = —————————- x 100 (dmax + dmin) Where, C = percentage deviation from circularity of the drums. d = internal diameter at one cross-section.

(c)In order to determine the deviation from a straight line, a stretched line should be placed longitudinally against the sides and its largest distance from the shell line concerned shall be measured. From this distance, and from the cylindrical shell, the deviation from the straight line shall be calculated.

156. Mechanical test and test plates of fusion welded seams.─(1) Fusion welded joints shall comply with the mechanical tests herein specified. Not less than one set of test plates shall be provided to represent the welding of each longitudinal seam and the test plates may by attached at each end of the longitudinal seam or the set may be located at one end only.

(2) Where the drum shell is formed in two or more courses, the staggered longitudinal seam shall be regarded as a continuous longitudinal seam provided the welding be effected in one reasonably continuous operation and by the same operator or operators.

(3) Where there are circumferential seams only or where the method of welding the circumferential seam differs from that employed for the longitudinal seams, the method of providing the test plates shall be decided by the Inspecting authority.

(4) (a) The test plates shall be of a size sufficient for the preparation of the test pieces specified in sub-regulation

(1) of regulation 157 and for any repeat test pieces that may be required.

(b) The material for each set of test plates shall be cut from the respective plate or plates forming the appropriate seam, and before being cut shall be stamped by the Competent Person.

(5) When the analysis of the plate is approved by the Inspecting authority and is considered sufficiently similar, the test plates may be cut from one drum shell plate only.

(6) In the case of insufficient material being available on the shell plate to permit the cutting of test pieces, these shall be acceptable if they are made from another plate provided it is made from the same cast.

(7) (a) The weld groove in the test plates shall be similar to that adopted for the corresponding edges of the longitudinal seam, and the respective fusion faces shall be in continuous alignment.

(b) The test plates shall be reinforced or supported during welding in order to prevent undue warping.

(c) The weld metal in the plates and the seam shall be of the same grade of electrode and shall be deposited continuously at the same operation.

(8) The weld in any test plate shall not be repaired unless agreed by the Inspecting Authority and the repairs shall be done in such a manner as will ensure that the repaired seam in the test plate is still representative of the material in the main seam.

(9) Where it is desired to straighten test plates which have warped during welding they may be straightened at a temperature below the temperature of heat treatment of the drum to which they belong. Straightening shall take place before final heat treatment.

(10) Heat treatment requirements shall comply with regulation 123.

(11) If any defects in the weld metal of a test plate are revealed by radiographical examination, the position of these shall be clearly marked on the plate and test pieces shall be selected from such parts of the test plates as may be decided by the Inspecting Authority.

157. Selection of test pieces*.─(1) From the test plates or plates on each longitudinal seam, test pieces shall be selected for the following tests, the specimens being cut out as showing Figs. 4/1 and 4/2 and stamped by a Competent Person for identification.

(a) One tensile test specimen for the welded joint.

(b) One all-weld metal tensile test specimen.

(c) Two bend test specimens.

(d) Three notched-bar impact test specimens.

(e) One specimen for micro and macro examination.

All dimensions are in mm.

1. Tensile test for joint

2. Tensile test for all-weld metal

3. Bend test, outer and inner surfaces of plate (at weld)

4. Notched bar impact test specimen.

5. Micro and macro specimen.

Fig. 4/1: Details of Test Plates All dimensions are in mm.

1. Tensile test for joint.

4. (a) Charpy impact test, outer surface of plate.

(b) Charpy impact test, inner surface of plate.

5. Micro and macro specimen.

2. Tensile test for all-weld metal

3. Bend test, outer and inner surfaces of plate (at weld) Fig. 4/2: Details of Test Plates *Examples showing approximate disposition of the test pieces in the test plates are shown in Figs. 4/1 and 4/2.

The remainder of each set of test plates shall be retained for any retests required. Any specimen for retest shall be cut off from the same set of test plate, as the original specimen.

(2) Surfaces of tensile, bend and impact test specimens corresponding with the outside of the drums shall be only lightly dressed so that the rolled surface of the parent metal is not wholly removed, except that where the rolled surfaces of the abutting plates are not level with one another, one plate may be machined at each face of the weld provided the depth of metal removed does not exceed 0.8 mm.

158. Tensile test pieces.─(1) (a)The dimension of the reduced tensile section shall be as shown in Fig.4/3 and the width of the reduced section shall be at least 25 mm.

(b) If the thickness of the plate does not exceed 30 mm, the thickness of the specimen shall be equal to the plate thickness and the plate surface of the specimen which shall be machined to take away the surface irregularities of the plate and the weld.

All dimensions are in mm.

Fig. 4/3: Reduced Section Tensile Specimen

(c)If the plate thickness exceeds 30 mm, the tensile test shall be carried out on several reduced section specimens, each having a thickness of at least 30 mm and a width at the effective cross-section of at least 25 mm. These Specimens shall be taken out of the test piece in such a way that the tensile test covers the whole thickness of the weld joints as shown in Fig. 4/4. In this case the result of the test of every test specimen concerned shall meet the requirements specified in sub-regulation (1) of regulation 159.

All dimensions are in mm.

Fig. 4/4: Reduced Section Tensile Specimen in Thick Plate All dimensions are in mm.

Fig. 4/5: All-Weld Metal Test Specimen

(2) The dimensions of the all weld metal tensile test specimens shall be those given in Fig. 4/5. The diameter shall be the maximum possible, consistent with the cross-section of the weld but in no case more than 20 mm. The gauge length shall be equal to five times the diameter.

159. Tensile tests.─(1)The tensile strength of different grades of material shall be within the limits specified in regulation 37.

(2) The upper yield point at room temperature shall be not less than 50% of the specified minimum tensile strength at room temperature.

(3) The breaking elongation in percentage shall be not less than (N-Rm)/C Where Rm = Tensile strength at room temperature in kgf/mm2 N = a quality index of 100 for plate thickness upto 50 mm or 95 for plate thickness over 50mm.

C = 2.2 for only gauge lengths of L= 5 do or L=5.65/  A Where L = gauge length A = original cross section of the rectangular test piece. do = original diameter of the round test piece.

NOTE: C= 1.9 for gauge lengths of 4  Afor test piece of Appendix-D.

(4) The minimum values of the stress at proof limit 0.2% at elevated temperature (Et) may be calculated by multiplying the minimum specified tensile strength at room temperature (R20) by the value of the ratio (Et/R20) given in the Table 2/5 in regulation 37.

160. Bend test pieces.─(1)One for direct and one for reverse bending shall be taken transversely to the weld and where the thickness of the plate permits, one should be above the other. The specimens shall be rectangular in section so as to have width equal to one and half times the thickness of the specimen but not less than 30 mm. The surface of the specimens shall be machined just to remove the surface irregularities of the plate and the weld. The corners of the specimens shall be rounded to a radius not exceeding 10% of the thickness of the specimen.

(2) Where plate thicknesses do not exceed 38 mm, the thickness of the specimen shall be equal to the full thickness of the test plate. Where the plate thickness exceeds 38 mm, the specimen shall in all cases have a thickness of at least 38 mm. The specimen to be tested with the outer surface of the weld in tension shall be prepared by cutting to waste the metal local to the inner surface of the weld so that the desired specimen thickness is obtained (see Fig.4/6, Specimen A). The specimen to be tested with the inner surface in tension shall be prepared by cutting to waste the metal local to the outer surface of the weld so that the desired specimen thickness is obtained (see Fig. 4/6, Specimen B). Where the thickness of the plate permits, both specimens may be cut from the same piece of plate, the specimen being located in the plate one above the other (see Fig. 4/6, Specimen C).

All dimensions are in mm.

Fig. 4/6: Specimen Three Bend Test

161. Bend tests.─ (1)The specimen shall be mounted in such a way that the axis of the former is in the middle of the weld, and on roller supports the faces of which are separated by a distance determined by the thickness of the specimen.

(2) The best specimen shall be pushed through the supports by a former having a diameter also determined by the thickness of the specimen. The requirements of table 4/3 given below, relating to the thickness of test specimen, shall apply.

(3) On completion of the test, no crack or defect on the outer surface of the specimen shall be greater than 1.5 mm measured across the specimen, or 3 mm measured along the specimen. Premature failure at the comers of the specimen shall not be considered as a cause for rejection.

Table 4/3: Bend Test Requirements Diameter of Serial Number Specified minimum tensile strength of plate Former Free space between support at the end of list

(i) 2 T

4.2 T

(ii) From 44 to 54 kgf/mm2 3 T

5.2 T

(iii) 4 T

6.2 T

162. Notched-bar-impact test.─ (1) The notched bar impact test specimens shall be of the type and dimension as shown in Fig. 4/7, the notch shall be contained in the weld-metal at approximately the axis of the weld and the axis of the notch is to be perpendicular to the surface of the plate.

(2) The test shall be carried out as follows:

For the V-Notch specimen at a temperature of 20 ± 2°C. In the case of V-Notch specimen, the machining of the bottom of the notch shall be done very carefully. The minimum result to be obtained from the impact test pieces shall be:

(a) V-Notch specimen: 3.46 kgfm/cm2

(b) One test shall consist of three test specimens and the value specified above is the average of three specimen values.

(c) Out of the three values, no single value shall be less than one third of the average value.

Note: Above value is equivalent to 2.76 kgfm divided by sectional area below the notch.

All dimensions are in mm.

Fig. 4/7: Standard V-Notch Test Piece

163. Additional tests before rejection.─ (1) In case any of the test specimens taken in accordance with regulations 156 to 162 fail to meet the specified requirements, one retest shall be allowed for each specimen that fails, provided the following minimum figures have been obtained.

(a) Reduction in area 30 per cent.

(b) Any other test except the bend and impact tests, 90 percent of the specified requirements.

(2) In case, a cold bend specimen fail to meet the specified requirements, two re-tests shall be taken from the same test plate and these shall comply with the requirements of regulations 160 and 161.

(3) If Charpy impact test fails to meet the specified requirements, two re-tests shall be taken from the test plate on each side of the original specimen and separated from it by not more than 5 mm. Both re-tests shall show a minimum Charpy impact test value of 2.76 kgfm.

(4) If it is found that there is insufficient metal to permit the preparation of specimen for re-test from the reminder of the test may be cut from the test plate relating to the opposite and of the same longitudinal seam.

164. Non-Destructive test.─ (1) Micro and Macro Examination─ A specimen, the full thickness of the plate and not less than 13 mm wide may be taken from each set of test plates by the Inspecting Authority for the purpose of micro and macro examination. Macro-etching of a complete cross-section of the weld including the heat effected zone, should show satisfactory penetration, fusion and absence of significant inclusions or other defects. In case of a doubt as to the condition of the weld as shown by micro-etching, the area concerned shall be microscopically examined for defect investigation.

(2) Radiographical examination─(a) Every portion of the longitudinal and circumferential welded joints of the drums shall be subjected to radiographical examination or ultrasonic examination. Where ultrasonic examination is used, it shall be demonstrated to the satisfaction of the Inspecting Authority that the equipment and the technique used are satisfactory. The manufacturer shall ensure that the operator employed is competent to use the equipment, apply the technique and interpret the results of the examination. NDE operator and evaluator shall possess qualification as per ASNT SNT-TC-1A or ISO 9712 or equivalent.

(b) The method employed in obtaining the radiographs shall be such as to show clearly defects having a magnitude equal to 2 per cent of the thickness of the welded joint. To determine whether this result is being obtained and indicator of approved form which includes a portion equivalent to not more than 2 per cent of the joint thickness, shall be placed in the vicinity of the weld so as to make a record on each radiograph.

Each section of every weld shall be marked so that the radiographs can be easily correlated to the particular part of the joint represented. Radiographic or Ultrasonic testing shall be carried out as per written down procedure specifying technique, IQI sensitivity, density requirements and acceptance norms in line with established International boiler codes subject to acceptance by the Competent person.

(c)The examination shall be made from the original films and the acceptability of the welds shall be rejected or dealt with under regulation 165 and be radiographed again.

(d) PAUT or TOFD or both in lieu of Radiographic test may be used subject to establishing a procedure to include techniques, set ups, calibrations, operator qualification, acceptance norms in line with International Codes.

(3) Magnetic Particle Flaw Detection- Magnetic method of flaw detection shall be employed wherever possible for ferritic steel.

(4) Dye-Penetrate Flaw Detection- Dye penetrants or equivalent method of flaw detection shall be employed for Austenitic or other non-magnetic steel.

(5) All butt welded joints shall be subjected to non-destructive examination by radiographic, radioscopy or other approved methods such as ultrasonic testing, magnetic particle inspection or liquid dye penetrant inspection.

When radioscopic examination is to be performed in lieu of radiography on welded components, the following requirements shall be met, namely: -

(a) A written procedure shall be submitted for approval to the Inspecting Authority which shall contain the following:

(i) material and the thickness range;

(ii) equipment qualifications;

(iii) test object scan plan;

(iv) radioscopic parameters;

(v) image processing parameters;

(vi) image display parameters;

(vii) image archiving requirements;

(viii) accept-reject criteria (Code reference);

(ix) performance evaluation;

(x) Operator identification.

(b) The system shall be aided with an image processor to enhance the quality of the radioscopic images and system performance quality shall exhibit:

(i) a thin section contrast sensitivity of 3%;

(ii) a thick section contrast sensitivity of 2%;

(iii) a spatial resolution of 3 line pairs per mm;

(iv) IQI sensitivity - 2% of the joint thickness when wire IQI's are to be used, the wire diameter axis shall be oriented along the axis of the least sensitivity of the system.

(c) Radioscopes shall be properly marked to co-relate with particular part of joint represented.

(d) The radioscopic examination data shall be recorded and stored on videotape, magnetic disk or optical disk or any other electronic storage device at the maker's plant for a sufficient period after the date of radioscopic examination as specified by the Inspecting Authority, Efficient radioscopic examination record recall shall be made available at any time over the record retention period and shall be traceable to the test objects.

(e) When repair has been performed as a result of radioscopic examination, the repaired areas shall be re-examined using the same radioscopic technique to evaluate the effectiveness of the repair.

(f) To aid in proper interpretation of the radioscopic examination data, the details of the technique used shall accompany the data. As a minimum, the information shall include the approved procedure requirements and system performance test data.

165. General.─ (1) (a) Any repair to a weld carried out by the manufacturer shall first be agreed to by the Inspecting Authority.

(b)Where defects occur in distributed positions in a seam, repairs to the extent of 15 per cent, of the total length of the seam shall be permitted to replace the defective welding.

(c)Where defects are located in a single continuous length, the permissible extent of repair shall be decided by the Inspecting Authority.

(2) Where the weld metal in any way fails to comply with the requirements specified, the whole of the weld metal may be removed and the seam re-welded provided that;

(a) the original test plates are similarly treated, or

(b) New test plates of the same thickness as the joint and of similar quality of material are attached to the end of the seam and re-welded with it.

(c)In either case the plates shall be tested in accordance with regulations 156 to 164 and the requirement for heat treatment shall be in accordance with regulation 166.

(3) With regard to the extent of permissible repair and when agreed by the Inspecting Authority, a circumferential seam shall be treated in the same manner as a longitudinal seam. The Inspecting Authority shall be entitled to call for representative tests for a re-welded circumferential seam.

(4) Defects shall be cut out by chipping of machining or by burning out by the Air Arc Weld Process.

(5) Where a defective part has been cut out, the Inspecting Authority shall be entitled to make an examination before re-welding.

(6) Only metallic arc welding shall be used for repairs.

(7) All repaired areas shall be subjected to radiographical examination or radioscopic examination by the approved techniques.

(8) The Inspecting Authority shall be entitled to call for specimen representing any welded repair, for the purpose of examination and test.

(9) On completion of all welded repairs, the manufacturers shall, if requested, supply for record purposes a detailed diagram showing the positions, length depth and width of all such repairs.

166. Heat treatment.─(1) (a)After completion of the welding of the seams and the welding on the stand pipes and including welding repairs but before the hydraulic test, each drum shall be heat treated for stress relieving; during this heat treatment the official test plates shall be lying inside the drum.

(b)For heat treatment requirements refer regulation 123.

(2) Where shell is subjected to a primary stress relieving treatment identical to the final heat treatment to be given to the drum, the test plates may be placed inside the shell during the primary treatment and thereafter cut up and tested without waiting for the final treatment of the drum.

(3) Where the shell is not subjected to primary stress relieving treatment or is subject to a primary stress relieving treatment which is not identical with the final heat treatment, the test plates may be placed inside any other drum of comparable dimensions which is to be heat treated in accordance with this Chapter. The heat treatment is to take place in the furnace in which the drum relating to the test plates will eventually be given its final heat treatment.

(4) Temperature charts shall be submitted to indicate that the test plates and the drum they represent have been subjected to identical heating soaking and cooling treatment.

(5) (a) For the heat treatment, the drum shall be placed in a furnace sufficiently larger to accommodate the whole drum.

(b) The furnace shall have proper means of temperature measurement and control and shall be capable of heating the whole of the drum to a uniform temperature. It shall be so designed as to prevent direct impingement of flame on the drum.

(c) Thermocouples shall be attached to the drum at appropriate locations. A temperature time diagram of the stress-relieving process shall be provided for a welded shell or drum and a similar diagram for other welded pressure part shall be provided where inspecting Authority requires it.

(6) Alternative procedure for the heat treatment of the drum may be submitted to the inspecting Authority for approval.

(7) In special cases it may be permissible to heat treat the test plates separately from the drum, provided the inspecting Authority is satisfied with the means adopted to ensure that the following factors shall be the same for the drum as for the test plates, namely:-

(i) Rate of heating

(ii) Maximum temperature

(iii) Time held at maximum temperature

(iv) Conditions of cooling.

167. Hydraulic test at makers' works.─ (1) Boiler drum having internal diameter greater than 600 mm and other cylindrical component having internal diameters greater than 1000 mm shall be hydraulically tested on completion of manufacture at the makers works in the presence of Competent Person to 1½ times the maximum allowable working pressure without indication of weakness and defects.

(2) All components which are not reasonably accessible for inspection after assembly into the boiler or have been tested hydraulically prior to welding at a pressure less than that specified in sub-regulation (1) shall be tested hydraulically to 1½ times the maximum allowable working pressure before assembly into the boiler.

(3) Tubular products that have been hydraulically tested to the required pressure prior to welding or ultrasonically tested shall not require further hydraulic testing as components provided they were joined during assembly by circumferential butt joint which have been welded and non-destructively tested as per relevant provisions of these regulations.

Components other than tubular products shall not require hydraulic testing before assembly into the boiler if the completed boiler is tested hydraulically to 1½ times the design pressure at site.

In case of drums, headers which are to be fitted with tubes, the test may be made before drilling of tube holes but after attachments of nozzles and similar fittings.

The test pressure shall be raised gradually under proper control at all times so that it never exceeds by more than 6% of the required pressure and maintained for 30 minutes whereupon the pressure shall be reduced to maximum allowable working pressure and maintained for sufficient time to permit close visual inspection for leakages of the pressure parts.

The temperature of water used as medium of pressure testing shall not be less than 20°C and more than 50°C.

(a) In case of drums of 'composite' construction and partly reverted and partly welded seams or seamless forged drum shell with ends attached by fusion welding.

(b) The test pressure shall be the same as for fusion welded drums.

In case the hydraulic test reveal any defects in the welded steam, it shall not be repaired unless the Inspecting Authority permits to do so.

On completion of agreed repairs to a drum which has previously been stress relieved by heat treatment, further heat-treatment, if required by the Inspecting Authority shall be done and the drum shall again be subjected to hydraulic test.

PART-III

168. Inspection during construction.─ Inspection during construction and fees therefor shall be governed in accordance with the provisions of Appendix-A and regulation 324.

PART-IV MAXIMUM ALLOWABLE WORKING PRESSURE OF WATER TUBE BOILERS WITH FUSION WELDED DRUMS SHELLS

169. Shell of steam and water drums.─ (1)(a)The maximum allowable working pressure shall be determined by the following formula: 2fE (T – 0.75) P = —————————Equation (4/1) D + (T – 0.75) Where, P = Maximum Allowable Working pressure in kg/cm2 (g) T = Actual thickness of shell in mm, D = maximum internal diameter of shell in mm, F = permissible working stress in kg/cm2 (g) at working metal temperature (see regulation 170), E =the efficiency of ligaments between the tube holes or other uncompensated opening in shell, or the weld factor of the longitudinal joints, For Class I boilers, the weld factor shall be taken as 1,In the particular case of an unpierced wrapper plate of a fusion welded drum, E = 1,

(b) The minimum required thickness of shell shall be determined using the following formula:

P x D t = (2 x f x E)-P + 0.75 Equation (4/2) Where t = Calculated minimum required thickness of the shell in mm, P = Maximum Allowable Working pressure in kg/cm2 (g) D = maximum internal diameter of shell in mm, f = permissible working stress in kg/cm2 (g) at working metal temperature (see regulation 170), E = Ligament efficiency as per regulation 171).

Irrespective of the thickness obtained by the use of the foregoing formula "t" shall not be less than: -

(i) For tube plates (where the tubes are expanded therein) the thickness shall be at least such as to allow a minimum parallel belt width of tube seat of 10 mm, this seating to be measured as explained below.

(ii) All tubes shall be carefully expanded into the holes in the tube plates. The tubes shall be belled or beaded to resist withdrawal and if belled they shall project through the parallel tube seat at least 6 mm.

(2) The belling shall be as shown in Table 4/4 below:

TABLE 4/4 Serial Number Outside diameter of tube Amount of diameter of belling over diameter of the tube hole (in) mm

(i) Upto and including 38 mm 2 mm 3 mm

(ii) Over 38 mm upto and including 50 mm 4 mm

(iii) Over 50 mm upto and including 83 mm 5mm

(iv) Over 83 mm upto and including 101 mm

(3) The tube holes in the tube plates of drums pockets of headers shall be formed in such a way that the tubes can be effectively tightened in them. Where the tube ends are not normal to the tube plate, there shall be a neck or belt of parallel seating at right angle to the axis of the tube at least 13 mm in depth measured in a prime containing the axis of the tube at the hole.

(4) Where the tubes are practically normal to the tube plate or the header, this parallel seating shall, where or practicable be not less than 10 mm in depth.

170. Permissible working stresses for shells of boiler and integral super heater drums and headers.─(1) The maximum permissible stress for drum shells and headers shall be taken as available in governing Boiler codes of the country of the material to which it belongs. In case of non-availability of the value, the following procedure for evaluating shall be adopted.

(i) For temperatures at or below 454°C, the smaller of the two values:Et R f = ——— or ———

1.5

2.7

(ii) For temperatures above 454°C, the least of the following three values:Et Sr

(a) ———

(b) ———

(c) Sc

1.5

1.5 Where, t = Working metal temperature, R = Minimum specified tensile strength of the steel at room temperature, E = Minimum specified Yield point at room temperature, Et = Yield Point (0.2% proof stress) at the temperature 't', Sc = The average stress to produce an elongation of 1% (creep) in 100,000 hours at temperature 't', Sr = The average stress to produce rupture in 100,000 hours at the temperature 't' and in no case more than 1.33 times the lowest stress to produce rupture at the temperature.

Note: In case Sc values are not available in Material Standard and such materials are known to have been used in boilers in India or abroad, then for such materials the allowable stress may be taken as the lower of Et Sr ——— or ———

1.5

1.5

(2) For fusion welded drums, when the wall thickness exceed 60 mm, a deduction of 1 per cent in the value of "f" so determined shall be made for each increase of 5 mm in the thickness. Such deduction need not be made provided the minimum specified values of R and E at any part of the section of the steel plate used in the manufacture of the Boiler drum are guaranteed by a "Well known Steel Maker" or certified by an Inspecting Authority.

(3) The working metal temperature shall be taken as:-

(a) For saturated steam, water and mud drums, the saturation temperature corresponding to the pressure WP plus 28°C.

(b) For superheated steam the designed maximum steam temperature for that drum plus 28°C.

(4) Where the drums are adequately protected from the gases of combustion or swept by such gasses in the third or subsequent pass of a boiler, the working metal temperature shall be taken as the saturation or designed maximum steam temperature as defined above, whichever applies. A covering of refractory or insulating material which may be liable to become dislodged shall not be deemed adequate protection.

NOTE: Where steels are for service at temperatures in excess of 370°C it shall be so stated as the silicon content shall be 0.10 per cent minimum or alternatively the material must pass the "Proof Test for Creep Quality of Carbon Steel Plate of Boiler Plate Quality" as in the Appendix-E.

171.Efficiency of ligament.─ When a shell or drum is drilled for tubes in a line parallel to the axis of the shell or drum, the efficiency of the ligament between the tube holes shall be determined as follows:

(a) When a pitch of the tube holes on every row is equal (as in Fig. 4/8), the formula is: p – d ——— = efficiency of ligament Equation (4/3) p Where, p = pitch of tube holes, mm. d = diameter of tube holes, mm.

The pitch of tube holes shall be measured either on the flat plate before rolling or on the median line after rolling.

Example: Pitch of tube holes in the drums as shown in Fig. 4/8 = 133.3 mm. Diameter of tube = 82.5 mm. Diameter of tube holes = 83.3 mm. p – d

(b) When the pitch of tube holes on any one row in unequal (as in Fig. 4/9 &4/10), the formula is: p - nd ———— = efficiency of ligament Equation (4/4) p Where, p = unit length of ligament, mm. n = number of tube holes in length p. d = diameter of tube holes, mm.

Example: Spacing shown in Fig. 4/9 Diameter of Tube hole = 83.3 mm p – nd

304.8 – (2 x 83.3) ——— = —————————- = 0.453, efficiency of ligament. p

(c) The strength of those ligaments between the tube holes which are subjected to a longitudinal stress shall be at least one-half times the required strength of those ligaments which come between the tube holes which are subjected to a circumferential stress.

(d) (i) When bending stresses due to weight are negligible and the tube holes are arranged along a diagonal line with respect to the longitudinal axis, the efficiency (z) of corresponding ligaments is given in Fig. 4/11, with the ratio b/a on the abscissa and the ratio 2a – d d ———- or — used as a parameter. 2a a Where, a and b are measured as shown in Figs. 4/13 and 4/14, d = diameter of the tube holes.

Notes: 1. The dimension should be measured either on the flat plate before rolling or on the medium line after rolling.

2. The data given on Fig. 4/11 are based on the following formulae:

Z = A+B+√(A-B)2+4C2 Where, A= cos2α+1 2 x (1- d cos α a ) B= (1 2) x (1- d cos α a ) (sin2α+1) C= cosα sinα 2 x (1- d cosα a ) cosα= ; sinα= √1+ a2 √1+ b2 a2 b2 ( = angle of centre line of cylinder to centre line of diagonal holes).

(ii) The same rule as in paragraph (i) should apply for the case of drilling holes to a regular saw- tooth shown in Fig. 4/15.

(iii) In the case of a regular staggered spacing of tube holes (see Fig. 4/13, the smallest value of the efficiency z of all the ligaments, longitudinal, circumferential and diagonal, is given in Fig. 4/12 by the ratio Pc/PL on the abscissa, and the ratio PL – d d ———- or — used as a parameter.

PL a Where, d = diameter of tube holes, mm.

Pc = 2b = twice the distance between circumferential rows of holes;

PL = 2a = twice the distance between axial row holes;

Note:

The dimension Pc should be measured on the flat plate before rolling or on the medium line after rolling. The data on Fig. 4/12 are based on the same formulae as shown in Fig. 4/13.

(e) When holes spaced longitudinally along a drum are hot in a straight line, the equivalent longitudinal pitch for each spacing may be used in the application of the above rules. The equivalent pitch is obtained by multi plying the actual longitudinal pitch by the equivalent efficiency obtained from Fig. 4/11 for each spacing.
