172. Longitudinal stress.─ (1) Notwithstanding the maximum allowable working pressure as calculated by equation (4/1), the thickness of drum or cylindrical header shells shall be such that in no case does the longitudinal stress resulting from the combination of stress arising from internal steam pressure, the self-weight of the drum or header and its contents and all externally applied loads, exceed the permissible working stress corresponding to the working metal temperature as prescribed in regulation 170.

The maximum direct longitudinal stress due to the internal steam pressure acting on the drum ends shall be calculated as follows:

PD2 fd = —————

Equation (4/5)

1.273A Where, fd = Maximum direct longitudinal stress in kg/cm2, P = Maximum Allowable Working Pressure in kg/cm2 (g), D = Internal diameter of the drum or header in cm, A = Net cross-sectional area of the drum or header in cm2 taken through the tube holes in a plane at right angles to its axis.

The resultant bending moments M at any section shall be the algebraic sum of the bending moments due to the eccentricity of the end pressure and that due to the externally applied loads.

M = Me + Mw Equation (4/6) The bending moment due to the eccentricity of end pressure shall be calculated as follows.

PD2 .e Me = —————

Equation (4/7)

1.273 Where, Me= Resultant bending moment due to eccentricity in kgf-cm, P = Maximum Allowable Working Pressure in kg/cm2 (g), D = Internal diameter of drum or header in cm, e = Eccentricity of the net cross-section i.e., the distance from the neutral axis of the net cross section to the drum or header axis in cm.

The bending moment (Mw) due to externally applied loads shall be calculated by treating the drum or header as a beam carrying the externally applied loads, including the self-weight of the drum or header and its contents under working conditions.

(4) The stress due to bending shall be calculated as follows:

Mr Y fb = ————

Equation (4/8) I Where, fb = Stress due to bending in kg/cm2.

Mr= resultant bending moment at the section in kgf-cm, Y= Distance from the neutral axis of the net cross-section to the extreme fiber of the drum of header shell in cm.

I = Moment of inertia of the net cross-section taken about its neutral axis in (cm4).

The resultant longitudinal stress is the algebraic sum of the stresses given under (2) and (4).

(5) In calculating the longitudinal stress due to bending in a drum supported at or near its ends and connected to a lower drum by a bank of tubes (so arranged as to form substantial struts between the drums) the value of the moment of inertia Ia used in the formula in sub-regulation (4) shall be : - Moment of inertia of upper drum (Ib) plus a proportion (S) of the moment of inertia of lower drum

(Ic). 2 S = 1 - ——Equation (4/9) Where,  the angle in degrees between the vertical and the line joining the centers of the upper and lower drums. Where  is equal to or greater than 15½°; S shall is be taken as 0. In no case shall the actual value of Ia used in Equation (4/8) be taken as more than 1.33 times the moment of inertia of the upper drum (Ib).

In the foregoing, unless otherwise agreed the term "bank of tubes" shall be defined as consisting of four or more rows of tubes extending over at least three quarters of the drum length between supports, and pitched longitudinally at not greater than an average pitch of four tube diameters.

173. Intermediate boiler drum supports.─ Where a boiler drum is supported at Intermediate points in its length at a distance greater than one internal diameter from the end of the parallel portion of the drum shell, the shell shall be thickened locally to the supports or so designed that when the local stress set up by the supports in the drum shell are added, algebraically to those caused by the internal steam pressure and supported loads, the maximum resultant stress does not exceed the permissible working stress at the working metal temperature.

PART-V

174. Shape of dished end plate.─ When the end plate is dished to semi-ellipsodial, partial spherical or hemispherical form, it shall comply with the following: (see Fig. 4/16, 4/17, and 4/18);

(a) Hemispherical heads without limitation;

(b) Elliptical heads sufficiently dished so that H ≥ 0.2 D;

(c) Partial spherical heads satisfying all the following requirements: r  0.1 D r  3 T R D H  0.18D A value of R < 1.2 D is permitted for ends in which the ratio H/D falls between 0.18-0.2.

In accordance with Figs. 4/16, 4/17, 4/18, 4/19, 4/20, 4/21 – D = outside diameter of the head;

H = height of the head measured on its outside surface from the junction of the dished part with the cylindrical shell;

R = inside radius of the spherical part of partial spherical heads; r = inside knuckle radius of partial spherical heads.

Fig. 4/19: Opening in Heads Fig. 4/20: Reinforced opening DEPTH RATIO H/D Note 1: The curve drawn with continuous line in the series is for determining value of K for plain unpierced heads.

175. General.─ (1) The thickness 'T' specified in equation (4/10) of regulation 177 is the thickness of the end plate after manufacture and is applicable over the whole area of the end plate up to the point where, for ends of partially spherical shape, the dishing radius joins the corner radius; from this joint a gradual thinning is permissible up to maximum of 10 per cent of the thickness 'T' at the point where the corner radius joins the straight portion of flanged end plate.

(2) A similar gradual thinning is permissible for end plates of semi-ellipsoidal shape.

(3) This permissible reduction in thickness applies also to the flange for the manhole opening. In no case, however, when the end plate is butt welded to the drum shell, shall the thickness of the edge of the flange for connection to the shell be less than the thickness of an unpierced seamless shell of the same diameter and material, determined by equation (4/2).

176. Dished end with opening.─ (1) (a) Holes cut in the heads (manholes or tube holes) shall be round or elliptical.

(b) Small size openings complying with conditions of sub-regulation (3) do not require any additional thickness of head or reinforcement of opening.

(c) Large size openings require an increase in thickness of the head, according to sub-regulation (2) or a reinforcement of the opening according to sub-regulation (4) .

(2) (a)The shape factor 'K' to be used in calculating the thickness of heads varies depending on the height of the head 'H' and on the dimension of the largest opening. The value of K is indicated in Fig. 4/21, by means of curves, each of which corresponds to a value of the ratio— d ——— DT Where, d = diameter or the largest opening in the head (in the case of an elliptical opening, the larger diameter of the ellipse);

D = outside diameter of the head; and T= minimum thickness after dishing.

(b) In addition, the following conditions shall be satisfied;

T < 0.1 and D d < 0.5 D

(c) The same condition and the same curves apply for openings simply cut in the plate of the head as well as to those which are flanged inwards. In the latter case, the radius 'r' of the flanging (see Fig. 4/18) should not be smaller than 25 mm. The thickness of the flanged portion may be smaller than the calculated thickness 'T'.

(d) Unreinforced openings and flanged-in openings in dished heads shall be arranged so that the distance from the edge of the head is not less than as shown in Fig. 4/19.

(e) In all case the width of the band separating two adjacent openings should, in projection, be at least equal to the diameter of the smallest opening as shown in Fig. 4/19.

(3) (a)Small openings which do not require any reinforcement; Fig. 4/21 indicates that, for a given ratio of H/D, the shape factor K of a plain head corresponds to a certain value of the ratio, d ——— DT

(b) Piercing of holes with a diameter smaller than or equal to that which corresponds to this value can thus be made without any reinforcement in a head, the thickness of which is equal to the minimum required for a plain head.

(c) The position of those openings shall however comply with the conditions stated in sub-regulation (2).

(4) (a)Reinforcement of large openings; large holes may be cut without increasing the thickness of the head provided they are sufficiently reinforced.

(b) The reinforcement may consist either of a welded nozzle or one or two reinforcing welded plates, or of a combination of these two procedures.

(c) To determine the strength of the construction, one shall proceed as follows (see Fig. 4/20) Let the imaginary diameter d' of the opening be given by the formula.

A d' = d - ——

T Where, d = actual diameter of the opening in the head (outside diameter of the nozzle);

T = minimum thickness of the head; and A = effective cross-section of reinforcement.

(e) The shape factor K can then be read from Fig. 4/21, corresponding to the ratio d' / √DT

178. Internal doors.─ Requirements of regulation 137 shall apply.

PART-VI

179. Stand Pipes, nozzles and openings.─ (1) Where openings in drums, shells, pipes are drilled in a definite pattern, the efficiency of the ligaments shall be determined by the methods given in regulation 171 provided the largest hole in the group doesn't exceed the following:-.

The maximum diameter of any unreinforced opening shall not exceed 'd' as calculated from the following equation (subject to a maximum of 203mm).

K = PD / 1.82 f t where , D = nominal OD of the Drum, Shell, Header, Pipe or Dished end containing opening (mm) t = actual thickness (mm) P = f = maximum permissible stress as available in the governing codes of the country to which When K has a value of unity or greater, maximum diameter of unreinforced opening should be taken as 51mm. Larger openings shall be compensated in accordance with sub-regulation (2) of regulation 179.

(2) (a) Where standpipes or nozzles are secured by welding, adequate compensation for the hole cut shall be provided. Compensation shall be considered adequate when the sectional area 'X' to be compensated measured through the axis of the shell is less than the compensating area 'Y' given in Fig. 4/22 and Fig. 4/23.

(b) In case where 'Y' is less than 'X', a compensating plate shall be fitted to the shell at the standpipe and secured by fillet welds as in Fig. 4/22 and Fig. 4/23.

Fig. 4/22: Compensation of Set through branch openings Fig. 4/23: Compensation of Set on branch openings Area to be compensated (X) = dn x es Note: If compensation pad is provided, the thickness of compensation pad to be added with shell minimum thickness.

Area available for compensation from shell = 2 x (ts-es)x Ci Area available for compensation from nozzle projecting outside shell surface = 2 x (tn-en)x Bo x fn fs Area available for compensation from nozzle projecting inside shell surface = 2 x tn x Bi x fn fs Area available from weld = Aggregate cross sectional area of weld x fn fs Area available for compensation (Y) = sum of all the above areas.

Where, ts = actual thickness of the shell es = thickness of unpierced shell designed to withstand the required pressure and temperature dn = nozzle maximum inside diameter for set on type nozzles or hole diameter for set through type nozzles tn = actual thickness of nozzle en = thickness of nozzle required to withstand the required pressure, temperature and external loads on the nozzle. fs = allowable stress for shell material at the working metal temperature fn = allowable stress for nozzle material at the working metal temperature Ci = greater of (ts + 76, dn/2) Bo & Bi = least of (2.5 times the actual thickness of shell, 2.5 times the actual thickness of nozzle).

Note: In any case if the length of nozzle is less than the condition specified above, then 'Bi' & 'Bo' should be limited to actual length of the nozzle. w = width of weld h = height of weld No credit should be taken for additional strength of material having higher stress value than that of shell used for nozzle or reinforcing pad.

The area of cross-section of the compensation plate shall be governed by the ratio between the permissible stress at the working metal temperature for the materials of the shell and the compensating plate.

(3) Where the standpipe or nozzles are welded to dished end plates, the compensation shall be considered adequate if calculated on the same basis as in sub-regulation (1) and (2) above. But the cross-sectional area of the end plate to be compensated shall be calculated on the basis of Equation (4/10) and the height of standpipe above the outer surface on within the inner surface of the end plate shall be measured along the radial line passing through the centre of the opening.

(4) The minimum thickness of the flanges and the body of standpipes shall be in accordance with regulation 127. Flanges conforming to International codes and standards such as ASME, BS, EN, DIN can also be used if it is meeting required pressure and temperature ratings.

180. Attachment of stand blocks and branch pipes by welding.─ (1)Typical methods of attachment are shown in figures, namely:Fig. 4/24, 4/25, 4/26, 4/27, 4/28, 4/29, 4/30, 4/31, 4/32, 4/33, 4/34, 4/35 and 4/36.

All weld dimensions shown shall be regarded as minimum dimensions those defining the grooves refer to the actual preparation for welding. tn = Specified thickness of standpipe or branch pipe. ts = Specified thickness of drum or header shell at standpipe or branch connection. te = Actual thickness of compensating ring.

The application of certain of the constructions illustrated may be limited by consideration of design pressure and temperature or size (in combination) and although certain dimensions are given, each case must be considered on its merits.

Other methods may be adopted subject to agreement between the Inspecting Authority and manufacturer.

(2) Welding consumables shall comply with the requirements of regulation 71 to 76.

(3) Not less than two runs of metal shall be deposited at each weld. Each run of weld metal shall be thoroughly cleaned and freed from slag before the next run is deposited. In the case of water tube boiler or boilers fitted with super heater, the feed water connection and stop valve need not be shut off and if the total valve area is lifted and found to be adequate by calculations, the requirement of the accumulation test may be assumed to have been satisfied if the valves area has been found so adjusted that at least one safety valve on each boiler shall lift at or below the design pressure of the boiler and all valves shall lift so that are steam which can be generated by the boiler can be discharged with a pressure rise not exceeding 10 per cent of the design pressure.

(4) The final finish of the weld shall be such that change of section from drum to standpipe, or from flange to standpipe is gradual and free from sharp notches. After completion of welding (except in the case of seal welds) the drum shall be stress-relieved by heat treatment.

Fig. 4/29: Screwed and Seal Welded Connection Fig. 4/31: Welded in Stand Pipe or Branch Fig. 4/30: Welded in Stand Pipe or Branch with Fig. 4/32: Welded in Stand Pipe or Branch Compensating Ring All dimensions are in mm.

The branch must be loose fit in the hole but the gap at any point, should not exceed 3 mm or en/2 whichever is less.

Welded sizes B1 + F1 = 1.5en.

F1 should not exceed 16 mm not be less than en/2. Weld size B + F = 1.5en minimum or 1.5es minimum, whichever is less.

F should not exceed 16 mm nor be less than en/2. en = Thickness of nozzle. es = Thickness of shell.

All dimensions are in mm. .

CHAPTER-5 VALVES, GAUGES AND AUXILIARIES PART-I

181. Requisite Mountings, fittings and Auxiliaries. ─ (1) Every boiler shall be provided at least with the following, namely: -

(a) Two safety valves, one of which may be a high steam and low water type safety valve and in no case the bore of the seat of the valve should be less than 19 mm.

(b) Two means of indicating the water level;

(c) A steam pressure gauge;

(d) A steam stop valve;

(e) A feed check valve;

(f) One feed apparatus;

Provided that for boilers fired by gaseous, liquid or solid fuels in suspension where heating surface exceeds 20 m2, shall be provided with minimum two feed apparatus which shall have a combined capacity of not less than the maximum continuous rating of the boiler: Provided further that for other boilers where residual heat is there even after fuel supply cut-off, two feed apparatus each having a capacity of not less than maximum continuous rating of the boiler shall be provided. (For boilers in battery see regulation 242);

(g) A blow-down cock or valve;

(h) Fusible plugs as provided under Regulation 236;

(i) An attachment for Competent Person's test gauge;

(j) A manhole, where size and construction permit, and such mud holes or sight holes as are necessary for effectively cleaning the boiler.

(2) In the case of boilers fitted with integral super heaters, an additional safety valve shall be fitted at the end of the super heaters outlet header.

(3) In the case of boilers with no fixed steam and waterline, the fitting of such accessories that are manifestly not needed or used, such as water gauges, water columns and gauge cocks, may not be insisted upon.

(4) In the case of a single boiler of the Shell type and not connected in a battery with other boilers, the heating surface of which does not exceed 102 m2, two independent source of power supply to the two feed apparatus shall not be necessary.

(5) In the case of miniature boilers coming under Chapter-14, the Steam pressure gauge may be connected to the steam space or to a steam connection to the water column by a syphon tube or equivalent device that will keep the gauge tube filled with water and if brass or bronze composition is used, the minimum size of the syphon tube shall be 6 mm standard pipe size; for other materials the minimum inside diameter of the pipe or tube shall be 13 mm.

(6) For Electrode boilers, see regulation 356.

(7) In case of boilers with reheaters, the reheaters shall be protected with one or more relieving devices of 105% capacity (15% of the total capacity shall be located in the steam flow path between reheater outlet and first stop valve) to avoid over pressure in case of internal failures.

182. Additional Requirements for Automatic Boilers.─(1)Every boiler which is provided with the appropriate water level and /or firing control, to allow for automatic working, shall comply with the following requirements:

(a) In the event of failure of automatic control, boiler shall be capable of being brought under immediate manual control.

(b) In those cases where the control and alarm devices are housed in chambers external to the boiler, the following shall apply, namely:-

(i) The boiler shall have provision made for isolating the steam and water connections of the chambers from the boilers. Such isolating valves shall be capable of being locked in the open position and shall preferably be of the parallel slide type but if screw down stop valves are fitted, those on the water connection shall be mounted with the horizontal spindle to avoid the possibility of an air lock.

The steam and water connections of the chambers shall be not less than 25 mm bore. In those cases where sequencing control water valves are fitted, the steam isolating valve may be omitted;

(ii) The boiler shall be provided means to test operation of the control or alarm and to blow through separately the water connection and the chamber to prevent the accumulation of deposits. The means provided for flowing through shall be sequencing valves or equivalent devices so arranged that the water connection to the boiler cannot be shut off unless the drain connections to the chamber is open;

(iii) The boiler shall have a drain system from the chambers which provides a visual indication of flow.

Tundishes of adequate size, placed in a prominent position shall be used wherever practicable.

(c) Where the boiler is provided with electrical equipment for water level and firing control, it shall have this equipment so designed that any fault in the circuits caused, both in the fuel and air supply, the boiler to be automatically shut off. Positive means requiring manual resetting shall be provided to cut off the fuel and air supplies to the boilers should there be a failure of electricity supply to water level and / or firing control equipment. All electrical conductors and equipment in connection with water level and firing controls shall be of adequate size and shall be properly insulated and protected against danger including adequate protection against the ingress of moisture and the effects of high temperature.

(d) Where the evaporating capacity of the boiler is greater than 3700 Kg/h, it shall have automatic water level alarms and firing controls and can be tested regularly without altering the level of water in the boiler. The water level alarms may be incorporated in the automatic control system. With a perceptible water level which are arranged for automatic working and not intended for continuous supervision, shall be provided with an entirely independent and separately operated over-riding control in addition to the water level and firing control specified in sub-regulations (2) and (3). The over-riding control shall comply with the following requirements:

(i) Cut off fuel supply to the burners or the fuel and air supply to solid fuel stoker and operate an audible alarm when the water level in the boiler falls to a pre-determined low water level;

(ii) Be of the lock-out type and so arranged that control or its electrical circuit has to be manually reset before the boiler can be brought back into operation;

(iii) Be provided with its own entirely independent electrical control circuit.

(e) Where mounted externally to the boiler, the over-riding control shall -

(i) be provided with its own chamber;

(ii) Comply with the requirements of clause (b).

(2) Automatic water level control shall be as follows:-

(a) float or displacer operated; or

(b) operated by electrical probe or thermostat; or

(c) operated by other approved principle to the satisfaction of the Inspecting Authority.

The control equipment shall regulate the feed-water supply to the boiler in order to effectively maintain the level of water in the boiler between predetermined limits.

(3) Automatic firing controls shall comply with the following requirements-

(a) At all times they shall effectively control the supply of fuel and air to the combustion equipments.

(b) They shall shut off the fuel supply to the burners or the fuel and air supply to the stoker under the following conditions:-

(i) Flame failure or pilot flame failure in the cases of gas, oil or pulverised fuel fired boilers.

This control shall be of the lockout type required to be manually reset;

(ii) Failure to ignite the fuel at the burner within a pre-determined time. The control shall be of the lock-out type required to be manually reset;

(iii) When the water level in a boiler falls below a pre-determined safe level. The control shall also cause an audible alarm.

(iv) Failure of forced or induced draught fans or any automatic flue damper.

(v) Increase in furnace pressure to a pre-determined value.

Note: The above provisions shall not apply to once-through forced circulation boiler.

PART-II

183. Materials, Temperature and Pressure Limits.─(1)The materials used in the manufacture of the bodies of the fittings shall comply with the following requirements, namely: -

(i) Castings of Carbon, Alloy and Stainless Steel shall comply with regulations 50 to 57.

(ii) Steel forgings shall comply with regulations 58 to 62.

(iii) Iron casting shall comply with the regulations 63 to 70.

(iv) Bronze castings shall comply with the following table 5/1:

0.50%

0.50%

1. Tin

5.5 to 8.0% 4 to 6%

2. Lead

1.0 to 3.0% 4 to 6%

3. Zinc

3.0 to 6.0% 4 to 6%

4. Total of all elements other than those set out above and excluding Copper and incidental Nickel

5. Copper plus incidental Nickel Balance Balance

6. Physical Properties Ultimate tensile Strength

7. Sand Cost Elongation per cent minimum (cast on)on standard test piece C 8% 15%

8. Ultimate tensile Strength

9. Sand cost Elongation percent minimum (separately cast) on standard test piece C 12% 15%

(v) The seats and discs of valves shall be made of non-corrodible metal*. *By non-corrodible metal is meant specially resistant to corrosion and /or erosion.

Cast Iron shall not be used for:-

(i) Temperatures above 220°C.

(ii) Pressure exceeding 13 bar gauge.

(iii) Valves exceeding 200 mm nominal bore which are connected directly to the boiler.

(iv) Wedge or double disc gate valves which are connected directly to the boiler.

(v) Boiler blow down fittings.

Explanation- The expression connected directly to the boilers covers the valve which cannot itself be isolated from the boiler.

Note: Spheroidal iron may be used for temperature not exceeding 282°C and pressure not exceeding

17.58 kg/cm2(g) provided it complies with the following requirements in respect of the physical properties.

(a) 0.2% proof stress 250 kg/mm2;

(b) Minimum elongation percent 12.

(3) Copper alloy shall not be used for temperatures above 225°C, where the valves are connected directly to the boiler. In the case of valves not connected directly to the boiler this temperature limitation may be increased to 260°C.

PART-III

184. Castings.─ (1)All castings shall be smooth, sound and free from flaws or other injurious defects. Variations in thickness at any part shall be gradual and substantial fillets shall be provided.

(2) The body of a boiler mounting shall be connected to the boiler flange by a strong and stiff neck. In no case, the thickness of the neck of bronze mounting shall be less than 5 mm for sizes up to and including 19 mm bore or 6 mm size over 19 mm bore.

185. Packing of Cocks.─(1)The bodies of all cocks except those for pressure gauges shall be packed with any non-asbestos heat resisting material and shall be of substantial design and those over 25 mm bore shall have special provision, other than the gland for securing the plug.

(2) Water gauge column cocks may be any non-asbestos heat resisting material packed or solid plug type.

186. Covers and Spindles.─(1)Valves of 38 mm bore and above directly connected to boilers, and other valves of 63 mm bore and over where fitted with a cover shall have such cover secured by bolts or studs and the screwed portion of the spindle shall be outside the stuffing box. All valves for superheated steam shall be fitted with an external screwed spindle.

(2) (a) Where the covers of valves are secured by studs shall be screwed into the body with full thread for a length of at least one diameter.

(b) The stud holes shall not penetrate into the pressure space of any casting.

(3) Valves with screwed covers shall be fitted with suitable locking device to prevent rotation of the cover.

187. Direction of Operation.─Valves shall be clearly marked to indicate the direction of flow. Valves directly connected to boilers shall be fitted with indicators to clearly show whether the valve is closed or open and all valves shall open by anti-clockwise rotation.

188. Moving Parts.─ The valves and spindles shall be efficiently guided and means shall be provided in every case to prevent them leaving their guides. The working parts shall have sufficient clearance to ensure freedom of movement under all conditions of service.

189. Flanges.─(1) Flanges shall be in accordance with the tables for the appropriate pressure (Appendix-F).

(2) In the case of safety valves the drilling, thickness and bolting of inlet flanges shall be in accordance with tables for the appropriate pressure and material and for the diameter of flange adopted. In the case of outlet flanges the pressure taken shall be the pressure on the outlet side of the valve.

(3) For the purpose of determining particulars of flanges for blow down and scum pipe fittings, the design pressure of the fittings shall be assumed to be design pressure of the boiler.

(4) All flanges shall be machined on the face, spot faced, or machined at the back, and the bolt holes shall be drilled.

190. Valve Seatings.─ Where separate seats are fitted, they shall be efficiently secured and means shall be provided in every case to prevent the valves leaving their guides.

191. Chests and fittings in General.─(1) (a) All chests and fittings shall be smooth, sound and free from flaws, cracks or other injurious defects and after completion, the chests shall be hydraulically tested at minimum one and half times the maximum allowable working pressure of the valve for which they are intended.

(b) Hydraulic test shall be made with water which may contain a corrosion inhibiter, with kerosene or with other suitable liquid provided its viscosity is not greater than that of water at a test temperature not above 52°C.

(2) (a) (i) Valves may be fabricated from seamless steel pipes for pressures not exceeding 17.6 kg/cm2(g) and temperatures not exceeding 426°C.The welding shall conform to regulation 97 and valve chest shall be stress relieved after fabrication.

(ii) Valves meant for use in pipe work may also be fabricated by welding from seamless steel pipes but no restriction as above regarding pressure and temperature shall apply to them provided the welding complies with all the relevant requirements of fusion welding such as stress relieving and radiographic inspection of the weld as prescribed in Chapter-4 of these regulations.

(b) The maximum allowable working pressure of the chests shall be determined by equation (7/3), where the term '2 fE' shall be substituted by the term '1.8 x f'.

(3) The maximum allowable working pressure and the thickness of the bronze and cast Iron valve chests shall be determined by the following formulae, subject to the minimum thickness as specified in sub-regulation

(2) of regulation 184.

For Bronze Chests C = 1.5 mm For Cast Iron Chests C = 5 mm

(5) (a) The number of valves and fittings which shall be made available to the Competent Person (excluding mechanical tests) shall be, following, namely:Size of valves and fittings Number Table 5/1A Serial No.

(b) If the Inspecting Authority is satisfied that the manufacturer has adequate facilities for testing and Inspection of valves intended for service pressure exceeding 10.5 kg/cm2(g) or temperature exceeding 204°C and actually tests each fitting at his works, the Inspecting Authority may, at his discretion, undertake test on a sample basis.

For Cast Steel Chest C = 5 mm.

For forged or stainless steel chest C = 2.5 mm.

PART-IV

192. Special requirements for safety valves.─ Safety valves, ordinary, high lift, and full lift, shall be so constructed that breakage of any part shall not obstruct the free and full discharge of steam from the boiler.

193.ordinary Lift Safety Valve.─ (1) A safety valve in which the valve head lifts automatically a distance of at least D/24 with an over-pressure not exceeding 10% of the set pressure.

(2) There shall not be any mechanical stop which would prevent the valve head from being lifted a distance of at least D/8. D is the minimum bore of the body of seat.

194.High Lift Safety Valve.─ (1) A safety valve in which the valve head lifts automatically a distance of at least D/12 with an over-pressure not exceeding 10% of the set pressure.

(2) There shall not be any mechanical stop which would prevent the valve head from being lifted a distance of at least D/8. D is the minimum bore of the body seat.

195. Full Lift Safety Valve.─ (1) A safety valve in which the valve head lifts automatically a distance such that the area of discharge which limits the flow through the valve is between 100% and 80% of the minimum area at any section at or below the body seat.

(2) This lift is achieved by a rapid opening within an over pressure not exceeding 5% of the set pressure.

196.Discharge Capacity of Safety Valves.─(1)The rated discharge capacity of a safety valve which discharges saturated steam shall be calculated using the following equation;

E = CAP Equation (5/7) Where, E = is the rated discharge capacity of saturated steam (kg/h.) P = is the highest pressure of any safety valve mounted on the boiler (bar absolute), A = is the area (mm2).

For the ordinary lift and high lift safety valve, "A" is the area of the minimum bore of the body seat. For full lift safety valve, "A" is the area of discharge described regulation 195 and its value can only be obtained from the safety valve manufacturers. "C" is a constant taken from the following table, appropriate to the type of the valve or as established by tests carried out in accordance with Appendix-G Table 5/2 Serial No.

Type of valve Value of the constant 'C'

1.

Ordinary lift

0.05

2.

High lift

0.10

3.

Full lift

0.24

(3) The rated discharge capacity of a safety valve which discharges superheated steam shall be calculated using the following equation :E Es= ———————Equation (5/8) 1 + 2.7 Ts ———Where, Es = is the rated discharge capacity of superheated steam (kg/h), E = is the rated discharge capacity of saturated steam calculated using equation (5/7) (kg/h), Ts = is the degree of superheat (°C) Equation (5/8) may be used to determine the rated discharge capacity of safety valves at supercritical steam pressure. In these cases, the saturation temperature of the steam shall be taken as 375°C.

(3) (a) The Total peak load evaporation of a boiler shall be calculated on the basis of evaporation not less than 30 kg/hr/m2 of heating surface (exclusive of super heater and non-steaming economiser).

(b) In the case of Waste Heat Boilers, however, when the evaporation per square meter of the heating surface is certified by the manufacturers to be less than 30 kg. The minimum number of safety valves required may be calculated on the basis of the actual maximum evaporation of the boiler.

197. Over Pressure of Safety Valves.─ (1) The safety valves shall be so designed that they attain rated discharge capacity with the over pressure not greater than that given in regulation 193 to 195; provided that the safety valves which have a discharge area less than 80 per cent of the flow area, the over pressure at which the design lift is attained shall not exceed 10 percent of the set pressure.

(2) For safety valves having discharge area 80 per cent of more of the flow area, the over pressure at which the design lift is attained shall not exceed 5 per cent of set pressure.

198. Pressure Drop (Blow Down).─ (1) The safety valves shall be reset at a pressure at least 2.5% below, but not more than 5% below the safety valves set pressure.

(2) The 5% limit shall be increased to 6% for set pressures up to 25 kg/cm2 and 10% for valves having a body seat bore less than 32 mm.

199. Mountings of Safety Valves.─(1)(a)Safety valves shall be mounted without any intervening valve on pads or branches used for no other purpose. The axis of the valve shall be vertical.

(b) The cross – sectional area of the bore of each pad or branch shall be at least equal to the area of the bore at the inlet of the safety valve, or where two or more safety valves are mounted on the same pad or branch, at least equal to the sum of the areas of the inlet bores of all the safety valves.

(2) (a) Branches shall be as short as possible so as not to impair proper action of the safety valves or impose any undue stress on the branches at their point of attachment to the boiler.

(b) Nothing shall obstruct free flow to the safety valve. Branches, particularly when full lift safety valves are mounted on them, should be radiused at the inlet.

(c) The inlet and outlet flanges shall be drilled in accordance with the approximate table in Appendix-F for the diameters of flanges adopted.

200. Openings in Shell.─(1) No accessories other than those integral with the Safety Valve shall obstruct the openings in the boiler shell.

(2) Discharging steam shall have direct access to the safety valve without flowing through internal pipes.

201. Discharge Passage.─(1) (a) The safety valve discharge pipes shall comply with the requirements of subregulations (2) , (3) and (4) of this regulation.

(b) The discharge pipe shall be as short and straight as possible and be fitted with open drain to prevent accumulation of water in the pipe. Suitable arrangement shall be provided in the discharge pipe system so that the discharge can readily be heard by the Boiler Attendant.

(2) Where a waste steam–pipe is fitted the pipe and the passage leading to it shall have a cross-sectional area not less than the minimum combined area of the safety valves required by regulation 196.

(3) For full lift valves, the area of waste steam pipe and passage leading to it shall have a cross-sectional area not less than twice "A", or such area above this minimum as maybe required for valves having a higher approved constant, where "A", "E" and "P" are as defined in regulation 196.

(4) (a) The area of the discharge pipe from an economiser safety relief valve shall be at least twice the area of the valve seating.

(b) Where the discharge from several economiser safety relief valves is connected to the main discharge pipe, the diameter of the main discharge pipe shall be designed to prevent accumulation of pressure due to the formation of steam under the particular conditions of temperature and pressure which may be applicable.

202. Drainage.─(1) For each enclosed safety valve chest, a means of draining shall be provided.

(2) The drain pipe shall be laid with a continuous downward gradient clear of the boiler to a place where the discharge is visible and cannot do injury to any person.

203. Moving Parts.─(1) The valves and spindles shall be efficiently guided and means shall be provided in every case to prevent their lifting out of their guides.

(2) The working parts shall have sufficient clearance to ensure freedom of movement under all conditions of service. The spindle shall not be fitted with a stuffing box.

204. Bearings for Levers.─

(1) The bearings of the levers of lever valve shall be so designed as to allow free working of the valve under all condition of service.

(2) Where the lever is mounted on pin bearings, the holes in the lever shall be bushed with non-corrodible metal, or the pins shall be of non-corrodible metal.

205. Attachments of Weights and Springs.─(1) In a lever and weight safety valve, the weight shall be in one piece and attached to the lever in such a way that the safety valve cannot be over loaded.

(2) (a) In the case of spring loaded safety valves, washers or ferrules shall be fitted under the adjusting screws so that the valves cannot be over loaded when under the steam. Interference with load on the spring after the safety valve has been adjusted shall be prevented by the fitting of a ferrule under the adjusting screw collar, or by using a lock nut on the adjusting screw which shall be further safe-guarded by means of a padlock or other suitable device.

(b) Where springs are in tension, links or the suitable stops shall be fitted to prevent the spring being extended a greater amount than that corresponding to a valve lift of D/4.

Where, D = diameter of valve seating.

206. Easing Gear.─ Safety valves shall be so arranged that they can be eased off seats when under pressure and the easing lever shall positively lift the valve.

207. Lift.─ Safety valves shall be capable of being lifted a distance such that the area of the discharge edge shall not be less than the minimum aggregate area "A" in regulation 196.

208. High and Low Water Alarms.─ High and low water alarms shall be adjusted so that the alarm is sounded with the water level visible in the gauges.

209. Final Settings.─ Before leaving the maker's works, each safety valve shall be adjusted to blow-off at the specified pressure.

210. Adjustment of Safety Valve Set Pressure.─ The set pressure of a safety valve may be adjusted using air or other gas as the test medium provided that the safety valve has been subjected previously to hydraulic test in accordance with the requirement of sub-regulation(1) of regulation 191.

211.Change of Safety Valve Set Pressure.─ The spring in a safety valve shall not be reset for any pressure more than 5% above or below that for which the valve is marked and the set pressure may be changed to the satisfaction of registering authority.

212. Materials.─(1)(a)The springs shall be manufactured from steel made by the Acid Open Hearth or Electric Process and the Chemical composition shall be within the following limits:

(b) The manufacturers shall supply an analysis of each cast when required by the Inspecting Authority to do so.

Should independent analysis be required, these shall be made at the rate of one per cent.

(c) All springs shall be formed hot and suitably hardened and tempered.

(d) All the springs above 16 mm bar diameter shall be formed hot and the springs below 16 mm bar diameter shall be formed hot or cold. The springs shall be suitably hardened and tempered.

(2) Torsion bars shall be manufactured from steel which shall have a yield point of not less than 141 kg/mm2.

Note: Special steels developed by individual manufacturers may be accepted by the Chief Inspector of Boilers at their discretion.

213. Dimensions.─(1) For ordinary lift valves, the compression or extension of safety valve springs required to load the valves to the set pressure shall not be less than one quarter of the diameter of the valve due consideration being given in the case of spring loaded level safety valves to the ratio of leverage.

(2) The proportion of unloaded length to external diameter of the spring shall not exceed 4:1.

(3) These requirements do not apply to full lift and high lift safety valves.

214. Determination of Maximum Allowable Working Pressure.─(1)The maximum allowable working pressure to be allowed for steel springs of round, square or rectangular section shall be determined from the following formulae for springs in extension.

(a) Round section —10,000 πd3 P = Equation (5/9) DACK

(b) Square Section —33,333d3 P = Equation (5/10) DACK

(c) Rectangular section —160,000 B2 H2 P = Equation (5/11) DACK (3B + 1.8H) Note: For Springs in Compression, the maximum allowable working pressure calculated by the above equation may be increased by 25 per cent.

Where, (all dimensions in inches):4 (D – 1)/d

0.615 K = + Equation (5/12) D D (In case of rectangular section substitute B for d) P = maximum allowable working pressure in lb. Square inch (set pressure) A = Loading area of valve, D = diameter of round or side of square steel, B = Breadth of wire (radial to spring axis).

H = Depth of wire (parallel to spring axis).

D = Mean diameter of coil, L1 + L2 C = Constant = ————Equation (5/13) L1 L1 = Initial compression or extension of the spring to give the required loading (P x A), L2 = the further compression or extension of the spring to give the lift as defined in regulations 193 to 195 and 207.

EXAMPLES:

C = 2 where compression or extension of spring to give the required loading is ¼ diameter of valve.

C =

1.5 where compression or extension of spring to give the required loading is ½ diameter of valve.

C =

1.25 where compression or extension of spring to give the required loading is full diameter of valve.

Note:

The above formulae are based on the maximum allowable safe stress of 5,624 kg/cm2(g) on the section of the springs under extension and 7,030 kg/cm2(g) where they are in compression.

The above values of "C" apply only to the case of "full-lift" Safety Valves, where L2 is equal D/4 in case of "high-lift" and "ordinary lift" Safety Valves, respective values of "C" may be worked out by using appropriate values of L2.

(2) In the case of torsion bars, the maximum shear stress as calculated from the following formula shall not TDC fs = ———- Equation (5/14) 2J Where, fs = Shear stress T = Torque at set pressure, D = Outside diameter of the bar, J = Polar moment of inertia of the section the bar, and Angle of twist at full lift valve C = ———————————————————Angle of twist at set pressure (as furnished by the manufacturer).

The dimensions of sleeve transmitting the torque shall be of substantial proportions for the material used.

215. Test.─(1)The permanent set in the spring (defined as the difference between free height and height measured ten minutes after the spring has been compressed solid three additional times, after pre-setting at room temperature) shall not exceed 0.5% the free height.

(2) Torsion bars shall not show a permanent distortion after being twisted to an angle fifty per cent in excess the working angle.

216. Extension Springs.─ Extension springs shall be made from round section wire.

217. Number of Effective Coils.─ The number of effective or free coils in a compression or extension spring shall be determined from the following formulae:

(i) For Round or Square wire KCd4 N = Equation (5/15) SD3

(ii) For Rectangular Wire 66B3H3K N = Equation (5/16) (B2 + H2) SD2 Where, N = Number of effective coils, K = Compression or tension in inches at set pressure, C = 22 for round, 30 for square steel, d = diameter or side of square steel in 16th of an inch, S = load on spring in lbs. at blow off pressure, D = mean diameter of coil in inches, B= breadth of wire in 16th of an inch, H= Depth of wire in 16th of an inch.

218. Spacing of Coils.─ The space between the coils when the valve is lifted to 1/4th of its diameter, shall be not less than 1.6 mm for full lift valves and 0.8 mm for ordinary and high lift valves.

219.Finishing of Ends.─Compression springs shall have their ends ground flat and smooth and at right angles to the axis of the springs over the full circumference so that when placed on end on a horizontal plane the springs will stand perpendicular.

PART-V

220.Lever valves.─ Where the valve is operated by means of a lever, whether hand or mechanically controlled, the lever shall be of ample strength and the bearings shall be so designed as to allow free working of the valve under all conditions of service.

221. Steam stop valve.─(1) (a) The stop valve connecting the boiler or super heater to the steam–delivery pipe shall be located as near the outlet from the final superheater header as is convenient and practicable or in case of boilers without super-heaters as close as practicable to the drum.

(b) Where two or more boilers are connected with a steam receiver or any other vessel, a steam stop valve shall be fitted between the boiler and such receiver or vessel.

(2) (a)Steam stop valves shall be attached direct to the boiler shell or to suitable pads or stand blocks and the neck or the valve chest shall be reasonably short and of strong construction.

(b) The steam stop valve may also be attached to a stand pipe, a connecting pipe or the super-heater header.

(c) The attachment may be made by bolted flanges or butt-welded joints. Such butt- welded joints shall be suitably stress relieved, where necessary:

Provided that in the case of a larger boiler in which it is proposed for the purpose of drainage or owing to obstruction to connect the steam–pipes to the steam stop valve at a higher level than would be obtained under this regulation, a vertical stand pipe not exceeding 5 diameters in height will be permitted between the stop valve and the boiler. Such stand pipes shall be of strong construction and of wrought iron, mild steel, or cast steel. The flanges of wrought or mild steel pipes shall be welded to the pipes, and there shall be no branch on the stand pipe for any other connection:

Provided also that in the case of large boiler in which it is desired to fit a tee piece for the purpose of providing a branch connection between the stop valve and the boiler, such a tee piece may be so fitted, the tee piece shall be of strong construction, of wrought iron, mild steel, or cast steel and shall not exceed 2½ diameters in height and a stop valve shall be fitted direct to each tee piece branch.

(3) When two or more boilers are connected to a common steam main and there are no combined stop and isolating valves fitted to them an automatic isolating valve shall be compulsory. In the case of boilers fitted with welded pipes, fitting of an independent automatic isolating valve shall be compulsory.

PART -VI

222. Blow-down cock or valve and pipes.─ (1) The blow-down cock or valve shall be of substantial construction.

(2) The waste pipe attached to the cock or valve shall not be bound fast in arch or brick work and shall discharge at a point where there is no danger of injury to any person.

(3) These pipes shall not be connected to a pipe common to another boiler.

(4) The continuous blow-downs may, however be connected to a common header discharging freely to an adequately vented tank or sump.

(5) The headers shall be of sufficiently large cross- section and the blow- off tank shall be provided with a vent pipe free from valves and of sufficient size to prevent accumulation of pressure. If, however, the tank is intended for collecting flash steam, a safety valve of adequate capacity shall be provided.

(6) The tank shall be so located that all parts will be accessible for inspection. 223 Blow-Down mountings.─(1) Each boiler shall be fitted with a suitable blow-down valve or cock placed at, or as near as practicable, to the lowest point of the boiler.

(a) For locomotive, vertical and marine type boilers, the valve or cock shall be attached direct to the boiler or to a suitable pad or stand pipe.

(b) For water tube boilers the valve or cock shall be outside the brick work with a substantial steel pipe between it and the mud box.

For Cornish and Lancashire boilers, the cocks or valve may be attached to a cast steel elbow pipe of substantial section bolted to a suitable stand pipe or pad welded to the boiler. But cast iron elbow pipes shall not be permitted.

224.Blow-Down Valve or Cock.─(1) Each valve or cock shall be fitted with a device which shall clearly indicate its open and closed positions, and any key or similar device for operating the valve or cock shall be such that it cannot be removed unless the valve or cock is fully closed.

(2) The locking feather shall be secured by welding.

(3) Cocks fitted with taper plugs shall be of the bolted cover type with separate packing glands.

PART -VII

225.Water Gauges.─(1)Every boiler shall have two means of indicating the water in it, out of which one shall be conventional gauge glass:

Provided that in the case of boiler drums below 91 cm diameter where there is difficulty in fitting two water gauges, two test cocks and a glass water gauge shall be fitted.

(a) The lowest visible part of the glass of the water gauge and lower test cocks shall be fixed at safe working level.

(b) For locomotive type and vertical boilers this shall not be less than 50 mm above the highest part of the firebox roof plate. Minimum length of the visible portion of the gauge glasses shall be 200 mm.

(c) The length may be increased depending upon the capacity of the boiler by the Inspecting Authority.

(a) Glass water gauges shall be so placed as to be easily seen and reached by the boiler attendant.

(b) The fittings of glass water gauges and test cocks shall be of substantial make with large passage ways through them to facilitate cleaning.

(c) The gauge cocks when open shall have their handles in a vertical direction and each handle at its junction with the plug shall be plainly marked with a deep line to indicate the direction of the passage way through the plug.

(d) When detachable handle are provided arrangement shall be made to prevent incorrect fitting of the handles.

226. Drains.─ A drain cock or valve with a suitable discharge pipe shall be fitted to each water gauge.

227. Protectors.─(1) Where tubular glass water gauges are fitted, substantial protectors shall be provided.

(2) The glass shall be suitably treated to prevent splintering.

228. Glass Size.─Tubular water gauge glasses shall be not less than 13 mm and not more than 19 mm outside diameter.

229. Safety Devices.─ Water gauge glass mountings shall be fitted with self-closing devices in the bottom arms and it is recommended that a similar device may be fitted in the top arm.

230. Gauge Columns.─ Where the gauges are mounted on a column, there shall be no connecting passage between the top and bottom arms of the column unless valves or cocks are fitted between the columns and the boiler.

231. Isolating Cocks.─ Where isolating cocks or valves connecting water gauge pipes to the boiler are fitted, they shall be not less than 25 mm bore and of the bolted cover type with separate packing gland.

PART -VIII

232. Pressure Gauges Dials.─ (1) For pressure upto35 kg/cm2 (g), pressure gauge dials shall be graduated in kg/cm2(g) from zero to twice the pressure as nearly as may be practicable.

(2) For pressure exceeding 35 kg/cm2(g),the range of the graduation shall be from zero to one and a half times the maximum allowable working pressure, as nearly as may be practicable, but in no case shall the maximum graduation on the gauge be less than 70 kg/cm2 (g).

(3) The scale on the dial shall be clearly and permanently marked in kg/cm2 (g).

(4) The maximum allowable working pressure shall be marked in red on the dial of each pressure gauge.

(5) (a) Where the gauge is compensated for a head of water between the gauge and the boiler connection, the amount of such compensation shall be marked on the dial. Pressure gauge shall be calibrated within an accuracy of +1 percent.

(b) Boiler pressure gauges shall not be less than 150 mm in diameter.

(6) The travel of the pointer of the dial gauge shall not exceed 325°.

233. Connections.─ All pressure gauges shall be fitted with a syphon pipe and a cock or valve integral with, or adjacent to, the gauge in such manner that the gauge may be shut off and removed whilst the boiler is under steam.

234. Gauge Cocks.─ The handles of the gauge cocks shall be parallel to the pipes in which they are located when the cocks are open and marks shall be cut on the shank of the cock indicating open position.

235.Test Connections- Competent Person's Pressure Gauge Attachment.─ (1)Every boiler shall be fitted with a valve or cock carrying in a vertical position a receiving screw for attachment of the Competent Person's pressure gauge.

(2) The receiving socket shall be tapped for M/20 x 1.5 metric threads and shall be fitted with an easily removable cap.

PART -IX

236. Fusible Plugs.─(1)Fusible plugs shall be of sufficient height and fitted in such a position as to give early protection to all parts of the boiler liable to damage by the direct application of furnace heat in the event of shortness of water.

Note: For example - In Lancashire boilers the fusible plugs shall be in the crowns of the furnaces from 305 mm to 457 mm in front of the brick work fire bridge.

(a) On boilers having wet back reversing chamber, a minimum of two fusible plugs shall be fitted; one on the crown of the reversing chamber and the other on one of the tube plates. On boilers not having wet back reversing chamber, at least one fusible plug shall be provided on the front end tube plate.

(b) The plug shall be fitted in such a way that in the event of fusion of the plug, the pressure inside the boiler shall push the pellets out.

237.Type.─ (1) Fusible plugs shall consist of an outer body with a central conical passage.

(2) The smallest part to be not greater than 13 mm for plugs suitable for pressures up to 7 kg/cm2(g) and not greater than 10 mm for plugs for pressures exceeding 7 kg/cm2 (g).

(3) The passage shall be closed by a plug secured by an annual lining of fusible alloy so that the plug may drop clear if the lining melts.

(4) The portion of the body carrying the fusible metal shall preferably be detachable from the base to allow of easy replacement without removing the whole fitting from the boiler.

238. Material.─(1) The non-fusible portions of the plug shall be of bronze except where the nature of the boiler water precludes the use of non-ferrous material.

(2) The fusible metal shall be of an alloy melting readily at a temperature not less than 66°C in excess of the saturated steam temperature at the design pressure of the boiler.

239. Attachment to Boiler.─ (1) Wherever practicable, fusible plugs shall be screwed into the boiler plates from the water side.

(2) The screwed portion shall have threads of Whitworth form not more than 11 per 25 mm.

PART -X

240.Feed Valves.─ (1) Feed check valves shall be of the non-return type.

(2) Where the valve is not of screw-down non-return type, a separate screw-down valve shall be provided.

(3) The discharge from the feed check valve or from the internal feed pipe (if provided) shall be above low water level and in the case of Lancashire and Cornish boilers, shall be at least 152 cm beyond the fire bridge:

Provided that the aforesaid restriction regarding the position of feed discharge level shall not apply in the case of water tube boilers.

241. Operating Position.─ Feed check valves or regulating valves shall be so arranged as to enable them to be satisfactorily operated from the boiler floor.

242. Requirements as to Feed Apparatus in certain cases.─ In the case of a battery of boilers connected to a common feed range, the requirements ofregulation181 in respect of the number of feed apparatus shall be considered to have been fulfilled provided a total supply of feed water, not less than the combined maximum continuous rating of all active boilers can be maintained even if any one of the sources of power supply fails.

CHAPTER -6 BOILER AND SUPERHEATER TUBES, HEADERS AND OTHER PARTS PART-I

(b) a procedure test;.

(iii) Where the amount of thinning at any location around the bend extrados on cold formed bends exceeds 25% of the actual thickness of the tube on the straight the bends shall be suitably heat treated. The actual thickness of the same plans as the line of the extrados at each end of the bend.

(c) The departure from circularity at the bend apex shall not exceed 10% for bends performed in a single bending operation and 15% for bends which are hot pressed after the primary, bending operation.

PART-II

CHAPTER -7 PIPES AND FITTINGS

249. Pipes.─(1)(a) Steam-pipes may be of carbon steel, cast steel, alloy steel and nickel alloys.

(b) Pipes may be solid drawn (cold or hot finished), butt welded or electric resistance welded.

(2) Where, material used is in conformity with the code of the country of manufacture and in accordance with provisions of these regulations, the permissible stress figures specified in the code at different temperatures may be accepted in lieu of figures computed from the data required to be furnished under regulation 256 in any of the following cases, namely:-

(a) Where a certificate is furnished from the Inspecting Authority to the effect that the steel complies with requirements of the steel grade (to be specified by the Inspecting Authority) and that the permissible stress for the working condition as allowed for in the code of the country of manufacture falls within the permissible limits under these regulations.

(b) Where the basis upon which these stresses have been arrived at is made available and such basis is not found as to give rise to stresses higher than those permissible under the regulations.

(c) Where pipes have to be fabricated by longitudinal fusion butt electric arc welding of plates rolled to shape, the limits provided for butt welded pipes in Table 7/4 under regulation 255 shall not apply.

(d) The requirements other than those relating to the minimum thickness of shell for fusion butt welding provided in Chapter-12 shall be applicable to such pipes.

(3) Notwithstanding anything contained in Chapter-7, the hydraulic test for pipes in maker's premises may be dispensed with by the Inspecting Authority:

Provided that these pipes have been fully tested by non-destructive examination by radiographic or ultrasonic techniques:

Provided further that in the case of pipes having internal diameter 1000 mm and more, the plates used for the manufacture of pipes and the long seam welds of pipes are fully tested by non-destructive examination like ultrasonic or radiographic testing and the soundness of pipes both in the parent material and in the weld is established in the maker's premises; and the whole component of the pipes, after completion of any further fabrication and erection at site, are hydraulically tested at a pressure not less than 1.5 times the maximum allowable working pressure of the piping system:

Provided also that in the event of detection of any defect after conducting hydraulic test of these pipes at site, it shall be the responsibility of the manufacturer to repair or replace the defective pipes, as may be deemed necessary by the Inspecting Authority.

(4) Electric fusion welded pipes in which the butt-welds are fully radiographed or ultrasonically tested need not be hydraulically tested in the shops provided the pipe system as a whole is hydraulically tested at site to the requisite test pressure in accordance withregulation292.

(5) In the case of fusion welded pipes, test plates to represent all welded seams shall be attached at each end of longitudinal seam and tested in accordance with the Chapter -12 provided that one test plate may represent a lot of pipes up to 60 m in length and of the same grade of material and same thickness of the pipe subject to the same heat treatment.

PART-I

250.Material of steel pipes.─(1) (a) The pipes shall be made from steel made by an Open Hearth or Electric Process or by any of the Oxygen Processes.

(b) Hot finished seamless pipes may, however, be made of Bessemer Steel.

(c) Bessemer Steel shall not be used for pressures exceeding 21 kg/cm² or temperatures exceeding 260°C and If the Bessemer process is used, the steel shall be made by a manufacturer approved by Inspecting Authority.

(d) When pipe is used for temperatures exceeding 399°C the steel shall be of non-segregated or fully killed type.

(2) Carbon and alloy steel pipes shall not be used for design temperatures exceeding those given in Table 2/1 of regulation30.

(3) For design temperature over 427°C, precaution shall be taken to ensure that the surface condition of the pipe is suitable for such requirements.

(4) (a) The material from which seamless pipes are made, shall conform to appropriate specifications of pipes as in regulation 49 and electric resistance welded pipes are made, shall conform to the appropriate specification of tubes as in regulation 30.

(b) The materials from which butt welded pipes are to be made shall conform to the requirements of Table 7/3 under regulation 253.

251. Condition of pipes.─(1)All pipes shall be commercially straight, free from longitudinal seaming, grooving, blistering or other injuries surface marks and the ends of the pipes shall be cut square.

The pipes shall be made within the limits of tolerances as given below:Table 7/1 Serial No.

Class Tolerance on Outside diameter Nominal thickness Up to and including 51 mm Over 51 mm As per regulation 49.

1.

Seamless or Electric Resistance Welded(*E.R.W)

2.

Butt welded ± 1% ± 1% + no limitation - 12.5% PART-II

252. Number of sets of mechanical tests .─ (1) The number of pipes on which mechanical tests are to be performed shall be as follows:Table 7/2 Seamless pipes As per regulation 49 Welded pipes 2 per cent of the lengths of pipes as made.

(2) In the case of pipes for designed temperatures over 454°C, every pipe over 114 mm outside diameter shall be tested.

253. Tests.─ (1) For pipes up to and including 102 mm Nominal bore, flattening test shall be as per requirements of Chapter-2as under,-

(a) For cold drawn seamless construction – Sub-regulation (4) of regulation 48.

(b) For hot finished seamless construction – Sub-regulation (10) of regulation 49.

(c) For Electric resistance welded pipes – As per relevant material specifications.

For pipes where the ratio between the wall thickness and the diameter would not permit the maintenance of the distance between the flat surfaces specified in the above regulation, this shall be limited to 2/3rdofthe bore of the pipe.

(2) For cold Bend Test for pipes over 102 mm Nominal bore, a strip not less than 38 mm wide cut circumferentially from one end of each selected pipe shall when cold withstand, without showing either crack or flaw being doubled over in the direction of original curvature round a bar, the diameter of the bar being:

For pipes up to and including 10 mm thick 3 Times the thickness For pipes over 10 mm thick 4 Times the thickness

(3) For Bend Test on the Weld; a strip not less than 38 mm wide cut circumferentially from one end of each selected pipe with the weld near the middle of the strip, shall when cold withstand, without showing either crack or flaw, being doubled over in the direction of original curvature round a bar, the diameter of the bar being equal to eight times the thickness of the test piece, the weld being placed at the point of maximum bending.

In cases where the outside diameter of the pipe is less than 8 times the wall thickness, the diameter of the former shall be equal to 4 or 2/3rd of the nominal bore of the pipe, whichever is less.

(4) Additional Tests; In case a pipe selected for testing fail in any one or more of the tests specified above, two further tests of the same kind may be made from the same or another pipes from the same batch. In case either of these further tests fail, the pipes represented may be reheated and then re-tested. If the repeat tests are satisfactory, the pipes shall be accepted provided they comply with other requirements, but if failure again occurs, the pipes which the test pieces represent shall be rejected.

(5) Tensile Test shall be as per Chapter-2.

(6) Test for Valves and Fittings shall be as per sub-regulation (5) of regulation 191.

254. Method of manufacture, heat treatment and marking.─ (1) On completion of any work which involves heating, whether for hot bending of the pipe or for any other similar purpose, the pipe shall be carefully annealed.

(2) (a) The cold drawn seamless carbon steel pipes and welded carbon steel pipes shall be supplied in normalised condition.

(b) Hot finished seamless carbon steel pipes shall be supplied in hot finished condition or in normalised condition at the option of the manufacturer.

(c) The seamless alloy steel pipe either cold finished or hot finished and welded pipes shall be supplied in normalised and tempered condition.

(d) By agreement between the users and the manufacturer, the pipes may be delivered in a condition other than normalised and tempered condition in which case they shall be suitable for subsequent manipulation and the user shall be informed of the heat-treatment necessary to give required properties.

(3) (a) (i) Pipe bending and forming (reducing or swaging) operation shall be done by any hot or cold method and to any radius which shall result in a surface free from cracks, buckles or other defects as determined by the method of inspection specified in the design.

(ii) A post bending or post forming heat treatment for carbon steel material is required with a nominal wall thickness in excess of 19 mm unless the bending or forming operation are performed and completed at normalizing temperatures, where no further heat treatment is required.

(iii) The heat treatment where required, shall be normalised or stress relieved, as the case may be.

(b) A post bending or post forming heat treatment shall be required for all ferritic alloy steel material with nominal pipe size 100 mm and larger or with a nominal thickness 13 mm or greater.

(c) (i) If hot bending or forming is performed the material shall receive normalize and temper or tempering heat-treatment as required by the design.

(ii) For cold operation, tempering heat-treatment shall be applied.

(4) Marking shall be carried out in accordance with regulation 329.

255. Steam pipes and fittings.─ The pressure and temperature limits within which pipes, tees, branches, etc., are to be used, shall be in accordance with Table 7/4as given below:

256. Steel pipes.─ The maximum allowable working pressure allowed on steel pipes shall be determined as per the following formula: -

(a) Where the outside diameter is the basis for calculation. 2 f E (t – C) P = ——————Equation (7/1) D – t + C Where, t = minimum thickness of pipe (mm), P f = allowable stress as provided under regulation 170(kg/cm2), D = outside diameter of pipe (mm), E = efficiency factor, = 1.0 for seamless and for electric resistance welded steel pipes and for electric fusion welded steel pipes comply with the requirements of Chapter-2 in which the weld is fully radiographed or ultrasonically tested, = 0.95 for electric fusion welded steel pipes complying with the requirements of Chapter-2, = 0.90 for welded steel pipes for values of t up to and including 22 mm, = 0.85 for welded steel pipes for values of t over 22 mm and up to and including 29 mm, = 0.80 for welded steel pipes for values of t over 29 mm, C = 0.75 mm.

The minimum thickness of pipe required shall be as per the following formula:

P x D t = (2 x f x E) + P + C Equation (7/2) Where, t = calculated minimum required thickness of pipe (mm), P f = allowable stress as provided under regulation 170(kg/cm2), D = outside diameter of pipe (mm), E = Ligament efficiency as per regulation 171, C = 0.75 mm.

The minimum thickness of pipe required shall be as per the following formula:

P x d t = (2 x f x E) - P + C Equation (7/4) Where, t = calculated minimum required thickness of pipe (mm), P = maximum allowable working pressure in kg/cm2 (g), f = Allowable stress as provided under regulation 170 (kg/cm2), d = inside diameter of pipe (mm), E = Ligament efficiency as per regulation 171, C = 0.75 mm.

257. Cast steel pipes.─ (1) The material shall comply with regulations 50 to57.

(2) The maximum allowable working pressure allowed on cast steel pipes shall be as per the following formula: - 2S (t – 0.15D – C) P = ———————————
