PART-III

258. Flanges of carbon steel pipes.─(1)The material for carbon steel flanges, where forged, cast or cut from the plates (excluding branches forged integral with the pipes) shall be made of steel produced by an Open Hearth or Electric process or any of the Oxygen processes.

(2) Carbon steel flanges shall not be used for temperatures exceeding 454°C and such flanges shall be made without a weld and free from lamination or other defects secured by welding.

(3) Blank flanges shall be of mild steel or cast steel and shall be not less in thickness than the flanges to which they are attached. The material shall comply with the requirements specified in relevant regulations of chapter-2 or Chapter-4 of these regulations.

259. Flanges of alloy steel pipes.─ (1) (a) The material for alloy steel flanges where forged, cast or cut from plates (excluding branches forged integral with the pipes), shall be made from the steel produced by an Open Hearth or Electric process.

(b)Flanges shall be made without a weld and shall be free from lamination or other defects.

(c) The material of alloy steel flanges shall comply the provisions of regulation 30.

(d) The material for flanges shall be similar to that of the pipes to which they are to be attached.

(e) The flanges are to be so designed that the total stress induced in them does not exceed the maximum permissible stress as per criteria mentioned in regulation 170.

(2) Blank flanges of alloy steel shall be not less in thickness that the flanges to which they are to be attached and the material shall be similar to that of the flanges.

260.Non-ferrous flanges.─ (1) The material for non-ferrous flanges shall be of bronze.

(2) The chemical composition shall comply with the requirements of clause (iv) of sub-regulation (1) of regulation

183.

(3) When flanges are attached to copper pipes by brazing, they shall be secured in such additional way like forming a conical bore in the flange so that the resistance to withdrawal from the flange does not depend wholly on the brazing.

261. Screwed on flanges.─ (1) Where flanges are secured by screwing, the screw thread on the pipes and in the flanges shall be arranged to end at a point just inside the back of boss of the flange and after the flange has been screwed on, the pipe shall be expanded into the flange by a roller expander.

(2) Such screwed and expanded flanges may be used for steam for a maximum allowable working pressure of

31.5 kg/cm2 (g) and maximum temperature of 399 0C and for feed for a maximum allowable working pressure of

262. Loose flanges.─ (1) (a)Loose flanges may be used where the joints are made by metal faces integral with ends of the pipes and alternatively these joints may be welded or seal welded.

(b) The loose flanges shall conform to the table given in Appendix-F.

(2) Where the joint is formed by integral flanges, after machining these shall be not less in thickness than the designed thickness of the pipe as calculated by Equation (7/2)

263. Welded on flanges.─ (1) Where flanges are welded on, the welding shall be done by the oxy-acetylene or metal arc process; the latter with covered electrodes which shall comply with regulations 71 to76.

(2) (a)The proportion of the weld shall be as indicated in the Figs.7/10 to 7/34(part V of this chapter) for the following types as mentioned in the Table below,Type

1. Welding Neck Flange, Fig.7/10.

2. 'Face and Back' welded – on flange for metal arc welding. fig. 7/26.

3. 'Bore and Back' welded- on flange for metal arc welding. fig. 7/27 and 7/28.

4. 'Face and Fillet' welded- on flange for metal arc welding. fig. 7/29.

5. 'Bore and Fillet welded- on flange for metal arc welding. fig. 7/30 and 7/31.

6. 'Slip on' welded- on flange for metal arc welding. fig. 7/32.

7. 'Slip on' welded- on bossed flange for oxy-acetylene welding. fig. 7/33.

8. Socket welded flange, Fig.7/34.

The Flange shall not be a tight fit on the pipe.

(b) The maximum clearance between the bore of the flange and the outside diameter of the pipe shall be 3 mm at any point, and the sum of clearance diametrically opposite shall not exceed 5 mm.

Note:

All dimensions shown in the figures of types 1 to 7 relate to finished sizes.

The design conditions for each type are as follows:

Types 1, 2 and 3 flanges for all maximum allowable working pressure and temperature conditions.

Types 4 and 5 flanges for all pressure conditions up to and including 42 kg/cm2 (g) and design temperature not exceeding 399°C.

Types 6 and 7 flanges for all pressure conditions up to and including 17.6 kg/cm2 (g) and temperature not exceeding 399°C except that Bossed flanges of type 6 shall not be used on pipes over 304 mm in nominal bore, nor Bossed flanges of type 7 on pipes 152 mm in nominal bore.

Type 8 flanges for all maximum allowable working pressure and temperature conditions and this type shall be used only for pipes up to and including 51 mm nominal bore. These flanges shall not be used where severe erosion or corrosion is expected to occur.

Type 1,2,3,4 and 5 Carbon Steel types flanges welded on by the metal arc process shall have the welds stress relieved where the design depth of the weld recess is greater than 20 mm and all welds joining flanges to alloy steel pipes shall be stress relieved.

(4) Where type 1 flanges are attached by oxy-acetylene welding, the welds shall be normalised in accordance with the following requirements:

(a) Welds in mild steel pipes shall be normalised by heating the metal to a temperature between 900° and 950°C, the temperature then being maintained for not less than two minutes for pipes up to and including 102 mm bore, and for not less than five minutes for pipes over 102 mm bore.

(b) Welds in pipes made from the alloy steel shall be normalised by heating the metal to a temperature between 925° and 975°C, the temperature then being maintained for not less than two minutes for pipes up to and including 102 mm bore, and for not less than five minutes for pipes over 102 mm bore.

(c) The higher temperatures of between 950° and 975°C, shall be used for alloy steels having a carbon content up to and including 0.12 percent and the lower temperatures of between 925° and 950°C shall be used for alloy steels having a carbon content of more than 0.12 percent.

(d) After normalising the joint shall be covered with a suitable muff or asbestos cloth to ensure slow and even cooling.

(e) The normalising of welds in pipes up to 102 mm bore may be carried out with welding flames the temperature being judged with the aid of a colour card, or measured by means of one or more thermo-couples or an optical pyrometer.

(f) The normalising of welds in pipes over 102 mm bore shall preferably be carried out in a portable pipe normalising furnace or muffle, the temperature being measured by means of one or more thermo-couples or an optical pyrometer.

(g) The width of the zone to be heat treated to the specified temperature shall extend at least 13 mm on either side of the weld reinforcement.

(5) The procedure to be observed in the stress relieving of welds shall be in accordance with regulation 123 and stress relieving shall be carried out by any of the following methods:-

(a) (i) Local heating using a portable muffle furnace, induction coils or other suitable appliances and due care shall be taken to apply heat uniformly over the area to be treated.

(ii) The use of procedures not providing adequate control such as manual operated gas torches are not permissible.

(iii) The temperature shall be measured by the thermo-couples pinned, welded or otherwise suitably attached to the surface of the pipe and where necessary, protection from flame impingement shall be taken.

(b) The temperature of the joint during heating in a stationary industrial furnace shall be measured by thermo-couples appropriately placed within the furnace as to give a true measure of the joint temperature.

264. Standard flanges.─ The size and thickness of flanges and the number and size of their bolts shall be as shown in Appendix-F.

265. Joints.─ (1) These regulations provide for ordinary bolted flange joints and special types of joints may be used, subject to the approval of the Inspecting Authority or Chief Inspector, as the case may be.

(2) Welded butt joints of pipes or socket welded joints for pipes, valves and fitting shall be made by arc welding process and post weld heat treated effectively as per regulation 123.

(3) Heat treatment shall be carried out by one of the following methods:-

(a) (i) Local heating using a portable muffle induction coils, or other suitable heating appliance.

(ii) Due care shall be taken to apply heat uniformly over the area to be treated.

(iii) The use of procedures not providing adequate control such as manual operated gas torches are not permissible.

(iv) The temperature shall be maintained symmetrically over peripheral band of metal of a minimum width of three times the width of the butt welded preparation.

(v) The temperature shall be measured by thermo-couples pinned, welded or otherwise suitably attached to the surface of the pipe and, where necessary, protected from flame impingement.

(b) In case of heating in stationary furnace, temperature of the joint shall be measured by thermo-couples so disposed with in the furnace as to give a true measure of the joint temperature.

(c) In case of the materials made to the foreign codes or standards are accepted under clause (a) of subregulation (2) of regulation 3, the method of heat treatment shall conform to that specified in the codes or standards.

(4) Such Butt-welded joints conform to Fig. 7/10.

266.

Tests on completed welds on pipelines.─ (1) For tests on completed welds on pipelines, pipelines shall be divided into following two classes, namely:-

(a) Class-I: Pipelines for service conditions in which any one of the following limits are exceeded.

(i) Design temperature 218°C

(ii)

(iii) Feed water pressure

(b) Class II: Pipelines for service conditions in which none of the foregoing limits is exceeded.

(2) For identification of welds, the welders' identification mark shall be stamped without indentation, or stencilled adjacent to each completed weld.

267.

Tests for Class I pipelines.─(1) Where the completed pipelines are not tested by the application of hydraulic pressure, the welds shall be subjected to the following inspection procedure, namely:-

(a) (i) For piping of over 102 mm bore, all butt-welds shall be subjected to non-destructive examination by radiographic or other approved methods.

(ii) The entire circumference of the first five welds made by each welder shall be nondestructively examined and if these are found to be satisfactory and Competent Person is satisfied with the welding techniques, methods of control and supervision employed, relaxation in the extent of non-destructive examination may be considered, but this relaxation shall in no case permit the non-destructive examination of less than one-third of the circumference of each of remaining welds made by the welder concerned.

(b) For piping of 102 mm bore and under, but not less than 38 mm bore, five percent of the butt-welds made by each welder with a minimum of two welds per welder, selected at random shall be subjected to non-destructive examination by either radiographic or other approved methods or may be cut out for visual examination and bend tests.

(c) For piping of less than 38 mm bore, special tests are not normally required but two percent of the welds by each welder on a pipeline may be cut out from the pipe-line for visual examination and bend tests.

(2) Where the completed pipelines are tested by the application of hydraulic pressure, the welds shall be subjected to the following inspection procedure, namely:-

(a) For piping of over 102 mm bore, ten percent of the welds made by each welder on a pipeline with a minimum of two welds per welder, selected at random, shall be subjected to nondestructive examination by radiographic or other approved methods.

(b) For piping of 102 mm bore and under, but not less than 38 mm bore, two per cent of the welds made but each welder on a pipeline with a minimum of one weld per welder, selected at random, shall be subjected to non-destructive examination by radiographic or other approved methods or may be cut out for visual examination and bend tests.

(c) For piping of less than 38 mm bore, special tests are not normally required, but two percent of the welds by each welder on a pipeline may be cut from the pipeline for visual examination and bend tests.

268. Tests for Class II pipelines.─(1)All pipelines under this classification shall be subjected to hydraulic test on completion of erection.

(2) (a) On completion of the first ten production welds made by each welder, one of the welds shall be cut out for test purposes or alternatively, the welder shall prepare a test specimen made up of two similar lengths of piping welded in a position as closely representative of a selected production weld as is practicable.

(b) The test specimen shall be subjected to visual examination and bend tests.

(3) Two percent of the remainder of the welds made by each welder shall be selected at random and cut out for test purpose, or, alternatively the welders shall prepare a test specimen made up of two similar lengths of piping welded in a position as closely representative of a selected production weld as far as practicable and the test specimen shall be subjected to visual examination and bend tests.

(4) Notwithstanding anything contained in sub-regulations (2) and (3), the bend tests shall be dispensed with, in case the welds have been non-destructively tested in the following manner:

(a) For piping of over 102 mm bore, five per cent of the welds made by each welder on a pipeline with a minimum of two welds per welder, selected at random shall be subjected to a non-destructive examination by radiographic or other approved methods.

(b) For piping of102 mm bore and under, one per cent of the welds made by each welder on a pipeline with a minimum of one weld per welder, selected at random shall be subjected to non-destructive examination by radiographic or other approved technique.

269. Re-tests.─ (1) If any test specimen is unsatisfactory, two further weld specimen for re-test shall be selected from the production welds and subjected to tests.

(2) In the events of failure of any of the re-test specimens, the production welds carried out by this welder subsequent to the previous tests shall be subject of special consideration.

270. Socket weld joints.─ (1) Socket weld joints may be used with carbon and low alloy steel pipes not exceeding 60.3 mm. outside diameter.

(2) Such welds shall not be used where fatigue, severe erosion or severe corrosion is expected to occur.

(3) The maximum allowable working pressure of the socket weld fittings shall be determined in accordance with equation (7/1) (regulation 256).

(4) The thickness of the socket weld fittings shall not be less than 1¼ times the nominal thickness of pipe and the material shall be compatible with the associated piping.

271. Butt welded joints.─ (1) Butt welded joints as applicable shall be subjected to non-destructive examination by radiographic, radioscopy or other approved methods such as ultrasonic testing including phased array ultrasonic testing, magnetic particle inspection or liquid dye penetrant inspection.

(2) When radioscopic examination is to be performed in lieu of radiography on welded components, the following requirements shall be met:-

(a) A written procedure shall be submitted for approval to the Inspecting Authority which shall contain the following:-

(i) material and the thickness range;

(ii) equipment qualifications;

(iii) test object scan plan;

(iv) radioscopic parameters;

(v) image processing parameters;

(vi) image display parameters;

(vii) image archiving requirements;

(viii) accept-reject criteria (code reference);

(ix) performance evaluation;

(x) Operator identification.

(b) The system shall be aided with an image processor to enhance the quality of the radioscopic images and system performance quality shall exhibit-

(i) a thin section contrast sensitivity of 3%;

(ii) a thick section contrast sensitivity of 2%;

(iii) a spatial resolution of 3 line pairs per mm;.

(iv) IQI sensitivity - 2% of the joint thickness when wire IQI's are to be used, the wire diameter axis shall be oriented along the axis of the least sensitivity of the system.

(c) Radioscopes are to be properly marked to co-relate with particular part of joint represented.

(d) The radioscopic examination data shall be recorded and stored on videotape, magnetic disk or optical disk or any other electronic storage device at the maker's plant for a sufficient period after the date of radioscopic examination as specified by the Inspecting Authority, Efficient radioscopic examination record recall shall be made available at any time over the record retention period and shall be traceable to the test objects.

(e) When repair has been performed as a result of radioscopic examination, the repaired areas shall be re-examined using the same radioscopic technique to evaluate the effectiveness of the repair.

(f) To aid in proper interpretation of the radioscopic examination data, the details of the technique used shall accompany the data. As a minimum, the information shall include the approved procedure requirements and system performance test data PART-IV STEAM-PIPE FITTINGS AND CONNECTIONS

272. Wrought Bends.─ The minimum wall thickness at any point on the pipe bend shall be determined according to either of the following methods, namely :-

(a) (i)The minimum wall thickness at any point in the finished bend shall not be less than required by equation (7/2) or (7/4) given in regulation 256.

(ii) The bend thinning allowance shall be selected based on the manufacturing process to ensure the minimum thickness requirements after bending.

(iii) The bend thinning allowance thus selected is to be provided on all parts of the cross section of the pipe circumference without any detrimental effects on the final product.

(b) The minimum required thickness tm of a bend, after bending, in its finished form, shall be determined in accordance with equation (i) or (ii), namely:-

1.0 for seamless and for electric resistance welded steel pipes and for electric fusion welded steel pipes complying with the requirements of Chapter-2 in which the weld is fully radiographed or ultrasonically tested, =

0.95 for electric fusion welded steel pipes complying with the requirements of Chapter-2, =

0.90 for welded steel pipes for values of t (minimum thickness of pipe) up to and including 22 mm, =

0.85 for welded steel pipes for values of t ( minimum thickness of pipe) over 22 mm and up to and including 29 mm, =

The minimum thickness at the ends of the bends shall not be less than the requirements of equation (7/2) or (7/4) given in regulation 256 and the bend thinning allowance shall be selected based on the manufacturing process to ensure the minimum thickness requirements after bending.

274.Steel butt Welded fittings.─ (1) (a) Branches, bosses and drain pockets welded to the pipes shall conform to the requirements of regulations 148 to 152.

(b) Branches shall not be welded to any main at an angle of less than 60°.

(2) (a) If the thickness of the main or branches of a single or multiple branch piece is less than given by the Equation(7/6),external reinforcement shall be provided.

(b) Such reinforcement may take the form of multiple radial plates of 'horse shoe' form or the form of collars or other reinforcement, approved by the Inspecting Authority or Chief Inspector as the case may be, applied to or around the junction between the branch and the main.

(3) (a)Thickness where no external reinforcement is provided; where more than one branch is attached to a pipe, the opening of each branch shall be projected perpendicularly on the axis of the main and the distance between each pair of these projection measured. If the distance so measured between two branches is less than the sum of their bores, the two branches are deemed to affect each other; if this distance is equal to or greater than the sum of their bores, the two branches are deemed not to affect each other and in calculating the reinforcement required, each branch is to be considered in turn together with all the branches by which it is affected.

(b) In case a branch not affected by any other branch, where no external reinforcement is to be applied to a branch piece the thickness of the branch shall be determined from Equation (7/6).

275. Blow-down pipes .─ (1) Blow-down pipes which can be subjected to full boiler pressure shall be considered as saturated steam-pipes at that pressure.

(2) Blow-down pipes which cannot be subjected to full boiler pressure shall be considered as saturated steam-pipes at half the design pressure of the boiler.

276. Valves chests .─ (1) Chests of stop-valves, isolating valves, reducing valves, steam traps, etc., forming parts of wrought steel main steam piping when for use with saturated steam up to a gauge pressure of 11kg/cm2 (g)or a temperature of 204°C may be made of cast iron, cast steel or wrought steel in accordance with the requirements of regulation 191.

(2) When superheated steam is used above 204°C or when pressures are above 11kg/cm2 gauge, such chests shall be made of cast or wrought steel or other approved material in accordance with the requirements of regulation 191.

PART-VI

277. Materials in the construction of steam receivers, separators, catch waters , accumulators and similar vessels.─(1)Material for plates, bars and sections used in the construction of steam receivers, separators, catch waters, accumulators and similar Vessels shall comply in all respects with the requirements of relevant regulations of Chapters-2 and 4, depending upon the mode of construction.

(2) Branches, bosses and drain pockets may be solid forged, fabricated by fusion welding, machined from solid bars or made from tubes.

(3) Materials for flanges shall comply with the appropriate provisions of regulation 258.

(4) Forgings shall comply with the requirements of regulations 58 to 62

(5) Steel castings for shells or pressure parts of shell shall comply with regulations 51 to 57.

278. Construction and workmanship.─(1)Shell ends formed separately from the shell may be dished or flat or the ends of the shell may be forged down and closed by manhole doors or by plugs or branches welded in.

(2) (a) (i) Each dished end shall be made from one rolled plate.

(ii) If this is impracticable, owing to the large diameter, the dished end plate may be made from two plates fusion butt welded together and in such cases, the line of weld shall be parallel to the horizontal axis of the vessels and the weld shall be radiographed after forming.

(iii) The shape of dished end shall conform to the requirements of regulation 174.

(iv) Dishing end peripheral flanging shall be done by machine.

(v) Cold flanging shall not be adopted.

(vi) All plates which have been dished, flanged or locally heated shall afterwards be efficiently heat treated for the purpose of relieving internal stresses, unless during the last stage of manufacture, they have been uniformly heated throughout to a suitable temperature.

(vii) Care shall be taken to see that the flanges are cylindrical, of good surface and free from local irregularities.

(b) Where flats are pressed in dished ends for the attachment of connections, they shall be formed with an ample radius at the junctions of the flat and curved surfaces and shall be free from sharp corners and tool marks.

(c) Dished ends shall be attached to the cylindrical part of the shell by one of the following methods:

(i) Welding with butt Joints of the Single or Double U or V Type; where the internal diameter of the shell is 610 mm or over, the joints shall in all cases, be welded from both sides of the plate.

(ii) Where the internal diameter of the shell is less than 610 mm the joints may be welded from one side only, when efficient welding from inside is considered impracticable.

(iii) In the construction of unfired boilers when the dished ends do not form part of the heating surface, cold spun dished ends conforming to IS: 2825 may be used.

(3) (a) Flat ends shall be of forged steel or plate steel and shall be welded to the cylindrical part of the shell or bolted to flanges which shall be attached to the cylindrical part of the shell in accordance with regulation 263.

(b) The attachment of flat ends shall be by one of the methods shown in figure7/35, 7/36, 7/37 and 7/38 of this chapter.

(c) When ends are attached as shown in figure7/38, welding shall be from both side of the shell plate wherever practicable and where welding is done from one side only, care shall be taken to ensure full penetration using backing strips.

Fig. 7/35 Fig. 7/37 Fig. 7/36 Fig. 7/38 All dimensions are in mm.

Fig. 7/39

279. Access to Shells.─ (1) At least one manhole shall be provided to allow access for thorough cleaning and inspection, except that where the shell is too small to permit entry, cleaning and inspection openings of sufficient size and number shall be provided.

(2) When there is an elliptical opening in the cylindrical part of the shell, the minor axis shell, wherever practicable be parallel to the longitudinal axis of the shell.

280. Compensating rings to openings and doors.─ The material, construction and workmanship of compensating rings and doors shall comply with the relevant provisions of Chapters 2, 3 and 12.

281. Branches and other connections.─(1) (a) Connections to shell shall be made by means of branches, pressed plate saddles, forged pads or bosses or where the thickness of the shell is sufficient to allow suitable surface to be obtained, connections may be attached directly to the shell, provided that the minimum thickness at the hole in the shell is not less than the thickness required for the maximum allowable working pressure and temperature considering the shell as being unpierced and that the diameter of the hole does not exceed the maximum diameter of an uncompensated hole as defined in regulation 141 and 179.

(b) Studs for securing such connections shall have a full thread holding in the shell for a length of at least one diameter and the stud holes shall not penetrate the whole thickness of the shell.

(c) All holes for bolts and studs in branches, saddles and forged pads shall be drilled.

(d) Where such connections are secured by welding alone, a minimum of two runs of metal shall be deposited at each weld, except for seal welds.

(e) Each run of weld metal shall be thoroughly cleaned and free from slag before the next run is deposited.

(f) The final finish of the welds shall be such that change of section from shell to branch is gradual and free from sharp notches.

(g) Where the diameter of the shell is 610 mm and over, welding shall be from both sides of the plate.

(h) Where the internal diameter of the shell is less than 610 mm, external and internal welds shall be applied unless it is considered that efficient welding from both sides is impracticable.

(i) This does not apply to the methods of attachment shown in Fig.7/40 and 7/41 in which the welding shall always be from both sides.

(2) (a) Branches of not more than 38 mm nominal bore may be screwed into the shell with a taper thread and seal welded, provided that the thickness of the shell is sufficient to allow for a length of thread equal to the diameter of the branch.

(b) Where the thickness of the shell is not sufficient for the purpose, a boss may be welded on so that the total thickness of the boss and shell is at least equal to the required length of thread.

(c) Methods of attachment of such bosses are shown in Figs. 7/65 to 7/69.

(3) (a) Branches, pressed plate saddles, forged pads of bosses shall be secured to the shell by one of the following methods,-

(i) Welding;

(ii) Screwing and Seal Welding.

(b) (i) Methods of attachment secured by welding are shown in Figs. 7/40 to 7/64 of this chapter.

(ii) Branches may be provided with flanges for ordinary bolted joints. Joints of special type may also be used.

(iii) Flanges for ordinary bolted joints shall be in accordance with the appropriate table of Appendix-F and shall be forged solid with the branches or attached in accordance with regulation 263.

(iv) They shall be machined on the jointing and bolt bearing surfaces.

(c) (i) Where pressed plate saddles are used, they shall be formed to bed closely to the shell and shall be machined on the face adjoining the connection and machined or flame cut by machine on the edges.

(ii) The studs for the attachment of the connection, if screwed through the saddle, shall each be fitted with a nut on the inside.

(iii) Where the stud holes do not penetrate through the saddle, the length of the screwed portion of the stud in the plate shall be not less than the diameter of the stud.

(d) The join faces of all pads shall be machined.

(e) (i) The pads shall be sufficiently thick to allow the drilling of stud holes for connections without the inner surface being pierced.

(ii) The length of the screwed portion of the stud in the pad shall be not less than the diameter of the stud.

(iii) Method of attachment of pads secured by welding are shown in Fig. 7/70 to 7/76 of this chapter but where pads of the type shown in Fig. 7/70 are used, they shall be formed to bed closely to the shell.

(f) (i) Tapped bosses of not more than 38 mm standard pipe welded to the shell, may be used for maximum allowable working pressure not exceeding 14 kg/cm2 (g) and design temperatures not exceeding 343°C.

(ii) Such bosses shall have taper threads.

(iii) Methods of attachment of bosses secured by welding are shown in Fig. 7/65, 7/69 of this chapter

(g) (i) All welded attachments other than flange connections shall be by the metal arc process and the electrodes used shall comply with the requirements of regulations 71 to 76.

(ii) Where tack welds are used, they shall be sound and unless cut out, shall be carefully fused into the main runs.

(h) All parts secured by welding shall be effectively heat treated after completion of all welding and before hydraulic test.

282. Shell joints .─ (1)Fusion Welded Shells shall be classified as follows:

(a) Class I – No limit to scantlings.

(b) Class II – When none of the following limits exceeds:–

(i) maximum allowable working pressure- 35 kg/cm2 (g)

(ii) product of maximum allowable working pressure and internal diameter in centimeters –

3750.

(iii) design temperature – 343°C.

(c) Class III – When none of the following limits exceeds:–

(i) maximum allowable working pressure -7.4 kg/cm2 (g)

(ii) product of maximum allowable working pressure and internal diameter in centimeters –

940.

(iii) design temperature – 171°C.

(2) Fusion welds, preparation of seams, the method of formation of cylindrical shells including heat treatment after bending, method of making welded seams, types of welded joints, test plates and circularity of shells, all shall conform to the requirements of Chapter -4, except for the following, namely;-

(a) (i) Whenever practicable, seams shall be welded from both sides of the plate. Additional runs of metal shall be deposited at both surfaces of the welded seams to build up to a thickness 10 per cent greater than thickness of the plate.

(ii) The surfaces of welds wherever carried out on both sides shall be ground smooth and flush with the respective surfaces of the plates.

(iii) Where the seam is welded from one side only, these provisions shall apply to the exposed of the surface weld.

(b) Tests for Class I fusion welded seams shall comply with the requirements of the relevant regulations of Chapter -4.

(c) Tests for Class II fusion welded seams shall comply with the requirements of those of Chapter-12, except that an additional provision for one micro and macro examination shall be made.

(d) Tests for Class III fusion welded seams shall comply with the requirements of Chapter -12.

(e) Class I and Class II shells shall be effectively stress-relieved by heat treatment when the wall thickness exceeds 20 mm or the carbon content exceeds 0.25% or the pipes are made of alloy steel.

283. Determination of maximum allowable working pressure.─(1) (a) The maximum allowable working pressure of shells shall be determined as per the following formula: - 2f E (T – 0.75) P = ———————D + (T – 0.75) Where, T is thickness in mm, D is maximum internal diameter in mm, P is maximum allowable working pressure in kg/cm2 (g), f is maximum permissible working stress as prescribed in regulation 170 or 256 whichever is applicable in kg/cm2 at the working metal temperature, E is Efficiency factor for fusion welded shells as given in table below, is 1.00 for seamless shells or shells made from seamless tubes, is Efficiency of ligaments between holes or openings in shell expressed as a fraction.

0.75 is the additive thickness in order to take corrosion into account.

(b) Where steels are intended for service at temperatures in excess of 371°C, this shall be so stated and silicon contents shall be not less than 0.10 per cent or alternatively, the material must pass the 'Proof test for creep quality of carbon steel plate of boiler quality' as in Appendix-E.

(c) For compensation for openings in shells, where the major axis of diameter of any hole cut in cylindrical part of the shells is greater than 2½ times the thickness of the shell plate plus 70 mm, compensation shall be provided.

(d) The sectional area to be compensated measured in the plane parallel to the longitudinal axis of the shell, which makes this area a maximum, shall be the product of the length of the opening and the thickness of a seamless shell of similar material calculated in accordance with Equation (4/2) (regulation 169) for the same conditions of pressure and temperatures.

(e) Where frames, pads or branches are secured by welding, the compensating area shall be calculated as per the method given in regulation 141.

(2) (a)The maximum allowable working pressure for dished end plates with 'pressure on concave side' shall be determined as per regulations 175 to 177.

(b) For manholes formed by welding on pressed frames to dished end plates as in Fig. 7/77,the value of

"E" may be taken as unity when the diametral cross-sectional area of the compensating frame and or ring is equal to or greater than the diametral cross-sectional area of the opening in the end plate to be compensated. For ends which are butt welded to the cylindrical parts of the shell, the thickness of the edge of the flange for connection to the cylindrical part of the shell shall be not less than that required for seamless unpierced shell as determined as per Equation (4/2) as mentioned under regulation 169.

Fig. 7/77: Elliptical Manhole Frame Welded to Dished End Plate

170.

(c ) Where steels are intended for service at temperatures in excess of 371°C, this shall be so stated and silicon content shall not be less than 0.10 per cent or alternatively, the material shall pass the 'Proof test for creep quality of carbon steel plates of boiler plate quality' as in Appendix-E.

(d) Where flat end plates are bolted to flanges as in Fig. 7/39, the dimensions of the flanges shall be as given in Appendix-F. The thickness of the end plates shall be not less than that of the corresponding flanges.

(e) Where the diameter of a hole in the flat end plate exceeds 2½ T + 70 mm, compensation shall be provided in accordance with regulations 141and 179.

(b) The actual dimensions of the flanges shall be governed by the requirements of compensation for the opening.

(c) (i) The dimensions of flanges of branches remote from the shell for ordinary bolted joints and those of bolts of all pads and saddles shall be as per the appropriate table given in Appendix-F for the maximum allowable working pressure and temperature corresponding to the maximum allowable working pressure and temperature of the shell.

(ii) The dimensions of the flanges for special joints shall be subject to approval of the Inspecting Authority or Chief Inspector as the case may be.

(d) The maximum allowable working pressure for the body of the branch shall be determined by the equation as given in clause (a) of sub-regulation (1) above, subject to the requirements of compensation for the opening.

(e) (i) Notwithstanding the result obtained from the equation the minimum thickness of the body of the branches shall be such that in no case does the total stress, resulting from the combination of the stress due to internal pressure and to all externally applied loads, exceed the permissible stress corresponding to the design temperature.

(ii) The method of calculating the total stress shall be subject to the approval of the Inspecting Authority or Chief Inspector as the case may be. 284.Inspection during construction.─ All parts of steam receivers, separators, catch waters and similar vessels shall be inspected at all appropriate stages of construction detailed in Appendix-A of these regulations.

285. Steel screwed and socketed joints and mountings of steel.─(1) Steel couplings or sockets may be used on pipes within the limits given in Table-7/12 below:Table 7/12 Serial No.

(2) Screwed joints shall be permitted at temperatures exceeding 260°C and size or pressure limits in excess of those given in the Table 7/12 of this regulation provided that the following conditions are satisfied, namely:-

(a) all threads are tapered unless pressure tightness depends on a seal weld or a seating surface other than the threads and experience or test demonstrates the suitability of the joint.

(b) pressure or temperature rating in appropriate component standard like BS, ASME, DIN or calculated as per relevant regulation is not exceeded.

(c) minimum specified tensile strength of screwed pipes is greater than 30.5 kg/mm2.

(d) the minimum thickness of screwed pipes is not to be less than that given in Table 7/13 of this regulation.

(e) the design temperature does not exceed 495°C.

(f) the pressure limits given in Table 7/14 of this regulation are not exceeded.

(3) Screwed joints are permitted for temperatures in excess of 495°C and pressure in excess of those given in Table 7/14 of this regulation for instrument insertion and tapping and for plug for access opening for radiographic inspection provided following conditions are satisfied:-

(a) They do not exceed 50 mm nominal bore size or one quarter of nominal pipe size, whichever is the smaller.

(b) The minimum thread engagement is not less than –

(i) 6 for up to and including 20 mm nominal bore;

(ii) 7 for over 20 mm up to and including 40 mm nominal bore; and

(iii) 8 for 40 mm up to and including 50 mm nominal bore.

(c) The connection is seal-welded.

(d) The design of instrument insertion withstands the fluid characteristics, fluid flow and any vibrations.

(4) Screwed joints are permitted for pressure in excess of those given in Table 7/14 up to 345 bar for dead end instrument lines at the outlet end and downstream of shut off valves and instruments, control apparatus or discharge of a sample cooler provided that the nominal bore size of the pipe does not exceed 12 mm.

286. Bronze screwed and socketed joints and mountings and fittings of bronze.─

(1) Mountings and fittings of bronze up to 76 mm diameter may be attached directly to steel pipes by screwing, when the pressures and temperatures do not exceed 8.5 kg/cm2 (g) and 225°C respectively; where tapered threads are employed, such joints may be used for pressures not exceeding 17.6 kg/cm2 (g).

(2) Bronze fittings above 76 mm diameter shall be of flanged construction.

287. Reducing valve.─ Where a reducing valve is installed in a pipe line, the pipe line and accessories on the low pressure side of the reducing valve shall be protected by a suitable safety valve or valves so adjusted as to permit the steam to escape as soon as the maximum allowable working pressure is exceeded or by a suitable appliance for cutting off automatically the supply of steam as soon as the maximum allowable working pressure is exceeded.

288. Flexibility.─(1)The pipe shall be arranged so that the system is sufficiently flexible to absorb the whole of their expansion and the imposed movement of the connected equipment without the actual expansion stress (SE) exceeding the allowable stress range (SA) determined as per the following equation,─ SA = F (1.25 SC + 0.25 SH) Equation (7/11) Where, SC = Basic material allowable stress at the minimum (cold) temperature, SH = Basic material allowable stress at maximum (hot) temperature, SE = Actual calculated expansion stress obtained from the stress analysis, F = Stress range reduction factors of cyclic conditions for total number "N" of full temperature cycles over the total number of years during which the system is expected to be in operation as in table given below:

Table 7/15: Stress range reduction factors:

Serial No.

N F

1. 7,000 and less

1.0

2. 7,000 - 14,000

0.9

3. 14,000 - 22,000

0.8

4. 22,000 - 45,000

0.7

5. 45,000 - 100,000

0.6

6. 100,000 and above

0.5 Notes:

1. Value of SC and SH shall be guaranteed by the manufacturer of steel from which pipes are made and furnished to the pipe makers / Inspecting Authority until such time such values of SC and SH are incorporated in the regulations.

2. In case of imported pipes conforming to a recognized foreign standard or code the values of SC and SH may be taken from the table of allowance stress as given in that code or the value may be determined on the basis given in that code.

3. The sum of the longitudinal stress due to pressure, weight and other sustained load including bending stresses caused by external loads shall not exceed the allowable stress in the hot conditions

(SH), Where the sum of these stress is less than SH, the difference between SH and this sum may be added to the term 0.25 SH, in the above formula for determining the allowable stress range SA.

4. Calculation of the expansion stress, SE shall be based on modulus of elasticity at room temperature.

(2) Where practicable, the requisite flexibility shall be provided in the layout of the pipes without having recourse to special expansion bends or expansion joint except for the safety valves discharge piping.

Where lack of space or other considerations prohibits the use of this method, expansion fittings like expansion joints or the bellows type of corrugated pipes may be used provided that the limitations imposed by maximum shall be given to the design of anchors, guides and ties to ensure that they adequately protect the expansion fitting and accommodate the additional loads due to pressure.

(3) Notwithstanding anything in sub-regulation (1) of this regulation, the complete analysis of the piping system may be waived, if the system meets any of the criteria given below,-

(a) the piping system duplicates a successfully operating installation or replaces a system with a satisfactory service record.

(b) the piping system can be adjudged adequately by comparison with previously analysed system.

(c) the piping system is of uniform size with not more than two anchors and no intermediate restraints and satisfied the following :DY ————- = 208 (L-U) 2 Where, D = Nominal pipe size in mm.

Y = Resultant of movements to be absorbed by pipelines in mm, L = Developed length of line axis in meters, U = Anchor distance (length of straight line joining anchors) in meters.

Notes: 1.

If the system is not meeting the above criteria or where reasonable doubt exist as in the adequate flexibility of the system, shall be analysed on the basis given in sub-regulation (1) of this regulation by simplified, approximate or comprehensive methods of analysis that are appropriate for the specific case.

2.

Approximate or simplified method may be applied only if they are used for the range or configurations for which their adequate accuracy has been demonstrated.

3.

Data for thermal expansion, modulus of elasticity, poison's ratio, flexibility factors and stress intensity factors may be taken from any code or specification.

4.

Nominal diameter and nominal thickness may be used in the calculation of expansion stress.

289. Pipe work supports.─ (1) All pipe work shall be adequately supported in order to permit free movement for expansion and contraction, and the amount of such movement shall be proportioned throughout the whole of any main by the provision of anchors at suitable points.

(2) Where pipes may be subject to vertical movement, spring supports designed to carry the load under all conditions shall be provided.

(3) Points of supports shall, as far as practicable, be arranged adjacent to the pipe joints.

(4) Slings are in general preferable to roller supports and the latter shall be used only where necessary.

(5) All pipe supports shall as far as practicable, be of mild steel and pipe anchors either of mild steel or cast steel.

290. Drainage.─ (1) (a) In the case of steam mains, attention shall be paid to the adequate provision of drainage points in the form of drain pockets.

(b) Drainage points connected to stream-traps, shall be provided wherever water can collect under working conditions.

(c) Hand drains shall be provided at all points at which water can collect in any portion of the steam main, by valve leakage or other means, when shut down, or when warning up prior to use.

(2) Wherever practicable, a suitable gradient shall be provided in the pipe work to ensure the passage of condensed water in the direction of flow of steam towards the drainage points.

(3) It is recommended that a bypass shall be fitted at each steam trap.

(4) Where the volume of water deposited is likely to be of serious proportions, separator shall be installed.

(5) Each separator shall be furnished with a steam trap which shall be connected to it by means of a three-way cock, one end of which is connected to a manually operated drain.

291.Freedom from rust and other foreign matter.─ All pipes, valves and fittings shall be thoroughly cleaned as far as possible of rust and other foreign matter before erection and pipe lines shall be blown through with steam or air before being put into service.

292. Test Pressures.─(1) Each completed pipe and fitting shall be tested by hydraulic pressure as per Chapter-2.

(2) (a) Pipes and fittings with flanges for steam pressures exceeding 7 kg/cm2 (g) shall be tested with blank flanges bolted or clamped on.

(b) All other pipes, if straight, may be tested between the heads of an ordinary hydraulic pipes testing machine.

(3) (a) The piping system shall, after erection, be subjected to a hydrostatic test pressure.

(b) The hydrostatic test pressure at any point in the piping system shall not be less than 1.5 times the design pressure but shall not exceed the maximum allowable test pressure of any non-isolated components such as vessels, pumps or valves.

(c) At no time during the hydrostatic tests, any part of the piping system shall be subjected to a stress greater than 90 per cent of its yield strength (0.2 per cent off set) at test temperature.

Notwithstanding anything contained in this sub-section, for boiler external piping and non-boiler external piping which are open to atmosphere such as vent and drain pipes, the hydraulic test for the portions of such pipes located beyond the last isolation valve may be dispensed with provided the weld joints in the pipe under reference are tested 100% by approved non-destructive examination and found satisfactory.

(4) Special arrangement shall be made, according to circumstances, for testing bends and other fittings which are not flanged.

(5) Hydraulic testing of butt welded and socket welded joints shall be conducted either at makers work or at user's premises on completion of fabrication of pipes by the Inspecting Authority.

(6) (a) Hydrostatic testing of fabricated pipes having attachments of flanges, fittings, butt joints, branch joints and weldolets shall be done at manufacturer's works or alternatively at site at 1.5 times the maximum allowable working pressure.

(b) Where hydraulic testing of fabricated pipe joints is not carried out at manufacturer's works, dye-penetrant test shall be carried out on the welded joints along with five per cent radiography of such joints.

CHAPTER -8 INSPECTION AND TESTING OF BOILERS AND PIPES DURING CONSTRUCTION AND BOILER REGISTRATION

293. General.─ (1)The Inspecting Authority shall follow the procedure provided under these regulations during construction of a boiler at shop, field-site, in dealing applications for the registration of a boiler and examinations of boilers.

(2) The boiler components shall be erected at site based on the final inspection report as per Form-VIII approved by Competent Person and there is no need for any ground inspection or submission of any other document like manufacturing drawings, design calculations.

294. Boiler component's identification marking.─ (1) Convection bank tubes, loose tubes, coils - evaporator super heater and economiser, riser downcomer and saturated supply pipes, attemperator link piping, boiler integral and non integral piping, water wall panel without header shall be stencilled as per the table given below irrespective of material grade, outside diameter and thickness.

(2) There shall be no hard stamping of competent person on these component.

Table 8/1 IDENTIFICATION ON BOILER PRESSURE PART COMPONENTS Serial Identification No.

295. Preparations of Inspection.─ (1)(a)(i) At every boiler examination or grant of renewal of a certificate, the boiler shall be empty and all its parts shall be thoroughly cleaned. Except as provided for in sub-regulation (6), all doors of manholes, hand holes and sight holes and cleaning plugs and all caps in the headers and mud drums of water tube boilers, all fire bars, bearers, front plates, bridge plates, fire bridges brick arches, oil fuel burners and mechanical stoker fitting shall be removed.

(ii) All valves and cocks comprising the boiler mounting shall be opened up and taken apart and the valves or cocks grounded, when necessary, before the Competent Person's visit.

(b) The removal of fire bars, bearers, front plates, bridge plates, fire bridges, brick arches, oil fuel burners and mechanical stoker fitting is not required at the time of registration for boilers for which boiler pressure part assembly is completed and hydraulic test has been carried out at manufacturer works by Competent Person.

(2) (a) In case of requirement for inspection by the Competent Person, provision shall be made for removal of lagging or brick-work or other concealing part for drilling of plates, and for verifying the pressure gauge and safety valve dimensions and weights.

(b) The removal of lagging or brick-work or other concealing part shall not be required at the time of registration of boilers for which boiler pressure part assembly is completed and hydraulic test carried out at manufacturer works by Competent Person.

(3) All smoke tubes, exterior of water tubes, smoke boxes, and external flues shall be swept clean.

(4) Provision shall be made for effective disconnection of all steam and hot water communication with another boiler under steam as required in Chapter-9.

(5) No blank flange or plug shall be inserted between a safety valve chest and the boiler generally and where it is permitted by the Competent Person, the blank flange or plug shall be removed in his presence.

(6) At alternative annual inspections and subject to a minimum of three bottom rows or all tubes subject to the first pass of heat being opened up for inspection, the Competent Person may at his discretion relax the preparation for inspection specified under sub-regulation (1) above in favour of boilers having an evaporative capacity of 90 tons per hour and over, and using treated feed water to the satisfaction of the Competent Person.

(7) For boilers used for electric power generation, the inspection shall be carried out as per the provisions of Appendix-H.

(8) (a) For Waste Heat Boilers (Fired and Unfired) used exclusively in continuous process plants, the inspection shall be carried out as per the provisions of Appendix-I.

(b) The boilers used in corrosive environment such as sulphuric acid plants shall not be covered under the provisions of Appendix-I.

(9) In the case of forced flow and forced circulation types of boilers, proper circulation in every section of the circuit shall be ensured by the flow of water.

296. Hydraulic test of boilers.─ (1) Every boiler shall be hydraulically tested in the presence of an Inspector.

(2) Boiler working at a pressure of 50 kg/cm2 (g) or more, and used exclusively for electric power generation need not be hydraulically tested for issue of certificate every year, provided the inspection of such boiler is carried out as per the provisions of sub-regulation (7) of regulation 295.

(3) Waste Heat Boilers (Fired and Unfired) used exclusively in continuous process plants need not be hydraulically tested for issue of certificate every year, provided the inspection of such boilers is carried out as per the provisions of sub-regulation (8) of regulation 295.

297. Preparation for hydraulic tests.─ (1) The chest of all mountings subject to steam pressure shall be in place and shut tight or blank flanged.

(2) The safety valves shall be invariably removed and the chest opening blank flanged.

(3) The attachment for the Competent Person's pressure gauge shall be in order.

(4) (a) All doors shall be properly jointed and tightened up.

(b) The boiler shall be completely filled with water with care being taken to allow all air to escape and, if possible, a preliminary test not exceeding the design pressure of the boiler shall be taken before the Competent Person's visit, to test the tightness of the joints.

(5) When a boiler is hydraulically tested for the first time, it shall be entirely cleared of lagging or brick work; at subsequent tests the lagging or brick work, or portions thereof, shall be removed, if required by the Competent Person, provided the Competent Person may, at his discretion, allow the lagging and brick work to remain in situ, in case of boilers where the covered parts have been fabricated and tested before erection in position.

298. Procedure of hydraulic tests.─ (1) (a) Subject to the provisions of sub-regulation (1)of regulation300, every sub-critical boiler shall be hydraulically tested after erection at site in presence of the Competent Person to 1.25 times the design pressure as certified by the Inspecting Authority in Form-I(Part-B) or Form-I(Part-C) as the case may be, to be stamped on the boiler, as free from any indication of weakness or defects and every Super critical boiler, with no fixed steam and waterline, having pressure parts designed for different pressure levels along the path of water-steam flow, shall be hydraulically tested after erection at site in presence of the Competent Person to a pressure of not less than 1.5 times the design pressure at the super heater outlet but not less than 1.25 times the design pressure of any part of the boiler as certified by the Inspecting Authority in Form-I(Part-B) or FormI(Part-C) as the case may be, to be stamped on the boiler, as free from any indication of weakness or defects.

(b) If all components of the boiler in the manufacturer's premises have not been tested hydraulically as per regulation 167, the test, on completion, shall be taken to 1.5 times the design pressure.

(c) The temperature of the water used as medium of pressure testing shall not be less than 20°C and greater than 50°C.

(d) The test pressure shall be raised gradually under proper control at all times so that it never exceeds by more than 6% of the required pressure and maintained for 30 minutes whereupon the pressure shall be reduced to maximum allowable working pressure and maintained for sufficient time to permit close visual inspection for leakage of pressure parts.

(e) The hydraulic test at site for the components meant for renovation or retrofit or spares shall be carried out at 1.25 times of the design pressure and these components need not be hydraulically tested at manufacturer's premises.

(2) The boiler shall satisfactorily withstand such pressure without appreciable leakage or undue deflection or distortion of its parts for at least ten consecutive minutes. If the test if not satisfactory, the design pressure allowable by calculation shall be suitably reduced, unless the owner desires to make such alterations as will enable the boiler to withstand the hydraulic test satisfactorily, in which case the boiler shall again be examined after the alterations have been made, the pressure recalculated, if necessary and the boiler tested to satisfaction of the Competent Person.

(3) At the first hydraulic test of a boiler prior to the issue of an original certificate, deflection measurements shall be made before, during and after test of each furnace length, fire-box and flat end or other plates.

(4) After application of the hydraulic test, the Competent Person shall carefully examine the boiler inside and outside and satisfy himself that it has satisfactorily withstood the test.

(5) (a) In case any part of the boiler show undue deflection or indication of permanent set during the progress of the test, the pressure shall be released immediately such indications are observed.

(b) The maximum allowable working pressure for the part shall be 40 per cent of the test pressure applied when the point of permanent set was reached. This procedure shall apply to any boiler at any test.

(6) Hydraulic tests of boilers at subsequent examination shall be at 1.25 times the design pressure of the boiler.

(7) When the internal construction or size of a boiler does not permit the Competent Person to get inside it for examining all its parts closely, he shall see it tested by hydraulic pressure to 1.5 times the design pressure at each inspection or the grant or renewal of a certificate.

(8) Water tube locomotive type boiler and all tubular boilers shall be hydraulically tested at each inspection for the grant or renewal of a certificate, unless such test is waived under the orders of the Chief Inspector.

(9) The Competent Person may if he considers it necessary, apply a hydraulic test to any boiler at any inspection.

(10) Except in the case of vertical boilers having heating surface of less than 18.5m2portable and vehicular boilers, which do not require re-erection or building in brick work, the hydraulic tests of all boilers shall be conducted only after the erection of the boiler in situ and all boilers shall after re-erection in a position different from that in which were last examined by hydraulically tested.

(11) A hydraulic test shall also be taken granting an increased pressure certificate and after repairing a boiler.

However, in the case of minor repairs to the water tube boilers where non-destructive testing(NDT) has been carried out, hydraulic testing may be dispensed with provided non-destructive testing is carried out by an approved method.

(12) When carrying out hydraulic test, Competent Person shall ensure that the pressure gauges have valid calibration.

299. Steam tests.─ (1) Every boiler before registration and every boiler of which the design pressure has been altered shall, before the issue of an original or renewal certificate for such boiler, be tested under steam to the satisfaction of the Inspector. Inspector shall send notice to the owner for the date fixed for the steam test and on completion of the test the Inspector shall enter all relevant details in the memorandum of inspection books.

(2) At the time of test, the safety valves shall be left free and capable of being adjusted to the approved design pressure.

(3) After adjustment of the valves to the correct blowing pressure, the boiler shall be tried under full steam and firing with the feed water shut off and the stop-valve closed, and the Inspector shall note the accumulation of pressure and other details of the test as accumulation of pressure and other details of the test as well as the loading and adjustment of the safety valves.

(a) In the case of sub-critical water tube boiler or boilers fitted with super heater, the feed water connection and stop valve need not be shut off and if the total valve area is lifted and found to be adequate by calculations, the requirement of the accumulation test may be assumed to have been satisfied if the valves are or have been found so adjusted that at least one safety valve on boiler shall be set at or below design pressure and remaining safety valves can be set up to three per-cent above the design pressure:

Provided that all valves shall be lifted so that all steam which can be generated by the boiler can be discharged with a pressure rise not exceeding ten per-cent of the design pressure;

(b) In the case of super critical boiler or boilers, with no fixed steam and waterline, having pressure parts designed for different pressure levels along with the path of water-steam flow, safety valves shall be set up to ten per-cent above the maximum allowable working pressure:

Provided that all the valves shall be lifted so that all steam which can be generated by the boiler can be discharged with a pressure rise not exceeding thirteen percent of the individual component maximum allowable working pressure, provided the boiler is having automatic burner management system and Master Fuel Trip (MFT) on main steam pressure. High lifting of safety valves can be tested by hydraulically actuated methods.

(4) Before the safety valve test, the Inspector shall satisfy himself that the water gauges are in working order and that the feed apparatus is capable of supplying the boiler with sufficient water.

(5) Where as per rules, a person-in-charge of a boiler holding certificate of competency is not required, the Inspector may, when he thinks fit, satisfy himself by questioning or by practical test, whether the personin-charge of the boiler understands the use and purpose of the water gauges, the pressure gauge, the safety valves, the feed water-supply and blow down.

(6) When witnessing safety valves test, Inspector shall ensure that the pressure gauges have valid calibration.

(7) No steam gauge shall be used without a syphon filled with water between it and the boiler.

(8) When the accumulation of pressure at a steam test exceeds ten per cent of the design pressure the area of the safety valves shall be considered insufficient, and a certificate shall be refused until the safety valve area is increased.

(9) An Inspector may, when visiting a factory for any purpose, verify the correctness of the safety valve and pressure gauge of any boiler under steam by comparison with his standard pressure gauge. *Note: A steam test is primarily intended for ascertaining that the safety valves are sufficient to relieve boilers effectively of excess steam and operate at the time when the maximum allowable working pressure is reached.

300. Procedure for Registration.─ (1) On receipt of an application for registration under sub-section (1) of Section 12 of the Act, along with certificates and drawings as provided in regulation-8, the Inspector shall, when the boiler has been properly prepared for examination proceed to inspect it with a view to satisfy himself that the boiler has not suffered any damage during its transit from the place of manufacture to the site of erection and if he is satisfied with the correctness of the maker's certificate and other particulars relating to the material and construction as mentioned under clause (c) of sub-section (1) of section 19 of the Act and regulation 7, take due account of the workmanship and details of the construction of each part;

(2) Subject to any discretionary power exercised by the Chief Inspector, he may accept the permissible working pressure of the boiler as certified by the Inspecting Authority in Form-I(Part-B) or Form-I(Part-C) as the case may be and Form-IV(Part-A) and after inspecting the boiler, the Inspector shall conduct hydraulic test as per requirements of regulations 298 and a provisional order under section 14 in Form-X shall be issued after the hydraulic test.

(3) If the requirements of provisional order under sub-regulation (2) are not fulfilled, fresh provisional orders shall be issued for a maximum of another six months but not exceeding twelve months from the date of issue of first provisional order, without any subsequent inspection and payment of fees.

(4) The Inspector shall enter the above particulars and dimensions of the boiler, together with details of the hydraulic test, in Form-XI (Memorandum of Inspection Book) provided under regulation 306 and with all the makers' papers for the boiler, shall be submitted to the Chief Inspector with the Inspector's report after issue of registry number, the Inspector shall conduct steam test as per regulation 299 and enter the details of steam test in Form-XI.

301. Engraving of registry number.─ (1) (a) (i) The registry number of every boiler shall, within a period of one month from the date of receipt thereof, shall be cut in the front plate or any such position as shall be pointed out by the Inspector.

(ii) The device for each State or Union territory shall be distinguished by the following letters as mentioned in the Table below, namely:Name of State/Union territory Letters Table Serial No.

(b) (i) The distinguishing letters shall be engraved above a number and separated there from by a horizontal line 64 mm in length.

(ii) The letters and figures shall be 25 mm in height and of suitable breadth, provided that in the case of small boilers the letters and figures of the device may, in the discretion of the Chief Inspector, be reduced to 10 mm in height. The whole shall be enclosed in a rectangle, the upper and lower sides of which shall be 76 mm apart and 6 mm clear of the top of the letters and the bottom of the figures respectively as indicated below:

Example:MR

(c) In the case of registration of small industrial boilers, the letter "S" shall be added as a prefixed to the registration number below the distinguishing letters for State or Union territories as indicated below: - Example:UP S- ——

(d) The side lines shall be at equal distance clear from the figures and the engraving shall not be less than

0.4 mm in depth.

(2) The engraving shall be complete and ready for verification within one month from the date of receipt of registry number, and the fact shall be reported to the Chief Inspector of Boiler within this period.

(3) (a) Boilers having registry devices differing from those prescribed herein shall have such devices altered or crossed out and engraved a new one in conformity with those prescribed above.

(b) The original numbers of such boilers shall be retained in the new device, provided that in the case of boilers which were registered in a State or Union territory which has since become extinct, a new number shall be given by the State or Union territory where the Boiler is operating and any number once allotted to a boiler shall not be used again for another boiler.

302. Measurement of Heating Surface.─ (1) For the purpose of regulating the area of the safety valves and the amount of registration and inspection fees, the heating surface of a boiler shall be the total surface of all plates and tubes exposed to heat on one side and in contact with water on the other measured on the water side or the fire side, whichever is larger.

(2) (a) For Lancashire and Cornish boilers, the total heating surface shall include the wetted surface of the furnaces between the end plates, the fire surface of cross tubes where fitted, and the part of the external shell below the side flue covers. In estimating the areas, furnaces shall be considered as plain cylinders and the area of their wetted surface shall be taken as their mean external circumference X the length of the boiler between end plates. For the shell, the width of that part of the circumference below the flue covers shall be taken as = 2D, and this width multiplied by the length between end plates, shall be taken as the area of shell heating surface.

(b) The part of the surface of the back end plate exposed to heat shall be omitted from the calculation.

Example – The formula for the total heating surface of a Lancashire boiler having plain furnaces without cross tubes is as follows: - Heating Surface in square meter = 2 L (3.14 d + D). "L" is the length of the boiler between end plates in meter, "d" is the mean external diameter of the furnaces in meter and "D" is the internal diameter of the largest belt of shell in meter.

(3) (a) For steam and water drums of water tube boilers, the heating surface of the drum shall be taken as half the external mean circumference multiplied by the clear length of drum between the outer brick walls or centers of cross boxes, as the case may be.

(b) The heating surface of the tubes shall be taken as the external surface of the tubes between the tube plates or heaters.

(c) The heating surface of the heater shall be omitted from the calculation.

(4) (a) For marine boilers of the fire-tube type, the heating surface shall include the wetted surface of the furnaces between the tube plates (considered in the same way as for Lancashire boilers), the wetted surface of the combustion chambers (less the area of the tube holes) and the wetted surface of the tubes between tube plates.

(b) The parts of the front tube plate exposed to heat shall be omitted from the calculation.

(5) (a) For locomotive type boilers, the heating surface shall include the wetted surface of the fire-box above the foundation ring (less the area of the tube holes and the fire hole and ring) and the wetted surface of the tubes between the tube plates.

(b) The smoke box tube plate shall be omitted from the calculation.

(6) For vertical boilers of ordinary type, the heating surface shall include the wetted surface of the fire-box above the foundation ring (less the area of fire hole and ring and tube holes if any) and the surface of any cross or other tubes and uptake below the lowest water level shown in the gauge glass.

(7) For Electrode boilers, the heating surface shall be calculated as per the following formulae,E Heating surface = —

Where, E = the equivalent evaporation at 100°C under normal load, which x K.W, K.W.= the kilowatts absorbed at the stated volume when the water in the boiler has a specific resistance of not less than 200 ohms per inch tube at 65°C and while the boiler is delivering its normal output of steam at its design pressure with the feed water temperature of 15°C.

(8) No deduction shall be made for stays, in calculating the heating surface.

(9) For any other heating surface not provided for in the foregoing instructions, the same general procedure shall be observed.

303. Boiler rating.─ The boiler rating shall be the number of square meters (to the nearest whole number) in the heating surface of the boiler as determined under regulation 302.

304. Registration fee.─ (1) The fee required for registration under sub-section (2) of section 12 of the Act shall be as per table 8/2 of this regulation:

Provided that the Chief Inspector may direct that no fee shall be payable in respect of a fresh application made in pursuance of sub-section (2) of section 19 of the Act.

(2) Inspection fee for separately fired super-heater, re-heater and economiser shall be charged at the same rate as the registration fee.

(3) For heat recovery steam generators with finned tubes, the fee shall be calculated on heating surface area of bare tubes and on one fourth of heating surface area of fins.

305. Inspection fee for renewal of certificate. ─ (1) The required fee to accompany an application under subsection (3) of section 13 of the Act shall be as per table 8/3 of this regulation:

Provided that the Chief Inspector may direct that no fee shall be payable in respect of a fresh application made in pursuance of sub-section (2) of section 19 of the Act.

Inspection fee for separately fired super-heater, re-heater and feed water heater shall be charged at the same rate.

For heat recovery steam generators with finned tubes, the fee shall be calculated on heating surface area of bare tubes and on one fourth of heating surface area of fins.

For renewal of certificate for a period of six months or less on the request of owner, half of applicable fee shall be payable.

Applicable fee shall be payable again for the re-inspection of a defective boiler after completion of repairs.

For inspection on Sundays or on any public holiday notified under the Negotiable Instrument Act, 1881 (26 of 1881), on the request of owner, additional fee equivalent to half of applicable fee shall be payable.
