306. Memorandum of Inspection Book .─ (1) A Memorandum of Inspection Book shall be prepared for each boiler in Form-XI and the Inspector shall enter all particulars in ink and dimensions of the boilers with the particulars of hydraulic test and steam test and his inspection notes in this book;

(2) At subsequent inspection, Competent Person shall enter the dates of the Inspections, hydraulic tests and steam tests, when such are made with their notes thereon;

(3) the Inspector or Competent Person, as the case may be, shall also enter in the Memorandum of Inspection Book, the general condition of the boiler and of repairs, to what extent boilers have been cleared of brick work, a report of all casual visits for inspection of repairs, for inspection of main steam pipes, and reports on accidents;

(4) (a) On submission of the Memorandum Book to the Chief Inspector, he will in the case of newly registered boilers, check all particulars and calculations and approve the design pressure that is to be permitted.

(b) In the case of old boilers, the Chief Inspector shall examine the Inspector's notes of inspection and proposals made for repairs or reduction of pressure.

(c) A pressure once approved for the boiler shall not be altered without the written authority of the Chief Inspector.

(d)The Memorandum of Inspection Book shall be kept clean and up-to-date and the Inspection Book except when actually required by the Competent Person, shall be kept in the office of the Chief Inspector.

307. Registration book.─ (1) A registration book or copy of memorandum of inspection book containing all the particulars required for registration shall be maintained in the office of the Chief Inspector in Form-XI and any orders passed by him regarding the boiler shall be entered in the registration book under his initials.

(2) The Chief Inspector shall also see that the note of subsequent inspections entered in the Memorandum of Inspection Book are copied in the Registration book.

308. Maintenance of records.─ (1) The Inspecting Authority shall maintain the records pertaining to the boiler or the economiser, as the case may be, including all the certificates provided in Form-I(Part-B), Form-I(PartC),Form-IV(Part-A), Form-IV(Part-B), Form-IV(Part-C), Form-IV(Part-D), Form-V(Part-A) and Form-XVI and the copies of the approved drawings for a period of at least three years after registration of the boiler and thereafter these records may be sent to the owner of the boiler by the Inspecting Authority.

(2) The owner of a boiler or economiser shall maintain the records pertaining to the boiler or economiser as the case may be, including the certificates provided in Form-I(Part-B), Form-I(Part-C), Form-IV(Part-B), FormIV(Part-C), Form-V(Part-A), and Form-XV and the copies of the approved drawing for the entire life of the boiler or economiser as the case may be.

(3) In the event of any transfer of the ownership, the records mentioned above shall be transferred to the new owner and the new owner will maintain the record in accordance with the provision of sub-regulation (2) above.

(4) The manufacturer of boiler or economiser or steam pipe shall maintain the record pertaining to the boiler or economiser or steam pipe, as the case may be, including the certificates provided in Form-I(Part-B), FormI(Part-C), Form-IV(Part-B), Form-IV(Part-C), Form-IV(Part-D), Form-V(Part-A), Form-V(Part-B) , FormXV, and Form-XVI and the copies of the approved drawings for a period of at least five years.

309. Transfer of memorandum of inspection book and registration book.─ On transfer of a boiler from one state to another, the memorandum of inspection book and the registration book shall, on the request of Chief Inspector of the state to which the boiler has been transferred, be forwarded to that officer who shall take over their custody and maintain them.

310. Grant of Certificate.─ (1) A certificate for the use of a boiler shall be granted in Form-XII.

(2) In the certificate, the design pressure at which the boiler shall be worked, the load to be placed on the safety valves or the thickness of the washers or ferrules required as safeguard against overloading and the date and pressure of the last hydraulic test of the boiler shall be entered.

(3) The Inspector's remarks shall be as brief as possible.

311. Procedure for inspection of installed boilers.─ (1) (a)It is essential to have every part of the boiler that is accessible, open and properly prepared for examination, internally and externally.

(b)All boilers have openings through which an examination may be made and for which operation are closed; all such parts shall be opened whether for access to water surfaces, or heater surfaces.

(c)In cooling a boiler down for inspection or repairs, the water should not be withdrawn until the setting is sufficiently cooled to avoid damage to the boiler and when possible allowed to cool down naturally.

(d)It is not necessary, in order to comply with ordinary prudence, to remove insulation material, masonry, or fixed parts of the boiler, unless defects or deterioration peculiar to certain types of in accessible parts of boilers are suspected and where there is moisture or vapour showing through the covering should be removed at once and a complete investigation made.

(e)Particular attention shall be paid to the external parts of boilers in the way of seating blocks, especially when the situation is damp. Saddle tanks and engine fittings of locomotive type boilers shall be removed to facilitate the inspection of the parts underneath at the first inspection, and at any reasonable period afterwards, if the Competent Person cannot otherwise satisfy himself as to the condition of those parts.

(f)Upon sufficient visible evidence or suspicion due to age or other causes, every effort shall be made to discover the true condition, even to the removal of insulating material, masonry or fixed parts of a boiler. Sometimes drilling or cutting away of parts is justifiable and necessary to positively determine this condition.

(2) The Competent Person shall, whenever the size permits, go inside it and make a thorough inspection of all its internal parts. Before doing so, he shall, satisfy himself that proper provision has been made for disconnecting the boiler from any other boiler under steam. In case he find that proper provision for disconnection has not been made or that the boiler has not been properly cleaned, or scaled, or that is unreasonably hot, he shall decline to proceed with the inspection and shall report the facts to the Inspecting Authority or Chief Inspector as the case may be, for orders. When a boiler is of such a size or its construction is such that the Competent Person cannot go inside it, there should be sufficient sight holes or hand holes provided to enable him to see the principal internal parts; if any important part of a boiler is so constructed that the Competent Person cannot examine it, he should report the facts to the Inspecting Authority or Chief Inspector as the case may be, for orders.

In the case of forced circulation and forced flow boilers which are not accessible to close visual inspection, the Competent Person shall, besides thorough examination, ensure by the flow of water that proper circulation is maintained through all sections of the water circuits.

(a)Upon entering a boiler, the Competent Person shall examine all surfaces of the exposed metal to observe the action caused by the use of water, oil scale solvents, or other substances which may have intentionally or unintentionally gone in with the feed water. Any evidence of oil is dangerous and immediate steps shall be taken to prevent any further entrance of oil into the boiler.

(b)Oil or scale in the tubes of water-tube boilers or on plates over the fire of any boiler is particularly bad, often causing them to rupture.

A given amount of corrosion along or immediately adjacent to a seam is more serious than a similar amount of corrosion in the solid plate away from the seams. Grooving along longitudinal seams is especially significant as grooving or cracks are likely to occur when the material is highly stressed. Severe corrosion is likely to occur at points where the circulation of the water is poor and such places shall be examined most carefully for evidences of corrosive action.

(6) If the Competent Person decides that a boiler in one or more of its parts is no longer fit for the pressure approved for it, he shall without delay report his proposal for reducing the pressure to the Chief Inspector and at the same time submit his calculation for the wasted parts for check and approval of pressure.

(7) With regard to the pitting and wasting of shell plates, the Competent Person shall bear in mind that thickness of shell plates may reduce to an appreciable extent and still be stronger than longitudinal seams.

(8) (a)All flanging shall be thoroughly inspected and particularly the flanges of circular and plates that are not stayed.

(b)Internal grooving in the fillet of such heads and external grooving in the outer surfaces of heads concave to pressure is very common since there is slight movement in the heads of this character which produces this kind of defect. Some types of boilers have what is known as the OG or reversed flange construction in some of their parts that may be inaccessible to the eye, but the condition shall be determined by the insertion of a mirror which at a proper angle will reflect back to the eye the condition of such a place, or any other feasible manner.

(9) (a)All stays, whether diagonal or through, shall be examined to note that they are in even tension.

(b) All fastened ends shall be examined to note whether cracks exist where the stays are punched or drilled or rivets or bolts and, if not found in proper tension, the Competent Person shall recommend their proper adjustment.

(10) (a)The manhole and other reinforcing plates, as well as nozzles or other connection flanged or screwed into a boiler, shall be examined internally as well as externally to see that they are not cracked or deformed, and wherever possible observation shall be made for the inside of the boiler, as to the thoroughness with which its pipe connections are made to the boiler.

(b)All openings to external attachments, such as water column connections, openings in dry pipes and opening to safety valves, shall be noted to see that they are free from obstructions.

(11) (a)Particular attention shall be given to the plate or tube surface exposed to the fire. The Competent Person shall observe whether any part of the boiler has become deformed during, operation by bulging or blistering; bulging is a distortion of the entire thickness of the plate or tube where it takes place, while the blistering is a lamination or separation of the plate due to foreign material being embedded in the ingot before the plate is rolled.

(b)If bulges or blisters are of such size as would seriously weaken the plate or tube and especially when the leakage is noted coming from those defects, the boiler shall be discontinued from service until the defective part or parts have received proper repairs.

(c) Careful observation shall be made to detect leakage from any portion of the boiler structures, particularly in the vicinity of seams and tube ends.

(d) Fire tubes sometimes blister but rarely collapse, the Competent Person shall look through the tubes for such defects and if they are found with a sufficient degree of distortion they shall be removed.

(12) (a) Lap joint boilers are apt to crack where the plates lap in the longitudinal or straight seam; if there is any evidence of leakage or other distress at this point, it shall be thoroughly investigated and, if necessary rivets removed or the plate slotted in order to determine whether cracks exist in the seam. Any cracks noted in shell plates are usually dangerous except fire cracks that run from the edge of the plate into the rivet holes of girth seams.

(b) A limited number of such fire cracks is not usually a very serious matter.

(13) The Competent Person shall test stay bolts by tapping one end of each bolt with a hammer and when practicable a hammer or other heavy tool shall be held on the opposite end to make the test more effective.

(14) (a) (i)Tubes in horizontal fire tube boilers deteriorate more rapidly at the ends toward the fire, and they shall be carefully tapped with a light hammer on their outer surface to ascertain whether there has been serious reduction in thickness.

(ii)The tubes of vertical tubular boilers are more susceptible to deterioration at the upper open ends when exposed to the products of combustion without water protection.

(iii)They shall be reached as far as possible either through the hand holes if any, or inspected at the ends.

(b)

(i)The surfaces shall be carefully examined to detect bulges or cracks, or any evidences or defective welds.

(ii)Where there is a strong draft, the tubes may become thinned by corrosion produced by the impingement or particles of fuel and ash, or the improper use of soot blowers.

(iii)A leak from a tube frequently causes serious corrosive action on a number of tubes in its immediate vicinity.

(c)

(i)Where short tubes or nipples are employed in joining drums or headers, there is a tendency for waste products of the furnace to lodge in the junction points and such deposits are likely to cause corrosion if moisture is present.

(ii)All such places shall be thoroughly cleaned and examined.

(15) (i)The ligaments between tube holes in the heads of all types of fire-tube boilers and in shells of water-tube boilers shall be examined.

(ii)If leakage is noted, it may denote a broken ligament.

(16) Steam pockets on surfaces are sometimes found in new or replacement work, and wherever, this is possible or likely, the Competent Person shall make observation and, if any are found, recommend the necessary changes.

(17) (a)(i) The steam and water pipes, including connections to the water column, shall be examined for leaks, and if any are found, it shall be determined whether they are the result of excessive strains due to expansion and contraction, or other causes.

(ii)The general arrangement of the piping in regard to the provisions, for expansion and drainage, as well as adequate support at the proper points, shall be carefully noted.

(iii)The location of the various stop valves shall be observed to see that water will not be pocketed when the valves are closed and thereby establish cause for water-hammer action.

(b)The arrangement of connections between individual boilers and the main steam pipes shall be especially noted to see that any change of position of the boiler due to setting or other causes, will not produce an undue strain on the piping.

(c) It shall be ascertained whether all pipe connections to the boiler possess the proper strength in their fastenings, whether tapped into the boiler, a fitting, or flange riveted to the boiler.

(d) (i)The Competent Person shall determine whether there is provision for the expansion and contraction of such piping, and that there is no undue vibration tending to crystalize the parts subjected to it.

(ii)This includes all steam and water pipe and special attention shall be given to the blow off pipes with their connections and fittings, because the expansion and contraction due to rapid change in temperature and water-hammer action create a great strain upon the entire blow off system, which is more pronounced when a number of blow off pipes are joined in one common discharge.

(iii)The freedom of the blow off connection on each boiler shall be tested whenever possible by opening the valve for a few seconds, at which time it can be determined whether there is excessive vibration. Blow off pipes should be free from external dampness to prevent corrosion.

(18) (a)(i)The piping to the water column shall be carefully noted to see that there is no chance of water being pocketed in the piping forming the steam connection to the water column. The steam-pipe shall preferably drain towards to the water column.

(ii)The water pipe connection to the water column must drain towards the boiler.

(b) (i)The relative position of the water column to the fire surfaces of the boiler shall be observed to determine whether the column is placed in accordance with the regulations. The attachments shall be examined to determine their operating condition.

(19) (a)In Water-Tube boilers, it shall be noted, as far as possible, whether or not the proper baffling is in place.

(b) In many types of boilers, the absence of baffling often causes high temperatures on portions of the boiler structure which are not intended to be exposed to such temperatures, from which a dangerous condition may result. The location of combustion arches with respect to tube surfaces shall be carefully noted. These are sometimes arranged so as to cause the flame to impinge on a particular part of a boiler and produces overheating of the material and consequent danger of the rupture of the part.

(20) Localisation of heat brought about by improper or defective burner or stoker installation or operation creating a blow pipe effect upon the boiler, shall be condemned.

(21) Where boilers are suspended, the supports and setting shall be carefully examined specially at points where the boiler structure comes near the setting walls or floor. Often accumulation of ash and soot will bind the boiler structure at such points and produce excessive strains on the structure owing to the expansion of the parts under operating conditions.

(22) (a) As the safety valves are the most important attachments on the boiler, they shall be inspected with the utmost caution. There shall be no accumulations of rust, scale, or other foreign substances located in the casings so as to interfere with the free operation of valves. The setting and freedom of the safety valves shall be tested preferably by raising the steam pressure to the blow-in-off point, or if this cannot be done, the valves shall be tested by means of the try levers to ascertain if they are free. Where the steam discharged from a safety valve is led through a pipe the Competent Person shall determine at the time the valve is operating whether or not the drain, opening in the discharge pipe is free and in accordance with the Regulations.

(b)If the Competent Person deems it necessary, in order to determine the freedom of discharge from a safety valve, the discharge connections shall be removed. Under no circumstances a stop valve shall be permitted between a boiler and its safety valve.

(23) The steam gauges on all boilers shall be removed and the Competent Person shall test them and compare their readings with a standard test gauge. The readings of the steam gauges shall be observed and compared when making an inspection with steam on the boiler, where several boilers are in service connected to a common steam main. The location of the steam gauges shall be noted to see whether or not it is exposed to high temperature either externally, as would be the case if placed close to the smoke flue or other high heated part of the boiler or setting, or exposed to heat internally due to lack of protection of the gauge spring with a proper syphon or trap to prevent steam from coming in contact with the spring. The Competent Person shall see that the provisions are made for blowing out the pipe leading to the steam gauge.

312. Calculation of wasted shell.─(1)When any part is wasted and the Competent Person is doubtful of its fitness for the pressure, he shall cause one or more small holes to be bored and from them ascertain the average thickness of the parts from which he can satisfy himself by calculation, in accordance with the formula applicable to the part. He shall enter such gauging and calculations in the Memorandum of Inspection Book.

In making calculations for a wasted part of a boiler shell, e.g., along the line of seating blocks of a Lancashire boiler, the Competent Person shall use the following formula: 2t x S P = ————

Equation (8/1) D x F Where, t = thickness of wasted plate, where thinnest in mm, S = minimum tensile strength of material of shell in Kg/mm2, D = internal diameter of shell in mm, F = factor of safety, which shall not be less than 4.

P = maximum allowable working pressure in Kg/mm2

313. Ageing of shell type boilers.─(1) In order to take the ageing effect on boilers, the design pressure as calculated from the formulae in these regulations shall be reduced as per the table given below:

TABLE 8/4 Age of boiler exceeding (in years) Design Pressure (Percentage) For those boilers, the plates of which have already been cut and tested shall be given a further lease of life of fifty years from the date of the test of the boilers. The design pressure that shall be allowed after the testing shall be reduced as per the table given below:

TABLE 8/5 Period after date of test (in years) Design pressure allowed (percentage)

(3) (a)Shell type Waste Heat Boilers(un-fired), on completion of a life of twenty-five years, shall be nondestructively tested by a Remnant Life Assessment Organization (RLAO) approved by Central Boilers Board.

(b) Pressure parts, such as shell, end- plates, stand pipes etc., of such boilers, shall be inspected by Ultrasonic testing, Magnetic particle testing and Dye Penetrant testing.

(c) If results are acceptable as per the standards laid down by the Central Boilers Board, a certificate shall be issued by the Chief Inspector or Director of Boilers as the case may be, for extending the life of the boiler for a further period of twelve years or such less period as recommended by the Remnant Life Assessment Organisation.

(d) The working pressure of such boilers may be reduced on the recommendation of the Remnant Life Assessment Organisation.

(e) The assessment or remnant life by non-destructive testing shall be carried out thereafter every twelve years by a Remnant Life Assessment Organisation (RLAO) approved by the Central Boilers Board. Such agency shall work in close coordination with the office of the Chief inspector or Director of Boilers as the case may be.

Note:

(1) It shall be at the option of the owner to opt for the provisions of sub-regulation (1) or subregulation (2) above or opt for the Remnant Life Assessment of boiler as per subregulation(3).

(2) Wherever, it is not possible to carry out non-destructively testing of boiler for Remnant Life Assessment, provisions of sub-regulation (1) or sub-regulation (2) shall be applicable.

314. Ageing of Water Tube Boilers.─(1) (a) The boilers which are operating at temperature of 400°C (main steam outlet temperature) and above shall be tested by the Remnant Life Assessment Organisation for the components as per Table 8/6 of regulation 315 after they are in operation for 100,000 hours for assessment of the remnant life of the components.

(b) If results are acceptable as per the standards laid down by the Central Boilers Board, a certificate shall be issued by the Chief Inspector or Director of Boilers as the case may be, for extending the life of the boiler for a further period of six years or such less period as recommended by the Remnant Life Assessment Organisation. This assessment of remnant life shall be carried out thereafter every six years by a Remnant Life Assessment Organisation approved by the Central Boilers Board. The Remnant Life Assessment Organisation shall work in close coordination with the office of the Chief Inspector or Director of Boilers as the case may be, in the field of remnant life assessment and extension. The design pressure of such boilers may be reduced on the recommendations of the Remnant Life Assessment Organisation;

(2) (a)The boilers which are operating at a temperature of less than 400°C (main steam outlet temperature) on completion of a life of twenty-five years shall be tested by the Remnant Life Assessment Organisation for the components as per Table 8/7 given in regulation 315 for assessment of the remnant life of the components.

(b) If results are acceptable as per the standards laid down by the Central Boilers Board, a certificate shall be issued by the Chief Inspector or Director of Boilers as the case may be, for extending the life of the boiler for a further period of twelve years or such less period as recommended by the Remnant Life Assessment Organisation. This assessment of remnant life shall be carried out thereafter every twelve years by a Remnant Life Assessment Organisation approved by the Central Boilers Board. The Remnant Life Assessment Organisation shall work in close coordination with the office of the Chief Inspector or Director of Boilers as the case may be, in the field of remnant life assessment and extension. The design pressure of such boilers may be reduced on the recommendations of Remnant Life Assessment Organisation.

(c) Notwithstanding anything contained in this regulation, for boilers working at a pressure less that 50 kg/cm2 (g) and temperature less than 400°C (main steam outlet temperature), such elaborate remnant life assessment is not mandatory. However, in such cases, drums and headers of such boilers, shall be inspected by Ultrasonic testing, Magnetic particle testing and Dye Penetrant test.

315. Ageing of heat recovery steam generators (HRSGs).─Heat Recovery Steam Generators (HRSGs) which are operating at a temperature of 400°C (main steam outlet temperature) and above shall be non-destructively tested by the Remnant Life Assessment Organisation for the components as per Table 8/8 given below under this rule after they are in operation for 100,000 hours for assessment of remnant life of the components.

316. Repairs to boiler and steam pipes.─(1) Major repairs or replacements such as tubes, coils, headers, drums, fusion welded patches to shells, fireboxes and end plates of boilers and extensive building up of wasted parts of boilers permitted under these regulations, the renewal of furnaces and end plates, parts of shell, fireboxes, girders and steampipes, etc., shall only be entrusted to a firm who is recognised as a repairer under these regulations.

(2) Any firm seeking recognition shall apply in 'Form-XIII' to the Chief Inspector of Boilers of the State in which the recognition is sought along with the fee specified in table 8/9.

(3) A firm seeking recognition as a repairer shall meet the following requirements depending upon the class of boilers, namely: —

(a) For Class I boiler repairer, the owner of the firm himself shall have a degree in mechanical or Production or Power Plant or Metallurgical engineering or the firm shall have on its rolls a permanently employed engineer having a degree in mechanical or Production or Power Plant or Metallurgical engineering and the said owner or the permanently employed engineer shall also have an experience of at least five years in an organisation engaged in fabrication, erection, repair or maintenance and quality control of boilers; and the firm shall also have qualified and permanently employed welders as per the provisions of these regulations;

(b) For Class II boiler repairer, the owner of the firm himself shall have a diploma in mechanical engineering or the firm shall have on its rolls a permanently employed engineer having a diploma in mechanical engineering and the said owner or the permanently employed engineer shall also have an experience of at least three years in an organisation engaged in fabrication, erection, repair or maintenance and quality control of boilers; and the firm shall also have qualified and permanently employed welders as per the provisions of these regulations;

(c) For Class III boiler repairer, the owner of the firm himself or a person permanently employed with the firm shall have a minimum three years' experience in repair, maintenance, operation, erection or inspection of boiler and the firm shall also have qualified and permanently employed welders as per the provisions of these regulations;

(d) For Special class boiler repairer, the owner of the firm himself shall have a degree in mechanical or welding engineering along with specialised experience in welding technology for a minimum period of three years or the firm shall have on its rolls a permanently employed engineer having a degree in mechanical or welding engineering along with specialised experience in welding technology for a minimum period of three years, and have permanently employed welders as per the provisions of these regulations who should also have valid certificate for carrying out Tungsten Inert Gas welding;

(e)(i) Where the power stations, fertilizer plants, chemical and petrochemical plants or refineries apply for repair of their own boilers, they shall have all the facilities in-house and may apply to the Chief Inspector of Boilers of the State for permission to repair their own boilers in accordance with these regulations;

(ii) On receipt of the application for permission to repair to a boiler, the Chief Inspector of Boilers of the State shall reply to the request within a period of two days of the receipt of the application.

(4) The Chief Inspector shall scrutinise and evaluate the application along with the replies to the questionnaire and after satisfying himself that the following requirements are fulfilled, shall recognise the firm as a repairer within a period of thirty days, in the category applied for, namely: —

(a) that the firm possesses rectifier or generator, grinder, general tools and tackles, dye-penetrant kit, expander and measuring instruments;

(b) that the electric arc or oxy-acetylene welding sets and all other tools and plant in his possession for carrying out repairs are suitable for the work undertaken;

(c) that the quality of material used conforms to the specifications prescribed in these regulations;

(d) that the supervisory and operational staff employed by the firm possesses the necessary training and experience for the work undertaken;

(e) that all welders employed by the firm possess certificates issued as required under Chapter-13 of these regulations;

(f) Standard of work should be of high order and comply with all the requirements and test that may be specified by the Chief Inspector.

(5) The recognition of the firm as a repairer shall be for a period of two years and thereafter it shall apply for renewal of its recognition at least two months before the expiry of said period.

(6) A repairer recognised in one State shall be eligible to work in any other State by getting his recognition endorsed from the Chief Inspector or Director of Boilers of the concerned State on making payment of fee as prescribed in this regulation.

(7) In case the repairer is found indulging in violating the provisions of the Act or regulations, the firm shall be blacklisted under intimation to Chief Inspector or Director of Boilers of all the States and Union territories and Secretary, Central Boilers Board, and renewal shall not be done in any case.

Class III 2,500/1,250/1,000/-

317. Procedure for repairs.─(1)The repair work shall be carried out under the supervision of Inspector or Competent Person and when the fireboxes and smoke tubes of locomotive tube boilers are withdrawn, the internal parts, which are otherwise inaccessible to close inspection, shall be inspected by the Inspector or Competent Person, as the case may be.

(2) Repair to boilers shells shall be carried out by patching or by removing a strip of worn or damaged plate and inserting the new strip with covering straps over the longitudinal butt ends, the strength of the riveted joints shall be not less than that of the longitudinal joints of the shell.

(3) (a) Patches for fire exposed plates shall be fitted metal to metal without joint of any description;

(b) The affected part shall be cut out, leaving the corners of the hole well-rounded.

(c) Patches shall be secured, wherever possible, properly spaced rivets with a width of plate at least equal to the diameter of rivet between the edge of the rivet hole and the edge of the plate.

(d) Where riveting is impracticable, the plate shall be secured by welt fitting countersunk headed screw pins.

(4) The thickness of a patch plate shall not be less than the original thickness of the plate which it is used to patch.

(5) Bulged or distorted furnaces or circular section may, if the bulge or distortion is not too great, be pressed back to shape.

(6) Circular furnaces of horizontal boilers that have become distorted may be suitably reinforced.

(7) (a) Anti-collapse rings shall be substantial section either of single or double angles bolted back to back with screw stays not less than 22 mm in diameter and about 178 mm in pitch passed though flat of angle and screwed into the furnace, the ends being rounded over on the fire side and fitted with nuts at the other.

(b) The stay bolts shall be fitted with ferrules not less than 25 mm in depth between furnace and angle ring.

(8) Welding shall not be accepted for the repair of any part of a boiler for which welding is forbidden for a new boiler under these regulations and boiler shells shall not be repaired by welding beyond the filling up of a small isolated corroded or pitted part or the making up of wasted edges of openings.

(9) Cracks or grooving in dished or flat end plates of cylindrical shells or in the bends of furnace flanges in a circumferential direction may be weed out and welded.

(10) Wasted parts of circular furnaces and fireboxes and fire exposed flat plates as in rectangular fireboxes and combustion chambers may be cut out and be replaced by new pieces welded in or they may be built-up by welding.

(11) Longitudinal cracks in circular furnaces and fireboxes and cracks in rectangular fireboxes and combustion chambers may be welded.

(12) No stay bar shall be welded.

(13) Smoke tubes may be butt-welded either by fusion welding, flush welding or oxy-acetylene welding, and these may be tested hydraulically at the discretion of the Inspecting Authority.

318.

Procedure for welding during repairs. The repairer shall satisfy the inspecting Authority :-

(1) That the electric arc or oxy-acetylene welding sets and all other tools and plant in his possession for carrying out repairs are suitable for the work undertaken.

(2) That the quality of material used conforms to the specification that are prescribed.

(3) That the supervisory and operational staff employed by him possess the necessary training and experience for the work under taken.

(4) That all welders employed by him possess certificates issued as required under Chapter-13 of these Regulations.

(5) Standard of work shall be of high order and comply with all the requirements and test that may be prescribed by the Chief Inspector.

319. Riveting and other repairs.─ The repairer shall satisfy the Chief Inspector:-

(1) That he possesses the necessary equipment and tools and engages trained and experienced workmen and staff for the work underwork taken.

(2) Standard of work shall be of a high order and comply with all the requirements and test that may be prescribed by the Chief Inspector, and all material used shall be boiler quality.

320. Miscellaneous provisions for repairs.─ (1) (a)The repairs shall be supervised, so far as his other duties permit by the Competent Person.

(b) When the fire boxes and smoke tubes of locomotive tube boilers are withdrawn, the opportunity shall be taken to inspect the internal parts which are otherwise inaccessible to close inspection.

(2) Repairs to boiler shells shall be effected either by patching or by removing a strips of worn or damaged plate and inserting the new strip with covering straps over the longitudinal butt ends, the strength of the riveted joints shall be not less than that of the longitudinal joints of the shell.

(3) (a) Patches for fire exposed plates shall be fitted metal to metal without joint of any description. The affected part shall be cut out, leaving the corners of the hole rounded.

(b)Patches shall be secured, wherever possible, properly spaced rivets with a width of plate at least equal to the diameter or rivet between the edge of the rivet hole and the edge of the plate.

(c)Where riveting is impracticable, the plate shall be secured by welt fitting countersunk headed screw pins.

(4) The thickness of a patch plate shall not be less than the original thickness of the plate which it is used to patch.

(5) Bulged or distorted furnaces of circular section may, if the bulge of distortion is not too great, be pressed back to shape.

(6) Circular furnaces horizontal boilers that have become distorted may be suitably reinforced.

(7) Anti-collapse rings shall be of substantial section either of single or double angles bolted back to back with screw stays not less than 22 mm in diameter and about 178 mm in pitch passes through flat of angle and screwed into the furnace, the ends being rounded over on the fire side and fitted with nuts at the other. The stay bolts shall be fitted with ferrules not less than 25 mm in depth between furnace and angle ring.

(8) Welding shall not be accepted for the repairs of any part of a boiler for which welding is forbidden for a new boiler under these Regulations. Boiler shells shall not be repaired by welding beyond the filling up of a small isolated corroded or pitted or the making up of wasted edges of openings.

(9) Cracks or grooving in dished or flat end plate of cylindrical shells or in the bends of furnace flanges in circumferential direction may be weed out and welded.

(10) Wasted parts of circular furnaces and fire boxes and fire exposed flat plates as in rectangular fire boxes and combustion chambers may be cut out and be replaced by new pieces welded in or they may be built up by welding. Longitudinal cracks in circular furnaces and fire-boxes and cracks in rectangular fire-boxes and combustion chambers may be welded.

(11) No Stay bar shall be welded.

(12) For the purposes of these Regulations renewed part shall be deemed to be parts of a new boiler intended for use at the pressure at which the boiler under renewal is used.

(13) For Butt Welding of Smoke Tubes, Smoke tubes may be butt welded either by fusion welding, flush welding or oxy-acetylene welding, and these should be tested hydraulically at the discretion of the Chief Inspector.

321.Submission of manufacturing drawings and the particulars of boilers in advance.─ (1) In the case of land boilers and boiler components including spares made in India for use in the States, the manufacturing drawings and the particulars of materials, design and construction of boilers shall be submitted by the makers of the boilers or boiler components or spares, as the case may be to the Inspecting Authority of the State, where the principal parts of the boilers will be manufactured, for examination and approval before commencement of the manufacture of boilers.

(2) In the case of boilers and boiler components including spares made outside India for use in the State, the manufacturing drawings and particulars of the material, design and construction, of boilers shall be submitted initially to the Inspecting Authority in the country of manufacture for examination and approval.

(3) The manufacturing drawings and the particulars of materials, design and construction in the case of fired boilers of steam generating capacity 100 Tons/hour and above and that of Waste heat boiler of 20 Tons/hour and above including boiler components and spares thereof shall then be submitted to the Technical Adviser (Boilers) for selection of latest materials with high strengths and use in high temperature and pressure, for the safety.

Thereafter, the manufacturing drawings and the particulars of materials, design and construction approved by the Inspecting Authority shall be sent to the Chief Inspector of Boilers of the State where the boiler will be installed and used, for final examination and approval before commencement of manufacture so as to avoid questions arising at the time of erection and registration of the boiler.

(4) The manufacturing drawings and the particulars of materials, design and construction in the case of fired boilers of steam generating capacity less than 100 Tons/hour and that of Waste heat boiler of less than 20 Tons/hour including boiler components and spares thereof approved by the Inspecting Authority shall be submitted to the Chief Inspector of Boilers of the State where the boiler will be installed and used, for final examination and approval before commencement of manufacture so as to avoid question arising at the time of erection and registration of the boiler.

(5) The Inspecting Authority or the Chief Inspector or both, as the case may be, shall, after examination of the manufacturing drawings and the particulars intimate to the proposer whether they are satisfied with materials, design, construction and fitness of the parts for the intended pressure and, if not, what modification is necessary therein. When the manufacturing drawings and the particulars of boilers have been approved, the Inspector or Competent Person, as the case may be, in making his examination shall see that the design and the particulars of construction as approved have been carefully followed and that the material corresponds with the approved particulars.

(6) The fees for the scrutiny of the manufacturing drawings and the particulars of the materials, design and construction of boilers and boiler components including spares under sub-regulations (1) or (2) shall be on the scale prescribed in Table8/3 subject to a maximum of Rs. 50,000. When the manufacturing drawings have been scrutinised and in respect of them alterations have been suggested and the same are resubmitted for scrutiny, separate fee at the rate of ten percent of the fee for the first scrutiny of the drawing shall be payable, if the manufacturing drawings contain alterations other than those previously suggested.

(7) The arrangement drawings showings the broad details of modifications or conversions of the existing boiler shall be subject to the approval of the Chief Inspector of Boilers of the State where the boiler is intended for modification or conversion is registered.

(8) If the detailed manufacturing drawings are got approved by the Inspecting Authority of the State where parts will be manufactured and inspected, no separate compliance of sub-regulation (7) is required.

322. Inspection of pipes.─ (1) Pipes shall be inspected and hydraulically tested before use in place, in test pressure to be that prescribed in the standard conditions for steam pipes. A certificate from the Inspecting Authority in Form-IV(Part-B), stating that this has been done by him may be accepted. If the Competent Person is satisfied with the test, the pipes may be erected in position. The Chief Inspector shall intimate his approval to the owner regarding the suitability of the pipe line for use at the pressure and temperature to be specified.

(2) At subsequent inspection of the boiler or at any other time, the Competent Person may make an external inspection of the steam-pipes and for this purpose may require a part of the lagging at the flanges to be removed, and the pipe made bare. If as a result of this inspection the Competent Person is of opinion that the pipes or any positions of then are in an unsatisfactory condition he shall report the matter to the Chief Inspector, who may require the whole of the lagging to be removed and may require any pipe or pipes to be hydraulically tested. The test pressure at such hydraulic test shall be not less than one and half times, the design pressure of the boiler or the maximum allowable working pressure of pipe as the case may be.

(3) (a) A memorandum of Inspection Book shall be prepared in respect of steam pipe and fittings in Form-XIV. In this book, the Inspector shall enter in ink, all particulars and dimensions of the steam pipe and fitting with calculations for the various parts in detail, particulars of hydraulic test and his inspection notes.

(b) At subsequent inspection, the Competent Person shall enter the date of the inspection and hydraulic test together with notes thereon.

(c) The Competent Person shall also enter in the Memorandum of Inspection Book remarks regarding the general condition of the steam pipes and fittings, and repairs, replacements of alterations, if any, and the extent to which the steam pipes have been cleaned of lagging and covering so that the book provides a useful record of the steam-pipes and fittings for the information and guidance of Competent Person at subsequent inspection.

(d) In the event of an accident to a pipe line or its fittings, necessary remarks shall also be entered in the book.

(e) The Memorandum Book shall be submitted to the Chief Inspector, who will check all particulars and calculations and approve the maximum allowable working pressure and temperature that are to be permitted for the various parts of the pipe line and fittings. The pressure and temperature once approved for the particular steam pipe line shall not be altered except on a written authority from the Chief Inspector.

323. Submission of plans of steam-pipes.─ (1) Plans of steam pipes including mountings and fittings shall be submitted with the prescribed fee to the Inspecting Authority before construction and to the Chief Inspector at the time of registration of the boiler for his decision whether the pipes and their arrangement comply with the regulations.

(a) The fees for examination of plans and the particulars of materials, design and construction of steam pipes before the commencement of manufacture of the steam pipes shall be at the rate specified in Table – 8/11.

(b) The fees for examination of plans and the particulars of materials, design and construction of any fitting, mountings and for fittings like de-superheaters, steam receivers, separators, fee shall be as specified in Table 8/11.

327. Fees for inspection of components of valves.─ The fees for inspection of components of valves shall be charged as under:

(1) 50% of the inspection fees of the valves shall be charged by the chief inspector of the state where the components of the valves, are manufactured, for inspection of the components manufactured in a State other than the State where the valves are assembled and remaining 50% of the inspection fees shall be charged by the chief inspector of the state, where the valves are assembled, for inspection of assembled, and final inspection of the valves.

(2) Where the inspection, so far as the material testing is concerned, has been carried out by a "well Known Foundry" or "Well Known Forge" full inspection fees shall be charged by the chief inspector of the state where the valves have been assembled and finally tested.

(3) Where the components of the valves are inspected and tested in the same manufacturing work where the valves are assembled and finally tested, full inspection fees shall be charged by the chief inspector of the state where the manufacturing work is located.

(4) In case, in a state, components are manufactured by one firm and finally fitting and assembly of the valves are made by another, both the firms shall pay inspection fees on 50: 50 basis.

328. Inspection fee for feed water heaters and other fittings.─ Inspection fee for feed water heaters and other fittings shall be as specified in Table 8/15 given below,Table 8/15 Inspection fee for feed water heaters and other fittings Category Product Calculation Criteria Inspection Input No.

329. Markings.─ (1) Carbon steel tubes or pipes which are both over 50 mm outside diameter or over 1000 mm in length shall be marked legibly to show-

(a) The identification symbol for the type of the steel.

(b) The brand of the manufacture; and

(c) Whether seamless or electric resistance welded tubes or pipes.

(2) In the case of Carbon steel tubes or pipes less than 50 mm outside diameter or less than 1000 mm in length, the information specified in sub-regulation(1) shall be marked on a tag securely attached to the bundle or box.

(3) For alloy steel tubes or pipes markings shall not be stamped on the body of the tubes or pipes. The information specified in sub-regulation (1) shall be stamped on the end face of plain end pipes or on the rims of flanges or on identification plates suitably attached to the pipes or tubes. Inspection or identification marks may be painted on the alloy steel tubes or pipes provided iron oxide base or titanium base paints are used.

(4) Tubes or pipes conforming to a foreign standard or code, which have been accepted under regulation 3(2), marking shall be stamped in accordance with the provision of that standard or code, as the case may be.

330. Inspection fee for spares and boiler components.─ Inspection fee for spares and boiler components shall be as specified in Table 8/16 as given below,Table 8/16 Inspection fee for spares and boiler components Category Product Calculation Inspection fee Input No. criteria in Rs.

Heating Surface Fee specified Area in regulation Heating surface area specified in drawing Spares and Boiler Components (All types of Coils namely Economiser Coils, Superheater Coils, Reheater Coils) Tube and tube Bends Per Meter (Min Rs. 500) Length in Meter NOTE : Wherever boiler components are outsourced by the main boiler manufacturer for manufacturing in vendor works, Inspection fees need not be paid by the vendor if the inspection fees are paid by the main boiler manufacturer.

331. Fees for examination of welders or welding operators.─ (1) Fees for examination of welders or welding operators under these regulations shall be Rs. 600 and this shall be borne by the applicant or sponsor, as the case may be and in addition, the applicant or sponsor shall bear all other expenses like cost of material and workshop and testing facilities as fixed by the Competent Authority.

(2) The fee for endorsement of welder's certificate shall be Rs. 200 and will be borne by the applicant or sponsor, as the case may be.

(3) Fee for issue of duplicate certificate shall be Rs. 300.

332. Penalty.─(1)Whoever employs a person in welding a boiler component or steam-pipe in contravention of these regulations, shall be punishable with penalty as per section 31 of the Act.

(2) Any manufacturer of repairer who fails to maintain the records as required under regulation 542 shall be punishable with penalty as per section 31 of the Act.

CHAPTER-9 SAFETY OF PERSONS INSIDE BOILERS

333.Safety of persons working inside boilers.─ (1) The owner or person in charge shall not compel or allow any person to go inside a steam boiler for any purpose whatsoever unless the boiler is effectively disconnected from any steam or hot water communication with any other boiler, in the manner provided in this regulation.

(2) (a) Effective disconnection shall be made either by the removal of the boiler stop valve or of a length of piping from all steam and hot water connection with any other boiler, vessel or pipe containing steam or hot water by the insertion of substantial blank flanges between the boiler stop valves and piping.

(b) The shutting of a stop valve, stop cock or automatic isolating valve shall not be deemed to compliance with this regulation.

(c) In the case of welded pipe work, however, if a vent pipe of not less than 50 mm internal diameter (bore) is fitted between the main stop valve and the intermediate stop valve on the steam main and between the feed check valve and intermediate check valve on the feed line, this regulation shall be considered to have been complied with, provided such vent pipes are fitted with bolted-on blank flanges and the flanges are removed so as to effectively discharge any leakage steam or water to the atmosphere which the valves on either side of it have been closed.

(3) The owner of a boiler to which this regulation is applicable shall obtain the approval of the Chief Inspector in writing to the method of disconnection which he proposes to use and shall be responsible for ensuring that the method so approved is followed in practice.

(4) When electric light is used for work inside a boiler shell or drum or any confined space within a boiler, the voltage shall not exceed 24 volts and the owner of the boiler shall provide a hand lamp with lamp-guard, keyless socket, insulated handle and extension cord of approved type.

(5) Where power is used for working any equipment inside a boiler the metallic portion of the equipment shall be effectively earthed.

CHAPTER-10 ELECTRODE BOILERS PART-1

334. Application.─ This Chapter applies to electrode steam boilers for all voltages for any working pressure and for temperatures not exceeding 343°C and electrode boilers shall only be of the following construction:

(a) Fusion welded steel boilers;

(b) Seamless shell steel boilers.

335. General requirements.─ Wherever applicable, the provisions of Chapter-1 relating to certificates from makers, Inspecting Authorities etc., and of Chapter-3 relating to construction shall be followed.

336. Materials.─ The material specifications for structural parts of electrode boilers shall comply with Chapter-2, as regards the process of manufacture, chemical composition, mode of manufacture and test, and the certificates for the steel plates, provided that the ultimate tensile stress and elongation of materials shall be between the following limits:-

(a) For fusion welded construction, as mentioned in Chapter-2.

(b) For seamless construction as mentioned in regulation 250 and for seamless carbon steel pipes as mentioned in Table 7/3 of regulation 253.

PART-2

337. Construction and workmanship of fusion welded boilers.─ Construction and workmanship shall comply with the provision made in chapter-4 for fusion welded drums subject to the following conditions, namely:-

(a) Class I boilers shall comply with the requirement of chapter-4;

(b) For end plate connection by welding, the type of seam shown in Fig. 10/1 of this regulation may be adopted;

(c) Class II boilers shall comply with the following requirements;

(d) The longitudinal seam shall comply with regulation 152 and for end plate connections by welding, any of the following types of seams may be adopted (see Figs. 10/1 to 10/6 of this regulation).

TYPES OF WELDED JOINTS FOR CIRCUMFERENTIAL SEAMS – ACCEPTABLE FOR ALL CLASSES OF BOILERS Fig. 10/4 TYPES OF WELDED JOINTS FOR FLAT END PLATES FOR CLASS II BOILERS NOT Fig. 10/1 EXCEEDING 508 MM DIAMETER Fig. 10/2 Fig. 10/5 Fig. 10/3 Fig. 10/6 All dimensions are in mm.

Notes:

1. Class I Boilers, the design pressure of which exceeds 11 kg/cm2 (g) or the product of the design pressure in kg/cm2 (g)and the internal diameter exceeds 1400 centimeters.

2. Class II Boilers, the design pressure of which is 11 kg/cm2 (g) and below or the product of the design pressure in kg/cm2 (g)and the internal diameter is below 1400 centimeters .

3. Types of welded joints for circumferential seams acceptable for seamless shell boilers and for class II welded boiler for plates over 19 mm thick with gap between the edges of the plates shall be agreed upon by the Inspecting Authority and the manufacturer.

PART-3 TESTS FOR CLASS I FUSION WELDED SEAMS

338. Selection of test pieces.─(1) From the test plate of plates on each longitudinal seam, test pieces shall be selected for the following test, the specimens being cut out as shown in Fig. 10/7 of this regulation and stamped by the Competent Person for identification:

(a) One Tensile test specimen for the welded seam;

(b) Two bend test specimens;

(c) One nick break test specimen

(2) The remainder of each set of test plates shall be retained for any retest required and any specimen for retest shall be cut from the same set of test plates as the original specimen.

Fig. 10/7: Details of Test Plates

(3) Surface of tensile and bend specimens corresponding with the outside and inside of the shell shall be only lightly dressed so that the rolled surface of the parent metal is not wholly removed, except that where the rolled surfaces of the abutting plates are not level with one another, one plate may be machined at each face of the weld provided the depth of metal removed does not exceed 0.8 mm.

339. Tensile Test.─ (a) The specimen shall be cut out transversely to the welded seam (see Fig. 10/7 of regulation 338) and shall be of the full thickness of the plate at the welded seam and the breadth "W" shall be as large as the testing machine will reasonably allow, provided the effective cross-sectional areas is not less than 322.5 sq.mm (see Fig. 10/8 below).

(b) The ultimate tensile stress of the welded seam specimen shall be not less than the lower limit specified for the plate (see under regulation 37).

Fig. 10/8: Tensile Test for Joint

340. Bend Test.─ (1) (a) Two bend tests shall be made for welded seams.

(b) One specimen shall be tested with the outer surface or the weld in tension, and the other with the inner surface in tension.

(c) The specimen shall be rectangular in section and shall be cut out transversely to the weld so as to have a width of not less than 1½ times the thickness of the plate.

(d) The sharp corners of the specimens shall be rounded to a radius not exceeding ten percent of the thickness of the specimen.

(2) (a) Where the plate thickness does not exceed 32 mm, the thickness of the specimen shall be equal to the full thickness of the test plate.

(b) Where the plate thickness exceeds 32 mm, the thickness of the specimen shall be at least 32 mm.

(c) The specimen to be tested with the outer surface of the weld in tension shall be prepared by cutting to waste the metal local to the inner surface of the weld, so that the desired specimen thickness is obtained.

(3) (a) The specimen to be tested with the inner surface in tension shall be prepared by cutting to waste the metal local to the outer surface of the weld so that the desired metal thickness is obtained.

(b) Where the thickness of the plate permits, both specimen being located in the plate one above the other.

(c) Each specimen shall be mounted with the weld midway between the support, set apart at a distance of not more than 5.2 times the thickness of the specimen and pushed through the supports with a former having a diameter equal to three times the thickness of the specimen (see Fig. 4/6 of regulation 160 of chapter 4).

(4) (a) On completion of the test, no crack or defect at the outer surface of the specimen shall be greater than

1.5 mm, measured across the specimen or 3 mm measured along the length of the specimen.

(b) Premature failure at corners of the specimen shall not be considered cause for rejection (see Fig. 4/6, specimen A).

341. Nick Break Test.─ (a) The specimen shall be rectangular in section and cut transversely to the well so as to have a width not less than one-half times its thickness.

(b) The slot shall be cut in one side of the specimen through the center of the weld and perpendicular to the outer face of the vessel.

(c) The specimen shall then be broken in the weld and the fracture shall reveal a sound homogeneous-weld, substantially free from slag inclusions, porosity and coarse crystallinity.

342. Retests.─ In case any of the tests fail, two retests shall be made on specimens cut from the same plate and both retests shall meet the specified requirements.

343. Specimens after Tests.─ If required by the inspecting Authority, the specimens after test shall be forwarded for examination.

344. Heat Treatment.─ All fusion welded electrode boilers shall be stress relieved by heat treatment and the heat heat-treatment shall be in accordance with regulation 123.

345. Hydraulic Test.─ Hydraulic Test shall comply with regulation 167.

346. Determination for maximum allowable working pressure.─ (1)The maximum allowable working pressure of cylindrical shells with fusion welded seam shall be calculated as per the following formula:

(t - 2) x S x c Equation (10/1) P = —————————

D Where, t = thickness of shell plate in thirty- seconds of an inch, D = internal diameter of shell in inches, S = Ultimate tensile stress in tons per square inch, c = 32 for Class I boilers, c = 27 for Class II boilers,

(2) In no case, the thickness of cylindrical shells with fusion welded seams shall be less than those in table below:

Table 10/1: Minimum Thickness for fusion Welded Shells Serial No.

Class Internal diameter (mm) Minimum thickness (mm) II

(a) Upto and including 610

(b) Over 610 upto and including 914

(c) Over 914

(3) In no case, the factor of safety of the cylindrical shell and ends shall be less than 4.

347. Welds and compensation for manholes and branches.─ Welded compensating ring fitted to manhole and over opening shall conform to Figs. 10/9 to 10/13.

Fig. 10/10: Internal Compensating Ring Fig.10/9: External Compensating Ring Fig. 10/12: Elliptical Pressed Manhole in Dished Fig. 10/11: Elliptical Manhole Flanged Frame Welded End Plate to Shell Fig. 10/13: Elliptical Manhole Frame Welded to Dished End Plate All dimensions are in mm.

348. Manholes and other opening in shells.─ (a) Manholes and other opening in boilers shall be placed away from any welded seam and Oval opening shall be arranged with their minor axis parallel to the longitudinal center line of the boiler.

(b) Un-compensated and compensated openings shall comply with regulation 179.

349. Dished end plates with pressure on concave side.─ This shall comply with regulations174, 176 and 177.

350. Unstayed flat end plates.─ (1) The working pressure of unstayed flat-end plates shall be determined as per the following formula: 2S(t - 2)² Equation (10/2) P = ——————D² where, t = thickness of plate in thirty-seconds of an inch, P = working pressure in pounds per square inch, D = diameter in inches of the pitch circle of the bolts or rivets when the plate is attached to an outside flange or the internal diameter of the shell when the plate is attached to an inside flange, S = minimum tensile stress of the plate in tons per square inch.

(2) In no case the thickness of an unstayed flat-end plate shall be less than 10 mm.

(3) Compensation is not required where an unstayed flat-end plate is pierced for electrodes or other fittings by opening having a diameter not greater than 63 mm and the pitch of the openings is not less than four times of the diameter of the openings.

(4) Where the diameter of the opening in an unstayed flat end plate is greater than 63 mm or the pitch is less than four times the diameter of the holes, full compensation shall be provided as in regulation179.

351. Seating and Mounting.─ (1) Where the working pressure is not greater than 18 Kg/Cm², mountings having screwed ends not exceeding 19 mm, standard pipe thread may be used.

(2) The mountings may be screwed.─

(a) directly into the boiler shell plates, nuts being fitted on the waterside; or

(b) into steel distance piece (see Fig. 10/14) the length of thread engaged being is no case less than the bore of the mounting plus 6 mm.

Fig. 10/14: Typical mild steel Distance piece for mountings

(3) (a) The distance piece shall be made solid from mild steel and shall be screwed into the boiler pieces and fitted with nuts on the water side.

(b) The walls of the distance plates shall be not less than 6 mm in thickness at the bottom of the thread.

(4) Where castings are fabricated by fusion welding, they shall be stress-relieved heat treatment before attachment to the boiler.

(5) Method of attachment by welding of connectors to shells are shown in Figs 10/15 to 10/19 of this regulation.

(6) (a) Water gauges and pressure gauge syphons may be attached direct to the boiler shell or ends without the intervention of a pad or standpipe, provided they are flanged and secured by studs.

(b) Nuts of full thickness shall be fitted on inside of the boiler, if the studs are screwed through the plate.

(7) Where the design pressure exceeds 18 Kg/cm² or the nominal bore of the mounting exceeds 19 mm, mountings shall be attached by short standpipes or pads.

(8) Flanges and pads in contact with the boiler shall be formed to bed closely to the plate to which they are attached.

(9) (a) (i) Where short standpipes are used, the flanges shall be machined on the jointing and bolting surfaces and shall be close to the plate.

(ii) The caulking edge of the flange shall be machined or flame cut by machine.

(iii) Where separate flanges are fitted they shall be attached as shown in Figs. 7/10,7/26, 7/27 and 7//32 of chapter 7.

(b) Standpipes fabricated by fusion welding shall be stress-relieved by heat treatment before attachment to the boiler. Where the outside diameter of a standpipe is not greater than 127 mm plus twice the thickness of the shell plate in mm, the standpipe may be secured by fusion welding in the manner shown in Figures 10/20 and 10/21 of chapter 10 without subsequent stress-relieving by heat treatment.

(c) Where minimum weld attachment for standpipes bore is 127 mm and under, with plate thickness(Ts) equal to 1.5 Tn or greater as shown in Fig. 10/20 and 10/21, minimum weld attachments for standpipes shall not require compensating plates.

(d) Where the outside diameter of the standpipe exceeds 127 mm plus twice the thickness of the shell plate, the standpipe may be secured by fusion welding as shown in Figs. 10/22, 10/23, 10/24 and 10/25 of chapter 10 and the standpipe and the entire plate to which it is attached shall be stress-relieved by heat treatment.

(e) Standpipes shall be designed to withstand the internal pressure, but additional thickness may be required for:

(i) Compensation (see regulation 179).

(ii) Stresses imposed on the standpipe by expansion and contraction of externally attached pipe work.

(f) The thickness of the standpipe shall be not less than 6 mm or that given by the following formulae, whichever is the greater:

(i) for working pressure upto and including 250 lb./sq. in., t = D+4

(ii) for working pressure above 250 lb./sq. in., t = 1.4 D + 6 where, t = thickness of standpipe in thirty-seconds of an inch, D= outside diameter of the standpipe in inches.

352. Hydraulic Test.─ The hydraulic test shall comply with sub-regulation (1) of regulation 298.

PART-4 SEAMLESS SHELL BOILERS

353. Determination of maximum allowable working pressure.─(1) The maximum allowable working pressure shall be determined as per with regulation 346 except the minimum thickness of the shell shall be as mentioned in the following table: - Table 10/2 Serial No.

Internal diameter in mm Minimum thickness in mm

1.

Upto and including 610

6.3

2.

Over 610 upto and including 914

3.

Over 914

(2) In no case the factor of safety of the cylindrical shell and ends shall be less than 4.

(3) The value of "c" in the equation shall be taken as 35.

(4) Where the ends are welded to shell or mechanically secured and welded, they shall be stress-relived.

354. End Plates.─ (1) The end plates shall comply with regulations 174,176 and 177 or 350 as the case may be depending upon they are dished or flat .

(2) When end plates are mechanically secured and welded to seamless shell as shown in Figs. 10/4 and 10/5 of chapter 10 and the welded are stress-relived, the pressure and other limitations specified in respect of class II boilers in regulation 337 shall not apply.

355. Manholes and others opening in the shells.─ These shall comply with regulation 179 .

356. Mounting, fittings and connections.─(1) For all types of electrode boilers, mounting, fitting and connections shall comply with regulation 181 for all types of electrode boilers.

(2) For electrode boilers, one means of indicating water level of the tubular glass type water level gauge and one means of feeding the boilers shall be sufficient and the safety valves shall be spring loaded type.

(3) If an Electrode boiler is fitted with a magnetic valve, the said valve will be accepted in lieu of the second safety valve provided the source of power supply to both the valves and the boiler are the same and both are operated by one signal switch.

CHAPTER -11 STANDARD CONDITIONS FOR THE DESIGN AND CONSTRUCTION OF ECONOMISERS, FEED PIPES, FEED HEATERS AND OTHER SIMILAR VESSELS PART-1 ECONOMISERS

357. Application of standard condition and exceptions thereto.─ (1) An economiser shall not be registered under sub-section (5) of section 12 of the Act and a certificate shall not be issued under sub-section (6) of that section with reference to an economiser unless the standard conditions in respect of material design and construction, which are specified in this chapter are satisfied in respect of such economiser.

(2) Notwithstanding anything contained in sub regulation (1), the chief Inspector may, subject to the provisions of regulation 359, register an economiser and order the issue of a certificate authorising the use thereof, although the standard conditions are not fully satisfied in respect of such economiser.

358. General requirements.─ (1) All cast iron and steel headers and parts used in the assembly of an economiser shall conform to the requirements of this Chapter in respect of material specification and test workmanship and structural requirements.

(2) All economisers shall be constructed under the supervision of an Inspecting Authority and shall be so certified by that authority.

(3) A certificate from the Inspection Authority in Form—XV certifying that material was tested and the economizers built under their supervision shall be furnished to the chief inspector before or with the first application for registration.

(4) In advance or along with application for registration, the following shall be furnished:-

(a) A certificate of manufacture and test in Form-XVI signed by the maker or by a responsible representative of the Maker, containing the description of the economiser, particulars of the materials used in its construction and the dimensions of the several parts with the declaration that the limits of tensile breaking strength and tests comply with the standard conditions.

(b) A certificate from the maker of the material stating the tensile breaking strength and the elongation provided that if the maker and manufacturer be the same, the manufacturer's precise statements showing the above information shall be accepted.

(5) To suit owners' convenience, wherever possible it is recommended that economiser be installed in two or more sections.

359. Modification of formula.─ Where no certificate is produced, the maximum allowable working pressure as found by formula will be reduced by 10%. However, when the workmanship is doubtful in any way and the chief Inspector is not satisfied that any of the foregoing conditions would be sufficient to meet the circumstances, he may at his discretion reduce the maximum allowable working pressure by such percentage as he deems fit.

360. Maker's certificate for steel economisers.─ The maker shall furnish a certificate to the Competent Person in the following form:

We hereby certify that the material described below has been made by the Open Hearth or an Electric process acid/basic and has been satisfactorily tested in the presence of the Competent Person in accordance with the standard Tests.

361. Hydraulic test for new economisers.─(1) The hydraulic test for all new economisers shall be conducted as per Table11/1 given below,─ Table 11/1 Serial No.

On components before assembly Hydraulic test pressure in Maximum allowable working pressure in Twice Maximum allowable working pressure.

1.

Cast Iron tubes, headers and bends Minimum pressure 49.

2.

Steel tubes and bends 35 or less

3.

Steel tubes and bends Above 35 but not exceeding 70 Twice the Maximum allowable working pressure

4.

Steel tubes and bends Above 70 above the Maximum allowable working pressure

5.

Headers Below 70 Twice the Maximum allowable working pressure

6.

Headers Above 70 Maximum allowable Working

(2) For all assembled sections, the hydraulic test pressure shall not be less than 1.5 times of the maximum allowable working pressure.

(3) Maximum allowable working pressure shall be the highest pressure at which the economiser relief valves are to be set.

(4) The above test pressure shall be held for a minimum period of ten minutes.

362. Workmanship and manufacture.─ All materials used in the construction of pressure parts shall be tested and shall conform to the following requirements:-

(i) The workmanship shall be of highest standard. All castings shall be well finished, free from defects, porous places and blow holes, and true to dimensions without warping. Where chaplets are used, there shall be satisfactory fusion with the metal and chaplets shall be properly tinned with metal free from lead.

(ii) The screw threads of all bolts shall be of whitworth forms.

(iii) Wherever necessary, all component parts shall be manufactured to limit gauges to secure interchangeability throughout.

PART-2 CAST IRON, TUBES AND HEADERS

363. Process of manufacture.─(1) (a) The castings shall be cast from metal melted or refined in any suitable metallurgical plant other than an iron ore smelting furnace.

(b) The minimum test results required on test bars 30 mm in diameter shall be as per Table 11/2 given below, ─ Table 11/2 Serial No.

Material for tubes and Transverse test on span of 45.8 cm other pressure parts Minimum tensile strength in kg/mm2 Minimum deflection

1.

2.

Grade 16.5

(2) On analysis, the sulphur and phosphorus contents shall not exceed the percentages given in Table 11/3 below,─ Table 11/3 Serial No.

Components Maximum Sulphur Maximum Phosphorus percentage percentage

1.

Tubes

2.
