(3) (a) Minimum manganese content shall be related to sulphur content by the following formula:(% Sulphur x 1.7) + 0.3 = % Manganese.

(b) It shall be shown to be satisfied by routine quality control Laboratory analysis and in any case, the manganese shall not be less than 0.5 per cent and shall be within the range 0.50 - 0.90 percent.

(c) Phosphorous content shall be kept as low as economically possible but in no circumstances it shall exceed

1.0 per cent.

364.Test bars.─ (1) (a)When test bars are cast separately, they shall be poured at the same time and from the same ladle of metal as the casting or castings they represent.

(b) The number of test bars specified in regulation 365 shall be applicable to all casting of each melt.

(2) When the bars are cast on the mould for the casting and the mould for the test piece shall be joined together in such a manner that the liquid metal fills both moulds at the same operation.

(3) All test bars shall be cast in the same type of sand moulds viz. green sand or in loam or dry sand as used in casting or castings they represent.

(4) The test bar shall not be subjected to any heat treatment after leaving the moulds except where the castings are heat treated.

365. Number of tensile tests.─ (1) The number of tests required for each batch of castings shall be as per the table 11/4 given below where the castings have been divided in four groups and tests required have been mentioned there in, ─ Table 11/4 Serial No.

Group Weight of casting Test requirements

1.

Up to 13 kg One test for each 1524 kg of casting or part thereof.

2.

Over 13 kg and up to 50 kg One test for each 2000 kg of castings or part thereof.

3.

Over 50kg and up to 1000 kg One test for each 3000 kg of casting or part thereof.

4.

In the Groups 1,2 and 3, all castings represented by one test shall be poured from the same ladle of same heat as the bar or bars provided for the test.

5.

Over 1000 kg and important castings One test for each 4000 kg of castings or part thereof or for each casting weighing 4000 kg or more.

(2) Conditions for acceptance and for rejection shall be as under:

Table 11/5 Test Result Conclusion Serial No.

1. 1st Pass The batch or separate casting represented shall be accepted.

2.

Tensile or Transverse test fails Two more tests shall be made.

3. 2nd If both pass The batch or separate casting represented shall be accepted.

4.

If either fails The batch or separate castings represented shall be rejected.

Provided that, in the case of failure of any test piece showing obvious casting defects, another test piece shall be taken from spare test bars or cut from a casting from the same batch and the results obtained from this shall be substituted for those results obtained from the defective test bars.

366. Standard test piece.─ (1) The tensile test bar shall conform to the dimensions shown in the Figure 11/1 given below and bars may be tested with either plain or screwed ends, ─ Fig. 11/1 Table 11/6 B D A P R C E F Lp Ls mm mm. mm mm mm mm mm mm mm mm 29 SF 29 SW

(2) The test bars shall be cast as cylindrical bars of the diameters given in Column (1) and machined to the dimensions given in Columns (2) &(4) in the above Table.

PART-3 CONSTRUCTION REQUIREMENTSFOR CAST IRON ECONOMISERS

367. Design requirements.─ The manufacturer, on the basis of design details, shall satisfy the Inspecting Authority regarding the design and the strength of all parts of cast iron economisers subject to the following maximum allowable working pressures:

Table 11/7 Type Max allowable working Serial No. pressure in kg/cm2 (g)

1. Ordinary Vertical Tube

22.8

2. Ordinary Vertical Tube with Strengthened Tubes

26.3

3. Ring Stay Vertical Tube

33.3

4. Gilled Tube Type approved Design

45.7

368. Joint bolts and studs for cast iron economisers.─ (1) The maximum allowable working pressure for the bolts and studs shall be calculated as per regulation 375.

(2) The use of studs tapped into cast iron shall be permissible where;

(a) The studs and nuts are not in the path of the flue gases;

(b) The studs are carefully threaded to gauge and fitted into tapped holes of ample depth.

(3) For economiser of gilled tube type of approved design made from 16.5 grade iron, the limitation for a maximum allowable working pressure may be increased to 52.7 kg/cm2 (g).

369. Economiser water outlet temperature.─ The design condition shall be such that the maximum outlet water temperature of the economiser shall be at least 22°C below the saturated steam temperature in the boiler drum:

Provided that this requirement shall not apply to economizers used on low pressure water heating system working at pressure less than 36.5 m head of water.

PART-4 CONSTRUCTION REQUIREMENTS FORSTEEL ECONOMISERS WITH OR WITHOUT CAST IRON SLEEVES ON THE TUBES

370. Construction of Tubes.─ (1) The tubes shall be manufactured as per regulation 122.

(2) Suitable provision shall be made for supporting horizontal tubes at one or more interval in their length to relieve bending stresses and these supports as well as the end connections shall permit free expansion.

371. Maximum allowable working pressure of tubes.─ The maximum allowable working pressure of the tubes shall be calculated as per sub-regulation (1) of regulation 244, but the working metal temperature shall be taken as the maximum water temperature for which the part of the elements is designed plus 11°C.

372.Construction of headers.─(1)The headers shall be constructed in accordance with regulation 125.

(2) Branches shall be secured by one of the following methods, namely: -

(a) Expanded, belled and welded.

(b) Strength welded with additional securing means.

(c) Full depth strength weld.

(d) Branches less than 51 mm bore may be screwed and seal welded.

(3) Open ends of forged seamless steel tube headers may be closed by forging, or the ends may be secured by bolting, screwing, or welding in an approved manner.

373. Maximum allowable working pressure of headers.─(1)The maximum allowable working pressure of rectangular headers shall be calculated as per regulation 246.

(2) The maximum allowable working pressure of cylindrical headers shall be calculated as per regulations 169 and 170.

374. Attachment of tubes to headers.─(1)Tubes shall be connected to the tube plates by one of the following methods, namely:-

(a) Expanding with bell mounting or seal welding.

(b) Strength welding.

(c) Mechanical bolted joint.

(2) Roller expanded tubes shall project through the neck or bearing part in the holes by at least 6 mm and shall be secured from drawing out by being bell mouthed to the extent of 0.8 mm for each 25 mm in diameter plus

1.6 mm.

(3) (a) Tubes expanded into headers may be seal-welded inside or outside the header.

(b) Where a seal weld is provided inside the header, the tube end projection and bell mouthed specified in sub-regulation (2) is not required.

(4) In the case of roller expanded tubes the tube holes in the headers shall be formed in such a way that the tubes can be effectively tightened in them, there shall be a neck or belt of parallel seating of at least 13 mm.

(5) Where the tubes are strength welded direct to the headers, the technique followed shall be approved by the Inspecting Authority and all welds shall be suitably heat treated.

(6) Bolted joints shall be designed as per regulation 375.

(7) Tubes spacers supporting clips and lugs may be welded to the tubes.

(8) The tubes shall be so arranged that they are accessible for cleaning internally and externally.

PART-5

375. Bolts, studs joints and Flanges.─ (1) The maximum allowable working pressure for the bolts shall as per the following formula, ─ N x C

1.28 P = ———- D - ————

Equation (11/1) A n Where, N = No. of bolts securing the parts, n = No. of screws threads per inch, D = Bolt diameter in inches measured over the threads, A = The area exposed to pressure which is assumed to be bounded by a line midway between the pitch line of the bolts and the inner edge of the flange where flat joints are used with joints rings. Where conical joints faces are used with joint rings, of curvilinear cross-section, the area exposed to pressure shall be assumed to extend to the root of the thread where the tube ends are screwed, or to a corresponding boundary if the flanges are attached by other means.

C = 4,700 for steel bolts or studs of 28 tons/sq.in. minimum ultimate tensile stress where the diameter over thread is less than 3/4 inch, C = 5,100 for steel bolts or studs of 30 tons /sq. in. minimum ultimate tensile stress where the diameter over thread is less than 3/4 inch.

C = 5,600 for steel bolts or studs of 35 tons /sq. in. minimum ultimate tensile stress where the diameter over thread is less than 3/4 inch.

C = 5,600 for steel bolts or studs of 28 tons/sq. in. minimum ultimate tensile stress where the diameter over thread is not less than 3/4 inch and not greater than 7/8 inch.

C = 7,000 for steel bolts or studs of 28 tons/sq. in. minimum ultimate tensile stress where the diameter over thread is greater than 7/8 inch.

Where the bolts or studs used have a tensile strength exceeding 32 tons sq. in., a plate shall be permanently fixed to the economiser structure in a prominent position drawing attention to this fact in case replacements of bolts or studs are necessary. In no case shall the nuts be exposed to the action of the flue gases.

(2) The thickness of each flange shall be not less than that given by the following equation:

[P.D. (D1 - D)] t = ————————— Equation (11/2) Where, t = Thickness of flange in thirty-seconds of an inch.

P = Maximum Allowable Working pressure, in lbs. per square inch (gauge) D = Outside diameter of the tube in inches.

D1 = Pitch circle diameter or bolts or studs in inches.

Provided that such flange is cast integral with the body and with the appropriate internal corner radius.

In no case, however, the thickness of a flange shall be less than 24 thirty -seconds of an inch.

PART-6

376. Valves and mountings.─ (1) All economisers shall be provided with thermometers for measuring water temperature adjacent to the inlet and outlet connections.

(2) (a)A valve which shall prevent increase of pressure beyond a predetermined limit shall be fitted to every economiser and the design shall provide against unauthorized interference with the loading. Economisers with pressure parts of cast iron and arranged in groups of tiers connected by circulating piping shall have a relief valve fixed on each group or tier. Relief valves shall have a minimum orifice diameter of 19 mm.

(b)The relief valve capacity shall be calculated on the basis of economiser thermal capacity.

(3) Pressure gauge for indicating the pressure in the economiser shall be constructed as per regulation 232.

(4) Air release valves shall be provided for the release of air at all points where air accumulation may occur.

(5) Blow off drain valves shall be provided for complete drainage of water in the economiser.

(6) Economiser provided with means for heating the incoming feed by mixing it with hot water from the economiser outlet, shall have a non-return valve in the hot water return line.

(7) In every case, a reserve flue shall be provided for by passing the flue gases when no water is being fed to the boiler through the economiser where it is not integral with boiler.

(8) To relieve the economisers of excessive pressure accumulation due to internal explosion doors preferably of horizontally hinged 'self-sealing' type shall be fitted.

(9) All valves and mountings shall satisfactorily withstand a hydraulic test to the same pressure as will be applied to the economiser during its registration.

(10) Alternative means of feeding the boiler other than through the economiser shall be provided.

(11) A non-return valve shall be provided in the hot feed line between the economiser and the boiler to prevent the discharge of steam or water from the boiler into the economiser.

PART-7 FEED PIPES

377.Maximum allowable working pressure for feed piping .─The maximum allowable working pressure shall be the maximum pressure generated in overcoming friction and other losses in the feed piping under ordinary working conditions, or the maximum pressure which can be generated by the boiler feed pumps unless special provision is made to prevent that pressure being communicated to the pipe line.

Note: For boilers operating at design pressure 100 kg/cm2 (g) and above feed water discharge piping for feed water system without economizer inlet isolating valve can be designed based on boiler design pressure with provision of mechanical relief device or a three way valve for bypassing H.P Heater, to prevent Boiler Feed Pump (BFP) shut off pressure being communicated to the piping system and with such a feed water system, boiler shall be hydro tested up to the last isolation valve in the feed water line.

378. Steel feed pipes.─ Steel feed pipes shall be in accordance with chapter -7.

379. Feed Heaters and similar vessels fitted to feed pipes.─ (1)(a) If welded construction, such vessels shall comply with the requirements of Chapter-12, provided that radiographic examination of welds may be waived for pressure not exceeding 17.5 kg/cm2 (g).

(b) For parts of vessels of composite construction, the requirements of the appropriate regulations of the above Chapters shall be complied with as may be applicable to each such part.

(2) Maximum allowable working pressure of all parts shall be determined as per appropriate formulae given in these regulations and due consideration shall be given to the following additional points while calculating the stresses, namely. ─ The stresses due to self-weight of vessels and its contents, any super-imposed vessel or other equipments, pipes and other similar parts attached to the vessels, insulation and stresses due to static head, if any.

(3) Every such vessel shall be protected with a relieving device of sufficient capacity to avoid over pressure in case of internal failure.

(4) Inspection during construction, stamping and testing shall comply with the appropriate regulation of chapter-1.

PART-8 REGISTRATION AND INSPECTION OF ECONOMISER AND FEED PIPES

380. Preparation for inspection.─ (1) (a) At each inspection, the economiser shall be emptied and thoroughly cleaned internally and externally and in the flues.

(b) All mountings shall be opened up and all caps, doors and blank flanges removed to permit adequate inspection.

(2)The owner of an economiser to which this regulation is applicable shall obtain the approval of the chief inspector in writing to the method of disconnection which he proposes to use and shall be responsible for ensuring that the method so approved is followed in practice.

381. Procedure for registration.─ (1) On receipt of an application for registration, the Inspector after the economiser has been prepared for examination, shall take full particulars of the design and ascertain the maximum allowable working pressure allowed by the regulations.

(2) The fitness of the economiser parts shall be determined as per the following formulae:

(a) For Headers of approximately rectangular cross –section:– C (t - 2) P = ——————- Equation (11/3) b2 Where, t = Thickness in thirty-seconds of an inch. b = Distance between the sides of header supporting the surface, in inches.

P = Maximum allowable working pressure, in lbs. per square inch (gauge), C = 11 where the water side surface is flat. = 22 where the water side surface is curved, provided that the curvature is continued without interruption and the thickness at each end of the side is not less than that determined with C = 11.

In no case, however, shall the thickness of the side of a header be less than 16 thirty-seconds of an inch at any part.

(b) For Headers of approximately circular cross-section :–

0.6K (t - 5) P = ———————

Equation (11/4) D Where, t = thickness in thirty-seconds of an inch.

P = Maximum allowable working pressure, in lbs. per square inch (gauge), D = Outside diameter of header in inches.

K = 265 for Grade 16.5 Iron = 240 for Grade 14 Iron = 220 for Grade 12 Iron The minimum thickness of plain cylindrical portions of header in that part of the length which does not contain tube holes shall comply with the requirements of clause (d).

In no case, however, shall the thickness at any point may be less than 16 thirty-seconds of an inch.

(c) For Tubes :– K (t - c) P = —————Equation (11/5) D Where, t = Thickness in thirty-seconds of an inch.

P = Maximum allowable working pressure, in lbs. per square inch (gauge), D = Outside diameter of tube in inches.

K = 265 for Grade 16.5 Iron, = 240 for Grade 14 Iron, = 220 for Grade 12 Iron, C = 4 for portion where gills act as reinforcement. = 5 for portion not reinforced.

In no case, however, shall the thickness at any point may be less than 11 thirty-seconds of an inch.

(d) For Manifold pipes-

0.8K (t - 5) P = ———————Equation (11/6) D Where, t = thickness in thirty-seconds of an inch P = Maximum allowable working pressure, in lbs. per square inch (gauge), D = Outside diameter of pipes in inches, K = 265 for Grade 16.5 Iron, = 240 for Grade 14 Iron, = 220 for Grade 12 Iron.

In no case, however, shall the thickness of a manifold pipe or branch be less than 14 thirty-seconds of an inch.

(e) For Cast iron economisers with extended surface horizontal tubes, connector bends: – K (t - 5) P = ———————Equation (11/7) D Where, t = thickness of bend in thirty- seconds of an inch.

P = Maximum allowable working pressure, in lbs. per square inch (gauge).

D = Outside diameter of bend in inches.

K = 265 for Grade 16.5 Iron = 240 for Grade 14 Iron = 220 for Grade 12 Iron.

In no case, however, shall the thickness of a connected bend be less than 10.3 mm (13 thirty-seconds of an inch ) for 18.75 kg/cm2 (g) (12.0 tons sq. in.) and 22.00 kg/cm2 (g), (14.0 tons/sq. in.) Grade irons and

9.0 mm (11 thirty-seconds of an inch), for 26.00 kg/cm2 (g) (16.5 tons/sq. in.) Grade iron.

(f) Tubes to comply with equation (11/5).

(g) Manifold pipes to comply with equation (11/6).

(3) The Inspector shall enter full particular of the economisers together with the required calculations of various parts in a Memorandum of the Inspection book (Form-XVIII) and submit it to the Chief Inspector.

(4) If no formula or co-efficient applicable to any part, other than what is mentioned above, is contained in the regulations, the Chief Inspector shall at his discretion determine the fitness of the part.

(5) After inspecting the economiser and ascertaining, the maximum working pressure to which it may be worked, the Inspector shall witness the hydraulic test as per regulation 383 and may issue a provisional order in FormXVII.

(6) If the requirements of provisional order under sub-regulation (5) are not fulfilled, fresh provisional orders shall be issued subject to a maximum period of twenty four months from the date of issue of first provisional order, without any subsequent inspection and payment of fee.

382. Procedure at subsequent Inspection.─ (1) (a)After economiser has been cleaned, the Competent Person shall make a thorough examination so far as its construction permits.

(b) The external condition of the tubes shall be carefully noted for wasting especially at the feed inlet end and all accessible tubes shall be calibered.

(c) The internal surfaces of cast iron tubes shall be closely observed for graphitic wasting as far as it is possible and in the event of any tube failure, these shall be broken up for scrutiny so that the general internal condition of the other tubes may be estimated.

(2) Where tubes or other parts are wasted, the strength shall be re-calculated.

(3) The scraper gear shall be examined to note if any parts are missing, if the length of travel is adequate and if the scrapers are correctly adjusted.

(4) All cap bolts shall be inspected and also the condition and position of the dampers and baffles.

(5) The record of each inspection and calculations shall be entered in the Memorandum of Inspection Book.

383. Procedure for Hydraulic Test.─(1)Every economiser for registration shall be hydraulically tested in the presence of Inspector to 1½ times the maximum allowable working pressure. Hydraulic tests of economisers at subsequent examination when required by the Competent Person, shall be made after the inspection.

(2) The test pressure to be applied to economisers at subsequent examinations shall be from 1.25 to 1.5 times the maximum allowable working pressure of the economisers. During the test, all parts externally and in the flues shall be noted for leakage.

384. Memorandum of Inspection Book .─ (1) A Memorandum of Inspection Book shall be prepared for each economiser in Form-XVIII and the Inspector shall enter all particulars in ink and dimensions of the economisers with the particulars of hydraulic test and his inspection notes in this book;

(2) At subsequent inspection, Competent Person shall enter the dates of the Inspections, hydraulic tests when such are made with their notes pertaining to the condition of the various parts;

(3) the Inspector or Competent Person, as the case may be, shall also enter in the Memorandum of Inspection Book, the general condition of the economiser and of repairs;

(4) (a) On submission of the Memorandum Book to the Chief Inspector, he will in the case of newly registered economisers, check all particulars and calculations and approve the design pressure that is to be permitted.

(b) In the case of old economisers, the Chief Inspector shall examine the Inspector's notes of inspection and proposals made for repairs.

(c) A pressure once approved for the economiser shall not be altered without the written authority of the Chief Inspector.

(d)The Memorandum of Inspection Book shall be kept clean and up-to-date and the Inspection Book except when actually required by the Competent Person, shall be kept in the office of the Chief Inspector.

385. Registration book.─ (1) A registration book or copy of memorandum of inspection book for economiser containing all the particulars required for registration shall be maintained in the office of the Chief Inspector in FormXVIII and any orders passed by him regarding the economiser shall be entered in the registration book under his initials.

(2) The Chief Inspector shall also see that the note of subsequent inspections entered in the Memorandum of Inspection Book are copied in the Registration book.

386.Transfer of memorandum of inspection book and registration book - On transfer of an economiser from one state to another, the memorandum of inspection and registration book shall, on the request of the Chief Inspector of the State to which the economiser has been transferred, be forwarded to that Officer who shall take over their custody and maintain them as prescribed.

387. Grant of certificate.─ A certificate for the use of an economiser shall be granted in Form-XIX.

388. Casual visits.─ (1) The Inspector shall note that the economiser is working satisfactorily, the relief valves are correctly adjusted, the scraper gear is operating and the external brick work is free from cracks.

(2) He shall also ascertain that the maker's instructions for working are being strictly followed by the owner.

Note: It is recommended that the feed inlet temperature to the economiser shall not be less than 38°C, to prevent sweating and consequent external corrosion of the tube and bottom headers.

389. Economiser rating.─ The rating shall be equivalent to the area of the heating surface in square meters which shall be computed from the tubes and the headers.

390. Registration fee.─ An application for registration of Economiser shall be accompanied by appropriate fee as specified in regulation 304.

391. Fee for renewal of certificate.─ An application for renewal of certificate of registration of economiser shall be accompanied by appropriate fee as specified in regulation 305.

392. Engraving of registry number.─(1)(a) Every economiser shall have a plate with its registry number engraved, secured in a conspicuous place and wherever practicable stamped for identification with that number in a prominent place.

(b) The engraving shall be done within a period of thirty days from the date of receipt of the registry number.

(2) A letter "E" shall be prefixed to the registry number in the denominator of the appropriate device as shown in regulation 301.

Example: UP E-53

(3) The engraving shall be completed and ready for verification within thirty days from the date of receipt of the engraving slip from the Chief Inspector of the State.

(4) (a) Economisers having registry devices different from those prescribed in sub-regulations (1) and (2) shall have such devices crossed out or erased and engraved a new registry number in conformity with those prescribed in the aforesaid sub-regulations.

(b) The original numbers of such economisers shall be retained in the new device, provided that in the case of economisers operating in an area which consequent upon the reorganization of State forms part of another State, new numbers shall be given by the Chief Inspectors of the State.

(c) A number once allotted shall not be used again in another economiser.

393. Inspection of feed pipes.─(1) (a)Feed pipes shall be inspected and hydraulically tested before erection in place, and the hydraulic test pressure shall be not less than twice the maximum allowable working pressure of the economiser.

(b) A certificate from the recognized maker or Inspecting Authority certifying that this has been done may be accepted.

(c)If the Inspector or Competent Person, as the case may be, is satisfied with the test, the pipes may be erected.

(d) Neither separate certificate for feed pipes shall be issued, nor separate fee shall be charged for their inspection.

(e) The engraving shall be done within a period of two months from the date of receipt of the registry number.

At subsequent inspection or at any other time, the Competent Person may make an external inspection of feed pipes and if found in an unsatisfactory condition, he shall report the matter to the Chief Inspector who may require the pipes to be hydraulically tested.

394. Submission of plans of feed pipes.─ (1)The fees for the scrutiny of plans and particulars of the material, design and construction of feed pipes, before commencement of manufacture of the feed pipes, shall be at the rate of Rs. 200 for 30 metres of piping or part thereof.

(2) The fees for scrutiny of plans and particulars of the material, design and construction of any such fittings like feed water heater shall be charged at Rs. 2500 per fitting.

CHAPTER-12 SHELL TYPE BOILERS OF WELDED CONSTRUCTION PART-I

395. Application.─ (1) These regulations shall apply to all boilers of fusion welded construction other than Water Tube boilers and Electrode boilers referred to in Chapters-4 and 10 respectively.

(2) Where the design of the boiler is of composite construction viz. combination of Fire tube and Water Tube, it shall comply with the relevant regulations governing that particular type of construction.

396. General requirements. ─ Wherever applicable, the general requirements of Chapter-1 relating to certificates from Makers, Inspecting Authorities, and the like and of Chapter-3 concerning construction, shall be followed.

397. Equipment of workshop. ─ (1) The welding plant and equipment shall be of good quality and maintained in an efficient working condition. The welding apparatus shall be installed under cover and arranged so that welding work may be carried out in positions free from draughts and adverse weather conditions. The procedure shall be such that there is regular and systematic supervision of the welding works, and the welding operators shall be subjected to periodic tests for quality of workmanship by the works officials. Records of these tests shall be kept and shall be made available to the Competent Person for scrutiny.

(2) The works shall be equipped with welding plant equipment and quality control personnel. However, the equipment for destructive, non-destructive testing and heat treatment are not necessary. The manufacturer can tie-up for these facilities outside and a letter from the party with whom he makes a tie-up shall be made available to the Chief Inspector of Boilers or the Inspecting Authority or his representative and shall be allowed to inspect the material of the party in the laboratory during the test.

398.Materials of construction. ─ Plates, sections and bars used in the construction of these boilers shall comply with the requirements of Chapter-2in all respects. However, where the tensile strength exceeds 49.6 kg/mm2, the full details of the welding technique including pre-heating and post-heating, and the plate preparation to be used, shall be submitted to Inspecting Authority for approval before commencement of manufacture.

399. Welding consumables. ─ Where welding consumables are used , they shall comply with the requirements of regulations 71 to 76, save in the case of the weld metal as deposited, where the tensile strength shall not be less than the lower limit specified for the plate.

400. Plain and Stay Tubes. ─ (1) Tubes shall comply with the provisions of regulations 30,47 and 48.

(2) Seamless steel cross-tubes and uptake tubes shall come with regulations 99 and 100respectively.

401. Construction and workmanship.─ The manufacturer shall provide Inspecting Authority with a fully dimensioned sectional drawing showing the construction of the boiler in full detail for approval before putting the work in hand. Full details including dimensions of plates preparation for the longitudinal and circumferential seams shall also be shown on the drawing. Fully dimensioned sketches showing in details the attachment for stand-pipes, branch pipes and seatings, the position of these attachments relative to the longitudinal and circumferential seams and other opening shall also be given in the drawing.

402. Preparation of plates. ─ For preparation of plates, requirements of regulation 77 shall apply. When plates of different thickness are aligned, the welding shall be matched to the lower thickness plate and tolerance as required for thinner plate. (refer Figure 12/1).

403. Stress relieving. ─ Requirements of regulation 78 shall apply.

404. Cylindrical shells. ─ Requirements of regulation 80 shall apply.

405. Circularity. ─ (1) The difference between the maximum and minimum internal diameters of the shell measured at any one cross section shall not exceed one per cent of the nominal internal diameter.

(2) The profile as measured on the outside of the boiler by means of a gauge of the designed form of the exterior of the boiler, having a length equal to one quarter of the internal diameter, shall not depart from the designed form by more than the percentage given in Table 12/1.

TABLE 12/1: Maximum percentage departure from designed form of welded boilers Nominal internal diameter of boiler 'D' Maximum departure from designed form Serial Number in mm in percentage

1.

Up to and including 915

0.375

2.

Over 915, up to and including 1150

0.350

3.

Over 1150

0.300

(3) There shall be no flats at the welded seams and any local departure from circularity shall be gradual.

406. End plates, crown plates and tube plates.─ (1) End plate of welded Lancashire and Cornish boilers shall be flanged for attachment to shell plates.

(2) For requirements of end plates of boilers, sub-regulation (1) of regulation 86 shall apply.

(3) For requirements of hemispherical crowns, regulation 89 shall apply.

(4) For requirements of flanging, sub-regulation (2) of regulation 86 shall apply.

(5) In the construction of unfired boilers when the dished ends do not form a part of the heating surface, cold spun dished ends conforming to IS: 2825 may be used.

(6) Where flats are pressed in dished end plates, for the attachment of mountings they shall be formed with an ample radius at the junction of the flat and curved surfaces, and shall be free from sharp corners or tool marks. The plate shall not be unduly thinned.

(7) (a) Plates which are flanged in accordance with sub-regulation (4), shall be welded to the parts to which they are to be connected as follows:

(i) to shells or cylindrical fireboxes as shown in Figures 12/7, 12/8, 12/9, 12/10 or 12/11.

(ii) to internal flue tubes as shown in Figures 12/14, 12/15 or 12/16.

(iii) to uptake as shown in Figure 12/16.

(iv) to combustion chamber or firebox wrapper plates as shown in Figure 12/19.

(b) Plates which are not flanged shall be welded to the parts to which they are to be connected as follows:

(i) to shells as shown in Figure 12/12 or 12/13.

(ii) to internal flue tubes or uptakes as shown in Figure 12/17.

(iii) to combustion chamber or firebox wrapper plates as shown in Figures 12/20, 12/21 or 12/22.

(c) Where dished ends or crowns are used, they shall be flanged for connection to the shell or the cylindrical portion of the firebox. Flat shell and firebox crown plates of vertical boilers shall also be flanged for connection to the cylindrical portions of the shell or firebox.

(d)The opening in the firebox crown plate of a vertical boiler for the uptake shall be flanged and the connection to the uptake made by means of a circumferential butt-weld. (see Figures 12/3, 12/4, 12/5, 12/6).

407. Internal flues.─ (1) Sections of internal flues shall each be made from one plate, which shall be rolled while cold to circular form. The longitudinal seam shall be fusion butt-welded by the metal arc process.

(2) The maximum permissible variation in diameter of any cross-section shall not exceed 6 mm or half of the thickness of the plate, whichever is greater.

(3) The longitudinal welds shall be placed at lower part of the furnace and shall break joint in successive sections at least by 150 mm. This requirement shall not be applicable if successive sections are separated by tube plate or end plate or bowling hoops.

(4) Where flue sections are flanged for circumferential joints, the flanging shall be carried out at one heat by suitable machinery.

(5) Edges of all flue flanges shall be machined or flame-cut by machine (see regulation 402).

(6) Flanged circumferential seams shall be so arranged that they do not fall in line with those of the adjacent flue or with circumferential seams of the shell.

(7) The minimum mean internal diameter of furnaces shall not be less than those given in Figure 12/23 depending on the net heat input.

(8) Where furnace flue is made up of plain or corrugated sections or both, they may be connected to each other by fusion butt welded circumferential seams.

(9) (a) Plain furnaces shall not exceed 3 meter in length except in the case of reverse flame boilers, which are considered to be inherently flexible or as provided in clause (d). In all other cases flexibility shall be provided in the furnace by means of corrugations or blowing hoops or stepping of a minimum of 150 mm in the diameter of the furnace. (see figures 12/24 to 12/28.

(b)If corrugations are used to provide flexibility, at least one-third of the furnace length shall be corrugated.

(c)Breathing spaces as per regulation 483 shall be provided for boilers made as per this clause.

(d) Notwithstanding the provisions of clauses (a) and (b), plain furnaces longer than 3 meter are allowed to be used in a boiler when the length between boiler end plates do not exceed 6.5 meters provided that breathing spaces used in this case are in accordance with regulation 484 .

(10) Where a complete flue is constructed of short plain sections, the ends of each adjoining section shall either be swaged out to a radius to provide an adequate point of support and longitudinal flexibility and butt-welded together or be butt-welded to each side of a bowling hoop. The dimensions shall comply with Figures 12/31, 12/32 or 12/33.

(11) Stress relieving by heat-treatment shall be carried out when the construction of a flue involves the use of a circumferential weld such stress relieving being carried out on the completion of all welding in accordance with regulation 453.

(12) The forms of flue connections to end plates shall be as shown in Figures 12/14, 12/15, 12/16, 12/17 and 12/18 or be in any other form of welding groove subject to the approval of the Inspecting Authority.

408. Uptakes.─ (1) The uptakes shall be formed from seamless or electric resistance welded or fusion butt-welded pipes by electric arc process. The tolerances on these pipes shall comply with the requirements of regulation 251. The uptakes shall be fusion butt welded to the upward flange of the opening of the furnace crown plate in accordance with Figures 12/3 and 12/4. The depth of the flange of the firebox crown plate opening from the commencement of the curvature of flange shall be not less than twice the plate thickness with a minimum of 25 mm. Unless the whole boiler is to be subsequently stress relieved, the uptake and firebox crown plate shall be effectively stress relieved after welding. Where the firebox crown plate is also fusion welded to the body of the firebox as in Figure 12/16, the firebox complete with uptake shall be effectively stress relieved by heat treatment on completion of all welding.

(2) The uptake may be attached to the shell crown as indicated on Figure 12/17 before the boiler is finally stress relieved by heat treatment.

Where the vertical seam of the uptake is fusion welded, the welding shall comply with regulation 432 and the weld shall be so arranged that it is welded externally and internally (see Figure 12/42) It is desirable that the uptake shall be fitted with an internal cast iron liner extending below the low water level.

409.Cross-tubes.─Requirements of regulation 99 shall apply.

PART-2 Fireboxes and Combustion chambers

410.Furnace of vertical boilers.─ (1) The furnaces of vertical boilers may be constructed in one or more lengthwise sections, each section being rolled from one plate to a full circle. In such cases, the component sections may be jointed circumferentially by electric butt-welding. The welding shall be stress relieved in accordance with regulation 432.

(2) In the case of vertical boilers where tube plates form part of the firebox, the tube plate portion may be constructed in two vertical sections and the welding of the vertical seams when welded shall be stress relieved.

(3) The maximum permissible variation in diameter, at any cross-section shall not exceed 6 mm for fireboxes up to 900 mm in diameter, or 10 mm for fireboxes over 900 mm in diameter, or half the thickness of the plate whichever is the greater.

(4) Circular fireboxes shall preferably be tapered. A taper of 38 mm in diameter per 300 mm of height is recommended. The water space at the bottom between the firebox and the shell shall be not less than 50 mm for boilers up to and including 760 mm in diameter and shall be not less than 63 mm for boilers over 760 mm in diameter.

(5) The method of welding shall be in accordance with regulation 432.

(6) Flats formed in the firebox for the insertion of water tubes shall have an ample radius at the junction of the flat and the curved surfaces and shall be free from sharp corners or tool marks. The plate shall not be unduly thinned.

(7) Ogee flanging whether integral with the firebox or made as separate ring shall preferably be formed in one heat by suitable machinery and shall be allowed to cool gradually to avoid internal stresses.

(8) Rings of fire holes mouthpieces or foundation rings shall be made of mild steel. 'Z' sections shall not be used for foundations rings.

(9) Attachment of fireboxes to cylindrical shells shall be in accordance with Figures 12/34, 12/35, 12/36 or 12/37.

The method of attachment of fire holes mouthpieces shall be in accordance with Figures 12/40 or 12/41).

411. Hemispherical Fireboxes.─(1) Hemispherical fireboxes shall be pressed to form by machine in progressive stages without thinning and shall be annealed on completion.

(2) Methods of attachment of firebox to the shell be in accordance with Figures 12/34, 12/35, 12/36 or 12/37.

(3) The method of attachment of fire-hole mouthpieces and throat pieces shall be in accordance with Figures 12/40 or 12/41, 12/43 or 12/44.

412. Loco-type fireboxes.─ (1) Loco-type firebox wrapper plate sides shall preferably be tapered. The water space at the bottom between the fire-box and the shell shall be not less than 63 mm.

(2) Where the firebox tube plate or fire-hole plate is flanged for connection to the wrapper plate, the weld shall be located between the commencement of curvature of the flange and first row of screwed stays.

Rings for fire-hole mouthpieces of foundation rings shall be made of materials as specified in chapter -2.

Z-sections shall not be used for foundation rings. The attachment of the firebox to the outer casing shall be in accordance with Figures 12/37 or 12/30 or 12/31.

The method of attachment of fire hole mouthpieces shall be in accordance with Figures 12/40 or, where the outer casing and the firebox plate are flanged towards the water side to form the fire hole, a ring of screwed stays located at not more than half the average pitch of the stays distant from the commencement of the curvature of the flanging shall be provided (see Figure 12/45.

413.Water-cooled combustion chambers.─(1) The attachment of the combustion chambers tube plates and back plates to the wrapper plate shall be in accordance with Figures 12/19, 12/20, 12/21, 12/22 and where the tube plate is flanged for connection to the wrapper plate the weld shall be located between the commencement of curvature of the flange and first row of screwed stays.

(2) The method of attachment of furnace of the flue tubes shall be in accordance with Figures 12/15, 12/16 or 12/17 and access openings shall be in accordance with Figures 12/40 or 12/46.

PART-3

414. Bar stays and firebox stays.─ (1) Each bar stay or firebox stay shall be made from a solid rolled bar without weld in its length, except where it is attached to the plate it supports.

(2) Bar stays which have been worked in the fire shall be subsequently annealed.

(3) It is preferred that when the stay is in position in the boiler, its axis shall be normal to the plate it supports, but where this cannot be so arranged, then, if the stay is fitted with nuts, bevelled washers shall be provided between the nuts and the plate.

(4) Stays shall be secured to the plates they support by one of the following methods as given in regulations 415 to 418.

415.Bar stays.─ (1)Plain bars, passing through clearing holes in the plates and welded thereto (see Figure 12/50).

(2) Plain bars passing through clearing holes in the plates and fitted with washers on the outside, the stay and washers being welded to the plates in accordance with any one of the methods shown in Figures 12/52, 12/53, 12/54, 12/55, 12/56, 12/57, 12/58, 12/59.

Note: Clearing hole for the purpose of this regulation mean a machined cylindrical hole having a diameter not exceeding that of bar stay by more than 2%.

416.Firebox Stays.─ (1) (a) Screwed stays, screwed through the plates and riveted over at each end to form substantial heads, or fitted with nuts, or the projecting ends fillet-welded as indicated in Figures 12/47 and 12/48.

(b) It is recommended that where the stays are screwed, this shall be with a fine thread and the stays and holes shall be screwed with a continuous thread.

(c)The stays shall be screwed with fine threads of not less than 11 threads per 25 mm.

(d)The screw threads on the stays shall be clean, free from checks or imperfections, of full depth, or a correct standard form and a good fit in the holes. The middle portion shall preferably be turned down to the bottom of the thread.

(2) Plain stays shall be strength welded to the plates (see Figure 12/49 and 12/50).

NOTE: This form of attachment is also suitable for plain bar stays.

(3) All screwed stays less than 350 mm long shall preferably be drilled with a tell-tale hole of5 mm diameter to a depth of 13 mm beyond the inner face of the plate. Stays which are obscure on one side shall preferably be made from hollow stay bar.

417. Girder stays for firebox and combustion chamber crowns.─ (1) Where girder stays are attached with stay bolts and nuts, they shall be bedded to the corners or ends of side plates.

(2) The attachment of girder stays welded directly to the crown plates shall comply with Figures 12/60, 12/61, 12/62, 12/63 or 12/64. Such girders shall be welded to the crown plate prior to stress-relieving.

Each girder shall be of sufficient strength to support its due proportion of the load on the crown plate independently of the crown plate, and the bolts or weld attachments shall have sufficient cross-sectional area to carry the applied load.

The waterways between the underside of the girder and the crown plate shall be as large as practicable, but in no case less than 38 mm deep.

418. Gusset Stays.─ Requirements of sub-regulation(1) of regulation 112 shall apply.

PART-4

419. Access.─ (1) The requirements specified in sub-regulations (1), (2),(3) and (4) of regulation 135 shall apply in addition to the requirements given in sub-regulations (2), (3) and (4).

(2) At the bottom of the narrow water space in vertical boilers where internal access is not possible, mud holes shall be arranged at equal distances round the circumference of the boiler not less in number than as given in Table 12/2.

TABLE 12/2: Number of lower cleaning holes or mud holes Diameter of boiler No. of Holes Serial number 610 mm and over but not exceeding 915 mm Over 915 mm and not exceeding 1525 mm Over 1525 mm and not exceeding 1830 mm Over 1830 mm and not exceeding 2290 mm Over 2290 mm and not exceeding 2590 mm Over 2590 mm and not exceeding 2745 mm Cleaning holes or mud holes in loco-type boilers shall be provided at each bottom corner or as nearly as possible in the corners of the outer firebox casing and at the top corners above fire hole opening and also at each corner of the saddle plate in line with the bottom of barrel Smoke box tube shall be provided with a mud hole.

A manhole shall be provided in the lower part of front end plates of Lancashire boilers and in similar boilers with internal flues wherever practicable.

420. Manhole frames, mouthpieces and doors.─ (1) Manhole frames, mouth-pieces, doors or cover plates shall be of mild steel. Frames shall be oval and where of the flanged type, shall be formed to bed closely to the shell and provide a flat joining surface to the door. Alternatively, raised circular mouth-pieces may be fitted externally and where of the flanged type shall be bedded closely to the shell.

(2) Wherever practicable, the frame shall be attached to the inside of the shell with the shorter axis parallel to the longitudinal centre line of the boiler. In all cases where the shell thickness exceeds 14 mm, oval frames or circular mouth-pieces shall be fitted.

(3) Oval frames and externally raised circular mouthpieces shall be either:

(a) Formed in one piece without weld, or

(b) Formed from a suitable rolled section and forge-welded, or fabricated by fusion welding, provided they are stress-relieved by heat-treatment after welding and before attaching to the boiler unless the whole boiler is to be subjected to heat-treatment on completion.

Welds shall be positioned so that they are located on the transverse centre line of the boiler (see regulation 430).

(5) The joining flanges of mouthpieces and covers shall be machined on the face and edges and on the bearing surface for the bolts. Bolts and nuts shall be machined where in contact with the flanges and the joints shall be formed inside and outside the pitch line or pitch circle of the bolts to preclude the possibility of flange distortion. Cover plates shall be dished outwards to a depth of approximately one-eighth of the internal diameter of the frame.

(6) All edges shall be machined or flame-cut by machine (see regulation 402).

(7) Forms of manhole frames and attachments are shown in Figures 12/65, 12/66, 12/67, 12/68 and 12/69. For Manhole, head hole or access opening connections, where compensation calculation performed as per regulation 463, does not require compensating plates, Figure 12/108 to be used for attachment to shell.

421. Internal doors.─ Requirements of regulation 137 shall apply.

PART-5

422. Seatings for mountings.─ For requirements of seatings for mountings, refer regulation 129.For requirements of attachment of mountings, refer regulation 130.

423. Stand Pipes.─ Where stand pipes are used, the flanges shall be machined or flame-cut by machine on the edges.

The bolting flanges shall be machined on the jointing and bolting surfaces.

424. Saddles.─ Where saddles are used, the edges of the flanges shall be machined or flame-cut by machine. The joining surfaces shall be machined. The studs for the attachment of the mountings, if screwed through the saddle, shall be fitted on the inside with nuts of full thickness. Where the stud holes do not penetrate through the saddles the length of the screwed portion of the stud in the plate shall be not less than the diameter of the stud.

425. Pads.─ Where pads are used, the joining surfaces shall be machined. The pads shall have sufficient thickness to allow drilling of the stud holes for mountings without inner surface being pierced and the length of the screwed portion of the stud in the pad shall be not less than the diameter of the stud.

426.Attachment of standpipes.─(1)The following constructions as to standpipes attached to shells and end plates shall be permissible:-

(i) When the internal diameter of the standpipe does not exceed 25 mm the standpipe may be screwed into the plate with a nut on the waterside.

(ii) Where the internal diameter of the standpipe exceeds 25 mm but does not exceed 50 mm, it may be screwed in and seal welded.

(iii) Where the diameter of the standpipe exceeds 50 mm, it may be welded to the end plate.

(2) The provisions in sub-regulation (1) as to the standpipe attachment shall be regulated by the following conditions-

(i) When standpipes are screwed, the screwing shall be of standard pipe thread.

(ii) Standpipes shall be attached by one of the methods shown in Figures 12/104, 12/105,12/106,12/107, 12/108, 12/109, 12/110, 12/111, 12/112, 12/113 and 12/118 or by any other method approved by the Inspecting Authority.

(iii) Where the standpipes are fabricated by fusion-welding, they shall be stress relieved by heattreatment before attachment to the boiler, unless the whole boiler is subject to heat-treatment on completion.

(iv) Where the bore of the standpipe does not exceed 127 mm or the diameter of the opening in the shell does not exceed 127 mm plus twice the thickness of the plate to which it is attached, the standpipe may be welded to the plate without subsequent heat-treatment of the welds made. If these limits are exceeded, the whole plate to which the standpipe is attached shall be stress relieved by heattreatment on completion of welding.

427.Attachment of pads.─(1) Pads welded to the shell or end plate shall be welded internally and externally.

(2) Methods of attachment of pads shall be as shown in Figures 12/115, 12/116 or 12/117. Where the bore of the seating does not exceed 127 mm or the diameter of the opening in the shell does not exceed 127 mm plus twice the thickness of the plate to which it is attached, the pad may be welded to the plate without subsequent heat-treatment of the welds so made. If these limits are exceeded the whole plate to which the pad is attached shall be stress relieved by heat-treatment on completion of welding.

428.Attachment of water and pressure gauges.─ Water gauges and pressure gauge siphons may be attached direct to the front end plate without the intervention of a pad or standpipe, provided they are flanged and secured by studs.

If the studs are screwed through the plate, nuts of full thickness shall be fitted on the inside of the plate.

429.Bolts and Nuts.─ All holes for bolts and studs shall be drilled and bolts and nuts shall be machined where in contact with the flanges.

PART-6

430. Compensating plates.─ Compensating plates shall be of same material as that of shell and before attachment, shall bed closely to the plates to which they are to be connected. Any weld therein shall be on the transverse centre line.

431. Definition of the term 'fusion weld'.─ (1) For the purpose of this chapter, the term 'fusion weld' is applicable to all welded seams made by the metal arc process with covered electrodes or by any other electric arc process in which the arc stream and the deposited weld metal are shielded from atmospheric contamination.

(2) The relevant provisions of this chapter shall also apply to the single run or heavy run welding process and that welded boilers manufactured by that process shall comply with all regulations of this chapter excepting those in which divergence is necessary solely because of special requirements essential for the most efficient utilisation of that process. Thus all regulation governing quality of material construction workmanship, and testing (both non-destructive and otherwise) are applicable.

(3) Where welded shells conforming to the regulations of this chapter are made by single or heavy run process, it shall be understood that regulations 431,432 and 433 do not apply in their entirety. The method of making the welds shall be approved by the Inspecting Authority.

432. Methods of welding.─ (1) The seams shall be fusion welded from both sides of the plates. The main welding may be on either side of the plate, whichever it is most convenient to the manufacturer. Before the second side of the plate is welded, the weld metal at the bottom of the first side shall be removed by grinding, chipping, machining or other approved method.

(2) Provided the requisite quality of welding is achieved, the seams may be welded from both sides of the plate or from one side of the plate. Before the second side of the plate is welded, the metal at the bottom of the first side shall be removed by grinding, chipping, machining or other approved method.

(3) Additional runs of metal shall be deposited at both surfaces of the welded seams to ensure that the weld metal at the level of the surfaces of the plate is refined as far as possible.

(4) Thickness of such additional welding shall not exceed values given in following table.

TABLE 12/3: Thickness of additional welding Serial number Nominal thickness in mm Maximum reinforcement in mm Circumferential joint in Pipe and tubing Other Welds Upto 3

2.5

2.5 Over 3 to 5 incl.

3.0

2.5 Over 5 to 13 incl.

4.0

2.5 Over 13 to 25 incl.

5.0

2.5 Over 25 to 50 incl.

6.0

Note

4.0 Over 75 to 100 incl.

Note

5.5 Over 100 to 125 incl.

6.0 Over 125 Note

8.0 Note – The greater of 6 mm or 1/8 times width of weld in mm

(5) The surfaces of the welds which are to be subsequently radiographed shall be sufficiently free from coarse ripples, overlap, grooves, abrupt ridges and valleys, so as to avoid stress raisers and to permit proper interpretation of radiographs and other Non-Destructive Test (NDT) methods. If there is a question regarding the surface condition of the weld when interpreting a radiographic film, the film shall be compared to the actual weld surface or provide smooth contours and to be flush with the respective surfaces of the plate before radiography examination is done.

(6) In the case of circumferential shell seams welded by submerged arc or any other approved automatic process producing a similar surface finish, such circumferential welds, need not be flush dressed, subject to their surface finish and shape being acceptable to the Inspecting Authority.

(7) Undercut shall not exceed 0.8mm or 10% of wall thickness, whichever is less and undercut shall not encroach on design wall thickness.

(8) The positions of the welds shall be marked, compensation plates, doubling plates or manhole frames, not less than two runs or metal shall be deposited at each weld.

(9) When affixing standpipes, branch pipes, seatings, compensation plates, doubling plates or manhole frames, not less than two runs or metal shall be deposited at each weld.

(10) Each run of weld metal shall be thoroughly cleaned and freed from slag before the next run is deposited.

433. Types of welded shell seams.─ The longitudinal and intermediate circumferential seams shall be made with butt joints of the single or double U or V type (see Figures 12/3, 12/4, 12/5, and 12/6) or of any other type approved by the Inspecting Authority. End circumferential seams shall be in accordance with Figures 12/7, 12/8, 12/9, 12/10, 12/11 or 12/12 or with any other form approved by the Inspecting Authority.

434. Repairs to welded seams.─ (1) Any repair to a weld carried out by the manufacturer shall first be agreed to by the Competent person.

(2) (a)Where defects are distributed over the whole length of the seam, the total length of the portions which may be cut from any one longitudinal or circumferential seam to remove all defects shall not exceed 15 percent of the total length of the seam.

(b) When, however, the defects are all located in a single continuous length which may be cut out to remove all defects shall not exceed 10 percent of the total length of the seam.

(3) Defect shall be cut out by chipping, machining or other approved methods.

(4) In the case of Class I boilers, all repaired areas shall be subjected to radiographic examination.

435.Tests on welded seams of shell.─(1) Test plates to represent all welded seams shall be attached at each end of each longitudinal seam. These shall be of a size sufficient for the preparation of the test pieces specified. In the case of shell plates, the test plate may be located at one end only (see Figure 12/58). Where the shell is formed in two or more rings, the staggered longitudinal seam shall be regarded as a continuous longitudinal seam provided the welding be effected in one reasonably continuous operation and by the same operator or operators. The material for the test plates shall be cut from the respective plate or plates forming the appropriate seam.

(2) In the case of insufficient material being available on the shell plate to permit the cutting of test pieces, these shall be acceptable if they are made from another plate, provided it is made from the same cast. The test plates shall be stamped by the Inspecting Authority before being cut.

The weld groove in the test plates shall be similar to that adopted for the corresponding edges of the longitudinal seam, and the respective fusion faces shall be in continuous alignment. The test plates may be reinforced or supported during welding so that any relative displacement due to warping during welding does not exceed 5°. The weld metal in the test plates and the seam shall be deposited continuously at the same operation.

The weld in any test plate shall not be repaired. If any defects are revealed in the weld metal of a test plate the position of this shall be clearly marked on the plate and the test pieces shall be selected from such parts of the test plates as may be decided by the Competent Person.

Test plates warped during welding by not more than 5° may be straightened before heat-treatment. For the heat-treatment of the test plates (see regulation 453).

Where there are circumferential seams only or where the method of welding the circumferential seams differ from that employed for the longitudinal seams, the test plates shall be provided.

PART-7

436.Test of Class I boilers .─(1) Specimens for the following tests shall be selected from the test plate or plates and stamped by the Competent Person for identification (see Figures 12/71 and 12/72).

(i) One tensile test specimen for the welded seam.

(ii) One all weld metal tensile test specimen.

(iii) Two bend test specimens.

(iv) Two impact test specimens.

(v) One specimen from each test plate for macro examination.

(2) The dimensions of the specimens shall be in accordance with the sketches in Figures 12/73, 12/74, 12/76,12/77 and 12/78. The remainder of the test plates shall be retained for re-tests, if required. Any specimen for re-test shall be cut from the same test plate as the original specimen. Specimens representing welded seams shall, if necessary, be straightened cold before the commencement of machining. Tensile and bend specimens shall, if practicable, be of the full plate thickness. Surfaces of tensile, bend and Charpy specimens corresponding with the outside or inside of the boiler shall be lightly dressed only so that the rolled surface of the parent metal is not wholly removed. Where the rolled surfaces of the abutting plates are not level with one another one plate may be machined at each face of the weld so long as the depth of the metal removed does not exceed 0.8 mm.

437. Tensile tests.─ (1) Welded joint; the dimensions of the reduced tensile section shall be as shown in Figure 12/73.

The width of the reduced section shall be at least 25 mm.

(2) If the thickness of the plate does not exceed 30 mm, the thickness of the specimen shall be equal to the plate thickness and the plate surface of the specimen shall be machined to take away the surface irregularities of the plate and the weld.

(3) If the plate thickness exceeds 30 mm, the tensile test shall be carried out on several reduced section specimens, each having a thickness of at least 30 mm and width at the effective cross-section of at least 25 mm. These specimens shall be taken out of the test piece in such a way that the tensile test covers the whole thickness of the weld joints as shown in Figure 12/74. The tensile strength shall not be less than the lower limit specified for the plate.

(4) The dimensions of the all weld metal tensile test specimen shall be those given in Figure 12/77. The diameter shall be the maximum possible consistent with the cross section of the weld but in no case more than 20 mm, the gauge length shall be equal to five times the diameter. The tensile strength shall not be less than the lower limit specified for the plate. The elongation shall not be less than 20% on a gauge length of five times the diameter of the specimen or 5.65 times the square root of the cross-sectional area of the specimen, and the reduction in area not less than 35 percent.

438.Cold bend tests.─ (1) Two bend tests shall be made as given in this regulation.

(2) One specimen shall be tested with the outer surface of the weld in tension and the other with the inner surface in tension. The specimen shall be rectangular in section and shall be cut out transversely to the weld so as to have a width not less than one-and-a half times the thickness of the plates. The sharp corners of the specimens shall be rounded to a radius not exceeding 10% of the thickness of the specimen.

(3) Where the plate thickness does not exceed 32 mm, the thickness of the specimen shall be equal to the full thickness of the test plate. Where the plate thickness exceeds 32 mm, the specimen shall in all cases have a thickness of at least 32 mm. The specimen to be tested with the outer surface of the weld in tension shall be prepared by cutting to waste the metal local to the inner surface of the weld, so that the desired specimen thickness is obtained (see A, Figure 12/78).

(4) The specimen to be tested with the inner surface in tension shall be prepared by cutting to waste the metal local to the outer surface of the weld so that the desired specimen thickness is obtained (See B, Figure 12/78). Where the thickness of the plate permits, both specimens may be cut from the same piece of plate, the specimens being located in the plate one above the other (see C, Figure 12/78). Each specimen shall be mounted with the weld midway between the supports set apart at a distance of not more than 5.2 times the thickness of the specimen and pushed through the supports with a former having a diameter equal to three times the thickness of the specimen.

(5) On completion of the test, no crack or defect at the outer surface of the specimen shall be greater than 1.5 mm measured across the specimen or 3 mm measured along the length of the specimen. Premature failure at corners or the specimen shall not be considered cause for rejection.

439.Impact tests.─ Impact tests shall be as per regulation 162.

440.Macro and micro examinations.─(1) A specimen of the full thickness of the plate and not less than 13 mm wide shall be provided from each set of test plates for the purpose of macro and micro examinations.

(2) Micro-etching of a complete cross-section of the weld including the heat affected zone shall show satisfactory penetration, fusion and absence of significant inclusions or other defects. In case there is any doubt as to the condition of the weld as shown by macro-etching, the area concerned is to be microscopically examined for defect investigation.

441.Additional tests before rejection.─(1)In case any of the test specimens taken in accordance with regulations 435 to 443fail to meet the specified requirements, retests shall be allowed for each specimen that fails as follows:

(2) Where any result of the tensile test is not less than 90% of the specified figures, one retest shall be made. Where any result falls below 90%, two retests shall be made.

Where a bend specimen fails to meet the specified requirements, two retests shall be made.

(4) If an impact test fails to meet the specified requirements, two retests shall be taken from the test plate, one on each side of the final specimen and separate from it by not more than 5 mm.

(5) If it be found there is insufficient metal to permit the preparation of specimens for retests from the remainder of the test plate from which the original specimen was taken, the specimen for retest may be cut from the test plate relating to the opposite end of the same longitudinal seam.

(6) In case any of the additional tests fail to meet the specified requirements, the welded seams represented by these tests shall be rejected.

442. Specimens after tests.─ If required, the specimens after test shall be at the disposal of the Inspecting Authority for examination.

443. Radiographic examinations.─ (1) Every portion of the longitudinal and circumferential butt-welded seams of the shell of the boiler shall be subjected to radiographic or ultrasonic examination. Each Longitudinal and circumferential butt welded seams of Furnace, Reversal chamber, Access tube and drop pipes shall be subjected to 10% radiographic examination. The percentage of welds to be examined shall include each intersection of longitudinal and circumferential seams. Where ultrasonic examination is used, it shall be demonstrated to the satisfaction of the Inspecting Authority that the equipment and the technique used are satisfactory. The manufacturer shall ensure that the operator employed is competent to use the equipment, apply the technique and interpret the results of the examination.

(2) The methods employed in obtaining the radiographs shall be such as to show clearly differences equal to 2% of the thickness at the welded joints. To determine whether this result is being attained, an indicator of approved form which includes a portion equivalent to not more than2% of the joint thickness shall be placed in the vicinity of the weld so as to make a record on each radiograph. Each section of every weld shall be marked so that the radiographs can be easily correlated to the particular part of the seam represented.

(3) The examination shall be made from the original films and the acceptability of the welds shall be decided by the Inspecting Authority. The welds deemed unsatisfactory shall be rejected or dealt with under the condition of regulation 434 and can be radiographed again. The films shall be retained by the manufacturer for minimum three years for reference.

Notes: (1) Magnetic Particle Flaw Detection, Magnetic methods of flaw detection shall be employed wherever possible for ferrite steel.

(2) Dye-Penetrate Flaw Detection; Dye-penetrant or equivalent methods of flaw detection shall be employed for Austenitic or other non-magnetic steel.

(3)All butt-welded joints shall be subjected to non-destructive examination by radiographic, radioscopy or other approved methods such as ultrasonic testing, magnetic particle inspection or liquid dye penetrant inspection. When radioscopic examination is to be performed in lieu of radiography on welded components, the following requirements shall be met, namely:-

(a) A written procedure shall be submitted for approval to the Inspecting Authority which shall contain the following:

(i) material and the thickness range;

(ii) equipment qualifications;

(iii) test object scan plan;

(iv) radioscopic parameters;

(v) image processing parameters;

(vi) image display parameters;

(vii) image archiving requirements;

(viii) accept-reject criteria (Code reference);

(ix) performance evaluation;

(x) Operator identification.

(b) The system shall be aided with an image processor to enhance the quality of the radioscopic images and system performance quality shall exhibit-

(i) a thin section contrast sensitivity of 3%;

(ii) a thick section contrast sensitivity of 2%;

(iii) a spatial resolution of 3 line pairs per mm;

(iv) IQI sensitivity - 2% of the joint thickness when wire IQI's are to be used, the wire diameter axis shall be oriented along the axis of the least sensitivity of the system.

(c) Radioscopes are to be properly marked to co-relate with particular part of joint represented.

(d) The radioscopic examination data shall be recorded and stored on videotape, magnetic disk or optical disk or any other electronic media at the maker's plant for a sufficient period after the date of radioscopic examination as specified by the Inspecting Authority, Efficient radioscopic examination record recall shall be made available at any time over the record retention period and shall be traceable to the test objects.

(e) When repair has been performed as a result of radioscopic examination, the repaired areas shall be re-examined using the same radioscopic technique to evaluate the effectiveness of the repair.

(f) To aid in proper interpretation of the radioscopic examination data, the details of the technique used shall accompany the data. As a minimum, the information shall include the approved procedure requirements and system performance test data.

PART-8

444. Test for class II boilers.─ (1) Test pieces shall be selected from each longitudinal seam for the following tests and stamped by the Competent Person for identification.

(i) From the test plate or plates:

(ii) One tensile test specimen for welded seam.

(iii) One bend test specimen.

(iv) One reverse bend test specimen.

(v) One nick-break test specimen.

(2) The disposition of the specimen shall be in accordance with the sketches in Figure 12/79.

The remainder of each test plate shall be retained for retests, if required.

445.Tensile test.─ The dimensions of the test specimen shall be in accordance with the sketch in Figure 12/73:Specimen1A, and the specimen shall be cut out transversely to the welded seam. When the capacity of the available testing machine does not allow full specimen to be tested, two narrower tensile specimens shall be substituted. These specimens shall be the full thickness of the plate at the welded joint and their breadth shall be as great as the testing machine will reasonably allow, provided the effective cross sectional area of the test piece is not less than 968 mm2 (see Figure 12/74: specimen 1B). The tensile strength of the welded joint specimen shall not be less than the lower limit specified for the plate.

446.Cold bend tests.─ (1) Two bend tests shall be made as given in this regulation.

(2) One specimen shall be tested with outer surface of the weld in tension, and the other with the inner surface in tension. The specimens shall be rectangular in section and shall be cut out transversely to the weld so as to have a width not less than one-and-a-half times the thickness of the plate. The sharp corners of the specimens shall be rounded to a radius not exceeding 10 percent of the thickness of the specimen.

(3) The specimen to be tested with the outer surface of the weld in tension shall be prepared by cutting to waste the metal local to the inner surface of the weld, so that the desired specimen thickness is obtained (see A, Figure 12/78). The specimen to be tested with inner surface in tension shall be prepared by cutting to waste the metal local to the outer surface of the weld so that the desired specimen thickness is obtained (see B, Figure 12/78).

Where the thickness of the plate permits, both specimens may be cut from the same piece of plate, the specimens being located in the plate one above the other (See C, Figure 12/78).

(4) Each specimen shall be mounted with the weld midway between the supports set apart at a distance of not more than 5.2 times the thickness of the specimen and pushed through the supports with a former having a diameter equal to three times the thickness of the specimen.

(5) On completion of the test, no crack or defect at the outer surface of the specimen shall be greater than 1.6 mm measured across the specimen, or 3 mm measured along the length of the specimen. Premature failure at corners of the specimen shall not be considered cause for rejection.

447.Nick-break specimen .─ (1) This specimen shall have a width not less than one and a half times its thickness and the slot shall be cut in each side of the specimen through the centre of the weld and perpendicular to the outer face of the boiler.

(2) The specimen shall then be broken in the weld and the fracture shall reveal a sound homogenous weld substantially free from slag inclusions, porosity and coarse crystallinity.

448. Additional tests before rejection.─ In case any of the test specimens fails, two retests shall be made and both shall meet the specified requirements.

449. Specimen after tests.─ If required, specimens after tests shall be at the disposal of the Inspecting Authority for examination.

450. Radiographic or ultrasonic examination.─ Each longitudinal and circumferential seams in class II boiler (including shell to end plate) shall be subjected to spot radiographic or ultrasonic examination.

PART-9

451. Test for Class III boilers.─ Specimens for the following tests shall be selected from the test plate or plates and stamped by the Competent Person for identification.

(i)One forward bend test.

(ii)One reverse bend test.

452.Radiographic or ultrasonic examination.─ Each longitudinal and circumferential seams in class III boiler (including shell to end plate) shall be subjected to spot radiographic or ultrasonic examination.

PART-10

453. Heat treatment.─ All boilers constructed to Class I or Class II requirements shall be stress-relieved by heat treatment after completion of all welding and before hydraulic test as per regulation 123.

454. Classification of fusion-welded boilers.─ The boilers covered by this chapter shall be classified in accordance with Table 12/4.

Classification Limits of application Minimum thickness TABLE 12/4: Classification of fusion-welded boilers Serial number Boilers upto and including 915 mm internal diameter 8 mm.

Class I No limit 8 mm Class II When the following limits are not exceeded:

(a) Maximum Allowable Working pressure 7

(b) Maximum Allowable Working pressure in kg/cm2 multiplied by internal diameter in cm – Boiler over 915 mm internal diameter 10 mm.

Class III When the following Limits are not exceeded:

(a) Maximum Allowable Working pressure 2

(b) Maximum Allowable Working pressure in kg/cm2 multiplied by internal diameter in cm –

(b) In no case, however, shall be the factor of safety of shell be less than 4 or the plate thickness be less than specified in Table 12/4 given under regulation 454, C=27, where Class II requirements are complied with, C=23, for Class III boilers when stress relieved, C=21, for Class III boilers when no stress relieved.

(a)Each alternate tube in the outer vertical row tubes shall be a stay tube;

(2)The shell plates to which the sides of the tube plates are connected shall be not less than 1.5 mm thicker than is required by the formula applicable to shell plates with continuous circularity; and where gussets or other stays are not fitted to the shelves, the strength of the parts of the circumferential seams at the top and bottom of these plates from the outside of one tube plate to the outside of the other, shall be sufficient to with-stand the whole load on the boiler end, with a factor of safety of not less than 4.5.

457. Dished end plates for Lancashire and Cornish boilers.─ (1) For the dished ends of Lancashire and Cornish boilers without stays and subject to internal pressure, the maximum allowable working pressure shall be determined by the following formula:

( 32 𝑡−203.2) 𝑆 𝑃= 𝑅 ( 67.35617) Equation (12/5) Where, t = is minimum thickness of the end plate in mm, P = is maximum allowable working pressure in kg/cm2(g), R = is the inner radius of curvature of the end plate in mm, S = is minimum tensile strength of the plate in kg/cm2.

(2) The inner radius of curvature of the end plate shall not exceed 1½ times the external diameter of the shell which it is attached.

(3) The inner radius of flanging of the end plate shall be not less than 4 times the thickness of the plate but in no case less than 63 mm.

(4) Where the end plate has a manhole, compensation shall be obtained by flanging the edge of the opening or by providing a fabricated ring (see Figure 12/65).

(5) In either case the depth of the flanging or ring measured at the minor axis shall not be less than that determined by the following formula:

D = √TW Equation (12/6) Where, D = is depth of Flange or ring in mm measured from the outside of the plate to the joint face, T = is thickness of the plate in mm, W = is width of the opening in mm measured on the minor axis.

458. Dished Ends subject to internal pressure.─ (1) For unstayed ends of shells and tops of vertical boilers and the like boiler parts, when dished to partial spherical form the maximum allowable working pressure shall be determined by the following formula:

0.216 𝑆(𝑡−0.79) 𝑃= 𝑅 Equation (12/7) Where, P = is the maximum allowable working pressure in kg/cm2(g), t = is the thickness of the end plate in mm but in no case this shall be less than the thickness of the shell to which it is attached, R = is the inner radius of curvature of the end in mm, which shall not exceed the external diameter of the shell to which it is attached, S = is the minimum tensile breaking strength of plate in kg/cm2, or whatever is allowed for it.

(2) The inside radius to which a crown plate is dished shall be not greater than the external diameter of the cylinder to which it is attached.

(3) The inside radius of curvature of the flanges to the shell shall be not less than 4 times the thickness of the crown plate and in no case less than 63 mm.

(4) The inside radius of curvature of flanges shall be not less than twice the thickness of the crown plate and in no case less than 25 mm.

(5) When the end has a manhole in it (t-3.97), shall be substituted for (t-0.79) in the formula.
