(7) The depth of the crown plate opening from the commencement of the curvature of the flanging radius shall be not less than twice the plate thickness with a minimum of 25 mm.

459.Nothing in regulation 458 shall preclude the use of dished ends in compliance with regulations 174 to 177, where not fitted with an uptake.

460. Dished Ends subject to external pressure.─ (1) In the case of unstayed dished ends, for the co-efficient 0.216 in equation 12/7, the co-efficient 0.173 shall be substituted, and R shall be the outer radius of the curvature of plate, which shall not exceed the external diameter of the shell to which it is attached.

(2) For plates exposed to furnace-flame, the co-efficient shall be 0.151.

(3) In no case shall R/t exceed 2.75.

(4) The inside radius of curvature at the flange shall be not less than 4 times the thickness of the end plate and in no case less than 63 mm.

(5) The inside radius of the curvature of the flange to uptake shall be not less than twice the thickness of the crown plate and in no case less than 25 mm.

461. Hemispherical Crowns.─ (1) The maximum allowable working pressure for hemispherical crowns subjected to internal pressure shall be determined by the following formula:

462. Manholes and other openings in Shells.─ Requirements of regulation 139 shall apply.

463.Compensation for openings in Shells.─ (1) Where the width of any opening (measured in a direction parallel to the longitudinal axis of the boiler) exceeds two-and-a half times the thickness of the shell plate in mm plus 70 mm, compensation shall be provided.

(2) The area to be compensated shall be the product of the maximum width of the opening cut in the shell (measured in a direction parallel to the longitudinal axis of the boiler) and the thickness of an unpierced seamless shell of similar material. For Class I boiler, thickness will be determined in accordance with regulations169 and 170. For Class II & Class III boiler by Equation 12/1, with constant C = 35 for the same maximum allowable working pressure.

(3) Compensating Area shall be considered adequate when the sum of the following area is not less than the area to be compensated:

(a) The area obtained by multiplying the difference the actual thickness of the shell and the thickness required for an equivalent unpierced seamless shell by a length 2(75 mm + Ts), there Ts is the actual thickness of the shell plate in mm.

(b) The net cross-sectional area of the frame or pad, or in the case of a branch or manhole, head hole ring, the cross-sectional area of the wall of the branch or manhole, head hole ring minus the sectional area of a branch or manhole, head hole ring of the same bore having thickness equal to that calculated in accordance with regulations169 and 170, plus the thickness required to withstand external loads.

The area shall be measured within limits such that (a+b) in Figure 12/80does not exceed 100 mm.

(c) For Manhole & Head hole rings of Oval shape diameter to be taken in calculation is to be calculated from formula below Where, a is major axis of Oval frame in mm. b is minor axis of Oval frame in mm.

(d) Where material of a frame, pad or branch has a tensile strength which differs from that of the shell to which it is attached, the compensating area shall be multiplied by the ratio:

(4) (a) In cases where the sum of (a), (b) and (e)in sub-regulation (3) is less than the area to be compensated, a compensating plate shall be welded to the shell, its total cross-sectional area being equal to the amount of the deficit and due allowance being made as above when the tensile strength of the material of the plate differs from that of the shell.

(b) The compensating plate may be fitted on the inside or outside of the shell plate as most convenient and its diameter shall not exceed twice the longitudinal width of the opening in the shell.

464. Raised manhole frames, cover plates and joint bolts.─ Requirements of regulation 138shall apply.

465.Standpipes and branches.─ (1) The thickness and bolting of all flanges for joining the mountings and fittings shall be in accordance with the dimensions as laid down in Appendix-F to these regulations. In no case, however, shall the thickness of flanges be less than 13 mm. Flanges conforming to International codes and standards such as ASME, BS, EN, DIN can be used meeting required pressure and temperature ratings.

(2) (a) The wall thickness of nozzles and other connections shall not be less than that specified for the applicable loading, namely, internal pressure, bending and static loads.

(b)The pressure loading shall be determined by regulation 455, but in no case shall the thickness be less than that calculated by the following formula: t = 0.04D + 2.5 mm Equation (12/10) Where t is the thickness of standpipe and 'D' is the outside diameter of connection. If the branch is connected by screwing, the thickness 't' shall be measured at the root of the thread.

(3) Where a double flanged steam pipe is tapered, the thickness of flange joining the boiler and fitting shall correspond to the bore of pipe at the flange connection in accordance with Appendix-F to these regulations.

466. Stayed flat surfaces (other than crowns of vertical boilers).─ For various braced and stayed flat plates and those which by these regulations require staying as flat surfaces with braces or stay bolts of uniform diameter symmetrically spaced, the thickness shall be calculated by the following formula; namely:P e = Cd —- + e1 Equation (12/11) f1 Where, P = maximum allowable working pressure in kgf / cm2, e = thickness, in mm, d = diameter of the largest circle taken between staying points of the plate (in mm), f1 =

0.85 f ('f' as defined in regulation 169), kgf/cm2, e1= additional thickness equal to 0.75 mm, C = coefficient having values given in table 12/5below, depending upon the form or method of support adopted.

If the plate is Flame swept, the thickness shall be increased by 10%. iv. The welding material used in the manufacture of these plates shall not have the ultimate tensile strength less than that of the plate used.

467. Flat crown plates for vertical boilers.─ (1) Flat crown plates shall be supported by the uptake and/or bar stays.

(2) The inside radius of curvature of the flange to the shell or fire box shall be not less than four times the thickness of the plate and in no case less than 63 mm where the plate is flanged for attachment to the uptake, the inside radius of curvature of the flange shall be not less than twice the thickness of the plate and in no case less than 25 mm.

(3) The thickness of flat crown plates shall be determined in accordance with regulation 466.

468.Wide water spaces between and around tube nests.─ The thickness of plate at wide water spaces shall be calculated as per regulation 466 taking Where, A = is width of the wide water space between the tube nests in mm measured from centre to centre of the tubes; and B = is pitch of the tubes in the boundary rows of the wide water space in mm.

469. Flat tube plates and tubes nests.─ (1) Stay tubes are tubes having a weld depth equal to the nominal tube thickness plus 3 mm as shown typically in figures 12/84(i) to (iv). These stay tubes are not required within tube nests except when the tube nests comprise tubes which are expanded only as shown typically in figure 12/82(iii).

(2) If tube nests comprise plain tubes which are expanded and beaded, expanded and belled as shown typically in figure 12/82(iv) or expanded and welded (as shown typically in figures 12/82(i) and (ii), welded stay tubes [as shown typically in figures 12/82 (i) to (iv) shall be used in boundary rows in sufficient numbers to carry the flat plate loadings outside the tube area.

(3) For plain tubes and stay tubes exposed to flame or gas temperatures exceeding 600°C, the ends of welded tubes shall be dressed flush with the welds and the ends of the expanded tubes shall be as shown in figures 12/82(iii) and (iv). If not so exposed, the ends of welded tubes shall extend a maximum of 10 mm beyond the weld or, in the case of expanded tubes, the tubes shall project beyond the tube plate up to a maximum of 15 mm.

(4) Each stay tube shall be designed to carry its due proportion of the load on the plates which it supports. The thickness of stay tubes welded into tube plates shall be such that the axial stress on the thinnest part of the tube does not exceed 7.14 kg/mm2.

(5) If stay tubes are used within tubes nests, the thickness of the tube plate shall be calculated in accordance with regulation466.

470. Manhole openings in flat plates.─ (1) Where manholes are located in flat plates, the openings shall be suitably compensated.

(2) Compensation shall be obtained by flanging the edge of the opening, or by providing a fabricating ring (see Figures 12/65, 12/66, 12/67, 12/68 and 12/69).

(3) The manhole in the lower portion of the flat front end plates shall be fitted with a flanged strengthening ring, the thickness of the flat portion of which shall be not less than (1.5 T + 3 mm), where, T = the thickness of the end plate in mm, and in boilers of 2280 mm and over, the manhole frame shall be fitted with a peak to reinforce the portion of the end plate between and below the furnaces and this peak shall be securely welded thereto.

(4) In all cases the depth of the flanging or ring shall not be less than that determined by Equation12/6.

Note: Where the manhole is located in or between tube nests in Multi-tubular boilers or below the furnaces of other Boilers, the stay tubes in the boundary rows, or the gusset stays, as applicable, shall be arranged as closely as practicable to the manhole.

471. Plain Tubes.─(1) Plain tubes may be welded at both ends, welded at the inlet and expanded at the outlet end, or expanded at both ends. Where the tubes are expanded only, the process shall be carried out with expanders having parallel rollers, and the expanded portion of the tube shall be parallel through the full thickness of the tube plate. In addition of expanding tubes may be bell-mouthed or beaded at the inlet end. Where the tube is welded to the tube plate, the tube should be lightly expanded to grip the tubes hole before welding.

(2) The thickness of tubes under external pressure shall be calculated as under:

Higher value may be adopted if desired by user. f = allowable stress as per regulation 244.

(i) The spacing of tube holes shall be calculated such that the minimum width (in mm) of any ligament between the tube holes shall be not less than:

473. Stay Tubes.─ (1) Stay tubes shall be of seamless steel or electrically resistance welded steel.

(2) The minimum thickness of stay tubes shall be as per regulation 119.

(3) The maximum allowable working pressure for screwed in stay tube shall be calculated by the following formula:

414.81108

When the area contains tubes or parts of tubes their aggregate area, calculated from their smallest external diameter of body when in tension and smallest internal diameter when in compression, shall be deducted from the area of the containing figure and the remainder used as A in the formula.

(4) Where stay tubes have their thickness increased at the screwed ends to provide for plus threads, the increased thickness shall be obtained by upsetting and not by welding, and the tubes shall be subsequently annealed.

(5) Stay tubes may be attached to the tube plates either by screwing or by metal arc welding.

(6) Where stay tubes are screwed into the tube plates, they shall be screwed with a continuous thread not finer than 4 threads per centimeter at both ends and shall be expanded into the tube plates, by roller expander and, if desired, may be seal welded.

(7) Welded attachment of stay tubes be as shown in Figures 12/81, 12/82, 12/83, 12/84and12/85.

474.Compression of Tube Plates.─ (1) For fire-box or combustion chamber tube plates which are subject to compression due to the pressure on the roof plate, the maximum allowable working pressure shall be:

(2) Where girders rest on the side plates or the roof plate is so formed that the load is carried both by side and end plates, the compressive stress on the plates shall not in either case exceed 984 kg/cm2.

475.Girders for fire-box and combustion chamber crowns.─ (1) For girders supporting crown plates of rectangular fire-boxes, where the ends of the girders are supported by the vertical end or side plates, their proportions shall be calculated by the following formula:

CSTd2 P = ————

(2) Where girders are welded to the crown plate the dimensions of the welds shall be such that the stress calculated on an area equal to the sum of the effective lengths of the welds attaching each girder multiplied by the effective throat thickness shall not exceed 527 kg/cm2multiplied by the appropriate weld factor in Table 12/13, ("effective length" and "effective throat thickness" are defined in regulation486.The load on the welds shall be taken as that due to the maximum allowable working pressure acting on the area 'LY', 'L' and 'Y' being as defined in this regulation.

476. Girder sling stays.─ For slung girders, the proportion in slings, links, pins and connections to the shell shall be sufficient to carry the whole load that would otherwise be carried on the toes of the girders and for any of the above parts in tension a stress of 633 kg/cm2 of net section, and for parts in shear a stress of 562 kg/cm2 of net section shall not be exceeded. In the case of parts in double shear, the net area of the section should be taken as 1.875 times the single section.

477. Stays for fire boxes and circular furnaces.─ (1) For solid crew stays to combustion chambers and fire-boxes and for longitudinal and cross Stays, the maximum allowable working pressure for the stays is to be calculated from the appropriate one of the following formulae:

C 1.28 2 P = —— D - ——A N Equation (12/17) C x D1 2 P = —————

499.17943 for steel or special wrought iron screw stays to combustion chamber or fire-boxes, C =

(2) Where stays are made with enlarged ends and the body of the stay is smaller in diameter than at the bottom of the thread, the maximum allowable working pressure shall be calculated from the second Equation (12/18).

(3) In case of circumferential stay for circular furnaces and fire-boxes, the diameter of stay shall not be less than 19 mm or twice the thickness of the fire-box plate, whichever is greater. In the case of screwed threads the diameter shall be measured over the threads. The pitch of the stays at the fire-box shall not exceed 14 times the thickness of the fire-box plate.

478. Fire-box crown stays for Loco-type boilers.─(1) Where fire-box crown stays are directly attached to a semicylindrical outer casing, and the fire-box top is chambered and fitted with stays arranged as shown in Figure 12/94, the thickness of the outer casing shall be sufficient to provide not less than three engaging threads, of which not less than one shall be full thread in stays not normal to the stayed surface, but if the thickness of the plate is not sufficient to give one engaging full thread, the plate shall be reinforced.

(2) Where vertical stays only are used to take part of the load on the fire box top, boilers with shells 1370 mm diameter and over shall be fitted with transverse stays (see Figures 12/95. Stays may be attached as shown in Figures 12/47, 12/48 and 12/49.

479. Longitudinal bar stays.─ (1) The maximum allowable working pressure for longitudinal bar stays shall be calculated from the one appropriate formula out of the following two formulae:

C 1.28 2 Equation (12/19) P = —- D - ———A N C x D12 P = —————

(2) Where bar stays are fitted in vertical boilers, not less than four bar stays shall be fitted to boilers of 1200 mm and over but under 1500 mm in diameter; five bar stays to boilers of 1500 mm and over but under 1800 mm in diameter; six bar stays to boilers of 1829 mm and over in diameter.

(3) In no case, shall the diameter of the stay at any part be less than 25 mm. Where joined stays are fitted, the strength of the knuckle joint employed shall be at least equal to the strength of the remainder of the bar stay.

480. Loads on stay tubes and bar stays.─ Requirements of regulation 121 shall apply.

481. Flat plate margins.─ (1) The amount of support in relief of the stays which may be attributed to the shell, furnaces or flues to which flat plates are attached, shall not exceed that determined by the following formulae:

𝐶 ( 32 𝑡−25.4) Width of margin in mm = √𝑃 Equation (12/21) Where, t is plate thickness in mm P is maximum allowable working pressure in kg/cm2 (g) C = 0.92008 for plates exposed to flame.

C = 0.98107 for plates not exposed to flame.

(2) Where the plates are flanged, the margin shall be measured from the commencement of curvature of flanging, or from a line 3½ times the thickness of the plate measured from the outside of the plate, whichever is nearer to the flange. Where the flat plate is not flanged for attachment to the shell or flue tubes and is welded as shown in figured 12/12 or 12/17, the width of the margin shall be measured from the inside of the shell or the outside of the flue tube, whichever is applicable.

In no case, however, shall the diameter 'D' of the circle forming the boundary of the margin supported by the uptake of a vertical boiler be greater than that found by the following formula:

0.54488 𝐴 𝑑2 𝐷= √ 𝑃 +

PART-11

482. Breathing space.─ Breathing spaces as provided in regulations 483,484 or 485shall be provided depending on the design of the boiler with special attention to the provision of sub-regulation (9) of regulation 407 regarding furnaces.

483.For boilers as per clause (a)of sub-regulation (9) of regulation 407.─

(1) Stays shall be arranged to give sufficient breathing space around the furnace connections and tube nests (see figure 12/91 and 12/93), and shall divide the unstayed areas equally.

(2) (a) In case of multi-tubular boilers, for both front and rear attachment of furnaces, the breathing spaces between furnace and tube nests or between furnace and shell shall be minimum of 50 mm or 5% of the shell inside diameter, whichever is larger, with a maximum requirement of 100 mm.

(b) Clearances between the furnace and wet-back wrapper plates shall not require consideration as breathing spaces.

(c)In the case of reverse flame boilers, the breathing space at the front end between furnace and the tube nests shall be not less than 50 mm and the breathing space formed by the outer-annular area of the furnace rear plate shall be not less than 50 mm or 5% of the shell inside diameter, whichever is larger, with a maximum of 100 mm.

(d) Breathing spaces between gusset or link stays and tube ends shall be not less than 100 mm.

Clearances between the tubes and wet-back wrapper plates shall not require consideration as breathing spaces.

(e ) Breathing spaces between tubes and shell shall not be less than 40 mm.

(f) Breathing spaces between gusset or link stays and furnaces shall be not less than 200 mm except that for shell outside diameters exceeding 1800 mm and furnace length exceeding 6000 mm they shall be not less than 250 mm and for shell outside diameter less than 1400 mm and furnace lengths less than 3000 mm they shall be not less than 150 mm.

(g) All other breathing spaces shall be a minimum of 50 mm or 3% of the shell inside diameter, whichever is larger, with a maximum requirement of 100 mm.

(3) (a) For Cornish and Lancashire boilers, the circle defining the breathing spaces shall be as shown in Figure 12/89i.e. the distance 'AB' between the centre of the stay circle and the centre of the flue shall be not less than 3e+63, where, 'e' is the thickness of the endplate in mm.

Note: For Lancashire boilers, the proportions shown in Table 12/9 given below are recommended for the portion of the end plates above the furnace and flues.

TABLE 12/9: Breathing Space for Lancashire Boilers Thickness of end plate (in mm) Over 20 Breathing space (in mm) (see Figure 12/70)

(b) It is recommended that the breathing space below the flue be approximately one-half of the dimensions given in Table 12/9.

484.Forboilers as per clause (d) of sub-regulation (9) of regulation 407.─ (1) Stays shall give breathing spaces around the furnace connections and tube nests (see Figures 12/86 to 12/93, 12/96, 12/97, 12/98, 12/99 and 12/100) and equally divide the unstayed areas. Breathing spaces between furnace and tube nests shall be a minimum of 50 mm or 5% of the shell outside diameter, whichever is larger, but need not be more than 100 mm.

(2) Breathing spaces between furnace and shells shall be as given in Table 12/9 or 12/10 as applicable, but shall be not less than 50 mm or, for bowling hoop furnace, not less than 75 mm as measured from the outside diameter of the bowling hoop.

(3) For the boilers having more than one furnace, breathing spaces between furnaces shall be not less than 120 mm.

(4) Breathing spaces between gusset or link stays and furnaces shall be not less than 200 mm, except for as under;

Shell outside diameter >1800 mm 250 mm or furnace length >6000 mm Shell outside diameter <1400 mm 150 mm or furnace length <3000 mm

(5) Breathing spaces between gusset or link stays and tube nests shall be not less than 100 mm.

(6) When unflanged flat endplates as used, the breathing spaces between gusset or link stay and furnaces shall be increased as follows:

Increase 200 mm to 220 mm Increase 250 mm to 275 mm Increase 150 mm to 165 mm Increase 100 mm to 110 mm

(7) All other breathing spaces shall be a minimum of 50 mm or 3% of the shell outside diameter whichever is larger but need not be more than 100 mm.

485.For Boilers not covered under regulations 483 and 484.─(1)Gusset stays shall be arranged to give sufficient bearing space around the furnace connections and tube nests (see figures 12/86 to 12/93, 12/96, and 12/97 to 12/100). The proportions shown in Table 12/12 are recommended for the portion of the end plates above the furnace and the flues.

Thickness of end plate Dimension 'L' mm Table 12/12: Breathing Space Serial number mm < 13 > 13< 14 > 14 < 16 > 16 < 20 > 20

(2) The breathing space below the flues shall be approximately one-half the dimensions given in Table 12/12.

(3) For gusset stays above the tube nests, as fitted in multi-tubular waste heat boilers without internal furnaces, a breathing space of 20.3 mm from the centre line of the adjacent tubes to the toe of the gusset angle or plate, may be maintained. For the back end stays below the furnace of multi-tubular dry back boilers, a breathing space of 152 mm may be maintained where the flues are constructed in accordance with regulation 407.

(4) Where the flues are constructed of corrugated sections welded together, a breathing space of 114 mm may be maintained.

486. Gusset Link, brace and similar stays.─ (1) For welded Lancashire, Cornish and Cylindrical Horizontal Multitubular type boilers, all welded gussets stays shall not be used. To ensure flexibility, link stays, bar stays, brace stays other than the all-welded type shall be used. All welded gusset stays, however, may be used in cylindrical horizontal waste heat and cylindrical vertical multi-tubular boilers.

(2) Welded gusset stays and brace may be used for welded Lancashire Cornish and Cylindrical Horizontal Multitubular type boilers provided their method of attachment is approved by the inspecting Authority.

Each gusset or link stay supporting the flat end plate of a boiler shall be designed to carry the whole load due to pressure on the area it supports. The areas supported by any one stay shall be obtained by considering the total area to be supported which lies within the limits of the flat plate margins and dividing this area by boundary lines drawn between the stays. These boundary lines shall be at all points equidistant from the adjacent points of support in the area under consideration. The effective uniformly distributed load on a triangular gusset plate or on a diagonal link stay shall be assumed to be equal to the perpendicular load on the portion of the end plate to be supported (determined as above) multiplied by L/L1 (see Figures 12/101, 12/102 and 12/103).

(a)Gusset plates shall be so proportioned that the angle "V" (see Figures 12/101 and 12/102), is not less than 60°.

(b) The thickness of the gusset plate shall be such that the stress in the plate to withstand the effective uniformly distributed load (as defined above) calculated on the smallest cross-section on line XX or YY (Figure 12/101 and 12/102), shall not exceed one-seventh of the minimum tensile stress of the plate used but in no case shall the thickness be less than seven-eighths of the thickness of the shell, plate with a minimum of 11 mm.

(c) Where all-welded gusset stays are used a stiffening plate shall be welded at the toe of the gusset where it is secured to the end plate i.e. where the maximum stress occurs, (see Figure 12/101).

(5) Link stays shall be so arranged that the angle "V" (figure 12/103) is not less than 600 and the dimensions shall be such that stress in the stay at its weakest part does not exceed one-seventh of the minimum tensile stress of the plate used.

(6) The strength of anchor plates, angles and link pins calculated at the weakest section shall be as follows:

(a) Link pins shall be so designed that the shear stress does not exceed 562 kg/cm2, the strength of pins in double shear being taken as 1.875 times the strength of pins in single shear.

(b) Anchor plates or angles shall be so designed that the calculated stress does not exceed one-seventh of the minimum tensile stress of the material used, but in no case shall the thickness be less than seven-eighth of the thickness of the shell plate with a minimum of 12.7 mm, nor the length of the portion attached to the end plate be less than the distance between the lines of end to shell flat margin and the breathing space line around the flues of tube nests.

(7) (a)Where gusset plates, anchor plates or angles are welded to the shell and/ or end plates, the attachments shall be by mean of continuous fillet welds on each side or by butt-welds prepared in accordance with following paragraph and details l, m, n, or p of Appendix –J.

(b) The welds shall be of such dimension that the stress calculated on an area equal to the effective length of the weld multiplied by the effective throat thickness shall not exceed that permitted for the parent metal multiplied by the appropriate weld factor in Table 12/13.

(c)The effective length of a weld shall be taken as that length of weld which is of the specified size throughout.

(d) For open-ended fillet welds the effective length shall be the overall length less twice the weld size.

(e) For the purpose of stress calculation, the effective throat thickness of a butt weld shall be taken as the thickness of the gusset or another plate and the effective throat thickness of fillet weld shall be taken as 0.7 of the fillet size. For compound welds the effective thickness shall be the sum of those of the constituent parts.

0.65 PART-12 Furnaces, furnace components, wet-back reversal chambers and fire boxes of cylindrical form subject to external pressure

487. Furnaces.─ (1) The mean diameter of furnace shall not exceed 1800 mm.

(2) Notations shall be as under: b = is the pitch of the furnace corrugations (in mm);

C = is the corrosion allowance = 0.75 mm; d = is the mean diameter of furnace (in mm) (see Note 1); dmax = is the maximum mean diameter of the furnace (in mm); dmin = is the minimum mean diameter of the furnace (in mm); e = is the furnace plate thickness (in mm);

E = is Young's modulus of elasticity (in kg/cm2) at the design temperature.

Et = the specified minimum elevated temperature yield stress or the 0.2% proof stress at the design temperature (in kg/cm2) as available in the governing codes of the country of the material to which it belongs;

F = is the cross-sectional area of a longitudinal section of the corrugated furnace wall, of length b and thickness (e-c) (in mm2) [see figures 12/119(Fig.A to Fig.G)];

I = is the second moment of area of one complete corrugation about its neutral axis, excluding the corrosion allowance (in mm4);

Is = is the second moment of area of plain stiffener section (see figures 12/29 and 12/30) about its neutral axis, including a length of the furnace of 0.55√ (de) on each side of the stiffener (in mm4) (see Note 2);

L = is the distance between the centre of two effective points of support (in mm) (see Note 3);

P = is the maximum allowable working pressure (in kg/cm2) S1 = is the factor of safety: = 2.5 for furnace in Class I and Class II boilers = 3.5 for furnace in Class III boilers = 2.0 for furnace and wrapper plates not exposed to flame;

S2 = is the factor of safety: = 3.0 for Class I and Class II boilers = 3.9 for Class III boilers u = is the percentage out of roundness to be taken as 1.5 for plain furnace and 1.0 for corrugated furnace;

W = is the depth of corrugation (in mm).

Notes:

1. For corrugated furnace, mean diameter is equal to inside diameter plus full depth of one corrugation referred to in Fig. 12/119 which is equal to the inside diameter +e+W.

2. When calculating Is, it is only necessary to take into account a corrosion allowance on the furnace gas side.

3. Stiffeners complying with Figures 12/29 and 12/30 boiler and reversal chamber end plates are considered to be effective points of support.

4. The design temperature of the furnace = Cs + 4e +15 (saturation temperature plus four times the thickness of the furnace plus 15).

(3) Evaluation of Young's modulus at the design temperature. Value of 'E' shall be obtained from the following table (by linear interpolation if required):

488.Plain furnaces.─ (1) The maximum allowable working pressure of plain furnaces shall be the lower of those obtained using equations 12/23 and 12/24 as follows, but the thickness shall be not less than 7 mm and shall not exceed 22 mm: 2 Et (e-C) 𝑃= S1 d

(3) If the furnace/ combustion chamber has tubes radially to it, the thickness of that portion of the furnace is to be increased by the value of ligament efficiency.

489.Furnace components.─ The thickness of furnace components, e.g. ash drop-out tubes and fuel inlet connections, shall be calculated in accordance with regulation 488 with a minimum thickness of 10 mm and a maximum thickness of 22 mm.

490.Corrugated furnaces.─ The maximum allowable working pressure of corrugated furnaces shall be determined using the equation 12/27, but the thickness shall not be less than 10 mm and shall not exceed 22 mm.

Calculated value of 'I' and 'F' for some of the corrugations are given in figure 12/119 for other shapes and sizes calculations need to be made from basic principles. 2𝐹𝐸𝑡(1+0.1 𝑑 𝐿) Equation (12/27) 𝑃= ] 𝑆1𝑏 𝑑 [1+ 𝐹𝑊𝐷𝑢 } 800 𝐼 {1+5𝑑 𝐿(𝑒−𝑐 𝑊)

491. Tolerances and allowances.─ The calculated wall thickness contains a fixed allowance of 0.75 mm for corrosion and wear. For corrugated furnaces, the calculated wall thickness shall be the minimum thickness of the finished furnace. For plain furnaces and reversal chambers, allowance shall be made to take account of any minus tolerances on the plate thickness.

492. Out-of-roundness.─(1) The percentage out-of-roundness is as follows: 200 (dmax- dmin) u = ——————————- Equation (12/28) dmax +dmin

(2) This shall be included in the calculation as u = 1.0 for corrugated furnaces and u = 1.5 for plain furnaces.

493. Stiffeners.─ (1) Stiffeners shall have second moment of inertia not less than that given by the following equation:

Pd3 L Is = ———————— Equation (12/29)

1.35 x 107

(2) (a)If the stiffeners are made in sections from bar or plate, the abutting ends shall be prepared so as to ensure that full penetration welds are made.

(b) The thickness of the stiffening ring shall be kept to the minimum required (for limiting dimensions see Figures 12/29 and 12/30).

(3) Bowling hoops are considered as effective points of support. The minimum pitch of bowling hoop centres shall be not less than 500 mm. If bowling hoops are used, the furnace thickness shall be calculated from regulation 488. The dimensions of bowling hoops shall be in accordance with figures 12/120(Fig. A , Fig B and Fig C)and their second moment of area determined from the tables given in these figures, shall be not less than required by sub-regulation (1).

(4) If corrugated furnaces are equipped with several stiffeners, e.g. one on each corrugation or on each second corrugation, the cross-sectional area and the second moment of area of the stiffeners shall also be taken into consideration when using the equation 12/27. A height of not more than four times the furnace thickness shall be used for the calculation.

494. Circular reversal chambers. ─ (1) (a) The thickness of wrapper plates of cylindrical reversal chambers of horizontal multi-tubular boilers shall be calculated in accordance with the equations given in regulation 488. The thickness shall be not greater than 35 mm and shall be not less than 10 mm.

(b) However, the design temperature for reversal chamber wrapper plates shall be determined in accordance with the following equations: - t = (ts + 2e2) or (ts + 50) whichever is greater.

Where, t = Design temperature (in degree centigrade) ts = Saturation temperature of water (in degree centigrade) at the maximum allowable working pressure, for both steam or hot water boilers. e2 = nominal plate thickness (in mm).

(2) (a) The thickness of access tubes shall be calculated in accordance with regulation 488 with a minimum thickness of 10 mm.

(b) However, the design temperature for access tubes shall be determined in accordance with the following equations: - t = (ts + 2e2) or (ts + 50) whichever is greater.

Where, t = Design temperature (in degree centigrade) ts = Saturation temperature of water (in degree centigrade) at the maximum allowable working pressure, for both steam or hot water boilers. e2 = nominal plate thickness (in mm).

495. Plain furnaces of vertical boilers.─ (1) The maximum allowable working pressure of plain furnaces of vertical boilers shall be the lesser of the two values obtained by Equations (12/23) and (12/24).

Where, D = external diameter of the fire-box in mm. Where the firebox is tapered the diameter taken shall be the mean of that at the top and at the bottom where it meets the substantial support from the flange or ring, L = is length of the firebox in mm between points of substantial support measured from the commencement of flange curvature or from the foundation connection, or from a row of screwed stays which comply with regulation 416 and sub-regulation (3) of regulation 477, whichever is applicable.

(2) The thickness shall be not greater than 22 mm and shall be not less than 10 mm.

496.Corrugated fireboxes of vertical boilers.─ (1) For the semi-spirally corrugated fireboxes of 'Sentinel' standard motor wagon boilers the maximum allowable working pressure shall be determined by the following formula:

𝐶 (32 𝑡−25.4) 𝑃= 𝐷 Equation (12/30) Where, P is maximum allowable working pressure in kg/cm2(g) t is thickness of the firebox plate in mm D is mean of the external diameters of firebox measured over the plain part at each end at commencement of curvature of flange in mm C = 27.41972

(2) No corrugated furnace shall be less than 10 mm thick.

497.Hemispherical furnaces of vertical boilers.─ (1) When furnaces are hemispherical in form and subject to pressure on the convex side and are without support from stays of any kind, the maximum allowable working pressure shall be found by the following formula:

19.33441 (32 𝑡−25.4) 𝑃= 𝑅 Equation (12/31) where P is maximum allowable working pressure in kg/cm2 (g) t is thickness of the top plate in mm.

R is outer radius of curvature of the furnace in mm.

The thickness of these furnaces shall in no case exceed 22 mm.

PART-13

498. Foundations of Vertical Boiler Furnaces.─ When circular furnaces of fireboxes of vertical boilers are not connected to the shell crown by uptake tube, smoke tubes or bolt stays and the whole load on the firebox vertically is borne by the bottom part of the firebox where it is connected to the shell, the maximum allowable working pressure for the part, if firebox is joggled out to meet the shell or if any ogee ring is fitted, shall not exceed that found by the following formula:

𝐶(32 𝑡−25.4)2 𝑃= 𝐷(𝐷−𝐷1) Equation (12/32) Where, P is maximum allowable working pressure in kg/cm2 (g) t is thickness of the joggled firebox plate or ogee ring in mm.

D is inside diameter of the boiler shell in mm.

D1 is outside diameter of the joggled firebox in mm at the commencement of curvature above joggled part or the outside diameter of the firebox where it joins the ogee ring.

C = 9.84297 for Ogee rings (see Fig 12/34 and 12/35) C = 7.03070 where 'U' ring section is used (see Fig. 12/36).

499.Foundations of Loco type boiler fireboxes.─ (1) Where the firebox roof in locotype boilers is not stayed direct to the external casing crown or to girders carried by the casing or if not connected to the casing by slings, the maximum allowable working pressure for the parts, if plates are joggled out to meet the casing or if an ogee ring is fitted, shall not exceed that found by the following formula:

(t – 1) x (L + W) P = 70 ———————————

Equation (12/33) L + W (W – W1) Where, P =is maximum allowable working pressure in pounds per square inch, t = is thickness of the joggled firebox side plates of fire hole plate (whichever is less) or ogee ring in thirty-seconds of an inch, L = is length of firebox casing in inches measured between the water sides of front end plate and saddle plate at the foundation seam, W = is width firebox casing in inches measured between that water side of casing side plates at the foundation seam, W1 = is width of firebox in inches measured between the water sides of firebox side plates at the commencement of curvature above joggled part of where it joins the ogee ring.

(2) Where only a comparatively narrow strip of the firebox roof is stayed directly to the casing crown the area so stayed shall be deducted from the area represented by 'L x W' in the bottom line of the formula thus:

(L x W– A) (W – W1) and so used is Equation (12/33) in determining the working pressure for the parts. "A" being the area in square inches of the part of roof supported by the casing crown.

500. Uptakes of vertical boilers.─ (1) The maximum allowable working pressure for uptake tubes of vertical boilers shall be determined by the following formula:

(i) When an internal liner is not fitted:

7.03070 (32 𝑡−50.8) 𝑃= 𝐷 Equation (12/34)

(ii) When an internal liner is fitted extending below the low water level:

50.97255 (32 𝑡−25.4)2 𝑃= 𝐷(𝐿+609.6) Equation (12/35) Where, P is maximum allowable working pressure in kg/cm2 (g) t is thickness of the uptake in mm.

D is external diameter in mm.

L is length of the uptake in mm, measured between the circumferential seams.

(2) In no case, the thickness of an uptake tube shall be less than 10 mm.

501.Cross tubes.─ (1) Internal diameter of cross tubes shall not exceed 305 mm. The maximum allowable working pressure of the tunes shall be determined by the following formula:

14.06139 (32 𝑡−177.8) 𝑃= 𝐷 Equation (12/36) Where, t = is thickness in mm of the cross tube, D = is internal diameter in mm of the cross tube.

(2) In no case, the thickness of a cross tube shall be less than 8 mm.

502.Pads welded to shell or the attachment of flanged mountings.─ The size of outer peripheral fillet welds by which circular pads are attached to shell plates shall be determined by the Equation (12/37), but shall in no case be less than the inner welds or nor less than the minimum specified for plate thickness in Table 12/3: 2A – D1 L1 Lo = ——————Equation (12/37) D Where, Lo is size of fillet weld around outer periphery of pad, in mm, L1 is size fillet weld around inner periphery of pad, in mm, A is cross-sectional area of opening in shell (based on minimum thickness in square mm see regulation 463 ), D is outer diameter of pad, in mm, D1 is inner diameter of pad, in mm.

503.Hydraulic test.─ (1) (a) Each Class I Boiler shall, on completion of all welding and/or repairs and after heattreatment, be subjected to a hydraulic test of 1.5 times the design pressure without any indication of weakness or defect.

(b) Each Class II and Class III Boiler shall be tested to a pressure twice the design pressure.

(2) (a) The test shall be witnessed by the Inspecting Authority or Competent Person.

(b) The test pressure shall be maintained for a period not less than 30 minutes.

(c) In the interest of safety, it is important that the boiler be properly vented, so as to prevent the formation of air pockets, before the test pressure as applied.

Notes:

1. It is recommended that the temperature of the water shall not be less than 7°C during the hydraulic test.

2. It is further recommended that before the boiler is approached for close examination, the pressure shall be reduced to not less than 1.1 times the design pressure and not more than 0.9 times the hydraulic test pressure.

3. On completion of hydraulic test, release of pressure shall be gradual.

4. Deflection of different pressure parts shall be examined carefully and there shall not be any permanent set after release of pressure.

(3) If any repairs are found to be necessary during or subsequent to the hydraulic pressure test, the boiler shall again be subjected to the pressure test specified above after completion of the repairs and after any heat treatment provided repaired weld joint requires heat treatment as per regulation 453.

Note: Observations and remarks of Competent Person/ Inspecting Authority shall be recorded for filing with maker's papers.

504.Safety valves.─ Boiler shall be provided safety valves complying with regulations 192 to 219, with following changes:-

(i) The safety valves shall be reset at a pressure at least 2.5% below, but not more than 5% below the safety valve set pressure.

(ii) The 5% limit is increased to 10% for valves having a body seat bore less than 32 mm and or having a set pressure of 24 bar gauge or less.

CHAPTER-13 QUALIFICATION TESTS FOR WELDERS AND WELDING OPERATORS ENGAGED IN WELDING AND REPAIR OF BOILERS AND BOILER COMPONENTS, PIPES, HEAT EXCHANGER AND HEAT EXCHANGER COMPONENTS UNDER CONSTRUCTION, ERECTION AND FABRICATION AT SITE.

505. Application.─ (1) These regulations shall apply to testing of welders and welding operators engaged in welding and repair of boilers and boiler components, steam-pipes, heat exchanger and heat exchanger components of ferrous and nonferrous material under construction, erection and fabrication at site.

PART-1 Definitions

506. Welder.─ Welder means a person who performs manual or semi-automatic welding.

507.Welding operator.─ Welding operator means a person who operates machine or automatic welding equipment.

508. Reasonable continuity of service.─ The continuity of service of the welder or welding operator shall be considered as continuity of service if he retains the initial skill level though not engaged in welding for a period of upto six months.

509. U-Number.─(1) U Number grouping of electrodes and welding consumables in table 13/5 of regulation 520 is based essentially on their usability characteristics, which determine the ability of welders to make satisfactory welds with a given welding consumables.

(2) This grouping is intended to reduce the number of welding performance qualifications.

PART-2 Welding Positions

510.Plate groove welding.─ The welding positions as mentioned in Figure 13/1 of this chapter shall be as following, namely:-

(i)Flat position 13/LGF; Plate in a horizontal plane with the weld metal deposited from above.

(ii) Horizontal Position 13/LGH; Plate in a vertical plane with the axis of the weld horizontal.

(iii) Vertical Position 13/LGV; Plate in a vertical plan with the axis of the weld vertical.

(iv) Overhead Position 13/LGO; Plate in a horizontal plane with the weld metal deposited from underneath.

511. Plate fillet welding.─ The welding positions as mentioned in Figure 13/2 of this chapter shall be as following, namely:-

(i) Flat Position 13/LFF; Plates so placed that the weld is deposited with its axis horizontal and its throat vertical

(ii) Horizontal Position 13/LFH; Plates so placed that the weld is deposited with its axis horizontal on the upper side of the horizontal surface and against the vertical surface.

(iii) Vertical Position 13/LFV; Plates so placed that weld is deposited with its axis vertical.

(iv) Overhead Position 13/LFO; Plates so placed that the weld is deposited with its axis horizontal on the underside of the horizontal surface and against the vertical surface.

512. Pipe groove welding.─ The welding position of pipe groove welding as mentioned in Figure 13/3 of this chapter shall be as following, namely:-

(i) Horizontal Rolled 13/PGR; Pipe with its axis horizontal and rolled during welding so that the weld metal is deposited from above with the axis of the weld horizontal.

(ii) Vertical Fixed 13/PGV; One test of groove welded joint with the axis of the pipe in a vertical position and the seam welded circumferentially in a horizontal position.

(iii) Horizontal Fixed 13/PGH; One test of groove welded joint with the axis of the pipe in a horizontal position and the seam welded without rotating the pipe.

(iv) Pipe Inclined 13/PGI; One test of groove welded joint with the axis of the pipe inclined 45 degree to the horizontal axis and the seam welded without rotating the pipe.

FIGURE13/LGF: Plate Groove Flat Position FIGURE13/LGH: Plate Groove Horizontal Position FIGURE13/LGV: Plate Groove Vertical Position FIGURE13/LGO: Plate Groove Overhead Position Size: 16 x 150 x 400 mm — 2 Nos.

TEST POSITIONS FIGURE :13/1 PLATE GROOVE JOINTS FIGURE13/LFH: Plate Fillet Horizontal Position FIGURE13/LFF: Plate Fillet Flat Position FIGURE13/LFV: Plate Fillet Vertical Position FIGURE13/LFO: Plate Fillet Overhead Position Size: 16 x 75 x 300 mm — 2 Nos TEST POSITIONS FIGURE13/2: PLATE FILLET JOINTS (TEE) FIGURE13/PGR: PIPE Axis Horizontal FIGURE13/PGV: Pipe Axis Vertical Welding from Top Flat Position (Pipe Rotated) Weld Horizontal position (Pipe Fixed) FIGURE13/PGH: PIPE Axis Horizontal FIGURE13/PGI: Pipe Axis Inclined at 45o Weld All around (Pipe Fixed) Weld All around (Pipe Fixed) Size: Pipe Dia 127.5mm x 12.5mm thick or more Tube Dia 76.1mm X 8.8mm thick or less TEST POSITIONS FIGURE13/3 PIPE/TUBE GROOVE JOINTS PART-3

513. Engagement of certified welders or welding operators.─ (1)No welder or welding operator shall be engaged for welding on boilers or steam-pipes under construction, erection or fabrication at site or in the repair of boilers or steam-pipes, unless he possesses a certificate as required under the regulations and attains the age not less than eighteen years.

(2) He shall have undergone a regular apprenticeship of two years from a recognised Industrial training institute or have at least one year regular on the job industrial experience as a welder or welding operator.

(3) The holder of such certificate shall be bound to produce it when called upon to do so by the Inspecting Authority or Competent Person.

(4) In case of engaging experienced welders or welding operators, reasonable continuity of service shall be ensured by the employer before engaging them by maintaining proper records of test conducted by the employing organisation.

514. Initial qualification test and issue of certificate.─ (1) Every welder or welding operator shall meet the qualification requirement to the satisfaction of the Competent Authority who shall assess his performance for qualifying for the certificate.

(2) The Competent Authority may, thereafter, issue a certificate in the Form-II indicating the class and type of welding in which he has qualified.

515. Issue of duplicate certificate.─ (1) In case of loss of a certificate issued to a welder or welding operator in FormXXI, a duplicate certificate may be issued by the Competent Authority who issued the original certificate provided that the Competent Authority is satisfied with the genuineness of the case.

(2) Fee for issue of duplicate certificate shall be as per regulation 331.

516. Validity of certificate.─ (1) The certificate issued to a welder or welding operator under these regulations shall remain valid for a maximum period of twenty-four months from the date of issue, provided that the holder thereof has, subsequent to the test, been employed with reasonable continuity on the class and type of work for which he is qualified.

517. Revalidation or renewal of certificate.─(1) The certificate may be renewed after the validity of 24 months from the date of qualification from time to time, for a period not exceeding twenty-four months at a time, in case the welder or welding operator is continuously engaged in qualified process and certified by the organisation for which he is working.

(2) The details of welding continuity shall be filled up in the Form-XX duly signed by the organisation and preserved along with the original certificate and put up to Competent Authority for renewal.

(3) In case of non-fulfilment of continuity criteria the welder or welding operator shall appear for the re-qualification test to the satisfaction of the Competent Authority.

518. Revoking of qualification.─ (1) The performance qualification of a welder or welding operator can be revoked by the Competent Authority if there is a specific reason to question his ability to make welds that meet the specification.

(2) The qualification that support the welding that he is doing shall be revoked.

519. Hierarchy of qualifications. ─ (1) (a) Initially, a welder or welding operator may be qualified in carbon steel plate and subsequently, allowed to appear for qualification testing in alloy steel plate, carbon steel or alloy steel pipe and tube qualification. or, a welder or welding operator may be tested for higher grades, pipe and tube directly.

(b) The range of qualification covered by testing of a particular grade of material or product form shall be as shown in Table 13/1.

(2) The qualification certificate of such a welder or welding operator who has successfully completed qualification test, may be extended as per regulation 517.

Table 13/1. Range of qualification for a welder or welding operator in material and product form Serial No.

Tested with Qualified for Carbon steel plate Carbon steel pipe and tube Alloy steel plate Yes No No No Alloy steel pipe and tube

1.

Carbon steel plate Yes Yes No No

2.

Carbon steel pipe and tube

3.

Alloy steel plate Yes No Yes No Yes Yes Yes Yes

4.

Alloy steel pipe and tube

520.Essential variables for welder or welding operator requalification. ─ A welder or welding operator shall require requalification whenever a change is made in one of the following essential variables, namely:-.

(i) An omission of backing- A welder or welding operator needs requalification when the backing is removed and double‐welded groove welds are considered welding with backing.

(ii) An addition of the backing in gas welding - A welder or welding operator shall require requalification when the backing is introduced in gas welding process.

(iii) A change in U number of electrode - A welder shall require requalification when the consumable group designation is changed from one U number to other U number (refer Table 13/5 & Table 13/6 of this regulation).

(iv) A change in the base metal (M number) to be welded - A welder shall require requalification when the base metal is changed (refer Table 2/1 in Chapter-2 and Table13/7 in this Chapter). However, a welder who is qualified in a higher grade of steel shall be permitted to weld a lower grade of steel.

(v) A change from one welding process to any other process - A welder shall require requalification when the welding process is changed.

(vi) A change from direct current to alternating current, and vice versa - A welder shall require requalification when the current type is changed from 'dc' to 'ac' and vice versa.

(vii) A change in welding position –(a) A welder shall require requalification when the position in which he is qualified is changed.

(b) A welder who is qualified in more difficult position shall be deemed to be qualified for a less difficult position as given in tables 13/2, 13/3 and 13/4.

(viii) Change in pipe diameter from qualified range.

(ix) Change in welding progression in vertical welding.

(x) Change in deposited weld metal thickness qualified (see Table 13/8) Table 13/2: Additional positions in which a welder is qualified (plate groove welding) Serial No.
