521. Tests for initial qualification of a welder or welding operator.─ For qualification of a welder, the welder or welding operator has to appear for the tests as given in regulations 522 to 526.

522. Theoretical examination.─ A welder or welding operator shall be required to answer questions on the following subjects, orally or otherwise-

(i) Weld preparation

(ii) Elementary knowledge of the working of welding equipment.

(iii) Elementary knowledge of properties of material to be welded – cold and hot working, thermal conductivity, fusion point, oxidation (for welders engaged in alloy steel welding).

(iv) Elementary knowledge of electro-technical principles, - kinds of current, striking arc voltage, welding – arc voltage (for arc welders).

(v) Kinds of welds, welding defects and their prevention, welding technique (forward and backward), influence of electrical parameters like amperage (for electric arc welders), preheating of base material and subsequent heat treatment.

(vi) Electrodes and filler metals for different types of welds and steels and joints in different positions.

523.Practical tests.─(1) The welder or welding operator shall be required to undergo and pass the tests as given in regulations 524 to 526.

(2) The material of plates, tubes, pipes and electrodes used for these tests shall conform to the requirements of appropriate regulations as may be applicable in each case.

524.Plate Welding.─ (1)One groove welded joint of two pieces of plates with single V, double V or double U grooves on boiler quality plates in the required positions (see Fig. 13/1 of regulation 512).

(2) One fillet welded joint over a length of 381 mm using plates of not less than 16 mm thick in the required positions (see Fig. 13/2).

525.Pipe and tube Welding.─ (1) A butt welded joint of two pieces of pipes with the axis in a horizontal position.

(see Fig. 13/PGH) with a pipe diameter 127.5 x 12.5 mm thickness (minimum).

(2) A butt welded joint of two pieces of boiler tubes of diameter 76.1 mm x 7.1 mm thickness (maximum) with the axis in a vertical position and tubes of same size fixed in adjoining positions at a pitch distance of 300 mm (see figure 13/PGV).

(3) A weld between a branch tubes of diameter 88.9 mm (minimum) to a pipe (see Fig. 13/7). The branch tube shall not be of size less than 89 mm diameter x 6 mm thick.

526. Cladding.─(1) This test is required only for welders or welding operators doing corrosion resistant/hard facing overlay.

(2) The qualification test coupon shall be of minimum size of 150 mm × 150 mm x T (thickness of base metal).

(3) The weld overlay cladding shall be of 38 mm wide (minimum) and 5 mm thick (minimum two layers).

(4) The weld overlay shall consist of a minimum of three weld beads in the first layer. (See Table 13/9 of chapter 13 for qualification limits.) Note - Every welded test piece for the examination of welders shall be stamped with an identification mark on either side of the weld.

527. Tests for requalification of a welder / welding operator.─ (1)The material of plates, tubes, pipes and electrodes used for practical tests shall conform to the requirements of appropriate regulations as may be applicable in each case.

(2) For requalification, the welder or welding operator need not be examined in theoretical subjects. He shall be required to qualify in the following practical tests, namely: -

(i) Plate Welding; a groove welded joint shall be made in a position originally qualified.

(ii) Pipe and Tube welding; as mentioned in regulation 525.

(iii) Cladding; as mentioned in regulation 526.

(3) When a welder is continuously engaged on production weld, test on the actual production joints to the satisfaction of the Competent Authority may be accepted as an alternative to the tests prescribed as above under clauses (i), (ii) and (iii) of sub-regulation (2).

PART-5 Examination of Test Specimens for Initial Qualification Tests

528. Groove welded plate specimens.─(1) (a) The Groove welded plate specimens after welding may be lightly pressed cold to remove any distortion due to welding.

(b)The test specimen shall be then subjected to visual examination and radiographic examination as mentioned in sub-regulation (2) of regulation 164 and thereafter test pieces shall be marked and cut out in the order shown in Fig. 13/4.

Fig. 13/4: ORDER OF REMOVAL OF TEST SPECIMEN FROM TEST PLATE FOR INITIAL QUALIFICATION TEST

(2) The tensile test specimens (T1 and T2) shall be prepared as per regulation 158. The bend test specimens two each (FB & RB) shall be prepared and tested as per regulations 160 and 161. Two specimens (M1& M2) shall be prepared, one for macro and the other for micro examination as per sub-regulation (1) of regulation 164 and the results of macro and micro examination shall be satisfactory

(3) The test shall be deemed to have been satisfactory when the requirements as given in sub-regulation (4) are met. The tensile strength of the both the test specimens shall meet the strength requirement for the material.

(4) The convex side of the bend test specimen after bending to 180° with a mandrel diameter of three times the thickness of the specimen shall show no discontinuities more than 3 mm in size in any direction. In the case of alloy steel specimen, the angle of the bend shall be decided by the competent authority, provided that in no case, the angle shall be less than 120°.

529.Fillet welded plate specimens.─ (1) (a) The preparation of the fillet-weld test specimen shall conform to Fig. 13/5 of chapter 13.

(b)The test specimen shall not contain any visible cracks. It shall be cut transversely to provide a centre section (254 mm) long and two end sections each approximately (25 mm) long.

(2) (a) The centre section shall be loaded laterally in such a way that the root of the weld is in tension.

(b) The load shall be steadily increased until the specimen fractures or bends flat upon itself.

(3) The cut end of one of the end sections shall be polished and etched with a suitable reagent to give a clear definition of the structure of the weld.

(4) The test shall be deemed to have been satisfactory when the requirements as specified in sub-regulation (5) are met.

(5) (a)The bend specimen shall not fracture during loading and if the specimen fractures, then the fractured surface shall show no evidence of cracks or incomplete root penetration, and the sum of the lengths of inclusions and gas pockets visible on the fractured surface shall not exceed (51 mm).

(b) Visual examination of the cross-section of the weld shall show the following:

(i) Complete fusion at the root and freedom from cracks.

(ii)Convexity or concavity of the weld profile not more than 1.6 mm

(iii) The length of two legs of the fillet within 1.6 mm difference.

FIG. 13 / 5: FILLET WELD SOUNDNESS TEST FOR PERFORMANCE QUALIFICATION

530. Groove and Fillet Welded Pipe and Tube Specimens.─ (1)(a) The groove welded pipe and tubes specimens shall be marked and test pieces cut out as in Fig. 13/6.

(b) The test pieces shall be prepared for face bend, root bend and etch test.

(2) (a) The pipes shall not be flattened, but tested in their curved conditions with the reinforcement at the root not exceeding 0.8 mm.

(b) Any excess weld metal may be removed by grinding.

(3) The bend test specimens shall show no discontinuity more than 3 mm on the convex side of the specimen.

FIG. 13/6: ORDER OF REMOVAL OF TEST SPECIMEN FROM WELDED PIPE / TUBE

(4) Fillet welded branches shall be tested by visual examination and macro examination as under:

(a) The test weld so obtained shall be visually examined for root penetration, freedom from undercut, disposition of runs, uniformity of surface, shape of profile, smoothness of joint, freedom from cavities and slag, dimensions of weld deposit, quality of weld metal (overheating, surface cracks, spongy surface, etc.) Any piece showing serious defect shall be rejected.

(b) Each branch weld test piece shall be sawn as shown in Fig. 13/7 and each portion so produced shall be prepared and etched. The sections shall then be examined for degree of fusion, degree of root penetration, absence of non-metallic (slag) inclusions and quality of weld metal. The joint should show good root fusion, complete freedom from basal cracking, and the absence of any very minor defects.

Outside Dia. of Pipe Main D1 – Dia.127 x 10 mm thick or more Outside Dia. of Branch D2 – Dia. 89mm x 6mm thick or more FIG. 13/7: ORDER OF REMOVAL OF SPECIMEN FOR BRANCH WELDS

531.Welded Tube Joints.─ (1)A test length approximately 203 mm long shall be cut from the tube with the welded joint disposed approximately at the centre. After visual examination, one test piece not exceeding 13 mm, for tubes 6 mm in thickness and below and thrice the thickness of the tube for thickness of over 6 mm shall be cut longitudinally.

(2) (a) The test piece shall not be less than 76 mm in length on either side of the weld.

(b) The slight excess of internal bead may be filed until it is not more than 0.8 mm proud of the general surface.

(c) The test piece shall then be bent back with the root in tension through 90° round a former of diameter equal to three times the thickness of the tubes.

(d) With regard to alloy steel pipes and tubes, the bend test specimen shall be bent without being straightened, but after removal of the weld reinforcement down to the level of but not below the surface, round a former of diameter 4t and through an angle of 90°.

(e) The bending may be carried out by blows or by pressure. Care shall be taken to ensure that the former is so positioned that the weld is maintained at the crown of the bend.

(f) The test specimen shall be capable of being bent through 90° without signs of failure, although slight premature failure at the edges of a specimen need not be considered a cause for rejection.

(g) The opening out of a slight defect due to incomplete root penetration or lack of root fusion need not be considered a cause for rejection; provided the defect has sound metal at the back and on either side of it.

532.Cladded Specimen.─ (1) The corrosion resistant or hard facing overlay weld surface shall be examined by the liquid penetrant method and shall meet the acceptance standards in sub-regulation (2) of regulation164.

(2) The corrosion resistant test coupon shall be sectioned to make four side‐bend (B1, B2, B3, B4) test specimens perpendicular to the direction of the welding as per regulations 160 and 161. Test specimens shall be removed at locations specified in Fig. 13/8.

Plate size 150x150 X T (Base metal thickness) mm FIG. 13 / 8: ORDER OF REMOVAL OF TEST SPECIMEN FROM CORROSION RESISTANT OVERLAY FOR INITIAL / RE-QUALIFICATION TEST

(3) (a) The hard facing overlay base metal shall be sectioned transversely to the direction of the hard‐facing overlay.

(b)The two faces of the hard‐facing exposed by sectioning shall be polished and etched with a suitable etchant and shall be visually examined with 5X magnification.

(c) Cracks, lack of fusion, or other linear defects in the weld metal or the heat‐affected zone shall be examined (see Fig. 13/9).

Plate size 150x150 X T (Base metal thickness) FIG. 13/ 9: ORDER OF REMOVAL OF MACRO TEST SPECIMEN FROM HARD FACING OVERLAY FOR INITIAL / RE QUALIFICATION TEST

533. Additional tests before rejection.─(a) Where any test piece tested as per the regulations 528 to 531 fail to meet the specified requirements, one further strip may be cut from the specimen and subjected to the same test and if any test piece does not reach the required standard, two further welds shall be made and the procedure in the above regulations shall be repeated.

(b) If any of these welds does not reach the required standard, the welder shall be deemed not to have qualified for a certificate.

534.Decision of the competent authority.─ The decision of the competent authority on refusal to grant Welder Certificate shall be final.

PART-6 Examination of test specimens for requalification tests

535.Groove Welded Plate Specimens.─(1) (a) The Groove welded plate specimens after welding may be lightly pressed cold to remove any distortion due to welding.

(b) The test specimen shall be then subjected to visual examination and radiographic examination as mentioned in sub-regulation (2) of regulation 164 and thereafter, test pieces shall be marked and cut out in the order shown in Fig. 13/10.

(2) (a) The face and root bend test specimens (FB & RB) shall be prepared and tested as per regulations 135 and

136. Two specimens (M1& M2) shall be prepared, one for micro and the other for macro examination as per subregulation (1) of regulation164.

(b) The test shall be deemed to have been satisfactory when the following requirements are met.

FIG. 13 / 10: ORDER OF REMOVAL OF TEST SPECIMEN FROM TEST PLATE FOR RE QUALIFICATION TEST

(i) The convex side of the bend test specimen after bending to 180° with a mandrel diameter of three times the thickness of the specimen shall show no discontinuities more than 3 mm in size in any direction and in the case of alloy steel specimen, the angle of the bend is to be decided by the competent authority, provided that the angle shall, in no case, be less than 120°.

(ii) The macro / micro examination reveal satisfactory microstructure of the weld metal and Heat Affected Zone (HAZ).

536.Fillet Plate Welded Specimen.─ They shall be conducted as mentioned in regulation 529.

537. Pipe and Tube Welding.─ Specimens shall be prepared and tested as mentioned in regulation 530 or 531, as the case may be.

538. Cladded Specimen.─ The cladded specimen evaluation shall be carried out as per regulation 532.

539.Additional test before rejection .─ (1) (a) in case any of the test pieces tested in accordance with the regulations 535 to 538fail to meet the specified requirements, one further weld may be cut from the specimen and subjected to the same test.

(b) If any test piece does not reach the required standard, one further weld shall be made after sufficient retraining, and the procedure in the above regulations shall be repeated.

(c) If the repeat weld does not reach the required standard, the welder shall be deemed not to have qualified for a certificate.

(2) The decision of the competent authority on refusal to grant welder certificate shall be final.

PART-7

540. Awarding of marks.─ (1) (a) Marks shall be awarded as per the Form-XXI.

(b) Candidates obtaining marks not less than 60 per cent in the oral or written examination and not less than 75 per cent in the practical tests, shall be deemed to have qualified for a certificate provided that the bend tests are found to be satisfactory and in case a candidate fail in the oral or written examination but passes in the practical test, he may be re-examined in oral or written examination within a period of twelve weeks from the date of examination of practical test, and if he obtains not less than 60 per cent marks in the re-examination, he shall be deemed to have qualified for a certificate.

(2) The Form shall be attested by the Competent Authority.

541. Award of Certificate.─ (1) On successful completion of theoretical tests mentioned in regulation 522 and practical tests mentioned in regulations 523 to 526, the welder may be awarded a certificate in Form-II to the extent of the tests passed by him.

(2) (a) Welder or welding operator who pass the tests for groove welds in plates shall be considered as having qualified for making fillet welds in plates.

(b) Welders or welding operators who pass the tests for fillet welds, shall be qualified to make fillet welds only.

(c) Any certificate issued for any purpose under any rules made by the State Government in this behalf shall be deemed to have been issued for that purpose under this Chapter and any action taken under the said rules, shall be deemed to have been taken under this Chapter.

542. Maintenance of records.─ (1) Every manufacturer or repairer of boilers and steam-pipes shall maintain a record of all welding personnel engaged on welding and repairing of boilers and steam-pipes, particulars of each welder and welding operator and the work handled by him. Such record shall be produced to the Inspecting Authority or Competent Person on demand.

(2) In the case of welders or welding operators who undertake welding under their own responsibility, the record as aforesaid shall be maintained by themselves.

543. Fees for examination of Welders or Welding operators.─ Fees for examination of Welders or Welding operators shall be as per regulation 331.

544. Penalty.─ Penalty shall be as per regulation 332.

CHAPTER -14 SMALL INDUSTRIAL BOILERS PART-1 GENERAL

545. Scope.─ This Chapter shall apply to the design, construction, inspection, registration, operation and maintenance of small industrial boilers.

546.For the purpose of this chapter, "small industrial boiler" shall include;-

(a) A shell type boiler generating steam for use external to itself under pressure up to 7 kg/cm2 (g) and having a volumetric capacity exceeding 25 litres (such capacity being measured from the feed check valve to the main steam stop valves), but not exceeding 500 litres including the volumetric capacity of all pressure parts being heated from the same heating source and connected to the boiler; or

(b) A coil type or a once through boiler or a water tube boiler having the conditions specified in clause (a) above except -

(i) The limitation of pressure shall be 12 kg/cm2 (g), and

(ii) The capacity shall not be exceeding 150 litres;

Provided that in case of boilers having combined features of clauses (a) and (b), the design pressure shall be restricted to 7 kg/cm2 (g) and the volumetric capacity of particular pressure parts shall not exceed the limits specified in the respective clauses above subject to an aggregate of 500 litres.

PART-2 Extent to which variation from the standard conditions laid down in the preceding chapters is permissible

547.─Variations from standards.-The variations from the standard conditions provided in the preceding chapters of these regulations shall be permitted for small industrial boilers as provided in regulations 548 to 554.

548. Materials.─(1) The materials used in the construction of small industrial boilers shall, except as otherwise provided under this chapter, conform to the provisions made in chapter-2 of these regulations.

(2) (a) Steel plates used in the construction of the boilers shall be of IS: 2062 fully killed or equivalent and the plate for shell and heads shall be not less than 6 mm in thickness and the heads as tube sheets for tubes rolled in shall be at least 8 mm in thickness.

(b) The steel maker certificate provided in Part A of Form V is not required for steel plates of IS: 2062 from the steel maker provided all other certification requirements as specified in applicable regulations are complied with.

(3) (a) Heads of parts of boilers when not exposed to direct impact of flame may be made of cast iron or malleable iron provided they comply with other requirements of these regulations.

(b) The allowable stress on cast iron or malleable iron shall be based on the tensile strength of material with a factor of safety of not less than 4.5.

549. Design, construction, workmanship and method of computing the Maximum Allowable Working Pressure.─ (1) (a) The construction except where otherwise specified shall be the same as those provided in the relevant provisions of the preceding chapters of these regulations.

(b) In case of fusion welded boiler, neither stress-relieving nor radiography of welded joints shall be required.

(2) (a) In small industrial boilers, unflanged steel tube plate may be inserted into the shell and welded for the entire thickness as shown in Figure 14/1 of this regulation with a fillet weld having a throat not less than 1¼ times the thickness of the shell or tube sheet whichever is smaller.

Figure14/1

(b) The minimum thickness of flanged welded tube sheets shall be 10 mm.

(c) The temperature of the heating element of electrically heated steam boilers shall be so controlled that it shall not exceed 650°C.

(3) (a) The tubes shall be made of steel.

(b) (i) The tubes holes shall be drilled full size with shells butt straps and ends bolted up in position or may be punched at least 13 mm smaller in diameter than full size and then drilled, reamed or finished full size with a rotating cutter.

(ii) The sharp edges and chips shall be removed, the plates and butt straps re-assembled metal to metal with pins fittings the holes and with tack bolts.

550. Inspection and Testing.─ The small industrial boilers shall be subjected to inspection during construction by an Inspecting Authority and the inspection of the boilers shall be made at the stages as given in regulations 551 and 552:

551. Shell type boilers.─(1) The inspection of shell type boilers shall be made at the following stages, namely:-

(i) material identification and inspection;

(ii) inspection of each component after completing welding and drilling;

(iii) assembly of boiler;

(iv) hydraulic test.

(2) In case of a boiler wholly or partly welded construction, the hydraulic test pressure shall be equal to two times the design pressure of the boiler.

(3) A test coupon shall be provided by the maker for conducting root and face bend tests.

552.Coil type boilers, once through boilers and water tube boilers.─ (1) The inspection of coil type boilers, once through boilers and water tube boilers shall be made at the following stages, namely:-

(i) Identification of materials.

(ii) Hydraulic test,

(2) The pressure for hydraulic test shall be two times of the design pressure of the boiler.

553.Certificates and maker's stamps.─ (1)The certificate and maker's stamp shall be in conformity with the provisions of Chapter-1 of these regulations.

(2) Where all information cannot be visibly stamped on the boiler plate due to its small size, maker's certificate accompanying the boiler shall contain the necessary details, but in all cases, name, works number and the year of the make shall be stamped or exhibited on the boiler.

PART-3

554. Valves, gauges, fittings and feed supply.─ (1) (a) (i) Every boiler shall be fitted with at least three brass wash plugs of not less than 25 mm diameter which shall be screwed into the openings in the shells near the bottom.

(ii) In the boilers of closed system type heated by removable internal electric heating elements, the opening for these elements when suitable for cleaning purposes may be substituted for wash out openings.

(b) (i) Boilers not exceeding 300 mm internal diameter may have two 25 mm openings for cleaning, one of which may be used for the attachment of the blow off valve.

(ii) Wherever possible, the openings shall be opposite to each other.

(iii) All threaded openings in the boiler shall be provided with welded reinforcement, if necessary, to give four full threads therein.

(c) (i) Electric boilers of a design employing removable top cover flange for inspection and cleaning need not be fitted with washout openings and washout plug.

(ii) Level gauges are not required for once through boilers.

(2) Every boiler shall be provided with at least one feed pump or other feeding device except where it is connected with a water main carrying pressure to feed that boiler or where the steam generator is operated with no extraction of steam (closed system). In the latter case, in lieu of the feeding device, a suitable connection or opening shall be provided to feed the boilers. Such connection shall be not less than 13 mm in diameter.

(3) (a) Every boiler shall be fitted with necessary mounting and fittings as provided in Chapter-5 of these regulations, and they shall be of substantial construction suitable for 7 kg/cm2(g).

(b) All these fitting, except safety valves shall be certified by manufacturers of the fittings themselves.

(4) (a) The feed pipe shall be provided with a check valve and a stop valve of a size not less than that of the pipe.

(b) The feed water may be delivered to the boiler through the openings of the same size in the shell as that of the blow off connection.

(5) (a) Each boiler shall be equipped with a blow off connection, not less than 18 mm in size located to drain the boiler water from the lowest water space practicable when the boiler is under pressure.

(b) Feed water shall not be introduced through the openings or connection needed for the water column, the water gauge glass or the gauge cock.

(6) (a) Every boiler other than a coil type or a once through boiler shall be fitted with two gauge glasses not less than 150 mm long for determining the water level.

(b) The boiler shall have the lowest visible part of the water gauge located at least 25 mm above the lowest permissible water level.

(7) (a) The lowest permissible water level of vertical boilers shall be at a point one-third of the height at the shell above the bottom head or tube sheet.

(b) Where the boiler is equipped with internal furnace, the water level shall not be less than one-third of the length of the tubes above the top of the furnace tube sheet.

(8) (a) The steam pressure gauge shall have its dial graduated to not less than twice the maximum allowable working pressure of the boiler.

(b) The diameter of the dial shall be at least 100 mm and the pipe connecting the pressure gauge shall be minimum 10 mm and the connection shall be through siphon.

(9) (a) Each boiler shall be equipped with two safety valves for relieving the steam pressure. The diameter of the valves shall not be less than 13 mm.

(b) The minimum relieving capacity of the safety valve shall be sufficient to discharge all the steam that can be generated by the boiler without allowing the pressure to rise more than 10 percent above maximum allowable working pressure.

(c) The safety valve shall be connected to the boiler independently of any other steam connections, without any unnecessary intervening pipe or fitting. Such intervening pipe or fitting if unavoidable shall not be longer than the corresponding face to face dimension of a tee fitting of the same diameter and the minimum opening there through shall be at least equal to the valve inlet.

(10) No valve of any description shall be placed between safety valve and the boiler not on the discharge pipe from the safety valve to the atmosphere.

(11) The electrically heated boilers shall be effectively earthed with a lead of substantial cross section.

555. Registration, operation and maintenance.─(1)(a) The fee required for registration under sub-section (2) of section 12 of the Act shall be as per table 8/2 of regulation 304.

(b) The annual inspection fee for renewal of certificate shall be as per table 8/3 of regulation 305.

(2) The certificate of manufacture and test for small industrial boilers shall be issued in Form-XXII.

(3) Operator for these boilers shall possess minimum educational qualification of matriculation or equivalent

CHAPTER-15 FEED WATER FOR BOILER

556. Scope.─ This chapter lays down specifications for feed water and boiler water for.─

(a) Low and medium pressure boilers (boilers operation up to 60 kg/cm2 (g).

(b) For boilers operating at pressures higher than 60 kg/cm2 (g), better quality of water to IS: 10496 shall be adopted.

557. Requirements.─ The water shall comply with the requirements given in Table 15/1 of this chapter when tested by the methods provided in Columns 6 and 7 of the table.

558. Sampling.─ Sampling shall be done by following general directions mentioned in Part-2 of IS: 3025 issued by Bureau of Indian Standards and in particular, the following points shall be observed, namely:-

(a) A stainless steel or monel metal coil shall be fitted on the sampling cock so that the temperature of the water sample shall be well below the boiling point at atmospheric pressure and there is no risk of aeration and concentration due to flashing into steam; and

(b) Samples of feed water shall be collected from the delivery of the boiler feed pump, samples of boiler water from the top drum, and samples of condensate from the delivery of the condensate extraction pump.

559. Test methods.─ Tests shall be carried out as provided in the appropriate clauses of IS: 3025 and IS: 3550 and indicated against the characteristics in Table 15/1 of this chapter.

1.

Recovery Boilers: The boiler feed water used shall be completely de-mineralized and also the boiler feed water and boiler water shall be conditioned in accordance with high pressure boilers working at 60 kg/cm2(g)and above (see IS: 4343).

2. When feed water heaters are of copper or copper alloy constructions, the pH of the feed water shall be maintained between 8.5 and 9.2 while when feed water heaters are of iron constructions, the pH of feed may be maintained between 8.5 and 9.5.

3. Silica in Boiler Water: Lower concentration of silica may be advisable for steam of turbines, which generally requires less than 0.02 mg/l silica in steam. * Methods of test for routine control for water used in industry. + Methods of sampling and test (physical and chemical) for water used in industry. φ Total alkalinity should preferably be about 20 percent of total dissolved solids. β Shall not apply if reducing agents other than sodium sulphite are used. α For shell type boilers depending on parameters, the limits can be relaxed.

CHAPTER -16 HEAT EXCHANGERS AND PRESSURE VESSELS PART-1

560. Application.─(1) This chapters hall apply to steam accumulators, steam coil air preheaters, high pressure(HP) heaters, deaerators and blow down tanks operating above 3.5 kg/cm2(g).

(2) Any steam consuming equipment where steam is condensed or open to atmosphere shall not be covered under this chapter.

561. Construction and workmanship.─ The construction and workmanship shall conform to the requirements of relevant regulations for the particular type of construction.

562. Materials.─ Materials used in the construction of these vessels shall comply with the applicable regulations of Chapter-2.

563. Welding consumables.─ Welding consumables shall comply with the relevant provisions of Chapter-2.

564. Tubes and pipes.─ The tubes and pipes shall comply with the relevant provisions of Chapter-2.

565. Heat treatment.─ All fusion welded vessels shall be heat treated for stress relief as per regulation 123.

PART-2 Determination of working pressure of vessels of welded construction

566. Shells.─ The working pressure shall be calculated as per Equation (4/1) mentioned under regulation 169 of chapter 4.

567.Dished end plates.─ The maximum working pressure of dished end plates subject to pressure on the concave side shall be determined as per Equation (4/10) of regulation 177 of chapter 4.

568.Flat end plates.─ The maximum working pressure of flat end plates shall be determined as per appropriate regulations in Chapter -3

569. Girth flange.─(1) Non-standard bolted flange connections when used shall satisfy the requirements of these regulations .

(2) The design and construction of a flange shall involve the selection of the gasket (material, type, and dimensions), flange facing, bolting, hub proportions, flange width, and flange thickness.

(3) Flange dimensions shall be such that the calculated stresses in the flange, does not exceed the allowable flange stresses.

570. Standard flanges.─ Standard flanges used in heat exchanger shall conform to the Codes or Standards like British Standards (BS), European Standards (EN), American Society of Mechanical Engineers (ASME) Boiler and Pressure Vessel Code and Tubular Exchanger Manufacturers Association (TEMA), commonly used in industrially advanced countries.

571. Bolts and Studs .─(1) Bolts and studs connecting the parts of the vessel such as shell, end plates , tube plates shall be made of steel.

(2)The maximum working pressure for the bolts and studs shall be as per by the following Equation,─ N x C

1.28 2 P = ———— D - ——Equation (16/1) A n Where, P Maximum allowable working pressure in tons/sq.in, D is the diameter of bolt or stud over threads in inches, N is the number of bolts or studs securing the part, n is the number of threads of screws per inch, C 4,700 for steel bolts or studs of 28 tons/sq. in. minimum ultimate tensile stress where the diameter over thread is less than 19 mm, C 5,100 for steel bolts or studs of 30 tons/sq. in. minimum ultimate tensile stress where the diameter over thread is less than 19 mm, C 5,600 for steel bolts or studs of 35 tons/sq. in. minimum ultimate tensile stress where the diameter over thread is not less than 19 mm, C 5,600 for steel bolts or studs of 28 tons/sq. in. minimum ultimate tensile stress where the diameter over thread is not less than 19 mm and not greater than 22 mm, C 7,000 for steel bolts or studs of 28 tons/sq. in. minimum ultimate tensile stress where the diameter over thread is greater than 22 mm. 'A' is the area in square inches of the surface supported by the bolts or studs. For jointed flanges the area shall be taken to extend to mid-way between the pitch lines of the bolts and the inner edge of the flange by which the part is secured.

PART-3

572. Tubes.─ Where the tubes are subjected simultaneously to both internal and external pressure, the thickness of the tubes shall be the higher of that determined independently by the equations 12/12 and 12/13 of regulation 471 of chapter 12.

573. Dished end plates.─ The shape of dished end plates shall be in conformity with the requirements of regulation174.

574. End plate attachments.─ These shall comply with any one of the figures provided in chapter-10 or chapter-12.

575. Tube plates.─ The minimum thickness of tube plates shall comply with the requirements of regulation

472.

576. Stand pipes, nozzles, pads and seating for mounting and their attachments.─Stand pipes, nozzles, pads and seating for mounting and their attachments shall comply the provisions of relevant regulations.

577. U-Tubes.─(1) U-tubes used in heat exchangers shall not have ovality more than 10% after forming.

(2) Carbon and alloy steel U-Tubes up to mean radius R = 5D (where D = Tube outside diameter) shall be stress relieved with minimum soaking time of 15 minutes.

578. Tube bundle assemblies. ─ Tube bundle assemblies shall be checked for free entry by using a ring gauge having inside diameter of baffle equal to Outside Diameter(OD) of tube + 1.5 mm.

579.Initial qualification of tube to tube sheet joints.─(1)The method of joining tubes to tube sheet shall be any one of the following, namely:-

(i)Strength expansion only (without welding);

(ii) Strength welding & light expansion, or

(iii) Strength expansion & seal welding.

(2) All the above three process shall be carried out with procedure qualified by the manufacturer.

(3) (a) The boiler or boiler component manufacturer shall prepare written procedures for joints that are expanded (whether welded and expanded or expanded only) for joint strength.

(b) The boiler or boiler component manufacturer shall establish the variables that affect joint repeatability in these procedures.

(c) The procedures shall provide detailed descriptions or sketches of enhancements, such as grooves, serrations, threads, and coarse machining profiles.

(a) For strength welded joints, minimum eight mock up welds are required to qualify each procedure.

(b) The mock up assembly shall essentially duplicate the tube hole configuration and tube-to-tubesheet joint design.

The materials group (M No. wise), tube hole size, tube size and tube fit up with tube sheet shall simulate production.

Test block arrangement shall be as given under,─ Minimum number of holes = 3-2-3 for triangular pitch. = 3-3-3 for Square pitch Thickness = Tube sheet thickness or 50 mm whichever is less.

(a) The test block simulating the tube-sheet may be circular, square or rectangular in shape, essentially in general conformity with the tube pitch geometry.

(b) The test assembly shall consist of an array of tubes such that the tube to be tested is in the geometric center of the array and surrounded by at least one row of adjacent tubes.

(c) The test block shall extend a distance of at least one tube-sheet ligament beyond the edge of the peripheral tubes in the assembly.

All tubes in the test block array shall be from the same grade of material and shall be installed using identical procedures.

(9) (a) The length of the tube used for testing the tube joint need only be sufficient to suit the test apparatus.

(b) The length of the tubes adjacent to the tube joint to be tested shall not be less than the thickness of the test block to be qualified.

580. Procedure for testing the test block.─(1) The mock-up welds shall be subjected to the tests given in regulations 581 to 583sequentially and shall meet the applicable acceptance criteria.

581. Acceptance criteria for visual examination.─ (1) The accessible surfaces of the welds shall be examined visually with no magnification required.

(2) The welds shall show complete fusion and no evidence of burning through the tube wall, and shall be free from cracking or porosity.

582. Acceptance criteria with liquid penetrant.─ Procedure and performance tests examined by liquid penetrant techniques shall be judged unacceptable when the examination exhibits any indication in excess of the limits specified as the following;-

(i) relevant linear indications;

(ii) relevant rounded indications greater than 5 mm;

(iii) four or more relevant rounded indications in a line separated by 1.5 mm or less (edge‐to‐edge).

583. Acceptance criteria for macro-examination.─ (1) The mock up welds shall be sectioned through the center of the tube for macro‐examination.

(2) The four exposed surfaces shall be smoothed and etched with a suitable etchant to give a clear definition of the weld and heat‐affected zone.

(3) Using a magnification of 10X to 20X, the exposed cross sections of the weld shall conform to as under;-

(i) minimum leak path dimension required by the design;

(ii) no cracking;

(iii) complete fusion of the weld deposit into the tube-sheet and tube wall face;

(iv) complete penetration of the weld deposit to within 0.4 mm of the root of the joint; and

(v) porosity shall not reduce the weld throat below the required minimum leak path thickness.

584. Tube thinning calculations.─ (1) Tube thinning calculations shall be using the equation given below, based on the measured values.

(2) The thinning shall be within 5% to 8%.

(T-t) - (D - d) % Thinning = ————————- x 100 Equation (16/2) 2 x Tube thickness Where, T = Internal diameter of the tube after expansion t = Internal diameter of the tube before expansion D = Tube sheet Hole Diameter d = Tube outer diameter.

CHAPTER- 17 SMALL SOLAR BOILERS

585. Scope.─This chapter shall apply to the design, construction, inspection, registration, operation and maintenance of small solar boilers.

PART-1 Definitions

586. Solar receivers.─ Solar receivers are the devices that receive solar radiation heat from the sun to generate steam.

587. Small solar boiler .─ (1) A small solar boiler shall be a solar steam generating system producing steam for use external to itself having area concentration ratio of solar radiation capturing aperture area to receiver area less than 300 and having a volumetric capacity more than 25 litres.

(2) A natural or forced circulation type boiler wherein the receiver is the solar heat receiving device generating steam at pressure up to 12 kg/cm2(g)

588. Heating surface area.─ The heating surface area of a small solar boiler shall be the area of receivers exposed to the receiving radiation.

589. Volumetric capacity.─ Volumetric capacity of a small solar boiler shall be the total volume from feed check valve to the main steam stop valve at the outlet of steam header.

PART-2 Extent to which Variation from the standard conditions laid down in the preceding chapters is permissible

590.Variations from standards.─ The variations from the standard conditions provided in the preceding chapters of these regulations shall be permitted for small solar boilers as provided in regulations 591 to 594.

591. Materials.─ (1) The materials used in the construction of small solar boilers shall conform to the provisions made in these regulations.

(2) (a) Plates used in the construction of the boilers shall be either of IS: 2062 or equivalent or as per Chapter-2.

(b) The plate for shell and heads shall be not less than 6 mm in thickness and the heads as tube sheets for tubes rolled in shall be at least 8 mm in thickness.

(3) The tubes and pipes shall be made of carbon steel, alloy steel, stainless steel or copper.

(4) Corrosion allowance of 0.75 mm shall be provided for parts of the boiler directly exposed to atmosphere and solar radiation.

(5) No corrosion allowance is required for stainless steel parts of the boiler.

(6) Materials need not be certified with Indian Boiler Regulations forms from the maker provided all other certification requirements as mentioned in applicable regulations are complied with.

592. Design, construction, workmanship and the method of computing the maximum allowable working pressure.─ (1) The construction except where otherwise specified shall be the same as those provided in the relevant provisions of the preceding chapters of these regulations.

(2) In the case of Fusion welded small solar boilers, radiography or stress relieving of the weld joints shall not be mandatory.

593. Inspection and testing.─ (1) The small solar boilers shall be subjected to inspection during construction and after completion of construction by an Inspecting Authority.

(2) The inspection of small solar boilers shall be made at the following stages, namely:-

(i) Material identification and inspection.

(ii) Hydraulic test; every completed boiler wholly or partly welded construction, the hydraulic test pressure shall be equal to two times the design pressure of the boiler.

594. Certificates and maker's stamps.─ (1) The certificate and maker's stamp shall be in conformity with the provisions of Chapter-1of these regulations.

(2) Where all information cannot be visibly stamped on the boiler plate due to its small size, maker's certificate accompanying the boiler shall contain the necessary details, but in all cases name, works number and the year of make shall be stamped or exhibited on the boiler.

PART-3

595. Valves, gauges, fittings and feed supply.─ (1) Small solar boiler shall be fitted with the following, namely:–

(i) wash plugs for cleaning;

(ii) suitable provision to indicate the water level;

(iii) one water feeding device;

(iv)one feed pipe shall be provided with a check valve and a stop valve of a size not less than that of the pipe;

(v) suitable provision to drain the boiler water;

(vi) one steam pressure gauge having minimum 100 mm dial size; and

(vii) one safety valve for relieving the steam pressure.

(2) Safety valves shall be certified under Indian Boiler Regulations and all other fittings may be certified by the manufacturers of such fittings.

596. Registration, operation and maintenance.─(1)The fees required to accompany an application under subsection (2) of Section 12 of t h e Act shall be such as provided in regulation 304.

(2) The inspection fee for renewal of certificate after every four years shall be as provided in regulation

305.

(3) The certificate of manufacture and test for small solar boilers shall be issued in Form-XXIII.

Operator for these boilers shall possess minimum educational qualification of matriculation or equivalent.

[See regulation 17(2)] QUESTIONNAIRE TO BE ANSWERED BY FIRMS/COMPANIES SEEKING RECOGNITION BY THE CENTRAL BOILERS BOARD TO BECOME AN "INSPECTING AUTHORITY"

1.

The registered name and address of the company/firm:

2.

Address for correspondence:

3.

The year in which the company/firm was established.

4.

Proposed countries/areas of operation as Inspecting Authority:

5.

Is company/firm registered in the proposed countries of operations? If so, please give details thereof.

6.

Have you any Branch or Associate Office? If so, please give their names and addresses.

7. Is company/firm functioning as an Inspecting Authority under the Indian or International codes and standards? If so, details thereof.

8. In case of renewal of recognition as Inspecting Authority under the Indian Boiler Regulations, 2026, had you conducted inspection during the last five years? If yes, details thereof.

9. Is company/firm involved in any commercial activity other than inspection, certification and related activities under the Indian or International Codes and standards? If so, details thereof.

10. Please state the types, size and the range of design pressure of the boilers which you have so far inspected during manufacture as an Inspecting Authority, also state the classes of service you render, namely: -

(a) Please name the various stages of manufacture at which inspections are carried out.

(b) Only hydraulic test after the manufacture of the boiler.

11. How many Inspectors/Competent persons have you in your employment? Please give details of the qualifications held by those persons.

12.

Have you any Testing Laboratory of your own to conduct all destructive and non-destructive tests required in connection with the manufacture of boilers? If so, details thereof.

13.

Have you in-house design and drawing inspection office? If so, details thereof.

14. Have you any documented quality programme established and maintained to fulfill the inspection requirements as per Indian Boiler Regulations, 2026? If so, details thereof.

15. Are you having Curriculum Vitae of all the inspectors/competent persons employed in the organization for inspection and certification work?

16.

Are you having a training programme for Inspectors/Competent persons? If so, details thereof.

17. Are you prepared to conduct the work of Inspection of boilers, economisers and their accessories strictly in conformity with the Indian Boiler Regulations, 2026?

18.

Are you prepared to accept full responsibility for the certificate issued by you?

19. Has your request for recognition as an Inspecting Authority been rejected by any Authority? If so, please give details.

20. Are you prepared to issue certificates for the products, you inspect, in the formats of the Indian Boiler Regulations?

21. Are you aware that the recognition is for a period of five years only, which is renewable after every five years on fresh assessment? NAME, SIGNATURE & SEAL Form VI(B):R01 Dated: 07.08.2026 FORM-VI (PART-B)

15.

1. Name and address of the owner

2. Registry number of the boiler

3. Steam pressure and temperature

4. Rate of steam generation

5. Heating surface

6. Year of make

7. Brief description of boiler

8. Type of construction (Whether riveted or welded)

9. Whether fired or waste heat boiler

10. Date of registration

11. Details of past exemption granted by the Government, if any

12. Date of last annual inspection

13. Expiry date of current certificate

14. Maximum working pressure at which last certificate was issued

15. Details of past repairs (year-wise)

16. Remark as entered in the last certificate

17. Quality of boiler feed water

18. Whether requisite number of feed pumps are in satisfactory working condition at present?

19. Number of safety valves mounted on shell/drum and super heater

20. Total number of soot blowers provided in boiler

21. Number of soot blowers in working condition

22. Whether safety valves are blowing satisfactorily at or below design pressure?

23. Whether safety valve assembly is free from jamming as verified by operating casing lever?

24. Whether high and low water level alarm is in good condition?

25. Whether main steam stop valves, feed check valves, blow down valves and master pressure gauge in working condition?

26. Whether additional requirements for automatic boilers as per regulation 182 are complied with? (If 'No', give details)

27. Last date of calibration for master pressure gauge, temperature indicator/recorder for superheater, hot reheat, cold reheat and main steam line.

29. Details of boiler stoppages in last twelve months with reasons and remedies thereof

30. Present irregularities in instruments and controls if any observed in control-room of boiler house

31. Details of present boiler leakage

32. Present operating pressure of the boiler

33. Whether water quality is tested on-line (enclose copy of test report showing values of analysis including Total Dissolved Solids (TDS)

36. State at what intervals such cleaning is carried out

40. List of Boiler Operation Engineers/Attendants

41. Irregularities, if any, noticed in the past in compliance of the Act

42. Whether guidelines laid down by Central Boilers Board for granting exemption to the waste heat boilers are fulfilled or not? Name and Signature of General Manager (Generation) Remarks of the Competent Person who verified correctness of above statement paying check visit to the Boiler House.

Date of Issue Form XII:R01 Dated: 07.08.2026 FORM-XII (Regulation 310) CERTIFICATE FOR USE OF A BOILER Registry Number of Boiler Type of Boiler Boiler Rating Place and year of manufacture Maximum Continuous Evaporation Name of Owner Situation of Boiler Repairs Remarks Hydraulically Tested on to I hereby certify that the above described boiler is permitted by me/the Chief Inspector under the provisions of Section 12/13 of the Boilers Act, 2025, to be worked at a maximum pressure of ……………… kg/cm2(g) for the period from ……………… to ……………… The loading of the ……………… safety valve is not to exceed ……………… kg/cm2(g) Fee Rs. ……………… paid on ……………… Dated at ………… this ………… day of ………… 20…… Name and signature of Competent Person Countersigned Chief Inspector See Reverse for "Conditions" CONDITIONS (REVERSE OF FORM XII)

(1) No structural alteration, addition of renewal shall be made to the boiler otherwise than in accordance with section 18 of the Act.

(2) Under the provisions of Section 13 of the Act this certificate shall cease to be in force:

(a) on the expiry of the period for which it was granted; or

(b) when any accident occurs to the boiler; or

(c) when the boiler is moved the boiler, except a vertical boiler, the heating surface of which is less than two hundred square feet, or a portable or vehicular boiler; or

(d) save as provided in section 17 of the Act, when any structural alteration, addition or renewal is made in or to the boiler; or

(e) if the Chief Inspector in any particular case so directs when any structural alteration, addition or renewal is made in or to any steam-pipe attached to the boiler; or

(f) on the communication to the owner of the boiler of an order of the Chief Inspector or Inspector prohibiting its use on the ground that it or any boiler component attached thereto is in a dangerous condition.

Under Section 15 of the Act, when the period of a certificate relating to a boiler has expired, the owner shall, provided that he has applied before the expiry of that period for a renewal of the certificate be entitled to use the boiler at the design pressure entered in the former certificate, pending the issue of orders on the application but this shall not be deemed to authorise the use of a boiler in any of the cases referred to in clauses (b), (c), (d), (e) and (f) of sub-section (1) of section 13 occurring after the expiry of the period of the certificate.

(3) The boiler shall not be used at a pressure greater than the pressure entered in the certificate as the Design pressure nor with the safety valve set to a pressure exceeding such design pressure.

(4) The boiler shall not be used otherwise than in a condition which the owner reasonably believes to be compatible with safe working.

(5) Form XII shall be countersigned by the Chief Inspector at the time of registration only, as per the provisions of sub-section (6) of section 12 of the Act.

Note: The particulars and dimensions regarding this boiler may be obtained by the owner on payment in the prescribed manner on application to the Chief Inspector.

Manufacturer (Name, Signature & Stamp) We certify that the above boiler was constructed under our supervision and inspected at various stages of construction as specified in chapter-17 by the Competent Person and that the construction, workmanship were satisfactory as per Indian Boiler Regulations, 2026.

Competent Person Inspecting Authority (Name, Signature & Stamp) (Name, Signature & Stamp) Dated ………… this ………… Day of ………… 20…… APPENDIX-A INSPECTION AND TESTING OF BOILERS, BOILER COMPONENTS AND OTHER PARTS DURING MANUFACTURE GENERAL. ─The Inspecting Authority or Competent Person shall have access to the works of the manufacturer at all reasonable times and shall inspect the manufacture of the boiler at least at the following stages and may reject any part that does not comply with the requirements of the Indian Boiler Regulations, 2026. In case of any doubt, the Inspecting Authority or Competent Person may examine at any stage other than the stages stipulated below. The manufacturer shall give at least four working days' notice to the Inspecting Authority or Competent Person before reaching each stage. Before undertaking any of the stage inspections, the Inspecting Authority or Competent Person shall satisfy himself that the testing equipment/instrument has been properly calibrated.

STAGES OF INSPECTION. ─ Each boiler shall be inspected during manufacture by a Competent Person nominated by the Inspecting Authority. Sufficient inspections shall be made to ensure that the materials, manufacturing and testing conform to the requirements of these regulations.

Stages of Inspection During Manufacture. ─ A. Shell Type Boilers. ─(a) (i) When the materials are ready for identification with the relevant material test certificates at boiler maker's works;

(ii) In laying out and cutting the plates, the plate identification mark shall be located so as to be clearly visible after the boiler part is completed. If the plate's identification mark is unavoidably cut out, it shall be transferred by the manufacturer to another part of the component to the satisfaction of the Inspecting Authority or Competent Person;

(iii) The Inspecting Authority or Competent Person shall identify weld test plate material if production weld tests are required;

(b) When the Shell plate and end plates have been formed with plate edges prepared for welding and test plates are attached;

(c) When the welding of main cylindrical shell is completed and checked for circularity;

(d) To examine radiographs and/or reports of non-destructive testing;

(e) When openings have been prepared and stand pipes and similar connections including end plates have been tackwelded in position and subsequently on completion;

(f) When welding of drum or shell is completed and to check the records of heat-treatment when heat treatment is required under these regulations;

(g) When weld test specimens have been prepared from the test plate, previously selected to witness the required testing;

(h) During hydraulic test, followed by external and internal examination and stamping.

B. Water Tube Boilers. ─

(I) Welded drums, headers, separators and vessels. ─(a) (i) When the materials are ready for identification with the relevant material test certificates at boiler maker's works;

(ii) In laying out and cutting the plates, the plate identification mark shall be located so as to be clearly visible after the boiler part is completed. If the plate's identification mark is unavoidably cut out, it shall be transferred by the manufacturer to another part of the component to the satisfaction of the Inspecting Authority or Competent Person;

(iii) The Inspecting Authority or Competent Person shall identify weld test plate material if production weld tests are required;

(b) When the plates are formed to cylindrical shape with the edges prepared for welding and set up in readiness for commencement of welding and attachment of test plates;

(c) When the welding of the main cylindrical shell is completed, the shell checked for circularity and the radiographic or ultrasonic test reports, records are available for scrutiny;

(d) When the end plates are ready for identification with the mill certificate, formed to shape with weld edges prepared and set on to the cylindrical shell in readiness for the circumferential welding operation;

(e) When the welding of the end plates to the drum or the header is complete and the radiographs or ultrasonic examination records are available for scrutiny;

(f) When each drum or header is prepared to receive any compensation plates and attachments, and when at least 10% of each type of branch of tube stub is set up ready for welding;

(g) When all welding on each drum or header is complete, the Inspecting Authority or Competent Person will check the records of heat treatment, and mark off of specimens for preparation and testing from test plates;

(h) During non-destructive examination, after stress relieving, on alloy steel drums and carbon steel drums with carbon content exceeding 0.25 % or thickness more than 100 mm;

(i) During hydraulic test followed by final examination and stamping.

(II) Seamless drums, headers, separators and vessels. ─(a) When materials are ready for identification with the relevant material test certificates, also when each cylinder is prepared for forming, or welding of separate end closures and to identify test plate material;

(b) When the end plates are ready for identification with the mill certificate, formed to shape with weld edges prepared and set on to the cylindrical shell in readiness for the circumferential welding operation;

(c) When the welding of the end plates to the drum or the header is complete and the radiographs or ultrasonic examination records are available for scrutiny;

(d) When each drum or header is prepared to receive any compensation plates and attachments, and when at least 10% of each type of branch of tube stub is set up ready for welding;

(e) When all welding on each drum or header is complete, the Inspecting Authority will check the records of heat treatment, and mark off of specimens for preparation and testing from test plates;

(f) During non-destructive examination, after stress relieving, on alloy steel drums and carbon steel drums with carbon content exceeding 0.25 % or thickness more than 100 mm;

(g) During hydraulic test followed by final examination and stamping.

C. Tubular and piping components. ─(a) When the tubes or pipes are ready for identification with the relevant material test certificates at the boiler makers' works;

(b) When all welding of tubes or pipes and their attachments are complete and the non-destructive examination and stress relieving report/records are available for scrutiny;

(c) During hydraulic test followed by final examination and stamping.

D. Inspections and tests to be carried out at Steel Makers' Works Foundry or Forging Units and pipe and tube makers' works by the Inspecting Authority or Competent Person. ─

(I) Steel Makers Works. ─(a) For manufacture of the billets, ingots, slabs, blooms, plates, bars, concast bars or any other material to be used in the construction of the boilers:

(i) Checking of the chemistry of steel as per regulations;

(ii) When ready for non-destructive examination and selection of mechanical test specimen after heat treatment;

(iii) Testing, final examination and stamping.

Note: Notwithstanding anything specified above where the steel is made by the Well-known Steel Maker as recognised under the Indian Boiler Regulations by the Central Boilers Board, these tests shall be carried out and certified by the Steel Makers themselves and the records maintained properly.

(II) Pipe and Tube Makers. ─(i) Identification of materials with relevant material test certificates;

(ii) When ready for non-destructive examination and selection of mechanical test specimens after heat treatment;

(iii) When specimen is tested and pipes or tubes are ready for hydraulic tests.

(iv) Final inspection and stamping Note : Notwithstanding anything specified above, when the pipes or tubes are manufactured by well-known pipes or tubes makers recognised under the Indian Boiler Regulations by the Central Boilers Board, all the above stages of inspections shall be carried out and certified by the manufacturers of pipes and tubes themselves and records maintained properly.

(III) Forging Units. ─ (i) Checking of the chemistry of steel as per regulations;

(ii) When ready for non-destructive examination and selection of mechanical test specimen afterheat treatment;

(iii) Testing, final examination and stamping.

Note: Notwithstanding anything specified above, when the forgings are made by well-known forging units as recognised under the Indian Boiler Regulations by the Central Boilers Board then tests shall be carried out and certified by the Forges themselves and all records maintained properly.

(IV) Foundry Units. ─(i) Checking of the chemistry of molten metal;

(ii) Verification of heat number and stamping of test bars;

(iii) Verification of non-destructive examination report and heat treatment reports/records, mechanical testing, final examination and stamping.

Note: Notwithstanding anything specified above, when the castings are made by well-known foundry units as recognised under the Indian Boiler Regulations by the Central Boilers Board then tests shall be carried out and certified by the Foundries themselves and all records maintained properly.

E. Valves and Mountings. ─(a) Identification of materials with the relevant material test certificates;

(b) Hydraulic test and stamping.

APPENDIX-B TRANSVERSE RUPTURE STRESS (MODULUS OF RUPTURE) The transverse strength of cast iron may be expressed by a figure shown as the Transverse Rupture Stress; this figure is obtained by dividing the maximum bending moment at failure by the modulus of the section. Thus, the Transverse Rupture Stress is the maximum stress which would have existed if the material had behaved in accordance with the assumptions made in the ordinary theory of bending, and in that event it would be independent of the size and shape of the section. Bars fractured in transverse, however, are stressed beyond the elastic limit, and under these conditions the theory of bending no longer holds good. Furthermore, the influence of rate of cooling in cast metals is such that the Transverse Rupture Stress of a Thick Bar is less than that of a thinner bar of the same metal. The Transverse Rupture Stress, therefore, is not strictly independent of the size and metal. The Transverse Rupture Stress, therefore, is not strictly independent of the size and shape, of the section, but nevertheless forces a convenient way of expressing the result of transverse tests without the necessity for giving full details of bar dimensions. The formula required is obtained as follows:

A bar supported at both ends and centrally loaded with a load 'W' is subject to a bending Moment WL/4, 'L' being the distance between supports. The resistance offered by the bar may be expressed as fZ, 'f' being the stress and 'Z' the modulus of the section. At fracture, 'f' becomes the breaking stress of Transverse Rupture Stress, and WL/4 = fZ, where f = (LW)/ (4Z). If 'W' is in tons and 'L' in inches, 'f' is in tons per square inch. For a round bar, Z =

0.0982d2, where d diameter in inches.

Since for a standard round bar 'L' and 'Z' are constant, the value WL/4Z is fixed and hence the Transverse Rupture Stress f = KW, where 'K' is a constant.

The factor 'K' for converting actual breaking loads into Transverse Rupture Stress are given in the following tables both for the Standard test bars and the test bars varying within the limits of regulation 65. In the same table are given factors for converting actual breaking loads into equivalent breaking loads on the bars of Standard diameter.

Factors X for converting Actual Breaking Loads into Equivalent Breaking Loads on Bars of Standard Diameter.

(Equivalent Breaking Load on Bar of Standard Diameter = X x Actual Breaking Load).

And Factors K for converting Actual Breaking Loads into Transverse Rupture Stresses.

(Transverse Rupture Stress in tons per sq.in. = K x Actual Breaking Load in lbs.) APPENDIX- C (See regulation 75)

(1) Parent metal for test plates.─ The parent metal used in preparing test pieces and test specimen shall be equivalent matching mild steel or non-ferrous material as per the relevant American Welding Society (AWS) or Filler Alloys (SFA) specification. A matching material shall be used with buttering with a minimum of 3 mm thickness on groove faces and on backing plate. The thickness and other dimensions shall be as per the relevant American Welding Society (AWS) or Filler Alloys (SFA) specification.

(2) All-weld metal test.─ Method of preparation of test pieces. The temperature of the parent metal shall be between 10°- 38°C minimum immediately before deposition the first run of weld metal. Preheat and interpass temperature shall be as applicable to the relevant American Welding Society (AWS) or Filler Alloys (SFA) specification. The test specimen shall not be subjected to any mechanical or thermal treatment other than the required American Welding Society (AWS) or Filler Alloys (SFA)specification. All-weld test pieces shall be prepared as per the applicable American Welding Society (AWS) or Filler Alloys (SFA) specification.

Fig. 2/2: Method of preparation of All-Weld Metal Test Specimen The assembly shall be welded together with these plates pre-set so that the gap at the top between the chamfered edges of the plates is 25 mm and the plates may be approximately level when the butt-weld is completed.

The weld metal shall be deposited in single or multi-run layers and the direction of deposition of each layer shall be alternatively from each end of the specimen. Each run of weld metal shall not be less than 1.5 mm and not more than 3 mm thick. The time interval between the completion of one run and the commencement of deposition of the succeeding run shall be not less than five minutes. The assembly shall not be quenched between the deposition of individual runs. The welding current used shall be within the appropriate range given by the manufacturer. The welding position for the assembly shall be flat, unless this is contrary to the recommended position for the electrode, in which case the position of weld shall be as recommended by the manufacturer.

On completion of the weld the specimen shall be allowed to cool in still air. The portion including the weld shall then be removed by cutting away the excess plates at the places indicated in Fig. 2/2.

The all weld test piece shall be heat-treated by raising to a uniform temperature and maintained at that temperature as per the relevant American Welding Society (AWS) or Filler Alloys (SFA) specification of electrodes.

The test plate shall be allowed to cool slowly outside the furnace, from 300°C onwards protected from draughts and chilling. Rate of heating and cooling shall be as per the relevant American Welding Society (AWS) or Filler Alloys

(SFA) specification. A tensile test specimen shall be machined from the test piece to the dimension given in Fig. 2/3 taking care that the longitudinal axis of the specimen coincides with the center line of the weld and the mid-thickness of the plate as indicated in Fig. 2/2. The specimen then shall be tested for tensile strength.

Fig. 2/3: TENSILE TEST SPECIMEN

(3) Impact test specimen.─ The charpy test specimen shall be machined from the weld metal test pieces to the dimensions as per the relevant American Welding Society (AWS) or Filler Alloys (SFA) specification. The test specimens shall be taken for test from the all weld test plate assembly.

Fig. 2/4: IMPACT TEST SPECIMEN

(4) Transverse tensile and bend tests.─ The transverse tensile and bend test pieces shall be made as shown in Fig. 2/5 by butt-welding together two 12 mm plates of suitable length and not less than 150 mm in width. The plate edges shall be prepared to form a single Vee joint the details of which shall be a follows:

Fig. 2/5: METHOD OF MAKING TRANSVERSE TENSILE AND BEND TEST SPECIMENS Fig. 2/6: GROOVING IN PREPARATION FOR DEPOSITION OF BACKING RUN (See Fig. 2/6) Angle between fusion faces 60°-70° Root face 3.2 mm.

Maximum gap 3.2 mm.

The plate edges shall be prepared as per Fig. 2/6, and pre-set to allow for slight distortion after welding.

The welding procedure in making out the test pieces shall be according to the position of welding as in Table 2/38 below:

Welding Procedure Table 2/38: Welding procedure for preparation of transverse tensile test and bend test piece Welding position for test pieces (all angles ≠ 5°) shown in Table 2/39 below FLAT Weld slope 0° Weld rotation 0°

1. All runs made with 4 mm electrodes.

2. First run – 5mm. electrodes. Subsequent runs 6.3 mm diameter electrodes (or largest size manufactured).

INCLINED Weld slope 30° Weld rotation 45° First – 4 mm electrodes.

Subsequent runs – 4./5.0 mm electrodes HORIZONAL VERTICAL Weld slope 0° First run – 4 mm electrodes.

Weld rotation 90° Subsequent runs – 4 /5 mm electrodes VERTICAL Weld slope 90° All runs made with 4 mm electrodes.

All runs made with 4 mm electrodes.

OVER HEAD Weld slope 0° Weld rotation 180° In all cases a backing run shall be made with 4 mm. electrodes in the welding position applicable to the test piece, after cutting out a groove of 3 mm deep if considered necessary, as in Fig. 2/6.

The test pieces for the inclined and vertical positions shall be welded using the 'upwards' methods unless the electrodes manufacturer especially recommends that only the 'downwards' method shall be used, if both methods are recommended, test pieces welded by each method shall be made. After welding, the test pieces shall be cut by sawing on machining to form one face-bend and one root-bend test specimen, as indicated in Fig. 2/5.

(5) Transverse tensile test.─ Transverse tensile tests specimens shall conform to the dimensions given in Fig. 2/7.

The upper and lower surfaces of the weld shall be filed, ground or machined level with the respective original surfaces of the plates. Where the plates are not level with each other the metal many be cut away to bring them approximately level, provided that the thickness of the plate is not reduced by more than a total of 1 mm.

The test specimens shall then be tested for tensile test as in Appendix-D.

(6) Transverse bend test.─ The bend test specimens shall be 38 mm in width. The upper and lower surfaces of the weld shall be filled, ground or machined level with the respective original surfaces of the plates, with the proviso as in item (6) above. The tool marks should be avoided as they lead to location of stress and may cause premature failure. For this reason the direction of machining of the surfaces should be along the specimens and transverse to the weld. The sharp corners of the test specimens shall be rounded to a radius not exceeding 1.3 mm.

The test specimens shall be bent through an angle of 180° over a former having a diameter equal to three times the thickness of the specimen, as shown in Fig. 2/8. One test specimen shall be tested with the face of the weld in tension and one with the root of the weld in tension.

Fig. 2/8: Method of Carrying Out Bend Tests Fig. 2/7: Dimensions of Transverse Tensile Test Specimen DEFINITION OF WELDING TERMS, CLASSIFICATION OF COVERED ELECTRODES, DEFINITIONS & PRINCIPLES – DEFINITIONS Weld Slope: The slope of a weld is the angle formed between the line of weld root and a horizontal reference plane placed below the lowest portion of the weld. (See Fig. 2/9) Fig. 2/9: Figure to Illustrate Weld Slope Weld Rotation: The rotation of weld is the angle formed between the upper portion of vertical reference plane which passes through the line of the weld root and a line drawn from the line of the weld root which intersects the weld surface at a right angle and a point equidistant from either edge of the weld (see Fig. 2/10) Note: Example of the slope & rotation of various welds are given in Fig. 2/11.

Fig. 2/10: Figures to Illustrate Weld Rotation Fig. 2/11: Examples of 'Slope' and 'Rotation' Fig. 2/12: Flat (F) NOT EXCEEDING 90° I Fig. 2/13 TABLE 2/39 ELDING POSITIONS: WELDING POSITIONS ARE DEFINED AS FOLLOWS POSITIONS SLOPE ROTATION SYMBOL ILLUTRATION FLAT NOT EXCEEDING 10° NOT EXCEEDING 10° F Fig. 2/12 INCLINED EXCEEDING 10°NOT EXCEDING 45° H Fig. 2/14 HORIZONTAL VERTICAL NOT EXCEEDING 10° EXCEEDING 10° NOT EXCEEDING 00° VERTICAL EXCEEDING 45° ANY V Fig. 2/15 OVERHEAD NOT EXCEEDING 45° EXCEEDING 90° O Fig. 2/16 NOTE: The five positions defined above cover any possible combination of slope and rotation so that every weld can be classified in one of these positions.

The table above is for Fig. 2/12.

2.8 APPENDIX-E PROOF TEST FOR CREEP QUALITY OF CARBON STEEL BOILER QUALITY PLATE– SPECIFICATION

1. NATURE OF TEST.─ The test shall consist of a tensile creep test carried out over a period of at least 48 hours at a temperature of 450°C and with stress of 11.25 kg/mm2. Under these conditions the slope of the chord to the creep curve between the 24th and 48th hour shall not exceed 50 x 10 = 4° strain per hour.

2. CONDITION OF MATERIAL. ─ The test shall be made on the material in the normalized condition. The normalizing temperature shall be between 875°C – 925°C, and the plate sample shall be maintained at the normalizing temperature for one hour per inch of thickness and cooled freely in still air.

3. TEMPERATURE OF TEST. ─ Temperature Measurement. ─ (a) The temperature of the specimen shall be measured by thermocouples suitable for the temperature specified. Two thermocouples situated one at each end of the gauge length shall be used in the case of test pieces having gauge lengths upto 51 mm, and for longer gauge lengths and additional thermocouple situated at the middle of the gauge length shall be used.

(b) Thermocouples shall make good thermal contact with the test piece and the protected from the direct heat of the furnace of being covered with asbestos tape or equivalent insulation.

(c) The cold junction shall be maintained at a known temperature which shall be registered by a mercury thermometer. Alternatively, a suitable means shall be provided for correcting automatically errors in reading due to changes in temperature of the cold junction.

Temperature Control. ─The two (or three) thermocouples specified in clause (a) shall agree with one another within 3°C and the average of these readings shall be taken as the test temperature which shall not vary from 450°C by more than ± 2°C throughout the test.

Either a continuous record of sufficient readings of the temperature shall be made to indicate that the temperature conditions have been satisfactory.

(3) Heating period before loading. ─ The specimen shall be maintained at the specified temperature for at least an hour prior to the application of the load.

4. STRESS. ─The load required to produce the specified stress shall be gradually applied without shock during a period not exceeding five minutes and the stress shall be accurate to ± 2 ½ percent.

5. SENSITIVITY OF STRAIN-MEASUREING EQUIPMENT. ─The sensitivity of the extensometer must be such that strains can be measured to the nearest 0.00002.

6. READINGS. ─ A sufficient number of strain readings shall be taken clearly to define the creep curve upto 48 hours, and the chord slope shall be measured from a chord drawn between points on the curve at the 24th and 48th hours.

7. REPEAT TEST. ─ If the materials fail to pass the test, but the chord slope does not exceed 60 x 10 = 6° strain per hour, a retest may be made at the request of the manufacturer and shall be accepted if satisfactory, or at the Competent Person discretion a pass test at a stress 5 percent less than that specified may be accepted.

If the temperature is higher than 427°C and does not exceed 482°C the following Table should be used for the next higher pressure.

Water pipes for a given pressure in which the water temperature does not exceed 232°C may be fitted with Flanges to the next lower Table than that required for steam at the stated pressure and a maximum temperature of 427°C.

In all cases the hydraulic test pressure should be twice the maximum allowable working pressure to which the pipes are subjected in use.
